### [1] [Regge-eikonal description of high-energy proton-proton elastic scattering: a single-channel Gaussian benchmark confronted with TOTEM data at $\sqrt{s}=13$ TeV](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28140)
*Ji Li*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper eikonalizes a single-Pomeron Regge Born amplitude with Gaussian t-dependence in impact-parameter space, deriving closed-form expressions for cross sections and forward slope using the exponential integral Ein(a) and series I₁(a). It tests the model against TOTEM 13 TeV data and finds that a single-Gaussian opacity fails to simultaneously describe total/elastic cross sections and the diffractive dip position; signature-phase restoration improves ρ but not the dip shape; multi-component or two-channel extensions only partially alleviate the discrepancy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether a minimal eikonal model—based on a single Pomeron exchange with Gaussian opacity—can simultaneously reproduce key observables in high-energy pp scattering at 13 TeV: total and elastic cross sections, forward slope, and the position/magnitude of the diffractive dip in dσ/dt.

Method: Eikonalization of a Gaussian-t-dependent single-Pomeron Regge Born amplitude in impact-parameter space; analytical evaluation of the resulting purely absorptive amplitude; derivation of closed-form expressions for σ_tot, σ_el, σ_inel, and B (forward slope) in terms of the entire exponential integral Ein(a) and auxiliary series I₁(a); comparison with numerical integration and Born limit; confrontation with TOTEM 13 TeV data using two parameterization strategies (DL trajectory fixed at 7 TeV vs. direct fit to σ_tot and σ_el/σ_tot); systematic extensions including signature phase, two-Gaussian opacity, and Good-Walker two-channel eikonal.

Result: A single Gaussian opacity cannot simultaneously fit σ_tot (overpredicts by ~26%), σ_el/σ_tot, and the diffractive dip position (predicted zero at |t| = 0.244 or 0.315 GeV² vs. measured dip at 0.47 GeV²); inclusion of signature phase yields ρ = 0.098 (consistent with data) but fills—not shifts—the dip; two-Gaussian opacity reduces dip excess from factor 15 to 2, yet fails to reproduce dip-bump structure and large-|t| tail; Good-Walker eikonal moves the minimum further from data.

Conclusion: The failure of the single-Gaussian eikonal model to jointly describe integrated cross sections and the diffractive dip geometry signals the need for more sophisticated opacity structure—e.g., non-Gaussian profile, dynamical fluctuations, or genuine multi-channel coupling—beyond simple Regge-based eikonalization.

Abstract: A single-Pomeron Regge Born amplitude with a Gaussian dependence on the momentum transfer is eikonalized in impact-parameter space, and the resulting single-channel, purely absorptive amplitude is evaluated in closed form. The total, elastic and inelastic cross sections and the forward slope are expressed through the entire exponential integral Ein(a) and a related series $I_1(a)$, and the closed forms are verified against direct numerical integration and against the weak-coupling Born limit. Two parameter choices are confronted with the TOTEM measurements at $\sqrt{s}=13$ TeV. With the Donnachie-Landshoff trajectory and a normalization fixed at 7 TeV, the model gives $\sigma_{tot}=139.2$ mb against the measured $110.6\pm3.4$ mb and places the first diffractive zero at $|t|=0.244$ GeV$^2$ against the measured dip at 0.47 GeV$^2$. Calibration of the two parameters to the measured $\sigma_{tot}$ and $\sigma_{el}/\sigma_{tot}$ describes the differential cross section to within five per cent for $|t|\lesssim0.1$ GeV$^2$, but places the zero at 0.315 GeV$^2$ and overestimates the cross section at the measured dip by more than an order of magnitude. A single Gaussian opacity therefore cannot simultaneously accommodate the integrated cross sections and the position of the diffractive dip. Restoring the signature phase gives $\rho=0.098$, in agreement with the TOTEM and ATLAS measurements, and fills the zero without moving it; a two-component opacity reduces the excess at the dip from a factor of 15 to a factor of 2 without reproducing the dip-bump structure and the large-$|t|$ tail simultaneously; a two-channel Good-Walker eikonal on a Gaussian profile moves the minimum further away from the measured dip.

</details>


### [2] [Design of a Resonator Array Axion Haloscope and its Projected Sensitivity from 2 to 4 GHz](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28500)
*ADMX Collaboration,Taj A. Dyson,Mohamed H. Awida,Chelsea L. Bartram,Christian Boutan,Thomas Braine,James H. Buckley,Thomas J. Caligiure,Madeleine Carhart,Gianpaolo Carosi,Aaron S. Chou,Nicholas Christman,John Clarke,Edward J. Daw,Nick Du,Leanne D. Duffy,Sean Durham,Kieran Enzian,Graeme Flower,Chandrashekhar Gaikwad,Joseph R. Gleason,Cyrus Goodman,Maxim Goryachev,Charles Hanretty,Michael T. Hatzon,Alexander T. Hipp,Julia Hoffman,Matthew Hollister,Mark Jones,Rakshya Khatiwada,On Kim,Stefan Knirck,Daria Kowsari,Chao-Lin Kuo,Erik W. Lentz,Morgan J. Lynn,Ben T. McAllister,Donald V. Mitchell,Claude Mostyn,Kater W. Murch,Trinh N. L. Nguyen,Noah S. Oblath,Emma C. I. Paterson,Mitchell G. Perry,Aaron Quiskamp,Sephora Ruppert,B. Jordan Russell,Gray Rybka,Pierre Sikivie,Raj A. Singh,James Sinnis,Mitchell E. Solano,Andrew Sonnenschein,Neil S. Sullivan,David B. Tanner,Matthew S. Taubman,Jonathan R. Tedeschi,Rick J. Tesarek,Michael E. Tobar,Matthew O. Withers,Andrew K. Yi,Barkotel F. Zemenu,Dan Zhang,Zijun C. Zhao,Kaiwen Zheng*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: ADMX-EFR is a next-generation haloscope designed to extend axion dark matter searches from 8–16 μeV (2–4 GHz) with projected sensitivity below the DFSZ benchmark in 36 months, using an 18-cavity array, low-noise cryogenic electronics, and digital coherent signal combination.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend axion dark matter search coverage beyond 8 μeV (2 GHz), where previous ADMX runs left a gap, and probe theoretically well-motivated QCD axion parameter space up to 16 μeV (4 GHz), including below the DFSZ coupling benchmark.

Method: Design and simulation-based performance projection of ADMX-EFR: an 18-cavity (1 m each) haloscope operating in a high-field magnet and dilution refrigerator; individual low-noise amplifier chains per cavity; digital coherent signal combination; frequency-dependent scan rate estimation via electromagnetic and electronics simulations, validated with prior ADMX diagnostic data; downtime modeling integrated into sensitivity projection.

Result: Projected instantaneous scan rate and total experiment runtime yield sensitivity below the DFSZ axion model across 8–16 μeV (2–4 GHz) within 36 months of operation.

Conclusion: ADMX-EFR is a scalable, technically feasible extension of ADMX that closes a critical gap in axion DM parameter space and achieves discovery-level sensitivity for QCD axions in the 2–4 GHz band under realistic operational assumptions.

Abstract: The QCD axion is a dark matter (DM) candidate particle which can also solve the long-standing strong charge-parity problem. The Axion Dark Matter eXperiment (ADMX) searches for resonantly-enhanced conversion of axion DM into photons in a strong magnetic field. Previous versions of the experiment probed most QCD axion DM from 2.7 $\mu$eV to 4.2 $\mu$eV, with ongoing and planned runs poised to cover up to 8 $\mu$eV (2 GHz). We present the design of a haloscope with 18 cavities 1 m in length read out using individual low-noise amplifier chains and digital coherent signal combination called ADMX extended frequency range (EFR). We discuss details on magnet and cryogenic infrastructure, cavity design, cold electronics and efficiency of coherent signal combination. We use detailed cavity and electronics simulations, as well as diagnostic data from previous ADMX runs, to estimate frequency-dependent instantaneous scan rate. When paired with a comprehensive study of experiment downtime, a projection of final axion coupling sensitivity is produced. Under the stated assumptions, the projected sensitivity is projected to reach below the DFSZ axion benchmark model from 8 $\mu$eV to 16 $\mu$eV (2 GHz to 4 GHz) in 36 months.

</details>


### [3] [Lorentz-invariant fiducial decay selection for the ATLAS $H\to ZZ^{*}\to 4\ell$ STXS measurement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30272)
*ATLAS Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper recasts ATLAS Higgs boson cross-section measurements in the H→4ℓ channel into a Lorentz-invariant fiducial (LIF) region to enable robust EFT interpretations by decoupling decay-side acceptance from Wilson coefficient dependence, while showing reconstruction efficiency remains largely EFT-independent.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard ATLAS STXS measurements in H→ZZ*→4ℓ use a fiducial definition based on SM decay kinematics; this introduces EFT dependence in the acceptance when interpreting results in effective field theory — compromising model independence.

Method: The authors define a single Lorentz-invariant fiducial (LIF) region for H→4ℓ decays, absorb EFT-dependent decay acceptance into the signal parametrization, and validate EFT independence of reconstruction efficiency using 13.6 TeV MC simulation (165 fb⁻¹); they quantify LIF acceptance stability and efficiency variations across Wilson coefficient space.

Result: LIF acceptance in SM is A^LIF_SM = 0.8546, production-independent at 0.07% level and stable across STXS bins at 0.19%; <1% of reconstructed signal lies outside LIF; reconstruction efficiency varies by ≤6% over probed EFT parameter space — significantly less than typical systematic uncertainties.

Conclusion: Existing inclusive STXS measurements can be reliably rescaled to the LIF phase space, enabling direct, model-independent EFT interpretations without applying EFT-dependent reconstruction-efficiency corrections.

Abstract: ATLAS measurements of Higgs boson production-mode cross-sections and Simplified Template Cross Sections (STXS), $\sigma\cdot B$, in the $H\to ZZ^{*}\to 4\ell$ decay channel rely on selection factors that fold in a decay-side fiducial acceptance evaluated with Standard Model decay kinematics. In an effective-field-theory interpretation that acceptance depends on the Wilson coefficients themselves. We recast these production-side $\sigma\cdot B$ results into a single Lorentz-invariant fiducial (LIF) decay region defined by the $H\to 4\ell$ decay, absorbing the EFT-dependent fiducial acceptance into the signal parametrisation, while the remaining reconstruction-efficiency corrections are shown to be largely EFT-independent. Using the Higgs boson signal Monte Carlo simulation at $\sqrt{s}=13.6$ TeV, corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 165 fb$^{-1}$, the LIF acceptance in the Standard Model is found to be $A^{LIF}_{SM}=0.8546$, production-independent to 0.07% and stable across STXS bins to 0.19%. Less than 1% of the reconstructed signal events originate from outside the LIF region, and the reconstruction efficiency for events in the region changes by at most 6% over the probed ranges of the EFT Wilson coefficients, as in the fiducial phase space of the published analysis. Consequently, the existing inclusive STXS cross-section measurements can be rescaled to the corresponding LIF fiducial phase space, allowing for EFT interpretations without EFT-dependent corrections for the reconstruction efficiency.

</details>


### [4] [Geometry-conditioned differentiable surrogate with adjoint Monte Carlo sampling for scintillator detector co-design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30580)
*Lorenzo Pepa,Mohammad Ali Marashli,Nicolas Babio Elguero,Tommaso Dorigo,Irene Fagnani,Carlo Gulizia,Pablo Martinez Ruiz del Arbol,Fredrik Sandin,Pietro Vischia*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper introduces a differentiable neural surrogate model for photon transport in scintillator-based imaging detectors, trained via an adjoint (reciprocal) Monte Carlo method to enable efficient, gradient-based optimization of light-guide geometry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Direct Monte Carlo simulation of photon transport is computationally prohibitive for iterative light-guide geometry optimization; there is a need for fast, differentiable surrogates to support detector readout co-design and information-theoretic optimization.

Method: A modular, event-driven forward Monte Carlo model of scintillation photon generation and transport is paired with a reciprocal (adjoint) Monte Carlo model that launches weighted photons from the light-guide readout face to compute spatially and temporally resolved optical response. This adjoint model trains a geometry-conditioned neural network surrogate predicting collection probabilities and conditional photon transport-time distributions across a parametrized family of curved, mirrored, single-clad light guides.

Result: The trained surrogate achieves high accuracy on held-out geometries, is natively differentiable, and integrates into gradient-based optimization pipelines; validation confirms fidelity against forward-reciprocal benchmarks while revealing limitations in extrapolation beyond training geometry bounds.

Conclusion: The differentiable surrogate enables rapid optical-response evaluation and facilitates co-design of scintillator light guides and readout systems, establishing a scalable foundation for information-optimized detector development.

Abstract: Optimizing light-guide geometries for scintillator-based imaging detectors requires repeated photon-transport evaluations, making direct Monte Carlo simulation computationally costly. Differentiable surrogates can accelerate these evaluations and support detector readout co-design aimed at improving information extraction from particle interactions. In this work we describe a surrogate model for photon transport. We develop it starting from a modular, event-driven Monte Carlo model for the generation and transport of scintillation photons, and a reciprocal Monte Carlo construction (an adjoint model) of a spatially and temporally resolved optical response field. The latter evaluates the collectible light output and its temporal structure from a scintillator volume by launching weighted photon trajectories from the light-guide readout face. We apply this approach to a scintillator cell with two mirrored, curved, single-clad light guides. We employ the adjoint model to train a geometry-conditioned neural surrogate that predicts collection probabilities and conditional transport-time distributions across a constrained guide-design family. The surrogate is natively differentiable, lending itself to be an element of a differentiable pipeline for gradient-based optimization. A forward-reciprocal benchmark and validation on held-out geometries establish the accuracy of the trained model and identify its limitations. The developed tools provide a foundation for rapid optical-response studies and detector readout co-design.

</details>


### [5] [Unbinned Likelihoods for Probability Densities with Poles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30877)
*Méril Reboud,Danny van Dyk,Matteo Verzeletti*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: Proposes a pole-subtraction method to accelerate unbinned likelihood fits involving narrow resonances, decoupling resonance width from detector resolution in numerical convolution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Unbinned likelihood fits become computationally prohibitive when narrow resonances (widths ≪ detector resolution) interfere with nonresonant amplitudes, because standard convolution must resolve sub-resolution features.

Method: Analytically subtracts one simple-pole term per resonance from the truth-level PDF; the smooth remainder is convolved numerically on a grid spaced only by detector resolution, not resonance width.

Result: Demonstrated accuracy, reduced computational cost, and preserved statistical power on two benchmark problems; method maintains validity for multidimensional PDFs and those defined only on the real axis.

Conclusion: Pole subtraction is a general, practical technique for accelerating unbinned likelihood fits in presence of poles—widely applicable across experimental particle physics analyses involving resonant structures.

Abstract: Unbinned likelihood fits to detector-level data become prohibitively expensive when narrow resonances interfere with a nonresonant amplitude. The convolution of the truth-level probability density with the detector resolution must then resolve the resonances, whose widths are much smaller than the resolution. We propose a simple approach that subtracts one simple-pole term per resonance from the probability density. This term captures the resonant contribution in closed form, and the smooth remainder is convolved numerically on a grid whose spacing is set by the resolution alone. The approach applies to multidimensional densities and to densities known only on the real axis. Using two benchmark problems, we quantify the accuracy, computational cost, and statistical impact of the approach. We emphasise that the subtraction is a general approach to the computational problems of unbinned likelihoods in the presence of poles in their probability density and that it is applicable to a wide variety of problems in experimental particle physics.

</details>


### [6] [Distillation of a Normalising Flow into a Compact Symbolic Expression for Real-Time Anomaly Detection at the LHC Level-1 Trigger](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30896)
*Justin Bollhalder,Florencia Canelli,Valentina Guglielmi,Roope Oskari Niemi,Maurizio Pierini,Chang Sun,Francesco Vaselli*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper proposes distilling a normalising flow-based anomaly detection model into an explicit symbolic mathematical formula using SymbolNet with adaptive pruning, enabling ultra-low-latency (42.8 ns), hardware-efficient FPGA deployment for Level-1 trigger applications at the LHC.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable unbiased, real-time anomaly detection at the LHC's Level-1 Trigger—where latency must be < few microseconds—by eliminating reliance on expressive but slow models and avoiding trigger-induced bias.

Method: Symbolic regression via SymbolNet with adaptive pruning to distill a normalising flow teacher into a compact, interpretable, input-sparse mathematical expression; implemented and validated on a Xilinx Virtex UltraScale+ VU13P FPGA.

Result: The distilled symbolic expression contains 577 operations, matches the teacher’s discrimination performance on test data, generalises across four new physics benchmarks, achieves 9-cycle (42.8 ns) latency, and consumes only 0.81% LUTs and 0.44% DSP slices on the FPGA.

Conclusion: Symbolic distillation enables high-fidelity, ultra-low-latency, resource-efficient anomaly detection suitable for deployment in the LHC’s Level-1 trigger system, bridging the gap between expressive ML models and stringent real-time hardware constraints.

Abstract: At the CERN Large Hadron Collider, anomaly detection techniques are becoming popular alternatives to traditional signature-aware new physics searches. For this approach to avoid any bias induced by the real-time event selection applied in the trigger system of ATLAS and CMS, it should be implemented before the first stage of selection, the Level-1 Trigger. This requires deciding to accept or reject each collision within a few microseconds, a latency budget that excludes the most expressive anomaly-detection models. Knowledge distillation has successfully addressed this issue by replacing a complex anomaly-detection model with a lighter student network. This work takes the compression one step further, distilling an anomaly detection model based on a normalising flow into an explicit mathematical formula. Using the SymbolNet symbolic-regression framework with adaptive pruning, the student is a learned functional form based on a chosen subset of the inputs, providing at once feature selection and compression through knowledge distillation. The final expression contains 577 operations and matches the teacher's discrimination on a test dataset, generalising to four benchmark new physics scenarios. Implemented on a Xilinx Virtex UltraScale+ VU13P FPGA, the symbolic student has a nine-cycle latency, corresponding to 42.8\,ns at the post-route maximum clock frequency, and uses 0.81\% of the device's LUTs and 0.44\% of its DSP slices.

</details>


### [7] [Underlying-event properties in proton-proton collisions at $\sqrt{s}=13.6$ TeV measured with the ALICE detector at the LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31077)
*Jesús Eduardo Muñoz Méndez*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper presents ALICE measurements of the underlying event in pp collisions at 13.6 TeV, analyzing charged-particle and transverse-momentum densities in toward/transverse/away regions relative to the leading particle; observes a steep rise then plateau in transverse-region densities above 5 GeV/c, and compares results to lower-energy data and PYTHIA 8/EPOS4 models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize the underlying event (UE) — soft QCD activity uncorrelated with the hard scattering — at the new LHC Run 3 energy of 13.6 TeV, extending previous studies and testing Monte Carlo generator predictions in an unexplored energy regime.

Method: Analysis of LHC Run 3 pp collision data at √s = 13.6 TeV recorded by ALICE; measurement of charged-particle number density (dN_ch/dη) and summed transverse-momentum density (d∑p_T/dη) as functions of p_T^leading in toward, transverse, and away azimuthal regions; comparison to prior ALICE measurements at lower energies (√s = 0.9, 2.76, 7, 8, 13 TeV) and to PYTHIA 8 (Monash tune) and EPOS4 model predictions.

Result: Transverse-region particle and ∑p_T densities rise sharply for p_T^leading < 5 GeV/c and saturate (plateau) above that threshold; the magnitude of the plateau increases with √s, confirming stronger UE activity at higher energies; both PYTHIA 8 and EPOS4 qualitatively reproduce the plateau but quantitatively overestimate (PYTHIA 8) or underestimate (EPOS4) the measured densities in the transverse region.

Conclusion: The observed plateau behavior confirms the onset of multi-parton interactions dominating UE dynamics above p_T^leading ≈ 5 GeV/c; the energy dependence highlights increased parton density and MPI probability at 13.6 TeV; discrepancies with models suggest need for refined tuning of non-perturbative parameters—especially color reconnection and hadronization—in high-energy pp simulations.

Abstract: This contribution reports measurements characterizing the underlying event at midrapidity in pp collisions at $\sqrt{s}=13.6$ TeV, based on LHC Run 3 data collected by ALICE. The charged-particle number and summed transverse-momentum densities are measured as a function of the leading-particle transverse momentum ($p_{\rm T}^{\rm leading}$) in the toward, transverse, and away regions, defined relative to the azimuthal direction of the leading charged particle. The particle densities in the transverse region are observed to increase steeply at low $p_{\rm T}^{\rm leading}$, followed by a plateau for $p_{\rm T}^{\rm leading}>5$ GeV/$c$. The results are compared with measurements at lower center-of-mass energies to illustrate the energy dependence of the underlying-event activity, and with predictions from PYTHIA 8 and EPOS4.

</details>

### [8] [Study of the $N^*(1535)$ and $N^*(1650)$ resonances from $πN$ $S_{11}$ scattering data and lattice QCD spectra](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28691)
*Zhi-Xuan Li,Derek B. Leinweber,Anthony W. Thomas,Jia-Jun Wu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses a symmetry-constrained nonperturbative Hamiltonian framework (NPHF) with coupled-channel dynamics to simultaneously describe the $N^*(1535)$ and $N^*(1650)$ $S_{11}$ resonances, combining infinite-volume $\pi N$ scattering data and finite-volume lattice QCD spectra; it finds the spin-$1/2$ bare state is heavier than the spin-$3/2$ bare state—contrary to standard quark-model predictions—and confirms both bare states and meson-baryon couplings are essential.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve discrepancies between conventional quark-model predictions and empirical properties of the $S_{11}$ nucleon resonances $N^*(1535)$ and $N^*(1650)$, particularly their masses, internal structure, and coupling patterns, by incorporating dynamical coupled-channel effects and lattice QCD constraints in a unified, symmetry-respecting framework.

Method: Nonperturbative Hamiltonian formalism (NPHF) with SU(3)-constrained bare-state–channel vertices and Weinberg–Tomozawa channel–channel interactions; includes two three-quark bare states ($J^P = 1/2^-, 3/2^-$) and four meson-baryon channels ($\\pi N$, $\\eta N$, $K\\Lambda$, $K\\Sigma$); fits jointly to infinite-volume $\\pi N$ $S_{11}$ scattering data and finite-volume lattice QCD energy spectra; uses Hamiltonian eigenvectors for model-basis decomposition and a contamination function to match operator-mixing patterns with lattice correlation-matrix eigenvectors.

Result: The combined analysis reveals the spin-$1/2$ bare state is heavier than the spin-$3/2$ bare state—opposite to naive quark-model expectations; both bare states and coupled-channel dynamics are indispensable for reproducing resonance properties; contamination analysis validates operator assignments and aligns with recent relativistic wavefunction studies; SU(3) and spin constraints significantly reduce model parameters while enabling a unified description.

Conclusion: The symmetry-constrained NPHF provides a quantitatively consistent, unified description of $N^*(1535)$ and $N^*(1650)$, demonstrating that their observed masses and structures emerge from strong interplay between quark-core degrees of freedom and meson-baryon dynamics—not from isolated three-quark configurations. This challenges simplistic quark-model interpretations and underscores the necessity of coupled-channel and lattice-informed approaches in baryon spectroscopy.

Abstract: We investigate two of the lowest-lying odd-parity nucleon resonances, $N^*(1535)$ and $N^*(1650)$, within a nonperturbative Hamiltonian framework (NPHF). The coupled-channel model contains two three-quark bare states with total quark spins $1/2$ and $3/2$, together with four meson-baryon channels: $\pi N$, $\eta N$, $K\Lambda$, and $K\Sigma$. Constraints from SU(3) flavor symmetry and the bare-state spin wave functions are imposed on the bare-state-channel vertices and the channel-channel Weinberg-Tomozawa interactions. This substantially reduces the number of free parameters. We combine fits to infinite-volume $\pi N$ $S_{11}$ scattering data with finite-volume lattice QCD spectra. We use Hamiltonian eigenvectors to analyze the model-basis compositions. A contamination function then compares the resulting operator-mixing pattern with lattice correlation-matrix eigenvectors. Within the scenarios considered, both bare states and coupled-channel interactions play important roles in describing the two resonances. From our combined infinite and finite volume analysis, in contrast to conventional quark-model expectations, the spin-$1/2$ bare state is heavier than the spin-$3/2$ bare state. The contamination analysis is consistent with the associated operator assignment and with a recent analysis of the relativistic wavefunctions associated with these states. The symmetry-constrained NPHF provides a unified description of the two $S_{11}$ resonances. The combined analysis also constrains their model interpretation.

</details>


### [9] [Quark and gluon gravitational form factors of the nucleon at next-to-leading order](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30124)
*Ulaş Özdem*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes nucleon gravitational form factors (A, J, D, and trace form factor c̄) using three-point QCD sum rules at next-to-leading order in αₛ for both quark and gluon energy-momentum tensors in the MS-bar scheme; finds significant O(αₛ) corrections reducing all form factors, good agreement with lattice QCD for A_tot, J_tot, D_tot in 1–2.5 GeV², highest reliability for D due to perturbative dominance, and limitations in gluon-sector modeling and Borel window constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reliably compute the quark and gluon contributions to the nucleon's gravitational form factors — especially the poorly constrained D-term and trace anomaly-related form factor c̄ — using QCD sum rules with consistent higher-order accuracy, while assessing methodological robustness via conservation identities and comparison to lattice QCD.

Method: Three-point QCD sum rules at next-to-leading order in αₛ for both quark and gluon energy-momentum tensors in the MS-bar scheme; uses antisymmetric Borel moments for protected estimators of D and c̄; separates quark/gluon contributions; analyzes Borel stability, condensate contributions, and conservation identity violations.

Result: O(αₛ) corrections reduce A_tot by 32–40%, J_tot by 22–27%, |D_tot| by 21–27%; at Q² = 1.5 GeV²: A_tot = 0.337 ± 0.113, J_tot = 0.206 ± 0.100, D_tot = −0.206 ± 0.121; total form factors agree with lattice in 1–2.5 GeV²; D is most reliable (perturbative correlator, quark/gluon parts match lattice); gluon contributions to A and J are suppressed relative to lattice, likely due to incomplete gluonic coupling beyond exchange + condensate; conservation identities show ~2σ deviation suggesting missing finite g_μν term; c̄ reported only at leading order with condensates.

Conclusion: QCD sum rules at NLO in αₛ provide quantitatively improved, largely consistent estimates of nucleon gravitational form factors, especially D, but reveal structural limitations: gluon EMT modeling is incomplete at this order, Borel analysis restricts validity to β ≈ 1, and separation of constant transition terms is unfeasible; thus, D emerges as the most trustworthy probe of nucleon mechanical structure within this framework.

Abstract: We compute the quark and gluon gravitational form factors $A$, $J$ and $D$ of the nucleon in three-point QCD sum rules at next-to-leading order in $\alpha_s$ for the quark and gluon energy-momentum tensors (EMT), treating both at the same order in the $\overline{\rm MS}$ scheme. The nucleon EMT also tests the method, showing where the sum rules are reliable and why. Protected estimators of $D$ and of the trace form factor $\bar c$ require antisymmetric Borel moments, since none with equal Borel masses alone is protected. The $\mathcal{O}(\alpha_s)$ corrections reduce $A_{\rm tot}$ by $32$--$40\%$, $J_{\rm tot}$ by $22$--$27\%$ and $|D_{\rm tot}|$ by $21$--$27\%$, since the perturbative share of the two-point function is much larger. The total form factors agree with lattice QCD for $1\le Q^2\le2.5$~GeV$^2$. At $Q^2=1.5$~GeV$^2$, $A_{\rm tot}=0.337\pm0.113$, $J_{\rm tot}=0.206\pm0.100$ and $D_{\rm tot}=-0.206\pm0.121$. $D$ is the most reliable result: its correlator is mostly perturbative, and its quark and gluon parts agree with the lattice. In $A$ and $J$, a local gluon-condensate term that does not fall with $Q^2$ is the largest, and their gluon shares are well below the lattice, possibly because the gluon EMT couples at this order only through gluon exchange and the condensate. A working region exists only near $\beta=1$, and the constant part of transitions cannot be separated within the Borel windows. The individual conservation identities deviate at one point by two standard deviations, consistent with a missing finite term in the $g_{\mu\nu}$ structure, and $\bar c$ is therefore given at leading order with condensates.

</details>


### [10] [Pion scattering in finite volume within the Inverse Amplitude Method: Extension to moving frames](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30475)
*Julián A. Sánchez,Raquel Molina,Ángel Gómez Nicola*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes finite-volume corrections to ππ scattering near threshold using the one-loop Inverse Amplitude Method (IAM), extending beyond Lüscher’s formalism by including left-hand cut and t-, u-channel/tadpole contributions; it finds these exponentially suppressed corrections become comparable to or larger than the leading Lüscher term for m_πL ≲ 1.5–2.8 depending on frame, and only negligible for m_πL ≳ 5.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve precision in lattice QCD extractions of ππ scattering parameters by quantifying subleading finite-volume effects—especially those omitted in Lüscher’s method—near threshold in both rest and moving frames.

Method: Applies the one-loop Inverse Amplitude Method (IAM) to compute I = 0 and I = 2 ππ scattering amplitudes in a finite box with total momentum P⃗ = 2πd⃗/L; explicitly includes left-hand cut, t- and u-channel exchanges, and tadpole diagrams beyond Lüscher’s quantization condition; derives resulting corrections to near-threshold energy levels.

Result: Finite-volume corrections become comparable to the leading Lüscher term for m_πL ≲ 1.5–1.7 in the rest frame and up to m_πL ≲ 2.8 for d⃗ = [002] moving frame; corrections remain significant well beyond typical lattice volumes and only vanish for m_πL ≳ 5.

Conclusion: Standard Lüscher analysis underestimates systematic finite-volume errors near threshold, especially in moving frames; reliable extraction of ππ scattering lengths requires inclusion of these higher-order exponential corrections when m_πL < 5.

Abstract: We study the leading exponentially suppressed finite-volume corrections to $\pi\pi$ scattering near threshold in moving frames. Using the one-loop Inverse Amplitude Method (IAM), we compute the $I=0$ and $I=2$ amplitudes in a box with total momentum $\vec P=2\pi\vec d/L$, including the left-hand cut and the $t$-, $u$-channel and tadpole contributions neglected in Lüscher's quantization condition. We derive the resulting correction to the near-threshold energy levels and measure its size relative to the Lüscher term. We find that it becomes comparable to the latter for $m_\pi L\lesssim 1.5$--$1.7$ at rest, and that it is larger in moving frames, reaching $m_\pi L\lesssim 2.8$ for $\vec d=[002]$. The corrections become negligible only for $m_\pi L\gtrsim 5$.

</details>


### [11] [Revealing Dark Matter in the Dark-Electron Rate of Xenon Detectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28211)
*Lillian Santos-Olmsted,Rebecca K. Leane,Carlos Blanco,John F. Beacom*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes using the total raw dark-electron rate in xenon-based dark matter experiments to perform an annual-modulation search, improving sensitivity to sub-GeV DM-electron scattering by scaling with √B instead of B, leveraging existing DAQ infrastructure without hardware upgrades.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing xenon experiments suffer severely reduced exposure (e.g., ~10⁻⁴ retained) in the single-electron regime due to aggressive background cuts, limiting sensitivity to low-mass dark matter scattering off electrons; a more efficient statistical approach is needed.

Method: Proposes an annual-modulation analysis of the total dark-electron hit rate — a continuously recorded, triggerless observable in modern xenon DAQs (XENONnT, PandaX-4T, LZ) — enabling sensitivity scaling as S ≲ √B rather than S ≲ B, and estimates projected sensitivities using measured dark-electron rates.

Result: Projected sensitivity gains are dramatic across current (XENONnT, LZ, PandaX-4T) and future (PandaX-xT, XLZD) experiments, showing orders-of-magnitude improvement over standard cut-based analyses in the sub-GeV DM-electron scattering regime.

Conclusion: Annual modulation of the dark-electron rate is a powerful, hardware-free strategy to significantly enhance sub-GeV DM-electron sensitivity in xenon detectors, urging reanalysis of existing data and integration into future analysis frameworks.

Abstract: There is high interest in improving experimental sensitivity to sub-GeV dark matter (DM) that scatters with electrons, as compelling theoretical models seem within reach. At the lowest DM masses, most of the expected signal lies in the single-electron regime, but so do large backgrounds. Xenon experiments therefore either do not yet search there at all or, as in XENONnT, apply cuts so severe that only $\sim$$10^{-4}$ of the exposure is retained, with sensitivity set by the criterion $S \lesssim B$, where those are the signal and background counts. We advocate that xenon detectors instead utilize most of their dark-electron rate (i.e., the total raw electron hit rate) and perform an annual-modulation search, for which the sensitivity scales as $S \lesssim \sqrt{B}$. Modern DAQs already record this rate, continuously in triggerless systems such as the XENONnT and PandaX-4T DAQs, and within trigger windows in LZ, so no new hardware is required. Using measured dark-electron rates, we estimate the sensitivity of XENONnT, LZ, PandaX-4T, PandaX-xT, and XLZD. We find that dramatic gains in sensitivity are possible, motivating new analyses of present and future xenon data.

</details>


### [12] [Vector boson scattering in composite Higgs models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28214)
*Rosy Caliri,Manuel Kunkel,Werner Porod*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies vector boson scattering in Composite Higgs models with a fermionic origin and a single pseudo-Nambu-Goldstone Higgs, showing that although LHC data constrain new resonances to be above ~2 TeV, $WZjj$ production—especially with longitudinally polarized bosons—exhibits significant SM-deviating tails in kinematic distributions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the electroweak symmetry breaking mechanism via vector boson scattering, particularly in fermionic Composite Higgs models where only one pseudo-Nambu-Goldstone boson plays the role of the Higgs.

Method: Analyzes $WZjj$ production as a benchmark process; examines kinematic distribution tails (e.g., invariant masses, rapidities) for deviations from Standard Model predictions, with emphasis on longitudinal vector boson polarization.

Result: Significant deviations from SM predictions are found in the high-energy tails of distributions—even when resonances are excluded below 2 TeV by current LHC data—especially for longitudinally polarized $W$ and $Z$ bosons.

Conclusion: Vector boson scattering in $WZjj$ provides a sensitive probe of Composite Higgs dynamics beyond direct resonance searches, with longitudinal polarization enhancing sensitivity to the underlying symmetry breaking structure.

Abstract: Vector boson scattering is a powerful tool to test the mechanism of electroweak symmetry breaking. We focus on Composite Higgs models with an underlying fermionic description and assume that only one pseudo Nambu-Goldstone boson contributes to electroweak symmetry breaking, which we identify as the Higgs state of the Standard Model. In these scenarios, LHC data imply lower bounds on the predicted resonances below 2 TeV depending on the scenario. Using $WZjj$ production as a benchmark, we demonstrate that the tails of various distributions nevertheless show significant deviations from Standard Model predictions. This holds in particular for the case of longitudinally polarised vector bosons.

</details>


### [13] [Timelike electric dipole form factors of the baryon octet: short-distance kernels and baryonic rescattering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28492)
*Chao-Qiang Geng,Xiang-Nan Jin,Chia-Wei Liu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the leading-twist contribution of quark electric dipole moments (EDMs), especially d_s, to timelike baryon octet form factors at the J/ψ mass using lattice light-cone distribution amplitudes; it finds the Σ/Λ short-distance ratio deviates from the nonrelativistic quark model, attributes enhanced Σ contribution to kaon exchange, derives an upper limit |d_s| < 1.3×10⁻¹⁵ e·cm from BESIII data, and shows joint multi-baryon fits with rescattering improve sensitivity by >25%.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To constrain the strange-quark electric dipole moment (d_s) — a probe of CP violation beyond the Standard Model — via its effect on timelike electromagnetic form factors of baryon octet states measured in e⁺e⁻ → baryon-antibaryon processes at charmonium energies.

Method: Lattice QCD calculation of light-cone distribution amplitudes for baryons; leading-twist factorization applied to timelike baryon form factors at the J/ψ mass; incorporation of meson-exchange (especially K-exchange) and rescattering effects using effective models; statistical and systematic error propagation including renormalization-scale dependence.

Result: Short-distance Σ/Λ form factor ratio = −0.080⁺⁰·⁰³⁷₋₀.₀₉₈, exceeding the nonrelativistic quark-model prediction of −1/3; Λ form factor ≈ 5×10⁻⁴ d_s; BESIII data yields |d_s| < 1.3×10⁻¹⁵ e·cm (95% CL); joint Λ/Σ⁺/Ξ analysis with 1 GeV rescattering improves Λ-only limit by >25%; renormalization-scale variation alters projected limits by ~factor of two.

Conclusion: Lattice-based leading-twist analysis combined with meson-exchange and rescattering modeling significantly enhances sensitivity to d_s; multi-channel global fits are essential for optimal EDM constraints from timelike baryon form factors, and theoretical uncertainties — especially scale dependence — remain dominant.

Abstract: We calculate the leading-twist contribution of quark electric dipole moments (EDMs) to the timelike form factors of the baryon octet at the $J/\psi$ mass using lattice light-cone distribution amplitudes. For a strange-quark EDM $d_s$, the short-distance $\Sigma/\Lambda$ ratio is $-0.080^{+0.037}_{-0.098}$ over the lattice input ranges. This range lies above the nonrelativistic quark-model value $-1/3$. In the meson-exchange model, kaon exchange feeds the large $\Xi$ source into the $\Sigma$ channel and makes the $\Sigma$ and $\Lambda$ form factors comparable in magnitude. The short-distance $\Lambda$ form factor is about $5\times10^{-4}d_s$. The BESIII measurement then gives $|d_s|<1.3\times10^{-15}\,e\,\mathrm{cm}$ at 95% confidence level. With rescattering at a $1\,\mathrm{GeV}$ cutoff, joint fits to projected $\Lambda$, $\Sigma^+$ and $\Xi$ sensitivities lower the $\Lambda$-only upper limits by more than 25% at BESIII and the Super Tau-Charm Facility. Renormalization-scale variation changes the projected limits by about a factor of two.

</details>


### [14] [Towards a Global CE$ν$NS Picture](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30638)
*Heerak Banerjee,Philipp Eller*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a Bayesian framework using a generalized parametric basis to extract and interpret the CEνNS differential cross-section from COHERENT CsI and liquid argon data, yielding competitive normalization constraints and enhanced spectral shape sensitivity via combined analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome limitations in interpreting CEνNS data—such as model dependence and limited spectral sensitivity—with a flexible, model-agnostic framework that enables robust extraction of both normalization and spectral shape of the differential cross-section.

Method: A generalized parametric basis built from universal kinematic terms is used to represent the CEνNS differential cross-section; Bayesian inference is applied to COHERENT CsI and LAr datasets individually and jointly; modular forward modeling, parallel computation, and automatic differentiation are implemented in an open-source Julia package.

Result: Normalization constraints consistent with COHERENT Collaboration results; improved constraints on correlated spectral shape; enhanced sensitivity to cross-section features through combined CsI+LAr analysis due to complementary nuclear form factor and energy-threshold responses.

Conclusion: The framework provides a robust, reusable, and model-flexible approach for CEνNS data interpretation, enabling more precise and physically interpretable extraction of neutrino-nucleus interaction parameters beyond standard-model expectations.

Abstract: We present a general framework for extracting and interpreting the Coherent Elastic Neutrino-Nucleus Scattering (CE$\nu$NS) differential cross-section from experimental data. A generalized parametric basis, constructed from common kinematic terms recurring in generalized neutrino interaction models, is employed to numerically constrain the shape and normalization of the observed CE$\nu$NS response. The framework was applied to the COHERENT CsI and liquid argon datasets using Bayesian inference, yielding normalization constraints comparable to those reported by the Collaboration while providing additional constraints on the correlated spectral shape. A combined analysis was also performed, exploiting the complementary sensitivities of the two datasets to constrain features of the differential cross-section that are less accessible to either dataset individually. The framework has been implemented in the open-source Julia package this http URL, which supports modular forward modeling, parallel computation, and automatic differentiation.

</details>


### [15] [Exact analytic solutions for the $n=1$ chameleon field in planar systems with an arbitrary number of uniform layers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30712)
*Chad Briddon,Mario Pitschmann*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives exact analytic solutions for the n=1 chameleon scalar field in a planar multi-layer system, improves upon prior approximate methods, and applies them to the full 15-layer CANNEX experimental geometry—finding up to ~7% higher chameleon pressure but insufficient sensitivity gain to constrain new parameter space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The lack of exact analytic solutions for non-linear screened scalar field models (e.g., chameleon) limits theoretical precision and experimental forecast reliability; existing approximations often oversimplify realistic experimental geometries like CANNEX.

Method: Constructs exact analytic solutions for the n=1 chameleon field equation in a piecewise uniform planar medium with N discrete density layers; applies boundary conditions across interfaces; computes chameleon-induced pressure in the full 15-layer CANNEX configuration (not simplified two-body approximations).

Result: Exact solutions obtained for arbitrary N-layer planar systems; applied to CANNEX yields up to ~7% increase in predicted chameleon pressure versus two-body approximations; however, projected experimental sensitivity remains insufficient to exclude new regions of chameleon parameter space.

Conclusion: Exact analytic treatment of layered geometries is necessary for quantitative fifth-force forecasts, but current CANNEX sensitivity—even with full 15-layer modeling—does not extend constraints beyond existing bounds for the n=1 chameleon model.

Abstract: Scalar fields have long been investigated as a potential dark energy candidate. Of these models, screened scalar fields like the chameleon have received interest due to their ability to avoid observational constraints. However, the non-linear nature of these models has led to there being only a small number of exact analytic solutions within the literature. In this work, we present exact analytic solutions for the $n=1$ chameleon model in a planar system consisting of $N$ discrete regions of uniform density. We compare these to the relevant approximate solutions within the literature. We also use our solutions to forecast constraints from CANNEX, a recent experiment devised to measure Casimir forces and detect hypothetical fifth forces. Unlike in previous works, we consider the full fifteen-layered setup of CANNEX, as opposed to treating the plate and wall as single uniform objects. Doing so revealed an increase in the induced chameleon pressure of up to $\sim 7 \%$. However, the resulting gain is insufficient to probe new regions of parameter space at the projected sensitivities.

</details>


### [16] [A general formalism for electromagnetic production of two mesons on the nucleon](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30755)
*Reddy Malik Priandika,Samson Clymton,Terry Mart*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a general covariant formalism for electromagnetic double-meson production on nucleons (γ_v N → Φ₁Φ₂B), extending the Berends et al. decomposition to 16 gauge- and Lorentz-invariant current matrices; it maps Dirac amplitudes onto CGLN-like Pauli amplitudes to yield differential cross sections and response functions, demonstrates gauge invariance restoration in tree-level Born calculations for γp → π⁰π⁰p and γp → π⁺π⁰n near threshold, and establishes a unified framework for future coupled-channel photo- and electroproduction studies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a systematic, gauge-invariant, and model-independent formalism for double-meson photo- and electroproduction off nucleons—capable of handling both single- and coupled-channel analyses across broad energy ranges—addressing the lack of such a framework compared to well-established treatments for single-meson production.

Method: Covariant decomposition of the hadronic current into 16 gauge- and Lorentz-invariant Dirac matrices (4 vanish for real photons); mapping to CGLN-like Pauli amplitudes; derivation of five-fold differential cross section and transverse/longitudinal/interference response functions; explicit tree-level calculation using nucleon Born diagrams with inclusion of hadronic form factors to enforce gauge invariance.

Result: A complete, manifestly gauge-invariant amplitude decomposition is constructed; the formalism yields symmetric, simplified expressions for observables; explicit demonstration that gauge invariance—broken at the diagram level—is restored in the total amplitude and preserved under inclusion of form factors; quantitative predictions for near-threshold γp → π⁰π⁰p and γp → π⁺π⁰n cross sections.

Conclusion: The proposed formalism provides a versatile, first-principles basis for theoretical modeling and phenomenological analysis of double-meson photo- and electroproduction, enabling consistent treatment across channels, energies, and models while ensuring fundamental constraints like gauge and Lorentz invariance are rigorously satisfied.

Abstract: We develop a general formalism for the electromagnetic production of two mesons on the nucleon, $\gamma_{\rm v}N\to\Phi_1\Phi_2B$, that can be used in single-channel as well as coupled-channel analyses over a wide range of energies. In the spirit of the decomposition introduced by Berends et al. for single-meson electroproduction, the hadronic current is expanded in a set of 16 gauge- and Lorentz-invariant matrices, four of which vanish for real photons. The corresponding Lorentz-invariant (Dirac) amplitudes can be obtained from any reaction model and are mapped onto 16 CGLN-like Pauli amplitudes, from which the five-fold differential cross section and its transverse, longitudinal, and interference response functions follow directly. Although these matrices are not all linearly independent, they follow directly from the covariant decomposition of the current, so the amplitudes can be read off from the Feynman diagrams and the observables take a simpler, more symmetric form. As a first application, we calculate the $\gamma p\to\pi^0\pi^0p$ and $\gamma p\to\pi^+\pi^0n$ reactions near threshold from tree-level nucleon Born terms. This example shows explicitly how the Dirac amplitudes are read off from Feynman diagrams and how gauge invariance, which is violated by the individual diagrams, is restored in the total amplitude and preserved when hadronic form factors are included. The formalism thus provides a versatile basis for future theoretical and phenomenological studies of double-meson photo- and electroproduction. A more complete abstract is provided in the paper.

</details>


### [17] [Light on GeV-scale seesaw](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30780)
*Enrico Bertuzzo,Michele Frigerio,Salvador Urrea*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores how photon transition dipoles in sterile neutrinos (N1, N2) can enhance production and enable new decay channels, making minimal seesaw-predicted GeV-scale sterile neutrinos detectable in T2K and SHiP experiments via charged-lepton pair signatures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the experimental inaccessibility of minimal seesaw-predicted tiny active-sterile mixing for GeV-scale sterile neutrinos, by leveraging electromagnetic transition dipoles to boost production and open new decay modes.

Method: Theoretical analysis of sterile neutrino production and decay via photon transition dipoles; identification and evaluation of charged-lepton pair signatures from off-shell photons; sensitivity projections for T2K near detector and SHiP experiment.

Result: T2K near detector can probe seesaw parameter space for m_N ≲ 500 MeV; SHiP extends reach to m_N ≲ 5 GeV; for dipole moment d ≳ (10 TeV)⁻¹, SHiP can resolve decay channels dependent on active-sterile mixing, yielding a characteristic signature hierarchy.

Conclusion: Photon transition dipoles render minimal seesaw sterile neutrinos experimentally accessible; the predicted hierarchical signature pattern in SHiP would constitute a direct test of the seesaw mechanism.

Abstract: The next generation of beam-dump and accelerator-neutrino experiments will probe GeV-scale sterile neutrinos with unprecedented sensitivity. However, the tiny active-sterile mixing predicted by the minimal seesaw mechanism is expected to remain out of reach. Oscillation data require at least two sterile neutrinos, $N_1$ and $N_2$, which may couple to the photon through a transition dipole. Such a coupling can radically change the picture, both by strongly enhancing sterile-neutrino production and by opening new decay channels. We identify charged-lepton pairs produced by an off-shell photon as the most promising experimental signature. We show that the seesaw band can be probed by the T2K near detector for sterile-neutrino masses $m_N\lesssim 500$ MeV, and by SHiP for $m_N\lesssim 5$ GeV. For a sufficiently large dipole, $d\gtrsim (10~\mathrm{TeV})^{-1}$, SHiP can selectively measure decay channels that require nonzero active-sterile mixing. This would give rise to a distinctive hierarchy of signatures, providing a direct test of the seesaw mechanism.

</details>


### [18] [Diffractive Photoproduction of High $p_T$ Photons in Ultraperipheral Collisions at the LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30966)
*João V. Bulhões,Victor P. Goncalves*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies diffractive photoproduction of high-pT photons in ultraperipheral LHC collisions (pp, pPb, PbPb), incorporating NLO BFKL Pomeron effects to assess observability and constrain high-energy QCD dynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe BFKL dynamics and high-energy QCD via a clean diffractive channel—photoproduction of large-pT photons—in ultraperipheral collisions, where photon–hadron interactions dominate and hadronic contamination is minimal.

Method: Theoretical calculation of cross sections and differential distributions for diffractive photoproduction of large-pT photons using the BFKL formalism; includes next-to-leading-order (NLO) corrections to the BFKL characteristic function and considers multiple collision systems (pp, pPb, PbPb) and alternative Pomeron models.

Result: Predicted cross sections are sizable across all three collision systems; differential distributions show sensitivity to BFKL resummation and NLO corrections; feasibility of experimental measurement is confirmed for current and future LHC runs.

Conclusion: Diffractive photoproduction of large-pT photons in UPCs provides a promising channel to test and constrain BFKL evolution and advance understanding of high-energy QCD, especially with upcoming high-statistics data from LHC.

Abstract: We investigate diffractive photoproduction of large-transverse-momentum photons in ultraperipheral collisions at the LHC, considering different collision systems and alternative descriptions of the BFKL Pomeron. We estimate cross sections and differential distributions including NLO corrections to the BFKL characteristic function and assess the prospects for measuring this process in ultraperipheral pp, pPb, and PbPb collisions. We find that the cross sections are sizable, and that future measurements in this channel can help constrain BFKL dynamics and improve our understanding of high-energy QCD.

</details>


### [19] [A proposal for an LHC search for baryon number violation in complementarity with trilepton nucleon decay searches](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31142)
*Amit Bhoonah,Francis M. Burk,Da Liu,Tong Ou,Deepak Sathyan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper bridges low-energy nucleon decay limits (Super-K) and high-energy collider searches (LHC) for Baryon Number Violation (BNV) of the $B - L/3$ type, deriving model-independent EFT constraints, showing Super-K trilepton limits are stronger than charm-baryon ones, and proposing a dedicated LHC search with enhanced sensitivity using Run 2 data and novel cuts.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile stringent low-energy trilepton nucleon decay limits from Super-Kamiokande with TeV-scale BNV, and to motivate a complementary, high-sensitivity LHC search for $B - L/3$-conserving BNV that remains viable despite those limits.

Method: Uses an effective field theory framework to derive model-independent bounds on higher-dimensional $B - L/3$-conserving BNV operators; compares loop-level trilepton nucleon decay constraints from Super-K against tree-level charm-baryon decays; constructs a UV-complete diquark–leptoquark model; performs collider phenomenology study including event simulation, background estimation, and development of optimized selection cuts for the final state ($\bar{t} \ell^+ \ell^+ \cancel{E}_T$).

Result: Super-K trilepton limits constrain the BNV scale to $\mathcal{O}(1\text{--}30)$ TeV — still allowing LHC discovery; a dedicated same-sign dilepton + $\bar{t}$ + $\cancel{E}_T$ search with tailored cuts significantly extends sensitivity beyond current ATLAS/CMS analyses; Run 2 data achieves reach complementary to Super-K and projected Hyper-K limits.

Conclusion: There exists viable parameter space for TeV-scale $B - L/3$ BNV consistent with existing nucleon decay bounds; a targeted LHC analysis with the proposed cuts provides a powerful and complementary probe, enhancing discovery potential and offering broad applicability beyond the specific diquark model.

Abstract: We investigate the compatibility of existing limits on trilepton nucleon decay by the Super-Kamiokande (Super-K) experiment with Baryon Number Violation (BNV) at the TeV scale and motivate a dedicated search using existing data at the LHC. Working within an effective field theory framework, we derive model-independent constraints on higher-dimensional operators associated with $B - L/3$ conserving texture of BNV. We demonstrate that existing Super-K limits on loop-level trilepton nucleon decay provide significantly stronger constraints compared to tree-level charm-baryon decays induced by these operators. These constraints translate into bounds of $O(1-30)$ TeV on the BNV scale, leaving open the possibility of an LHC search for $B - L/3$ type BNV. Motivated by this complementarity, we investigate the collider phenomenology of a representative UV completion featuring an $s$-channel diquark resonance that decays into a leptoquark--dilepton pair, producing a distinctive final state containing an anti-top quark, two charged anti-leptons, and missing transverse energy. Our results indicate that, although same-sign dilepton searches by ATLAS and CMS already place indirect constraints on this model, a dedicated analysis based on the selection cuts we develop significantly extends the reach for our chosen benchmarks. In particular, using only Run 2 data, we demonstrate complementary reach to limits on trilepton nucleon decay searches set by the Super-K experiment and to the projected reach of Hyper-Kamiokande. The selection cuts are applicable beyond our representative model.

</details>


### [20] [Two-loop correction to the complex-bion amplitude in a quasi-exactly solvable sextic oscillator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29701)
*Adrian M. Escobar-Ruiz*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes a quasi-exactly solvable sextic potential using diagrammatic methods, showing exact ground-state energy determination at positive coupling via factorization and Feynman diagram cancellations; for negative coupling, it identifies complex neutral bions—not instantons—as the dominant nonperturbative contribution to the ground-state energy, with two-loop diagrammatic calculation confirming the exact-WKB prediction of −3/4.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the nonperturbative structure of a quasi-exactly solvable sextic potential, particularly how factorization constraints, diagrammatic cancellations, and complex saddles (bions) govern the ground-state energy across coupling sign changes.

Method: Two diagrammatic approaches: (i) Feynman diagram expansion to verify perturbative coefficient cancellation up to third order at positive coupling; (ii) constrained family analysis with local median amplitude calculation through two loops for negative coupling, incorporating complex neutral bion contributions.

Result: At positive coupling: ground-state energy $E_0$ is exactly fixed by factorization; all perturbative coefficients vanish—confirmed diagrammatically to third order. At negative coupling: instanton one-loop contribution vanishes; leading nonperturbative correction arises from a complex neutral bion; two-loop diagrammatic sum yields $c_{\rm 2L}^{\rm diag} = -0.750000(5)$, matching exact-WKB result $-3/4$.

Conclusion: Factorization enforces exact solvability and perturbative triviality at positive coupling; analytic continuation to negative coupling reveals that complex neutral bions—not real instantons—control the leading nonperturbative energy shift, and diagrammatic quantization of constrained bion configurations reproduces exact semiclassical results.

Abstract: In this study, a quasi-exactly solvable sextic potential is analyzed by two diagrammatic calculations. At positive coupling, factorization fixes the ground-state energy $E_0$ exactly and forces every perturbative coefficient to vanish. Using only Feynman diagrams, we verify this cancellation explicitly through third order. After continuation to negative coupling, the formal zero mode becomes nonnormalizable. A real instanton remains, but its one-instanton contribution to \(E_0\) vanishes. In particular, the leading shift in energy arises from a complex neutral bion. Taking an exact constrained family, we calculate its local median amplitude through two loops. The direct diagrammatic sum gives $c_{\rm 2L}^{\rm diag}=-0.750000(5)$, in excellent agreement with the exact-WKB value $-3/4$.

</details>


### [21] [Scotogenic dark matter from gauged $L_μ+L_τ$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28223)
*J. Echeverria-Puentes,Sergey Kovalenko,Pablo San Martin Sartori,Carlos A. Vaquera-Araujo,Yoxara Villamizar,Jilberto Zamora Saa*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a scotogenic model with gauged $L_\mu + L_\tau$ symmetry, where spontaneous breaking by two units yields a remnant matter parity $M_P$, stabilizing a singlet-like CP-even scalar as viable Majorana neutrino mass-generating dark matter; it satisfies relic abundance, electroweak precision (up to $\epsilon \sim 10^{-2}$), and current leptophilic $Z'$ constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a theoretically consistent and phenomenologically viable scotogenic model that simultaneously explains neutrino masses, dark matter stability, and avoids conflicts with electroweak precision tests and collider bounds on leptophilic $Z'$ bosons.

Method: Construction of a scotogenic model based on gauged $L_\mu + L_\tau$ symmetry with spontaneous breaking by two units, yielding matter parity $M_P$; computation of thermal relic abundance for a predominantly singlet-like CP-even scalar DM candidate; analysis of $Z'$ phenomenology including kinetic mixing, electroweak precision tests, and collider signatures (visible/invisible).

Result: A viable parameter space exists where the singlet-like CP-even scalar satisfies observed DM relic density; $Z'$ remains compatible with electroweak data for kinetic mixing $\epsilon \lesssim 10^{-2}$; existing leptophilic $Z'$ searches constrain but do not exclude the model; both visible (e.g., $\mu^+\mu^-/\tau^+\tau^-$ resonances) and invisible ($Z' \to \text{DM DM}$) collider signals are possible depending on couplings and mass hierarchy.

Conclusion: The proposed $L_\mu + L_\tau$-gauged scotogenic model with matter parity provides a unified explanation of Majorana neutrino masses and scalar dark matter, with testable predictions at colliders and consistency with current experimental limits.

Abstract: In this work, we introduce the first scotogenic model based on a gauged $L_{\mu} + L_{\tau}$ symmetry whose spontaneous breaking by two units leaves a remnant matter parity $M_P$. The resulting light active neutrinos are of Majorana nature and the lightest electrically neutral state mediating the scotogenic loop is stabilized by $M_P$ and can be identified as a weakly interacting massive particle dark matter candidate. We analyze the phenomenology of a predominantly singlet-like CP-even scalar dark matter candidate and the representative regions of parameter space compatible with the observed thermal relic abundance. The $Z'$ boson is compatible with a broad range of parameters relevant for dark matter. In particular, electroweak precision constraints do not lead to significant tensions for kinetic mixing values up to $\epsilon\sim 10^{-2}$. We discuss the implications of existing searches for leptophilic gauge bosons and the conditions under which visible and invisible collider signatures become relevant.

</details>


### [22] [Chiral and deconfinement thermodynamics of the Polyakov-quark linear sigma model with and without charm quark: an SU(3) versus SU(4) comparison](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28429)
*Nourhan M. Rfeek,Hossam Wahid,Fatima M. Rfeek,Alexandra Friesen,Yuri Kalinovsky,Saleh O. Allehabi,Azzah A. Alshehri,Ashraf F. El-Sherif,Abdel Nasser Tawfik*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses the Polyakov-loop extended linear-sigma model (PLSM) to compare 3-flavor (u,d,s) and 4-flavor (u,d,s,c) formulations of strongly interacting matter, isolating charm-quark thermal effects; it reproduces lattice-QCD thermodynamics and shows charm remains suppressed near crossover (Tc ~ 210–218 MeV), contributing mainly via higher ideal-gas limit and high-T tail, with no qualitative change in crossover nature across μB.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To isolate the thermal contribution of the charm quark to QCD thermodynamics and phase structure by systematically comparing three-flavor (u,d,s) and four-flavor (u,d,s,c) PLSM formulations on equal footing, while maintaining consistency with lattice-QCD equation of state and vacuum meson spectra.

Method: Uses the Polyakov-loop extended linear-sigma model (PLSM) with explicit and spontaneous chiral symmetry breaking and a logarithmic Polyakov-loop potential calibrated to continuum lattice-QCD EoS; calculations performed within mean-field approximation for condensates, Polyakov loop, pressure, trace anomaly, entropy density, and pseudocritical boundaries across T and μB.

Result: Charm quark remains thermally suppressed (mc ≫ Tc) across the chiral crossover (Tc = 210 MeV in SU(3), 218 MeV in SU(4)); entropy density agrees with HotQCD 2+1(+1)-flavor data; SU(3) and SU(4) EoS coincide below ~2Tc after Stefan-Boltzmann normalization; charm modifies only the high-T tail and ideal-gas limit; hadron-parton transition remains a smooth crossover at all accessible μB.

Conclusion: The charm quark does not qualitatively alter the chiral or deconfinement crossover structure in the PLSM framework; its main thermodynamic role is quantitative—shifting the ideal-gas baseline and extending the high-temperature tail—without inducing a phase transition or changing crossover nature, even at finite baryon density.

Abstract: Using the Polyakov-loop extended linear-sigma model (PLSM), we investigate strongly interacting matter at finite temperature and baryon density by comparing the three-flavor (u,d,s) and four-flavor (u,d,s,c) formulations on equal footing, with the aim of isolating the thermal contribution of the charm quark. The model describes chiral dynamics through explicit and spontaneous symmetry breaking and reproduces the vacuum spectra of scalar and pseudoscalar mesons. Confinement is incorporated through a logarithmic Polyakov-loop potential calibrated to the continuum lattice-QCD equation of state (EoS). Within the mean-field approximation, we calculate light-, strange-, and charm-quark condensates, the Polyakov loop, and bulk thermodynamic observables, including pressure, trace anomaly, and entropy density, over broad ranges of temperature and baryon chemical potential. We also determine the chiral and deconfinement pseudocritical boundaries and represent them as phase boundaries and three-dimensional order-parameter surfaces. After tuning the Polyakov sector to lattice-QCD pressure and trace-anomaly data, the resulting entropy density agrees with HotQCD 2+1(+1)-flavor results throughout the crossover region. At zero baryon density, the non-strange condensate melts at Tc = 210 MeV in SU(3) and 218 MeV in SU(4), with curvature parameters kappa=0.018 and 0.017. Because mc>>Tc, charm remains thermally suppressed across the crossover. After normalization to the corresponding Stefan-Boltzmann limits, the SU(3) and SU(4) EoS nearly coincide below T~2Tc. Charm mainly contributes through a higher ideal-gas limit, a gradual high-temperature tail, and the flavor ordering u,d->s->c. For all baryon chemical potentials accessible within mean-field theory, the hadron-parton transition remains a smooth crossover.

</details>


### [23] [Compactification and non-zero density in (2+1)-dimensional four-fermion model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29823)
*K. G. Klimenko,A. N. Kozhakin,R. N. Zhokhov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates how compactification and finite fermion density affect chiral symmetry breaking in a (2+1)-dimensional Nambu–Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model with one compact spatial dimension and periodic boundary conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the interplay between dimensional reduction (via compactification), quantum statistics (finite density), and spontaneous chiral symmetry breaking in low-dimensional strongly-coupled fermionic systems.

Method: Analytical and/or numerical analysis of the NJL model in 2+1 dimensions with one spatial dimension compactified on a circle; use of periodic boundary conditions for fermions; computation of the chiral condensate as an order parameter to map the phase diagram.

Result: The phase diagram is determined, showing how critical temperature or coupling strength for chiral symmetry restoration depends on compactification radius and fermion chemical potential.

Conclusion: Compactification and finite density jointly modify the chiral phase structure—e.g., inducing new phase transitions or shifting critical points—demonstrating nontrivial dimensional crossover effects in NJL-type theories.

Abstract: The phase diagram of (2+1)-dimensional NJL type model has been studied with one compact dimension. The effect of compactification and fermion density on the chiral symmetry breaking is considered for periodic boundary conditions.

</details>


### [24] [Chiral crossover responses in a magnetic field: Finite-$μ_5$ matching in the NJL model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30535)
*Jingyi Chao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper determines the coefficient $c_r$ of a chiral chemical potential-dependent interaction term in the NJL model by matching scalar-susceptibility peak-temperature ratios to unitarized chiral perturbation theory at zero magnetic field, and demonstrates that this calibration significantly suppresses regularization dependence of pseudocritical temperature responses under finite $\mu_5$ and nonzero $eB$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the ambiguity in the coefficient $c_r$ of the $\mu_5^2(M-m_0)^2$ interaction term in the NJL model at finite chiral chemical potential, and to assess how vacuum regularization choices (SC vs. PV) combined with magnetic-field treatment schemes (D vs. MFDS) affect pseudocritical temperature responses—especially under external magnetic fields.

Method: The authors employ the Nambu-Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model with two vacuum regularization schemes—soft-cutoff (SC) and Pauli-Villars (PV)—each paired with either direct magnetic-field treatment (D) or magnetic finite-difference scheme (MFDS). They calibrate the unknown coefficient $c_r$ by matching the $eB = 0$ scalar-susceptibility peak-temperature ratio to predictions from unitarized chiral perturbation theory, yielding $c_{\mathrm{SC}} = -0.07112$ and $c_{\mathrm{PV}} = 0.22479$. These fixed coefficients are then used to compute pseudocritical temperature shifts induced by finite $\mu_5$ at $eB = 0.1, 0.2, 0.4~\mathrm{GeV}^2$.

Result: Calibrating $c_r$ at $eB=0$ reduces the spread across the four regularization/treatment combinations in the normalized $\mu_5$-induced mass-inflection temperature change by factors of ~7.5–15.3 ($\mu_5=0.15~\mathrm{GeV}$) and ~18.1–32.6 ($\mu_5=0.30~\mathrm{GeV}$); for the scalar-susceptibility peak-temperature ratio at $\mu_5=0.15~\mathrm{GeV}$, the spread reduction reaches ~74×, ~27×, and ~14× at $eB = 0.1, 0.2, 0.4~\mathrm{GeV}^2$, respectively.

Conclusion: The regularization dependence of finite-$\mu_5$ pseudocritical temperature responses in the NJL model is strongly suppressed—not only at $eB = 0$ but also at physically relevant nonzero magnetic fields—by a single calibration of $c_r$ using chiral effective theory constraints, without requiring additional $eB$-dependent tuning.

Abstract: We investigate how independent information at finite chiral chemical potential $\mu_5$ constrains pseudocritical-temperature responses in the Nambu-Jona-Lasinio model under a magnetic field. Vacuum calibration alone leaves the coefficient $c_r$ of the additional interaction $\delta\Omega_r=c_r\mu_5^2(M-m_0)^2$ undetermined. We compare soft-cutoff (SC) and Pauli-Villars (PV) vacuum regularizations, each combined with the direct treatment (D) or the magnetic finite-difference scheme (MFDS). The reference SC and PV treatments exhibit opposite finite-$\mu_5$ responses already at $eB=0$. Matching the $eB=0$ scalar-susceptibility peak-temperature ratio predicted by unitarized chiral perturbation theory gives $c_{\mathrm{SC}}=-0.07112$ and $c_{\mathrm{PV}}=0.22479$. Holding these coefficients fixed at $eB=0.1,0.2,0.4$ GeV$^2$ reduces the four-treatment spread of the normalized finite-$\mu_5$-induced mass-inflection temperature change by factors of approximately $7.5$-$15.3$ at $\mu_5=0.15$ GeV and $18.1$-$32.6$ at $\mu_5=0.30$ GeV. For the distinct scalar-susceptibility peak-temperature ratio at $\mu_5=0.15$ GeV, the spread decreases by factors of approximately $74$, $27$, and $14$ at the same magnetic fields, respectively. The reduction in regularization-dependent finite-$\mu_5$ responses thus persists at the sampled nonzero magnetic fields without further matching.

</details>


### [25] [Fate of Vainshtein Screening for UV/IR Mixing with a $P(X)$ Higgs Classicalizer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30764)
*Eléana Berteaux,Florian Nortier*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether Higgs classicalization—a non-Wilsonian UV-completion mechanism—can be realized in $P(X)$-type gauged k-essence/k-chameleon models; it finds that Vainshtein screening (required for classicalization) fails due to strong-coupling breakdown inside the screening region, ruling out this class of models—but not classicalization in general.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the little hierarchy problem by embedding classicalization in the electroweak Higgs sector, specifically testing whether $P(X)$ gauged k-essence and k-chameleon models can support Higgs classicalization.

Method: Analyzes whether $P(X)$ gauged k-essence and k-chameleon models admit Vainshtein screening around a sharply localized source—a necessary condition for classicalization—and examines the interplay with chameleon screening via gauge kinetic function, assessing strong-coupling behavior within the screening region.

Result: Vainshtein screening fails in all considered $P(X)$ models; attempts to combine it with chameleon screening still suffer from strong-coupling breakdown inside the screening region, invalidating classicalization in this framework.

Conclusion: The $P(X)$ class of gauged k-essence and k-chameleon models cannot realize Higgs classicalization due to insurmountable strong-coupling issues, though this does not preclude classicalization in other theoretical frameworks.

Abstract: Classicalization is a non-Wilsonian framework in which an effective field theory self-completes above its strong-coupling scale by generating semiclassical objects that decay into many soft quanta, thereby realizing ultraviolet-infrared mixing. A key requirement of this mechanism is that the force carrier be lighter than the strong-coupling scale. A natural application would be to embed classicalization in the electroweak Higgs sector to address the little hierarchy problem. In this work, we investigate whether Higgs classicalization can occur within the $P(X)$ class of gauged k-essence and k-chameleon models. For classicalization to occur, the theory must support Vainshtein screening around a sharply localized source. Our results show that it fails to do so, even when one attempts to engineer a symbiosis with chameleon screening through a gauge kinetic function, owing to a strong-coupling breakdown inside the screening region. These $P(X)$ theories therefore fail to provide a suitable framework for Higgs classicalization. Nevertheless, we emphasize that our results, obtained for a specific class of models, should not be interpreted as a no-go theorem for Higgs classicalization.

</details>


### [26] [New analytic constraints for the soft anomalous dimension bootstrap at three loops](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31168)
*Einan Gardi,Luke Lippstreu,Andrew J. McLeod,Laura Walsh,Zehao Zhu,Enrico Zunino*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives new constraints on the soft anomalous dimension at three loops using sequential discontinuities of Feynman integrals, applies them to bootstrap the massless and first-ever one-mass quadrupole functions, and reduces each to a single undetermined coefficient fixed by diagrammatic input.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve bootstrap approaches for the soft anomalous dimension by exploiting analytic properties—specifically sequential discontinuities in non-overlapping two-particle channels—which are underutilized compared to standard constraints like symmetry, collinear/Regge limits, and absence of unphysical branch points.
