Method: Re-examines the connection between unitarity cuts and sequential discontinuities of Feynman integrals; identifies vanishing sequential discontinuities in non-overlapping two-particle channels for all webs contributing to the four-parton quadrupole function; incorporates these as analytic constraints into a bootstrap framework over a suitable function space (e.g., multiple polylogarithms); combines with known physical limits and symmetries.

Result: New analytic constraints proven to hold for arbitrary parton masses; used to uniquely determine the massless three-loop quadrupole function up to one coefficient (previously underconstrained), and to perform the first complete bootstrap determination of the one-mass three-loop quadrupole function; all results verified against existing diagrammatic calculations.

Conclusion: Sequential discontinuities provide powerful, complementary constraints for bootstrapping multi-scale infrared singularities in QCD; this work establishes a viable path toward bootstrapping the three-loop soft anomalous dimension for amplitudes with several massive partons.

Abstract: The soft anomalous dimension governs the universal long-distance singularities of gauge theory amplitudes. It exhibits a highly constrained analytic structure and has been shown to be amenable to bootstrap methods. In this paper we revisit the relation between unitarity cuts and sequential discontinuities of Feynman integrals and use it to derive new constraints on the soft anomalous dimension. At three loops, we show that certain sequential discontinuities in non-overlapping two-particle channels vanish for every web contributing to the quadrupole function that correlates four coloured partons. These constraints hold for arbitrary parton masses, and we explicitly verify them for the known massless and one-mass cases. We then demonstrate their constraining power by substantially strengthening the massless bootstrap and carrying out the first bootstrap construction of the one-mass quadrupole function. The new constraints are largely complementary to those arising from the absence of unphysical branch points, symmetries, and collinear and Regge limits. With their help, we determine each quadrupole function within the assumed function space up to a single coefficient, which we fix by matching to existing diagrammatic results. These results open the way to bootstrapping the three-loop soft anomalous dimension for amplitudes with several massive partons.

</details>


### [27] [Superheavy gravitational dark matter from runaway bubble collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30113)
*Cheng-Wei Chiang,Van Que Tran*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes that gravity-mediated pair production of stable, gravitationally coupled particles during first-order cosmological phase transitions (via runaway bubble wall collisions) can generate the observed dark matter abundance, even for superheavy masses (~10^17 GeV) where conventional freeze-in fails.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the origin of dark matter for particles that interact only gravitationally — too weakly for thermal freeze-out and too massive for standard freeze-in — by exploiting non-thermal production during cosmological phase transitions.

Method: Computes the gravitational particle production rate of a stable Dirac fermion X during collisions of relativistic vacuum bubble walls in a first-order phase transition, using effective field theory and graviton exchange; derives analytic yield Y_X as a function of Hubble rate H, inverse transition duration β, reheating temperature T_reh, and Planck mass M_P.

Result: Obtains yield Y_X ≈ 6.8×10⁻³ (H/β)(T_reh/M_P)³; shows production remains efficient up to m_X ~ (H/β)M_P/ν², enabling viable dark matter candidates up to ~10¹⁷ GeV even when T_reh ≪ m_X.

Conclusion: Gravitational particle production from bubble wall collisions is a robust, model-independent mechanism for generating superheavy dark matter, circumventing limitations of thermal and conventional freeze-in scenarios.

Abstract: A stable particle that couples only through gravity is pair-produced in collisions of runaway bubble walls by graviton exchange. For a transition of duration $1/\beta$, a Dirac fermion reaches the yield $Y_X\simeq6.8\times10^{-3}(H/\beta)(T_{\rm reh}/M_{\rm P})^3$, where $H$ is the Hubble rate of the vacuum energy, $T_{\rm reh}$ the reheating temperature and $M_{\rm P}$ the reduced Planck mass. The yield is flat up to masses near $(H/\beta)M_{\rm P}/\nu^2$, where $\nu$ of order one sets the wall-field excursion. Gravity alone then gives dark matter up to about $10^{17}$~GeV at reheating temperatures far below its mass, where freeze-in fails.

</details>


### [28] [Heavy Quark-antiquark Systems in a Galilean Covariant MIT Bag Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28160)
*G. X. A. Petronilo,M. V. T. Machado,R.R. Luz,A.E. Santana*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the MIT bag model to be Galilean covariant for heavy quark-antiquark systems, deriving a Dirac-like equation in 5D Galilean spacetime, solving it analytically for wavefunctions and eigenfrequencies, and obtaining reasonable agreement with charmonium/bottomonium spectra—though failing for light quarks due to non-relativistic limitations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile the phenomenologically successful MIT bag model with Galilean covariance for heavy quarkonia while retaining a tractable non-relativistic framework.

Method: Constructs a Galilean-covariant, 5-dimensional generalization of the Dirac equation for quarks confined in a spherical bag; derives consistent boundary conditions; solves analytically for spinor wavefunctions and eigenfrequencies; computes meson masses and hyperfine splittings.

Result: Analytical spinor solutions and discrete eigenfrequencies obtained; numerical mass predictions align reasonably well with experimental charmonium and bottomonium spectra (including hyperfine splittings); poor agreement observed for light-quark mesons.

Conclusion: The Galilean-covariant MIT bag model is viable for heavy quarkonia within a non-relativistic approximation but breaks down for light quarks, confirming the domain of validity of the approach.

Abstract: This work presents a Galilean covariant extension of the MIT bag model to describe heavy quark-antiquark systems within a non-relativistic framework. Using a 5-dimensional formulation of Galilean covariance, a Dirac-like equation governing quark dynamics inside a confining bag is constructed, and the corresponding boundary conditions are derived. Analytical solutions for the spinor wavefunctions and eigenfrequencies are obtained. Numerical results show some agreement with experimental masses and hyperfine splittings for heavy mesons, particularly charmonium and bottomonium states. However, the approach is less reliable for light-quark systems, indicating the limitations of the non-relativistic approximation.

</details>


### [29] [Unveiling the low- and high-energy frontier: a simultaneous SMEFT-PDF fit with parity violation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28216)
*Ella Cole,Tom Tong,Maria Ubiali*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper performs a global SMEFT analysis combining high- and low-energy data—including parity-violation, beta decays, kaon/pion decays, Drell-Yan (neutral & charged current), Higgs, EWPOs, and diboson data—while simultaneously fitting SMEFT Wilson coefficients and proton PDFs using SIMUnet; it quantifies SMEFT–PDF interplay and shows robust bounds with only moderate PDF uncertainty growth.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve the rigor and consistency of SMEFT global fits by incorporating low-energy parity-violating observables and charged-current Drell-Yan data for the first time, and to assess the impact of SMEFT operators on proton PDF determinations.

Method: Systematic SMEFT-to-LEFT matching; joint likelihood fit of 15 independent Wilson coefficients using diverse high- and low-energy experimental datasets; simultaneous determination of SMEFT coefficients and proton PDFs via the public SIMUnet framework.

Result: First global SMEFT fit including charged-current Drell-Yan; consistent constraints on 15 Wilson coefficients; quantified SMEFT–PDF correlations; PDF uncertainties increase only moderately under SMEFT variations, confirming robustness of both SMEFT bounds and PDF stability.

Conclusion: Simultaneous SMEFT–PDF fitting is essential for reliable BSM interpretations; the inclusion of low-energy parity-violation and charged-current data significantly enhances sensitivity; SMEFT effects do not destabilize PDFs, supporting the validity of this unified EFT–QCD framework.

Abstract: We present a comprehensive global analysis of Standard Model Effective Field Theory (SMEFT) operators utilising a diverse set of high- and low-energy experimental probes. By systematically matching the SMEFT to the Low-Energy Effective Field Theory (LEFT), we incorporate parity-violating observables into a global fit of 15 independent Wilson coefficients. This low-energy dataset, comprising parity-violation observables, neutron and nuclear $\beta$-decays, as well as leptonic and semileptonic kaon and pion decays, is combined with high-energy measurements including high-invariant-mass Drell-Yan data in both neutral- and charged-current channels, with the latter included for the first time, Higgs boson measurements, Electroweak Precision Observables (EWPOs) from LEP, and diboson production at LEP and the LHC. Crucially, this framework features a simultaneous fit of both the proton Parton Distribution Functions (PDFs) and the SMEFT Wilson coefficients using the public {\tt SIMUnet} framework. We quantify the direct interplay between SMEFT operators and hadron structure, demonstrating the robustness of the SMEFT bounds and the relative stability of the resulting PDFs with respect to the inclusion of SMEFT effects, with only a moderate increase in their uncertainties.

</details>


### [30] [WIMPs as FIMPs: the Inert Doublet Model and low-temperature reheating](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28217)
*Geneviève Bélanger,Nicolás Bernal,Andreas Goudelis,Alexander Pukhov,Thomas Reggio*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper shows that WIMPs in the Inert Doublet Model can undergo freeze-in instead of freeze-out if the Universe's reheating temperature is low enough—challenging the conventional thermal history assumption and expanding viable parameter space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The conventional association of WIMPs with thermal freeze-out relies on assumptions about the early Universe's thermal history; this work questions that by exploring non-standard cosmologies (e.g., low reheating temperatures) in a well-motivated WIMP model (IDM).

Method: Analyzes the Inert Doublet Model under varying reheating scenarios (instantaneous vs. finite-duration), solving Boltzmann equations for dark matter number density to track production mechanisms—including freeze-out during radiation domination, freeze-out during reheating, and freeze-in with backreaction effects.

Result: Identifies novel relic density production pathways for WIMPs: freeze-in dominates at low reheating temperatures; annihilation backreactions can be significant; previously excluded IDM parameter regions become viable due to suppressed abundance.

Conclusion: WIMPs are not intrinsically tied to freeze-out; their relic abundance mechanism depends critically on cosmic thermal history—thus, WIMPs can effectively behave as FIMPs (Feebly Interacting Massive Particles) in low-reheating scenarios.

Abstract: Weakly Interacting Massive Particles (WIMPs) are conventionally assumed to acquire their dark matter relic abundance through thermal freeze-out. However, this association relies on assumptions concerning the thermal history of the early Universe. We revisit the Inert Doublet Model (IDM), a representative WIMP framework in which dark matter interacts with the Standard Model through electroweak gauge interactions, even when its Higgs-portal coupling vanishes. We demonstrate that sufficiently low reheating temperatures can prevent chemical equilibrium, allowing a genuine WIMP to acquire its relic abundance through freeze-in rather than freeze-out. Considering both instantaneous and finite-duration reheating, we identify different production histories, including freeze-out during radiation domination, freeze-out during reheating, and freeze-in with potentially significant annihilation backreactions. These scenarios open regions of the IDM parameter space that would otherwise be excluded by dark matter overabundance. Our results demonstrate that WIMPs can behave as FIMPs, depending on the thermal history of the Universe.

</details>


### [31] [TeV-Scale Leptogenesis in the Linear Seesaw Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28224)
*A. E. Cárcamo Hernández,J. Echeverria-Puentes,Stephen F. King,José W. F. Valle*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores resonant leptogenesis in the linear seesaw framework with TeV-scale quasi-Dirac heavy neutrinos, identifying viable baryogenesis scenarios—both resonant and non-resonant—in flavoured/strong-washout regimes, and linking them to Higgs- or leptophilic-scalar-mediated neutrino mass generation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To realise low-energy resonant leptogenesis naturally via the linear seesaw mechanism, where small neutrino masses arise from a small mass splitting in a quasi-Dirac heavy neutrino pair, enabling observable phenomenology at collider-accessible scales (TeV).

Method: Analytical and numerical analysis of baryon asymmetry generation in the purely flavoured and strong-washout regimes; parameter-space scans to identify viable regions; comparison of two neutrino-mass mediation scenarios: SM Higgs doublet vs. additional leptophilic scalar doublet.

Result: Viable leptogenesis solutions exist both near and away from the resonant maximum; distinct benchmark scenarios are identified depending on dominant scalar mediator; parameter regions satisfy current constraints from neutrino oscillation data, lepton flavour violation, and electroweak precision tests.

Conclusion: The TeV-scale linear seesaw provides a phenomenologically testable framework for resonant leptogenesis; complementary probes include collider signatures of heavy neutrinos and leptophilic scalars, LFV processes (e.g., μ → eγ), and precision electroweak observables.

Abstract: The linear seesaw mechanism relates the smallness of neutrino masses to the mass splitting within a quasi-Dirac pair of heavy neutrinos, thereby providing a natural framework for realising resonant leptogenesis at low energies. We investigate this possibility within its simplest effective realisation, in which neutrino masses are mediated by a quasi-Dirac pair at the TeV scale. We estimate the generation of the baryon asymmetry in the purely flavoured and strong-washout regimes, identifying phenomenologically viable parameter regions and benchmark scenarios in which the heavy-neutrino decays are dominated either by the SM Higgs doublet or by a second, leptophilic scalar doublet responsible for neutrino mass generation. We find viable leptogenesis solutions both near and away from the resonant maximum and discuss complementary phenomenological probes of the framework.

</details>


### [32] [Higgs-portal dark matter beyond kinetic equilibrium](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28230)
*Giorgio Arcadi,Michele Benaco,Mattia Di Mauro,Sami Nurmi,Kimmo Tuominen*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper revisits Higgs-portal dark matter models (scalar, vector, fermion) in the resonant regime without assuming kinetic equilibrium, solving the full momentum-dependent Boltzmann equation—finding significant deviations from standard momentum-averaged freeze-out calculations, especially for fermionic pseudoscalar couplings (up to factor-10 coupling shift); these deviations systematically tighten direct detection constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess accuracy of the standard momentum-averaged Boltzmann treatment for Higgs-portal DM freeze-out in the resonant regime, particularly when kinetic equilibrium is not guaranteed.

Method: Solving the full momentum-dependent Boltzmann equation numerically; comparing relic abundance predictions against the standard momentum-averaged approach; confronting resulting parameter space with direct/indirect detection and collider constraints.

Result: Standard treatment underestimates relic abundance below resonance—negligibly for fermionic scalar portal, but up to factor 10 in required coupling for fermionic pseudoscalar portal; up to factor 2 for scalar and vector portals; full momentum-dependent results systematically strengthen direct detection limits.

Conclusion: Momentum-dependence is critical for accurate relic density prediction in resonant Higgs-portal DM, especially for fermions with pseudoscalar couplings; ignoring it leads to biased exclusion limits and misinterpretation of viable parameter space.

Abstract: We revisit the scalar, vector, and fermion Higgs-portal dark matter models in the resonant regime without assuming kinetic equilibrium during the freeze-out. We compute the relic abundance by solving the full momentum-dependent Boltzmann equation and compare the results with the standard momentum-averaged treatment. We find that the standard treatment significantly underestimates the relic abundance below the resonance, apart from the fermionic case with a purely scalar portal coupling where the difference is negligible. The deviation from the standard treatment is largest for fermionic models with pseudoscalar interactions, corresponding to factor of 10 in terms of the coupling that reproduces the correct relic abundance. For the scalar and vector cases the corresponding difference is up to a factor of 2. We confront the resulting parameter space with the latest constraints from direct and indirect detection, as well as collider experiments. We find the full momentum-dependent results systematically strengthen the direct detection constraints.

</details>


### [33] [The Nambu--Jona-Lasinio model at isospin density in closed form: what a regularization can change](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28323)
*T. G. Khunjua*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives an exact closed-form expression for the zero-temperature thermodynamic potential of the two-flavour NJL model at finite isospin chemical potential, using Hermite reduction to express the divergent momentum integral in terms of boundary terms and incomplete elliptic integrals; it clarifies the regularization dependence—showing that momentum-only cutoffs are physically equivalent up to vacuum-fixable constants, whereas level-wise cutoffs violate Silver Blaze and define distinct models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in regularization of the divergent mean-field thermodynamic potential in the isospin-asymmetric NJL model, particularly regarding how ultraviolet cutoff choices affect physical predictions (e.g., onset of pion condensation, Silver Blaze property) and whether different cutoff schemes correspond to the same effective theory or distinct models.

Method: Exact analytical evaluation of the momentum-integral thermodynamic potential via Hermite reduction, expressing it as an algebraic boundary term plus three incomplete elliptic integrals (first, second, and third kinds); systematic analysis of divergence structure (power vs. logarithmic), comparison of momentum-global vs. level-wise cutoff prescriptions, and examination of vacuum subtraction and medium separation schemes.

Result: The thermodynamic potential is expressed in closed form involving one boundary term and three incomplete elliptic integrals whose arguments are determined by the zero-momentum quasiparticle level triangle; power divergences reside solely in the boundary term, while the remaining divergence is logarithmic and geometrically tied to the puncture at infinity on an elliptic curve; momentum-only cutoffs differ only by vacuum-determined constants, whereas level-wise cutoffs produce unphysical isospin density below condensation threshold and violate Silver Blaze.

Conclusion: The closed-form solution makes regularization choices explicit and reveals that momentum-global and level-wise cutoffs define physically inequivalent models—not mere regularization variants—since their difference contains non-vanishing, cutoff-dependent terms even after vacuum renormalization; thus, regularization is not innocuous but encodes physical content in the isospin sector.

Abstract: At zero temperature, the mean-field thermodynamic potential of the two-flavour Nambu-Jona-Lasinio model at isospin chemical potential reduces to a divergent momentum integral of two quasiparticle levels. In practice this integral is cut off at momenta not far above the chemical potentials being probed. We evaluate it exactly, at an arbitrary cutoff, using the Hermite reduction. The result is an algebraic boundary term and three incomplete elliptic integrals, one of each kind. Their modulus, parameter and amplitude at the end of the path are fixed by the triangle formed by the two levels at zero momentum. The power divergences reside in the boundary term, while the remaining divergence is logarithmic: the momentum axis is an open path on an elliptic curve, and the ultraviolet is the puncture at which it ends. The closed form makes the role of regularization explicit. As the cutoff is removed, sharp and smooth cutoffs acting on the momentum alone differ only by an additive constant and by two terms that the vacuum inputs fix. The medium separation scheme reproduces the resulting potential up to an additive constant. By contrast, a cutoff applied separately to the two levels generates an isospin density below the onset of condensation and violates the Silver Blaze property. The closed form gives the state that continues the vacuum along the isospin axis without numerical momentum integration. With the same vacuum fit, a cutoff kept finite gives a visibly different state. The two differ by explicit terms that vanish only as the cutoff is removed: they are different models, not two regularizations of one.

</details>


### [34] [Probing tensor-polarized gluon TMDs through \texorpdfstring{$J/ψ$}{J/psi} electroproduction off the deuteron](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28626)
*Xiupeng Xie,Zhun Lu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies tensor-polarized gluon transverse-momentum-dependent distributions (TMDs) in semi-inclusive $J/\psi$ electroproduction off the deuteron, deriving cross-section contributions for $LL$, $LT$, and $TT$ tensor polarizations, defining ten normalized observables, and analyzing their kinematic dependences using a spin-1 spectator model; it identifies direct sensitivity of certain observables to ratios of gluon TMDs, predicts a common zero for linearly polarized gluon moments at $Q^2 = M_\psi^2$, and isolates T-odd gluon TMDs via beam-helicity-dependent sine moments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe tensor-polarized gluon TMDs—particularly $f_{1LL}^g$, $f_{1LT}^g$, $h_{1LT}^g$, $f_{1TT}^g$, $h_{1TT}^{\perp g}$, $g_{1LT}^g$, and $g_{1TT}^g$—in a clean, experimentally accessible channel: semi-inclusive $J/\psi$ production on a tensor-polarized deuteron target, leveraging the color-octet mechanism in NRQCD.

Method: Derives leading-order cross sections in the color-octet NRQCD framework for $LL$, $LT$, and $TT$ tensor polarization configurations; constructs ten normalized observables for unpolarized and longitudinally polarized electron beams; employs gluon TMDs from a spin-1 spectator model; computes kinematic ($q_T$, $y$, $Q^2$, center-of-mass energy) dependences; analyzes moments—including beam-helicity-dependent sine modulations—to isolate T-even and T-odd TMD combinations.

Result: Identifies distinct observables directly sensitive to ratios of gluon TMDs: $f_{1LL}^g$, $f_{1LT}^g+h_{1LT}^g$, and $f_{1TT}^g-h_{1TT}^{\perp g}$; finds linearly polarized gluon moments vanish simultaneously at $Q^2=M_\psi^2$ due to adopted LDMEs; demonstrates beam-helicity-dependent sine moments selectively probe the T-odd distributions $g_{1LT}^g$ and $g_{1TT}^g$; provides fixed-scale model predictions neglecting transverse recoil and quarkonium formation effects.

Conclusion: Semi-inclusive $J/\psi$ electroproduction on a tensor-polarized deuteron is a theoretically robust and phenomenologically rich channel to extract tensor-polarized gluon TMDs; the proposed observables enable separation of individual TMDs—including elusive T-odd ones—and offer testable predictions for future experiments (e.g., at Jefferson Lab 12 GeV or the Electron-Ion Collider).

Abstract: We investigate tensor-polarized gluon transverse-momentum-dependent distributions (TMDs) in semi-inclusive $J/\psi$ electroproduction off the deuteron. At leading order in the color-octet channels of nonrelativistic QCD, we derive the cross-section contributions associated with longitudinal-longitudinal ($LL$), longitudinal-transverse ($LT$), and transverse-transverse ($TT$) tensor polarization and define ten normalized observables for unpolarized and longitudinally polarized electron beams. Using gluon TMDs obtained in a spin-1 spectator model, we study their $q_T$, $y$, $Q^2$, and collision-energy dependences. The observables involving $f_{1LL}^g$, $f_{1LT}^g+h_{1LT}^g$, and $f_{1TT}^g-h_{1TT}^{\perp g}$ depend directly on ratios of gluon TMDs. The moments involving linearly polarized gluons exhibit a common zero at $Q^2=M_\psi^2$ for the adopted long-distance matrix elements, whereas the beam-helicity-dependent sine moments probe the T-odd distributions $g_{1LT}^g$ and $g_{1TT}^g$. We present fixed-scale model estimates that neglect transverse recoil from soft radiation and quarkonium formation.

</details>

Abstract: We propose a novel, calculable model for realizing strong first-order phase transitions in the early Universe, in which the phase transition occurs along a pseudo-Nambu-Goldstone boson (pNGB) direction. The model involves two scalar fields whose interactions exhibit collective symmetry breaking, protecting the pNGB potential from quadratically divergent radiative corrections induced by these interactions. This structure allows strong first-order phase transitions to be realized without severe parameter tuning while maintaining perturbative control. We investigate the finite-temperature effective potential and bubble nucleation dynamics, identifying parameter regions where strong first-order phase transitions can successfully complete. The strength and characteristic time scale of the transitions are evaluated, and the resulting gravitational-wave (GW) spectra are computed. The predicted GW signals can fall within the sensitivity ranges of planned GW experiments.

</details>

*Andre Sopczak*

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The 2012 Higgs discovery confirmed the Standard Model's electroweak symmetry breaking mechanism but left open fundamental questions—such as naturalness, dark matter, and flavor hierarchies—that motivate BSM Higgs scenarios; thus, probing the minimality vs. complexity of the Higgs sector remains a central goal in particle physics.

Result: No conclusive evidence for BSM Higgs bosons has been found to date; however, increasingly stringent limits have been placed on extended Higgs sectors, and several intriguing (though statistically insignificant) anomalies have motivated refined analyses and novel search strategies—especially in challenging final states (e.g., exotic decays, boosted topologies, displaced vertices).

Conclusion: BSM Higgs searches are inherently interdisciplinary—bridging detector performance, reconstruction algorithms, statistical inference, and theoretical model-building—and their continued evolution reflects both experimental progress and deepening conceptual understanding; future high-luminosity runs and next-generation colliders will critically test whether the Higgs sector is truly minimal.

Abstract: The search for Higgs bosons Beyond the Standard Model (BSM) began intensively at the Large Electron-Positron (LEP) collider (1989-2000), continued at the Tevatron (2001-2011), and entered a new era with the start of the Large Hadron Collider (LHC) in 2010. While the discovery of a Higgs boson in 2012 established the Standard Model mechanism of electroweak symmetry breaking, it also opened a wide landscape of open questions that motivate ongoing BSM Higgs searches. This review traces the evolution of these searches across experiments, highlighting both experimental techniques and underlying theoretical ideas in an accessible manner. Emphasis is placed on how different collider environments shaped search strategies and how seemingly subtle experimental effects, such as small excesses, non-standard decay topologies, or suppressed couplings, can provide insight into the possible structure of extended Higgs sectors. By connecting historical developments with current analyses and future prospects, this article aims not only to summarise results but also to provide students and newcomers with intuitive insight into how Higgs searches are designed, interpreted, and continuously refined. It illustrates how experimental challenges translate into physics opportunities, thereby inviting the next generation of researchers to contribute to one of the central questions in particle physics: whether the Higgs sector is minimal or part of a more complex structure.

</details>


### [38] [Neutral and Charged Pion Modes in Magnetized Quark Matter with Chiral Imbalance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28838)
*Shijie Zhai,Lang Yu,Jie Mei,Mei Huang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies neutral and charged pion correlations in a two-flavor NJL model at finite temperature, under external magnetic field and chiral chemical potential μ₅, using mean-field and RPA methods; it finds distinct Landau-level effects in neutral vs. charged channels, and μ₅- and B-dependent mass shifts with implications for lattice QCD.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how chiral imbalance (via μ₅) and strong magnetic fields affect pion properties—especially masses—in hot dense quark matter, motivated by heavy-ion collision environments and discrepancies/insights from lattice QCD.

Method: Two-flavor Nambu–Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model analyzed within mean-field approximation and single-channel random phase approximation (RPA); pion masses extracted from positive-energy zeros of real parts of inverse propagators at zero spatial momentum; explicit treatment of Landau quantization and Dirac structure in neutral vs. charged channels.

Result: Neutral-pion mass shows weak μ₅ dependence at low T but its rapid decrease shifts to higher T with increasing μ₅; charged-pion mass monotonically decreases with μ₅; at T = 0, increasing B lowers m_π⁰ but raises m_π±; neutral channel has no Landau cut due to Dirac trace cancellation, while charged channel exhibits Landau-level transitions with pair-production and thermal-scattering contributions.

Conclusion: The distinct electromagnetic and chiral anomaly-driven responses of neutral and charged pions to μ₅ and B are robustly captured in the NJL+RPA framework; the observed mass trends provide a qualitative explanation for recent lattice QCD findings on pion screening and chiral magnetic effects.

Abstract: We investigate neutral and charged pion correlations in a two-flavor Nambu--Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model at finite temperature in the presence of a uniform magnetic field and a prescribed chiral chemical potential $\mu_5$, using the mean-field approximation and the single-channel random phase approximation (RPA). The pion masses are defined by the selected positive-energy zeros of the real parts of the corresponding inverse propagators. We demonstrate that, at zero external spatial momentum, the complete Dirac trace in the neutral channel eliminates mixed-branch contributions, thereby precluding a finite-energy Landau cut. In contrast, the charged channel allows non-diagonal Landau-level transitions and exhibits both pair-production and thermal-scattering contributions. With increasing $\mu_5$, the neutral-pion mass exhibits only a weak variation at low temperature, while the rapid decrease of the mass shifts toward higher temperatures. The charged-pion mass decreases with increasing $\mu_5$ over the selected solution segments. This behavior may help account for the lattice QCD results. At zero temperature and fixed $\mu_5$, increasing the magnetic field lowers the neutral-pion mass but raises the charged-pion mass.

</details>


### [39] [A billion colour states: exact soft-gluon evolution beyond leading colour for many-jet QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29072)
*Bakar Chargeishvili*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a novel, efficient method for constructing orthogonal colour bases and computing exact full-colour one-loop soft-gluon evolution for multi-parton processes—scaling to 14–15 partons using GPU/node-level parallelisation—and demonstrates that leading-colour approximations fail badly (e.g., 6.6× overestimation at 14 partons) due to non-negligible subleading-colour contributions like O(1%) singlet jet pairs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable exact full-colour soft-gluon evolution beyond the leading-colour approximation, which breaks down for many-jet final states where subleading-colour effects (e.g., singlet jet pairs) remain significant despite formal 1/N_c^2 suppression.

Method: Constructs an orthogonal, N_c-independent colour basis labelled by sequential coupling representations; derives closed-form norms and local colour-operator action (acting only on relevant label segments); enables on-the-fly basis vector generation and matrix-free anomalous dimension application; implements optimised CPU/GPU code for exact evolution in high-dimensional colour spaces.

Result: Exact soft-gluon evolution computed for up to 14 gluons on a single GPU and 15 partons (827M-dimensional colour space) on one node; validation against known few-parton results confirms correctness; quantification shows leading-colour overestimates full-colour exclusive jet rates by factors of 1.3 (8 partons), 1.8 (10), and 6.6 (14); O(1%) probability of hard jet pairs in colour singlets—strictly zero in leading colour.

Conclusion: Subleading-colour contributions are numerically essential for multi-jet phenomenology; leading-colour is insufficient beyond ~8 partons; the proposed basis and algorithm enable tractable, exact full-colour resummation for realistic LHC multi-jet processes.

Abstract: We present a new method, and its implementation, for constructing orthogonal colour bases and computing colour factors for any process with quarks, antiquarks and gluons, and use it to compute one-loop soft-gluon evolution exactly in full colour, keeping all colour interference. Each basis vector is labelled, independently of $N_c$, by the sequence of colour representations that appear when the partons are coupled one at a time. We derive a closed formula for the norms of the basis vectors, and colour operators act only on the part of a label between the partons involved, through small tables. Basis vectors can therefore be generated and used one at a time, without listing the complete basis: norms and matrix elements of single basis vectors are obtained for $10^4$ gluons in seconds. The same locality lets the soft anomalous dimension act on colour states without being stored. This allows optimised implementations on CPUs and GPUs, with which we evolve colour states exactly for up to fourteen gluons on a single GPU and for fifteen gluons, a colour space of $827\,888\,726$ dimensions, on a single compute node. We validate the basis and the evolution against independent codes and published results for processes with few partons, the only ones for which such results are available. We then measure the accuracy of leading colour as a function of the number of partons. For exclusive jet rates with a veto on soft radiation outside the jets, at a ratio of about 14 between the hard and the veto scale, leading colour overestimates the full-colour result by a median factor of 1.3 for eight partons, 1.8 for ten and 6.6 for fourteen. Pairs of hard jets are in a colour singlet with probabilities of several per cent, which vanish at leading colour. The formal $1/N_c^2$ suppression of subleading colour therefore does not make it small for many-jet final states.

</details>


### [40] [Quantifying the Impact of Galactic Dark Matter Halo Models on LZ Results](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29197)
*Jialiang Shao,Andrea Addazi,Xinran Wang,Guanwen Fang,Yi-Fu Cai*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper examines how anisotropic dark matter velocity distributions—departing from the standard isotropic, Maxwellian halo model—affect the interpretation of LZ direct-detection results, finding modest (~±30%) impact above ~40 GeV but order-of-magnitude variation below ~15 GeV and significant implications for single-event analyses (e.g., the 248 keV candidate) due to sensitivity to the high-velocity tail.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard dark matter direct-detection limits assume an isotropic, Maxwellian Standard Halo Model, but observational and simulation evidence shows the Milky Way's dark matter halo is radially or scale-dependently anisotropic; this mismatch motivates reassessing LZ constraints under realistic anisotropic models.

Method: The authors employ self-consistent anisotropic halo models (isotropic, Osipkov-Merritt, axisymmetric, triaxial) from Belli et al., apply a common Galactic escape-speed truncation, and recast LZ elastic-scattering limits via event-count ratios; for the 248 keV nuclear-recoil candidate, they use a background-free single-event approximation to evaluate kinematically allowed inelastic mass splittings.

Result: Above ~40 GeV, anisotropic models shift LZ elastic limits by ≤ ±30% relative to isotropic; below ~15 GeV, variations span up to an order of magnitude due to high-velocity tail sensitivity; for the 248 keV event, certain anisotropic models extend kinematically allowed inelastic mass splittings into regions where solar-neutrino constraints on Higgsino interpretations weaken.

Conclusion: Halo-model dependence is modest at high WIMP masses but critically important near kinematic thresholds—especially for low-mass WIMPs and rare-event interpretations—necessitating explicit treatment of velocity anisotropy in limit setting and signal interpretation.

Abstract: Most dark matter direct-detection limits are reported under the assumption of an isotropic, Maxwellian Standard Halo Model. However, the Milky Way's stellar halo exhibits substantial radial anisotropy, while cosmological simulations predict scale-dependent anisotropy in the dark matter velocity distribution. We investigate how departures from isotropy affect the interpretation of LZ results in two complementary regimes: background-dominated elastic-scattering searches and the recently reported 248 keV nuclear-recoil candidate, which we study using a background-free single-event approximation. For elastic scattering, we use the self-consistent isotropic, Osipkov-Merritt, axisymmetric, and triaxial halo models of Belli et al. and translate the published limits through the ratio of predicted event counts, applying a common escape-speed truncation in the Galactic frame. At WIMP masses above approximately 40 GeV, the resulting limits lie within about $\pm 30\%$ of the isotropic reference. Below approximately 15 GeV, the model envelope broadens to as much as an order of magnitude, reflecting sensitivity to the high-velocity tail. For the 248 keV event, the accessible inelastic mass splitting is likewise controlled by this tail. Some anisotropic models extend the allowed kinematic region towards splittings at which solar-neutrino constraints on a Higgsino interpretation become ineffective, although kinematic accessibility alone does not establish viability at the fixed Higgsino cross section. These results show that halo-model dependence is modest at high WIMP masses but becomes essential near kinematic thresholds.

</details>


### [41] [Infrared divergences in the $\mathcal{O}(α_s v^2)$ corrections to exclusive $J/ψ$ photoproduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29272)
*Zi-Qiang Chen,Long-Bin Chen*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes infrared singularities in exclusive $J/\psi$ photoproduction ($\gamma p \to J/\psi p$) at order $\alpha_s v^2$, identifying uncanceled soft-gluon poles arising from interactions between the $c\bar{c}$ pair and target-collinear partons; it confirms these poles via full one-loop computation and shows they cannot be absorbed by existing NRQCD vacuum-to-$J/\psi$ matrix elements, challenging current factorization frameworks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve whether infrared (soft/collinear) singularities at relative order $\alpha_s v^2$ in exclusive $J/\psi$ photoproduction are fully canceled within combined GPD and NRQCD factorization — a prerequisite for theoretical consistency and reliable phenomenology.

Method: Systematic analysis of singular loop-momentum regions (soft, collinear) within GPD + NRQCD factorization; explicit one-loop QCD calculation; renormalization, collinear factorization into GPDs, and NRQCD matching; examination of local NRQCD operator basis and vacuum-to-$J/\psi$ matrix elements.

Result: Residual infrared poles persist at $\alpha_s v^2$ after full QCD renormalization, collinear factorization, and NRQCD matching; these stem from incomplete cancellation of soft-gluon exchange between the $c\bar{c}$ pair and target-collinear partons; pole coefficients are verified by complete one-loop computation; no new independent local NRQCD operator or vacuum-to-$J/\psi$ matrix element exists to absorb them.

Conclusion: The persistence of infrared poles indicates a breakdown or incompleteness of the standard GPD+NRQCD factorization for exclusive $J/\psi$ photoproduction at $\alpha_s v^2$, demanding refinement of the factorization formalism or inclusion of additional dynamical ingredients (e.g., target-soft correlations).

Abstract: We investigate the infrared structure of exclusive $J/\psi$ photoproduction, $\gamma p\to J/\psi p$, up to relative order $\alpha_s v^2$ within the frameworks of generalized parton distribution (GPD) and nonrelativistic QCD (NRQCD) factorization. By analyzing the singular loop-momentum regions, we extract the soft and collinear singular terms and identify residual infrared poles at relative order $\alpha_s v^2$ after QCD renormalization, collinear factorization into the GPDs, and NRQCD matching. These poles originate from incomplete cancellation of soft-gluon exchange between the heavy-quark pair and the target-collinear partons. We verify the pole coefficients through a complete one-loop calculation. We examine the local NRQCD operator basis and find no additional independent vacuum-to-$J/\psi$ matrix element that can absorb these poles. These findings motivate further investigation of factorization in exclusive $J/\psi$ photoproduction.

</details>


### [42] [Neutron lifetime puzzle and Dicke coherence in spontaneous decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29328)
*Z.K.Silagadze*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper rules out collective quantum synchronization (Dicke superradiance) as a resolution to the neutron lifetime puzzle, showing it is suppressed in both terrestrial experiments and the early universe; its inclusion would overproduce helium-4, contradicting observations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether macroscopic Dicke superradiance—potentially enhancing decay rates of excited neutrons—could resolve the neutron lifetime puzzle between beam and bottle measurements.

Method: Analytical quantum many-body analysis: evaluates superradiance feasibility in weak beta decay considering phase-space continuity, fermionic antisymmetrization, density constraints in ultracold neutron traps, and collisional/thermal decoherence in the primordial plasma pre-BBN.

Result: Superradiance is fundamentally forbidden in standard beta decay; inactive in current neutron traps; and dynamically suppressed in the early universe by e± collisions and thermal photons—leading to thermalization of any excited neutron state before weak freeze-out.

Conclusion: The Koch–Hummel excited-neutron hypothesis, if realized via standard quantum electrodynamics and weak interactions, is cosmologically excluded due to excessive ⁴He production, tightening constraints on beyond-Standard-Model neutron physics.

Abstract: The long-standing discrepancy between beam and bottle measurements of the free-neutron lifetime---known as the neutron lifetime puzzle---remains an open anomaly in nuclear and particle physics. Here, we investigate whether collective quantum synchronization via macroscopic Dicke superradiance can influence free-neutron decay or mediate the relaxation of hypothetical excited neutron states. We show that superradiance is fundamentally obstructed in standard three-body weak beta decay by continuous final-state phase space and fermionic antisymmetrization, and remains inactive in terrestrial ultracold neutron traps due to low particle densities, even within the Koch--Hummel excited-neutron model. Extending this analysis to the primordial plasma prior to Big Bang nucleosynthesis, we demonstrate that rapid $e^\pm$ collisional dephasing and intense thermal photon backgrounds prevent a superradiant purge and instead enforce thermal detailed balance. Consequently, across the entire parameter space unconstrained by terrestrial experiments, an excited neutron state inevitably thermalizes before weak freeze-out, increasing the effective neutron statistical weight and severely overproducing primordial $^4\text{He}$, thereby excluding the standard realization of the Koch--Hummel hypothesis.

</details>

### [44] [Diffractive $D^0$ photoproduction in ultraperipheral collisions at the LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29527)

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To theoretically characterize coherent diffractive D⁰ photoproduction in ultraperipheral heavy-ion and proton-nucleus collisions at the LHC, disentangle exclusive vs. diffractive mechanisms, assess nuclear modifications, and enable direct comparison with experimental data—particularly CMS measurements requiring target-nucleus breakup.

Method: Uses the Color Glass Condensate (CGC) effective field theory to compute cross sections; incorporates both exclusive cc̄ pair production and diffractive production with a semi-hard gluon, modeled within diffractive transverse-momentum-dependent (TMD) factorization; defines and evaluates the nuclear modification factor R_pA at fixed photon energy; corrects inclusive predictions for CMS event selection (requiring target-nucleus breakup) using the diffractive cross section.
