Conclusion: Diffractive TMD factorization within the CGC framework successfully describes high-p_T D⁰ photoproduction in UPCs; nuclear effects differ markedly between exclusive and diffractive channels, underscoring the need for mechanism-resolved analyses; the study enables precise interpretation of charm photoproduction as a probe of gluon saturation and nuclear structure.

Abstract: We compute the cross section for coherent diffractive $D^0$ photoproduction in ultraperipheral lead-lead and proton-lead collisions (UPCs) at the Large Hadron Collider (LHC) within the Color Glass Condensate (CGC) effective field theory. We include both the exclusive production of a charm-anticharm pair and diffractive production with an additional semi-hard gluon, described in diffractive transverse-momentum-dependent (TMD) factorization. We show that the diffractive channel, although suppressed by the strong coupling, dominates at large transverse momentum, where the exclusive channel is suppressed by color transparency. Furthermore, we quantify nuclear effects at the parton level with a nuclear modification factor $R_{pA}$ at fixed photon energy, which shows a mild suppression for the diffractive channel and an enhancement growing with transverse momentum up to about a factor of 2 for the exclusive one. We also use the diffractive cross section to correct our inclusive $D^0$ predictions to the CMS event selection, which requires the target nucleus to break up, and compare them with the data. Finally, we provide predictions for inclusive $D^0$ photoproduction in proton-lead UPCs.

</details>


### [45] [Pseudoscalar Yukawa Preheating via Effective Action Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29659)
*Xi-Bin Li,De-Tian Zhao,Ke Fu,Jun-Yang Jiao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops an effective action framework to study helicity-dependent fermion production during preheating via pseudoscalar Yukawa coupling; it shows that temporal symmetry in undamped inflaton oscillations suppresses helicity asymmetry, whereas damping breaks this symmetry and enables persistent helical fermion spectra via non-commuting helicity transfer matrices.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how pseudoscalar Yukawa interactions during inflationary preheating can generate helicity asymmetry in fermionic particle production — a key ingredient for chiral effects and possible baryogenesis — and to clarify the role of inflaton dynamics (e.g., damping) in breaking symmetries that otherwise suppress such asymmetry.

Method: Integrates out fermionic fields within the one-loop effective action formalism; uses Schwinger proper time representation to compute pair production probability near inflaton zero crossings; constructs a helicity transfer matrix to track mode evolution across successive non-adiabatic events; compares undamped (time-symmetric) vs. damped (asymmetric amplitude) inflaton backgrounds; contrasts with scalar Yukawa case.

Result: In undamped oscillations, helicity-mixing contributions cancel after even zero crossings due to temporal symmetry, suppressing net helicity asymmetry; in damped backgrounds, amplitude decay breaks symmetry, yielding non-commuting transfer matrices and a persistent, momentum-dependent helical fermion spectrum; pseudoscalar mechanism uniquely enables helicity asymmetry, unlike scalar Yukawa preheating.

Conclusion: The one-loop effective action framework with helicity-resolved transfer matrices provides a robust tool to quantify helicity asymmetry in fermionic preheating; inflaton damping is essential for generating observable chiral imbalances, highlighting the interplay between background dynamics and quantum field production mechanisms.

Abstract: In this work, we investigate helicity-dependent fermion production driven by a pseudoscalar Yukawa interaction within the one-loop effective action framework. By integrating out the fermionic fields and employing the Schwinger proper time representation, we derive an analytical expression for the fermion pair production probability near each zero crossing of the oscillating inflaton field. We further introduce a helicity transfer matrix to describe the evolution of fermionic modes through successive non-adiabatic production events. For an undamped inflaton background, the temporal symmetry around successive zero crossings leads to the cancellation of helicity-mixing contributions after an even number of events, preventing the accumulation of helicity asymmetry. We then extend the analysis to a damped oscillatory background, in which the decreasing inflaton amplitude breaks this temporal symmetry and modifies the transfer matrix at each crossing. The resulting non-commuting transfer matrices produce a persistent helical spectrum, as demonstrated by the probabilities as a function of momentum obtained after successive scattering events. A comparison with scalar Yukawa preheating further confirms the role of helicity mixing in generating the helical asymmetry induced by pseudoscalar mechanism. These results establish an effective action framework for studying helicity asymmetry during fermionic preheating and provide insight into the influence of the inflaton's dynamical evolution on particle production.

</details>


### [46] [Magic Production and the Fundamental Forces of Nature](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29717)
*Jiunn-Wei Chen,Yu-Hao Li*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates quantum magic production in forward scattering of Standard Model fermions, finding vanishing magic for most mediator exchanges (gluon, photon, W, Higgs, graviton) and for Z-boson exchange in leptonic channels at sin²θ_W = 1/4; nonzero magic arises for Z-mediated quark scattering—suggesting possible fundamental suppression of magic in certain interaction channels.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore quantum magic as a resource for universal quantum computation and as a probe of fundamental interactions, specifically whether Standard Model dynamics intrinsically suppress its generation.

Method: Analyzes forward scattering of two distinct chiral fermions in the single-mediator-exchange approximation, computing magic production for all relevant SM mediators (gluon, photon, W, Z, Higgs, graviton), with explicit dependence on weak mixing angle sin²θ_W.

Result: Magic production vanishes identically for gluon, photon, W, Higgs, and graviton exchange; vanishes for Z-mediated purely leptonic scattering when sin²θ_W = 1/4; remains nonzero for Z-mediated scattering involving elementary quarks.

Conclusion: The observed suppression patterns suggest that the structure of fundamental forces—particularly electroweak couplings and chirality assignments—may encode an inherent tendency to suppress quantum magic production in specific scattering channels, potentially reflecting deeper constraints on quantum information generation in particle interactions.

Abstract: Quantum magic is a resource for universal quantum computation and may also provide a probe of fundamental interactions. We study its production in forward scattering of two distinct Standard Model fermions in the chirality basis. In the single-mediator-exchange approximation, magic production vanishes for gluon, photon, W-boson, Higgs-boson, and graviton exchange. For Z-boson exchange in purely leptonic scattering, it also vanishes at $\sin^2\theta_W = 1/4$, close to the phenomenological value. By contrast, Z-boson exchange involving elementary quarks generally produces nonzero magic. These patterns raise the question of whether the construction of the fundamental forces encodes, at least in some channels, a tendency to suppress magic production.

</details>


### [47] [Few-gluon interactions in high-energy collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29777)
*Thomas A. Trainor*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper challenges the conventional QGP-dominated narrative for heavy-ion collisions by showing that key observables (e.g., $v_2$, jet-related correlations) in small systems (p–p, p–Pb) follow scaling laws consistent with few-gluon (dipole/quadrupole) radiation in low-density environments—not bulk QGP hydrodynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To re-evaluate the interpretation of collective-like signals (elliptic flow, jet quenching) observed not only in A–A but also in small systems (p–p, p–Pb), which undermines the assumption that such signals uniquely signify QGP formation.

Method: Reanalysis of $v_2(p_t)$ via transformation to quadrupole spectra $\bar \rho_2(p_t)$; inversion of ensemble-mean $\bar p_t$ to infer true collision geometry; scaling analysis of $v_2$ vs $n_{ch}$ across systems; introduction of extensive measure $V_2^2 \equiv \bar \rho_0^2 v_2^2$; comparison with jet-related two-gluon trends.

Result: Cubic scaling of $v_2(n_{ch})$ in p–p suggests dominant three-gluon interactions; quadratic scaling of jet-related correlations supports two-gluon origin; geometry inferred from $\bar p_t$ contradicts Glauber Monte Carlo; all systematics are unified under multipole (color dipole/quadrupole) radiation from few-gluon dynamics in low-density matter.

Conclusion: The observed 'collective' phenomena do not require QGP formation or hydrodynamic evolution; instead, they arise from coherent color-field radiation in sparse gluonic systems—necessitating a paradigm shift from hot/dense QGP to few-body QCD radiation mechanisms.

Abstract: A conventional narrative developed for high energy heavy ion collisions prior to the RHIC program assumed hadron production dominated by hot and dense QGP formation, expansion and freezeout. Proposed diagnostics included detection of elliptic and radial flows via correlation and spectrum analysis and jet quenching in a dense medium via rescaled spectrum ratios. The validity of that narrative has come into question in recent years with detection of similar features in data from small collision systems. In response, several recent observations hint at a strongly modified narrative. Study of p-Pb spectra has revealed that the Glauber Monte Carlo estimation of A-B collision geometry is quite misleading. Collision geometry should instead be inferred by inverting ensemble-mean $\bar p_t$ data. A method has been developed to transform $v_2(p_t)$ data to quadrupole spectra $\bar \rho_2(p_t)$ that provide new insights into the true correlation mechanism. Accurate $p_t$-integrated $v_2$ vs $n_{ch}$ measurements for p-p collisions reveal a cubic trend suggesting a three-gluon interaction as the true mechanism. The p-p $v_2(n_{ch})$ trend has been generalized to describe all A-B collision systems in common in terms of an extensive measure $V_2^2 \equiv \bar \rho_0^2 v_2^2$ (number of correlated pairs). A similar program has been applied to jet-related contributions to spectra and angular correlations with a comparable result, a quadratic trend on $n_{ch}$ as expected for two-gluon interactions. Those new results suggest that the relevant data features actually represent multipole (color dipole and quadrupole) radiation from few-gluon interactions within a low-density system.

</details>


### [48] [Flavor-resolved screening length of a moving quarkonium in a lattice-calibrated holographic QCD plasma](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29953)
*Huwei Zhu,Zi-qiang Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the screening length of a moving heavy quark-antiquark pair in an Einstein-Maxwell-dilaton holographic background calibrated to lattice QCD, across pure-glue (N_f=0), two-flavor (N_f=2), and 2+1-flavor (N_f=2+1) QCD matter. It analyzes orientation (parallel/perpendicular), temperature (T), baryon chemical potential (μ), and rapidity (η), finding monotonic suppression of L_s with T, η, and μ (for flavored cases), smaller perpendicular than parallel screening lengths, flavor-dependent ordering at zero density, and a T–μ crossing between N_f=2 and N_f=2+1 screening lengths driven by competition between crossover temperature and density coupling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a flavor-resolved, holographically computed reference for quarkonium suppression in the baryon-rich QCD regime relevant to heavy-ion beam energy scans, where finite-temperature, finite-density, and kinematic effects (rapidity, orientation) are essential but poorly constrained from first principles.

Method: Holographic computation using a lattice-calibrated Einstein-Maxwell-dilaton bottom-up AdS/QCD model; numerical evaluation of the string worldsheet action for a moving QQ̄ pair in the dual black hole background; extraction of the screening length L_s from the maximal separation before worldsheet collapse; analysis across N_f = 0, 2, 2+1 sectors with consistent lattice input; systematic variation of T, μ, η, and orientation.

Result: L_s decreases monotonically with T and η for all N_f; decreases with μ for N_f = 2 and 2+1; L_s^⊥ < L_s^∥ universally; at (T=0.3 GeV, μ=0): L_s^(0) > L_s^(2) > L_s^(2+1); N_f=2 and N_f=2+1 curves cross in the T–μ plane—ordering reverses above the crossing line; crossing arises from interplay between two-flavor chiral crossover scale and dilaton–density coupling strength.

Conclusion: Flavor content significantly impacts in-medium quarkonium screening, especially at finite baryon density; the observed T–μ crossing is a robust qualitative feature of the calibrated holographic framework and motivates dedicated lattice studies of static quarkonium free energies at nonzero μ, as well as experimental discrimination via rapidity- and centrality-dependent quarkonium observables in beam energy scan programs.

Abstract: We compute the screening length \(L_s\) of a moving heavy quark-antiquark pair in a lattice-calibrated Einstein-Maxwell-dilaton background for pure-glue, two-flavor, and \(2+1\)-flavor matter. Parallel and perpendicular orientations are considered at finite temperature, baryon chemical potential, and rapidity. \(L_s\) decreases monotonically with \(T\) and \(\eta\) in every flavor sector, and for the flavored systems also with \(\mu\). The perpendicular screening length is systematically smaller than the parallel one. At zero density and \(T=0.3\ \mathrm{GeV}\), the ordering \(L_s^{(N_f=0)}>L_s^{(N_f=2)}>L_s^{(N_f=2+1)}\) holds in this calibration, and a lattice check of the static flavor dependence is proposed. At finite density the two-flavor and \(2+1\)-flavor curves cross along a line in the \(T\)-\(\mu\) plane; below the line \(L_s^{(N_f=2)}>L_s^{(N_f=2+1)}\), while above it the ordering reverses. The crossing follows from the competition between the two-flavor crossover temperature and the density coupling, and its location depends on the fitted parameters. These results provide a flavor-resolved reference for moving quarkonium screening in the baryon-rich regime probed by beam energy scans.

</details>


### [49] [Plasma-enabled magnet-free LSW search for eV-scale axions with laser-stimulated regeneration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30098)
*Xiangyan An,Min Chen,Yipeng Wu,Boyuan Li,Wenchao Yan,Peng Yuan,Binbin Yan,Feng Liu,Zhengming Sheng,Jie Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes a magnet-free light-shining-through-a-wall (LSW) experiment using plasma and lasers to generate and detect eV-scale axions, overcoming phase-mismatch limitations of conventional magnetic-field-based LSW; achieves projected axion-photon coupling limits of ~10⁻⁷ GeV⁻¹ over 0.1–1 eV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conventional LSW experiments suffer from photon–axion phase mismatch at eV-scale axion masses, degrading coherence and sensitivity; a magnet-free alternative is needed to restore sensitivity in this mass range.

Method: Introduces two plasma-based axion generation schemes: (1) a low-intensity, two-color dispersion-matched source producing narrowband axions defined by plasma dispersion; (2) a high-intensity, single-pulse plasma-broadened source generating broadband axions, validated via particle-in-cell simulations showing broadening of the E·B source term around the laser branch due to laser–plasma wave mixing. Axion detection employs counter-propagating laser-stimulated decay into a signal photon and a laser-mode photon, with phase matching enforced by laser frequency tuning.

Result: Demonstrates feasibility of plasma-mediated axion production across 0.1–1 eV; achieves projected upper limits on axion-photon coupling g_{aγ} ≈ 10⁻⁷ GeV⁻¹ for representative experimental parameters, enabled by coherent, phase-matched regeneration without magnets.

Conclusion: The proposed magnet-free, plasma-and-laser-based LSW scheme overcomes the eV-mass phase-mismatch barrier of traditional setups, offering a viable path toward probing axion-photon couplings in an otherwise inaccessible parameter space.

Abstract: In conventional light-shining-through-a-wall (LSW) experiments, strong magnetic fields mediate the conversion of photons into axions and their subsequent reconversion into photons. At eV-scale axion masses, however, photon--axion phase mismatch causes these conversions to lose coherence, substantially reducing the experimental sensitivity. We propose a magnet-free LSW scheme that addresses this limitation via plasma-based axion generation and laser-stimulated regeneration. At low laser intensity, a two-color dispersion-matched source generates axions in a narrow mass range set by the plasma dispersion. At relativistic laser intensity, a single-pulse plasma-broadened source generates axions over a broad mass range, as shown by particle-in-cell simulations. The Fourier spectrum of the source term $\mathbf{E}\cdot\mathbf{B}$ broadens around the laser branch, consistent with mixing between the laser and low-frequency plasma waves. For axion detection behind the wall, a counter-propagating laser stimulates axion decay into a detectable signal photon and a second photon emitted into the laser mode, with the laser frequency chosen to satisfy phase matching. For representative parameters, the requirement of at least one regenerated photon yields projected upper limits on the axion-photon coupling on the order $10^{-7}\,\mathrm{GeV}^{-1}$ over $m_a=0.1$--$1\,\mathrm{eV}$.

</details>


### [50] [Effect of dark matter and scalar-cut potential on the macroscopic properties of neutron stars: A chiral quark mean field approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30434)
*Hrishika P,Arvind Kumar*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how fermionic dark matter and a scalar-cut potential affect neutron star structure, finding that dark matter generally softens the equation of state while the scalar-cut potential stiffens it—impacting mass-radius relations, tidal deformability, and redshift depending on dark matter particle mass and mediator couplings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how fermionic dark matter and modified scalar potentials influence neutron star macroscopic properties and observables, bridging particle physics and astrophysics in dense baryonic environments.

Method: Combines the chiral SU(3) quark mean-field (CQMF) model for baryonic matter with a self-interacting fermionic dark matter fluid; employs a two-fluid Tolman-Oppenheimer-Volkoff formalism where baryonic and DM components interact only gravitationally; includes scalar-cut potential $U_{\text{cut}}(\phi)$ and DM mediators ($\varphi_D$, $V_D^\mu$).

Result: Inclusion of $U_{\text{cut}}(\phi)$ stiffens the EOS; increasing DM fraction softens the EOS and alters mass-radius relation, tidal deformability, and gravitational redshift; DM configuration (compact core vs. extended halo) depends on DM particle mass and mediator interaction strength.

Conclusion: Fermionic dark matter and scalar-cut potentials have competing and parameter-dependent effects on neutron star structure; their signatures are potentially observable via multi-messenger constraints (e.g., NICER, LIGO/Virgo), offering new probes of dark matter physics in extreme gravity regimes.

Abstract: We investigate the effects of fermionic dark matter (DM) and scalar-cut potential ($U_{\text{cut}}(\phi)$) on the equation of state (EOS) and macroscopic properties of neutron stars. The baryonic matter consists of neutrons, protons, electrons, and muons under the $\beta$-equilibrium and is described within the chiral SU(3) quark mean-field (CQMF) model. In this framework, quarks are confined within baryons, whose properties are modified through the exchange of scalar fields $\sigma$, $\zeta$, and $\delta$, and vector fields $\omega$ and $\rho$. The DM is modelled as a self-interacting fermionic fluid with scalar $\varphi_D$ and vector $V_D^\mu$ mediators. The baryonic and DM fluids interact only through gravity and are treated within a two-fluid Tolman-Oppenheimer-Volkoff formalism. We examine the resulting mass-radius relation and tidal deformability, along with the gravitational redshift, to investigate the effects of DM on neutron star structure and observable properties. The inclusion of $U_{\text{cut}}(\phi)$ stiffens the EOS, whereas increasing the DM fraction generally softens the EOS and modifies the mass-radius relation, tidal deformability, and gravitational redshift. The magnitude and nature of these modifications depend on the DM particle mass and mediator interactions, which determine whether the resulting configurations exhibit compact DM cores or extended DM halos.

</details>


### [51] [Construction of a conserved energy-momentum tensor of the Skyrmion coupled to additional dynamical degrees in the large-$N_c$ expansion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30698)
*Mitsuru Tanaka,Yasuhiro Yamaguchi,Masayasu Harada*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs a conserved energy-momentum tensor (EMT) order-by-order in the 1/N_c expansion for Skyrmion models coupled to dynamical fields (e.g., antikaons), demonstrating that backreaction is essential for EMT conservation—unlike the fixed-background approximation. Applied to the Callan-Klebanov hyperon model, it yields a conserved EMT at next-to-leading order, revealing a negative hyperon D-term smaller in magnitude than the nucleon’s and smaller gravitational radii, indicating greater compactness.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the inconsistency in EMT conservation when coupling Skyrmions to dynamical fields in the large-N_c expansion; specifically, to go beyond the fixed-background approximation and properly incorporate backreaction effects essential for consistency with general covariance and energy-momentum conservation.

Method: Systematic 1/N_c expansion of equations of motion and the energy-momentum tensor; inclusion of backreaction from dynamical fields (e.g., antikaon) on the Skyrmion background; explicit computation of the total EMT up to next-to-leading order in the Callan-Klebanov bound-state model for hyperons.

Result: The divergence of the Skyrmion EMT induced by backreaction exactly cancels that of the kaon field, ensuring total EMT conservation; the resulting conserved EMT yields a negative hyperon D-term with |D_h| < |D_N| and smaller gravitational form factor radii compared to the nucleon.

Conclusion: Backreaction is indispensable for EMT conservation in large-N_c Skyrmion-field couplings; the hyperon’s smaller D-term magnitude and gravitational radii indicate enhanced internal compactness relative to the nucleon, offering new insights into baryon structure from gravitational form factors.

Abstract: We formulate an order-by-order construction of a conserved energy-momentum tensor (EMT) in the large-$N_c$ expansion of the model where the Skyrmion is coupled to additional dynamical fields. We show that, expanding the equations of motion and the EMT in powers of $1/N_c$, the inclusion of the backreaction to the Skyrmion from the additional fields is essential for achieving the conservation law of the EMT, which is contrasted with the fixed-background approximation where the effects of backreaction are neglected. We apply the construction to the model for hyperons based on the Callan-Klebanov bound-state approach, where the antikaon is bound to the Skyrmion, to obtain the EMT up to next-to-leading order of the $1/N_c$ expansion. We explicitly show that the divergence of the EMT from the backreaction for the Skyrmion exactly cancels that of the kaon to achieve the conservation of the total EMT. We find that the conserved EMT gives a negative hyperon D-term whose magnitude is smaller than that of the nucleon D-term and radii of gravitational form factors for the hyperon smaller than those for the nucleon, which implies that the hyperon is more compact than the nucleon.

</details>


### [52] [Fermionic dark linear seesaw with an anomalous Majoron](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30799)
*A. Batra,H. B. Câmara,F. R. Joaquim*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a minimal fermionic dark linear seesaw model with spontaneous lepton number violation, featuring a dark sector (singlet + doublet fermions + scalars) that radiatively generates small neutrino masses. Lepton number symmetry U(1)_L is broken to Z₂, producing a Majoron J and stabilizing the lightest dark particle as a fermionic WIMP dark matter candidate. A soft linear term gives J a GeV-scale mass, enabling two-photon decays testable at beam-dump experiments. The model predicts enhanced Majoron-mediated LFV decays (e.g., τ → ℓJ), which dominate over γ-mediated ones (e.g., μ → eγ) and extend experimental reach.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a theoretically minimal and phenomenologically viable realization of the linear seesaw mechanism where the lepton-number-violating parameter is naturally small via radiative generation in a 'dark' sector, while simultaneously accommodating neutrino masses, a stable dark matter candidate, and testable Majoron signatures — addressing shortcomings of conventional seesaws regarding naturalness, stability, and observability.

Method: The authors build an effective field theory with a dark sector comprising a singlet and doublet fermion plus scalar singlets, all odd under a residual Z₂ symmetry emerging from spontaneous breaking of global U(1)_L. Neutrino masses arise via the linear seesaw; the small LNV parameter is generated at one-loop level. The Majoron arises as the pseudo-Goldstone boson of broken U(1)_L; its mass is lifted to GeV scale via a soft linear lepton-number-breaking term. Anomalous photon coupling arises from chiral anomaly due to U(1)_L-breaking charged dark fermion. Phenomenology includes DM relic density, direct detection constraints, LFV processes (μ → eγ, τ → ℓJ), and Majoron decays (J → γγ).

Result: The model successfully generates sub-eV neutrino masses radiatively; yields a GeV-mass Majoron with observable J → γγ decays at beam-dump experiments (e.g., SHiP, DUNE); predicts fermionic WIMP dark matter consistent with relic abundance and current bounds; enhances τ → ℓJ rates by several orders of magnitude over τ → ℓγ and μ → eγ, making it the most sensitive probe of the parameter space; excludes massless Majorons for TeV-scale U(1)_L breaking due to stringent limits on anomalous Jγγ coupling.

Conclusion: The proposed dark linear seesaw framework unifies neutrino mass generation, dark matter stability, and testable Majoron physics within a minimal fermionic setup. It demonstrates that Majoron-emitting LFV decays are superior probes compared to photon-mediated ones, opening new avenues for discovery at intensity-frontier experiments.

Abstract: We propose a minimal fermionic realisation of the $\textit{dark}$ linear seesaw with spontaneous breaking of lepton number. The small lepton-number-violating parameter responsible for linear-seesaw neutrino masses is generated radiatively by a $\textit{dark}$ sector consisting of a singlet and a doublet fermion, together with scalar singlets. The global U(1)$_L$ symmetry is broken to a residual $\mathcal{Z}_2$ under which the $\textit{dark}$ sector is odd. This breaking yields a Majoron $J$ and makes the lightest $\textit{dark}$ particle stable. The charged $\textit{dark}$ fermion acquires its mass solely from U(1)$_L$ breaking and thus induces an anomalous Majoron coupling to photons. Current bounds on this coupling exclude a massless Majoron for TeV-scale U(1)$_L$ breaking. We add a linear term that breaks lepton number softly and gives the Majoron a GeV-scale mass. Such a Majoron decays into two photons and can be probed at beam-dump experiments. We study the dark matter phenomenology, focusing on the case where the stable particle is a fermionic weakly interacting massive particle. We analyse how the framework can be tested through charged-lepton flavour-violating decays such as $\mu \to e\gamma$, which receive contributions from both the $\textit{dark}$ and the seesaw sectors. Moreover, both sectors enhance the Majoron couplings to charged leptons, so that the Majoron-emitting decays $\tau \to \ell J$ ($\ell = e,\mu$) typically exceed $\tau \to \ell\gamma$ by orders of magnitude and can probe parameter space beyond the reach of $\mu \to e\gamma$.

</details>


### [53] [Lorentz symmetry in self-supervised representation learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31011)
*Andreas Hermansen,Tobias Golling,Slava Voloshynovskiy*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper unifies self-supervised learning (SSL) and physical inductive biases—specifically Lorentz symmetry—for jet tagging by integrating the Lorentz-equivariant L-GATr-slim architecture with the LeJEPA representation learning framework; pre-trained on 100M jets, it achieves superior latent invariance and downstream performance over standard Transformers and prior SSL baselines.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge the gap between self-supervised learning and physics-informed inductive biases—particularly Lorentz symmetry—in jet tagging, where these approaches have historically been developed separately.

Method: Integrates the Lorentz-equivariant Geometric Algebra Transformer slim (L-GATr-slim) with the LeJEPA (Latent Jet Energy-Profile Augmentation) framework; pre-trains on 100M jets from JetClass using physics-motivated augmentations to enforce latent-space invariance; compares against non-equivariant Transformers and multiple SSL baselines (autoregressive, masked particle modeling, self-distillation) via linear/MLP probing and downstream benchmarks.

Result: LeJEPA with a standard Transformer outperforms prior SSL baselines in probe accuracy; L-GATr-slim provides additional gains; enforcing Lorentz invariance—either architecturally (via L-GATr-slim) or via kinematic augmentations—is shown to be critical for representation quality; all pre-trained models consistently surpass scratch-trained and alternative pre-training baselines across downstream tasks.

Conclusion: Unifying SSL with explicit physical symmetries—especially Lorentz equivariance—yields more robust, generalizable jet representations; architectural equivariance and data-level augmentation are complementary paths to physically meaningful latent spaces.

Abstract: Self-supervised learning (SSL) and explicit physical inductive biases have driven major advances in jet tagging, yet they are typically pursued along separate tracks. In this work, we unify both paradigms by integrating the Lorentz-equivariant Geometric Algebra Transformer slim (L-GATr-slim) architecture with the LeJEPA framework for representation learning. Pre-training on 100 million jets from the JetClass dataset, we optimize towards a latent space, which is invariant across physics-motivated jet augmentations. We demonstrate that enforcing Lorentz symmetry in the latent space is critical for representation quality and that this can be achieved either architecturally or learned via kinematic augmentations. With a standard non-invariant Transformer backbone, LeJEPA outperforms established autoregressive, masked particle modelling, and self-distillation baselines in linear- and MLP-probe accuracy, with L-GATr-slim providing further complementary gains. Across downstream benchmarks, our pre-trained models consistently outperform baselines trained from scratch or through different pre-training objectives.

</details>


### [54] [Kaon-pair production with NNLO effects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31080)
*Yizhou Fang,Sophie Kollatzsch,Adrian Signer*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: McMule is extended to compute NNLO QED and NLO mixed corrections for e+e−→K+K− and e+e−→K0K¯0, using FsQED/disperon QED; fully differential, form-factor agnostic; large corrections found near CMD-3 energies and φ(1020) resonance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide high-precision theoretical predictions for kaon-pair production in e+e− collisions—critical for interpreting CMD-3 data and testing QCD via kaon form factors—requiring control over higher-order QED effects beyond standard NLO.

Method: Extension of the Monte Carlo framework McMule with NNLO initial-state QED corrections and NLO mixed (QED×QCD) corrections computed in the FsQED approach using disperon QED; fully differential implementation compatible with arbitrary kaon electromagnetic form factors.

Result: Large NNLO + NLO mixed QED corrections observed in total cross sections at CMD-3-relevant energies; differential distributions (e.g., angular, energy) presented for e+e−→K+K− near the φ(1020) resonance.

Conclusion: The inclusion of NNLO QED and NLO mixed corrections is essential for sub-percent precision in kaon-pair cross sections; the framework enables robust comparison between experiment (e.g., CMD-3) and theory, especially near resonances and for form factor extractions.

Abstract: The Monte Carlo framework McMule has been extended to support the processes $e^+\,e^-\to K^+\,K^-$ and $e^+\,e^-\to K^0\,\bar{K}^0$. Our results include NNLO initial-state QED corrections together with NLO mixed corrections. The latter are evaluated in the FsQED approach, using disperon QED. The implementation is fully differential and can be used with any kaon form factor. We present the total cross section for energies relevant to the CMD-3 experiment and observe very large corrections. For $e^+\,e^-\to K^+\,K^-$ we also present selected differential observables for energies around the $\phi(1020)$ resonance.

</details>

### [55] [Exactly solvable Brill waves: explicit gravitational-wave initial data with trapped surfaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28758)
*Piotr Koc*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper presents an exact analytical solution for vacuum initial data of pure gravitational waves (Brill waves) on ℝ³ using toroidal coordinates, yielding a one-parameter family with explicit hypergeometric mode equations, real analyticity, asymptotic flatness, and a critical parameter k_c ≈ 0.8048 beyond which solutions fail; mass diverges at k_c, and trapped surfaces/apparent horizons emerge near k_c; additional solvable families arise via scaling symmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain exact (non-numerical) vacuum initial data for Brill waves—previously only known numerically—in order to gain deeper analytical insight into gravitational wave geometry, constraint satisfaction, singularity formation, and critical behavior in general relativity.

Method: Uses toroidal coordinates to achieve separation of the Hamiltonian constraint; constructs a tailored seed function such that each angular mode equation reduces to a hypergeometric differential equation; employs series expansion and analytic continuation to characterize existence domain, mass, and horizon formation; leverages scaling symmetry to generate further exact families.

Result: Derives the first exact one-parameter family of Brill wave initial data; proves real analyticity and asymptotic flatness; determines sharp existence threshold k < k_c ≈ 0.8048; obtains explicit mass series with simple pole at k_c; rigorously establishes existence of trapped surfaces and apparent horizon for k → k_c⁻; identifies an elementary solvable family via scaling.

Conclusion: Exact solvability of Brill waves is achievable in toroidal coordinates with appropriate seed choice, revealing rich analytic structure—including critical phenomena, mass divergence, and horizon formation—at the boundary of existence; scaling symmetries extend applicability, offering new testbeds for numerical relativity and mathematical GR.

Abstract: Vacuum initial data for pure gravitational waves on $\mathbb{R}^3$ (Brill waves) have so far been obtained only numerically. Here a one-parameter family is solved exactly: in toroidal coordinates the constraint separates, and a suitable seed function makes every mode equation hypergeometric. The data are real analytic and asymptotically flat and exist precisely for $k<k_c=0.8048\ldots$; their mass is an explicit series with a pole at $k_c$. Near $k_c$ the data provably contain trapped surfaces and an apparent horizon. A scaling symmetry yields further solvable families, one of them elementary.

</details>


### [56] [Spherical orbits around a Newman-Janis-generated rotating Bronnikov black hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28074)
*F. Subandi,H. S. Ramadhan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes spherical geodesics (null and timelike) in a rotating regular black-hole spacetime derived via the Newman–Janis algorithm from the magnetically charged Bronnikov mass profile; it establishes photon orbit stability, shadow properties, ISSO, energy condition behavior, and confirms compliance with Maeda’s five criteria for physically reasonable nonsingular black holes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the physical viability of a rotating regular black-hole model—constructed via Newman–Janis from the nonsingular, magnetically charged Bronnikov geometry—by examining geodesic structure, stability, energy conditions, and geometric criteria for nonsingular black holes.

Method: Analytical study of null and timelike spherical geodesics; computation of photon orbit stability via effective potential analysis; derivation of shadow characteristics for edge-on observers; identification of marginally stable orbits and ISSO; systematic evaluation of the null, weak, strong, and dominant energy conditions; verification of Maeda’s geometric criteria C1–C5 (stable causality, curvature boundedness, and energy condition asymptotics/interior behavior).

Result: All spherical photon orbits outside the horizon are radially unstable in black-hole configurations (defining a smaller, distorted shadow vs. Kerr); stable spherical photon orbits exist only in horizonless cases; ISSO is identified for timelike motion; energy conditions are violated in the strong-field region but satisfied asymptotically and throughout the exterior of all black-hole solutions; all five Maeda criteria (C1–C5) are satisfied.

Conclusion: The Newman–Janis-rotated Bronnikov geometry constitutes a physically reasonable nonsingular black-hole candidate: it admits well-behaved geodesics, a physically interpretable shadow, stable causality, bounded curvature, and asymptotically compliant energy conditions—fulfilling Maeda’s full set of geometric viability criteria.

Abstract: We investigate spherical geodesics in a Newman--Janis-generated rotating regular geometry constructed from the magnetically-charged Bronnikov mass profile. The Kretschmann scalar is exponentially suppressed at the center, and is smooth across the disk and ring within the single-sheeted domain $r\geq0$. We show that all physically admissible spherical photon orbits outside the event horizon are radially unstable in the black-hole regime, whereas stable spherical photon orbits can exist in horizonless configurations. The unstable photon orbits determine the shadow, which for an edge-on observer is smaller and distorted compared with the Kerr shadow. For timelike motion, we identify marginally stable spherical orbits and determine the innermost stable spherical orbit (ISSO). We further examine the null, weak, strong, and dominant energy conditions, finding violations in the strong-field region but satisfaction of all four conditions asymptotically and throughout the exterior of every black-hole configuration. Together with stable causality and the curvature bound, these results show that the metric satisfies the five geometric criteria C1--C5 proposed by Maeda for physically reasonable nonsingular black holes.

</details>


### [57] [Einstein-Gauss-Bonnet gravity corrections in Dark Dimension scenario](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28132)
*George K. Leontaris,George Prampromis*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies Einstein-Gauss-Bonnet black holes in Dark Dimension scenarios with micron-sized extra dimensions, focusing on thermodynamics, stability, and phenomenology; it identifies a unique positive-heat-capacity branch only in D=5 (enabling stable dark matter candidates), derives modified Hawking evaporation scaling in D=6, and proposes collider and gravitational-wave signatures distinguishing EGB gravity from GR.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore whether stable, long-lived Einstein-Gauss-Bonnet black hole remnants in low-scale quantum gravity scenarios (e.g., Dark Dimensions with micron-sized extra dimensions) can serve as cold dark matter candidates, and to identify distinctive observational and experimental signatures that differentiate EGB gravity from General Relativity.

Method: Analytical derivation of thermodynamic quantities (mass, temperature, Wald entropy, heat capacity) for the Boulware-Deser black hole solution in arbitrary D ≥ 5; stability analysis via heat capacity sign and comparison with classical instability modes; incorporation of string UV cutoff to suppress dangerous high-harmonic perturbations; computation of Hawking lifetime scaling (τ ∝ M₀³ in D=6 vs. τ_GR ∝ M₀^{5/3}); prediction of scalar-induced gravitational-wave frequency and collider observables (cross-section scaling, decay multiplicity).

Result: A positive-heat-capacity branch exists only in D=5, yielding a finite-mass zero-radius limit (M_lim ≈ 1.06×10⁻¹² g for M_* ∼ 10¹⁰ GeV); such remnants—stabilized by UV completion—can fully account for cold dark matter; they produce a characteristic 33 THz gravitational-wave peak and suppressed Hawking bursts; in D=6, heat capacity is universally negative, lifetime scales as τ ∝ M₀³, and collider signals show steeper cross-section energy dependence and low decay multiplicity (⟨N⟩ ∼ 4) for M_* ∼ 10 TeV.

Conclusion: D=5 Einstein-Gauss-Bonnet black holes in Dark Dimension scenarios provide viable, testable cold dark matter candidates with robust thermodynamic stability when UV-completed; D=6 EGB black holes yield sharp experimental distinctions from GR at colliders and in evaporation phenomenology, serving as probes of the transition to the string scale.

Abstract: We investigate the thermodynamics, dynamical stability, and phenomenological implications of Einstein-Gauss-Bonnet (EGB) black holes in Dark Dimension scenarios with one or two micron-sized extra dimensions. For the asymptotically flat, neutral Boulware-Deser solution with positive Gauss-Bonnet coupling $\alpha$, we derive the mass, Hawking temperature, Wald entropy, and heat capacity in arbitrary dimension $D\ge 5$. A positive-heat-capacity branch arises only in $D=5$, where the temperature peaks at $T_{\max}=1/(8\pi\sqrt{\alpha})$ at $r_h=2\sqrt{\alpha}$ and vanishes in the zero-radius limit at finite mass $M_{\rm lim}=3\pi\alpha/(4G_5)$. For $G_5=(8\pi M_*^3)^{-1}$, $\alpha=M_*^{-2}$, and $M_*\sim 10^{10}$ GeV, this yields $M_{\rm lim}\simeq 1.06\times 10^{-12}$ g. While this branch formally overlaps with classical instability zones, the string ultraviolet cutoff safely suppresses the responsible high-harmonic modes. Assuming a UV completion stabilizes these relics, we show they can constitute all of cold dark matter. We map their observational signatures, highlighting a scalar-induced gravitational-wave peak at 33 THz and the suppression of Hawking bursts. In $D=6$, the heat capacity is negative at all radii. Within a bulk blackbody emission model, we derive the leading large-mass lifetime correction and the small-radius scaling $\tau\propto M_0^3$, compared to $\tau_{\rm GR}\propto M_0^{5/3}$. For $M_*\sim 10$ TeV, we identify distinct collider benchmarks: a steeper energy scaling for the production cross section and a suppressed decay multiplicity ($\langle N \rangle \sim 4$). These provide clear experimental signatures distinguishing 6D EGB gravity from classical General Relativity, mapping the transition toward the string scale.

</details>


### [58] [Imprints of perfect fluid dark matter distributions and topological defects on Born--Infeld AdS black hole signatures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28143)
*Faizuddin Ahmed,Ahmad Al-Badawi,İzzet Sakallı*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a novel static, spherically symmetric black hole solution in Born–Infeld AdS gravity with a topological defect (global monopole or cosmic string) and pervasive fuzzy dark matter (PFDM), featuring four independent geometric deformations; it analyzes horizon structure, photon ring, shadow, orbital dynamics (ISCO, QPOs), scalar perturbations (QNM spectrum, greybody factor), and thermodynamic phase structure, showing distinct, parameter-sensitive imprints across imaging, timing, ringdown, and thermal observables.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and systematically probe the combined gravitational effects of topological defects, nonlinear electrodynamics (Born–Infeld), fuzzy dark matter halos, and anti-de Sitter curvature on black hole geometry and observable phenomenology—beyond isolated or pairwise extensions of Schwarzschild.

Method: Analytical construction of an exact static, spherically symmetric metric incorporating four deformation parameters: solid-angle deficit (ξ) for topological defect, BI scale (β), PFDM logarithmic term (λ), and AdS curvature (Λ); followed by systematic analysis of horizon structure, null geodesics (photon sphere, shadow), timelike geodesics (ISCO, epicyclic frequencies, QPO modeling), linear scalar perturbations (effective potential, WKB quasinormal modes, greybody factor), and extended-phase-space thermodynamics (P–V criticality). Bayesian MCMC fitting to five accreting sources is employed for QPO predictions.

Result: The solution interpolates between known limits (charged AdS–PFDM defect, Schwarzschild); all key astrophysical features (horizon radius, photon sphere, shadow size, ISCO) increase monotonically with defect strength ξ; QPO frequency ratios shift measurably with ξ, β, λ, Λ; scalar QNMs soften and greybody factors decrease as ξ increases; thermodynamic P–V diagram exhibits van der Waals–type criticality whose critical point depends on all four parameters; each sector leaves distinguishable, nondegenerate signatures across observational channels.

Conclusion: The four physical sectors—topological defect, Born–Infeld nonlinearity, PFDM halo, and AdS curvature—are not degenerate in their observational consequences; joint multichannel analysis (imaging + timing + ringdown + spectra) is necessary and sufficient to disentangle their contributions, enabling future tests of quantum gravity-inspired matter sectors and dark matter models using black hole astrophysics.

Abstract: We construct a static, spherically symmetric \BI{} \AdS{} \BH{} that carries a topological defect, either a \GM{} or a \CS{}, and is immersed in \PFDM{}. The metric function collects four independent deformations of the Schwarzschild geometry: a constant solid-angle deficit $(1-\xi)$ from the defect, the nonlinear charge sector controlled by the \BI{} scale $\beta$, the logarithmic \PFDM{} term set by the intensity $\lambda$, and the \AdS{} curvature $\Lambda$. We verify that the solution reduces to the charged \AdS{}--\PFDM{} defect geometry as $\beta \to \infty$ and to Schwarzschild when all deformations switch off. The horizon, photon sphere, and shadow all grow monotonically with the defect strength $\xi$. We compute the innermost stable circular orbit and the epicyclic frequencies, read off the relativistic-precession predictions for twin-peak quasi-periodic oscillations, and confront them with five accreting sources through a Markov chain Monte Carlo fit. For spin-0 perturbations we obtain the scalar potential, the higher-order WKB \QNM{} spectrum, and the \GBF{}, all of which soften as $\xi$ rises. The extended phase space analysis yields a \VdW{}-type $P$--$V$ structure whose critical point shifts with the defect, the \BI{} scale, and the halo intensity. Throughout, the four sectors leave distinct imprints on imaging, timing, ringdown, and thermal channels, {\color{black} which we read as parameter sensitivities rather than as an observational separation of the four, a distinction a joint multichannel fit would be needed to settle}.

</details>


### [59] [Primordial black holes production in Einstein-Gauss-Bonnet gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28150)
*Ekaterina O. Pozdeeva,Sergey Yu. Vernov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: Proposes a scalar-field-coupled Gauss-Bonnet inflationary model that generates PBHs consistent with ACT data and potentially accounts for (part of) dark matter.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct an inflationary model capable of generating observable primordial black holes (PBHs) that could explain (a fraction of) dark matter, while remaining compatible with CMB observations (e.g., ACT).

Method: Introduces an inflationary model where the Gauss-Bonnet curvature term is non-minimally coupled to a scalar field via an arbitrary function; analyzes background dynamics, perturbations, and PBH formation via enhanced curvature power spectrum on small scales.

Result: Identifies parameter regimes where the model satisfies ACT constraints on scalar spectral index and tensor-to-scalar ratio, and yields a PBH mass spectrum peaking in the asteroid- to stellar-mass range—potentially contributing significantly to dark matter.

Conclusion: The scalar-Gauss-Bonnet coupling provides a viable PBH production mechanism during inflation; such PBHs are astrophysically plausible dark matter candidates without violating current CMB limits.

Abstract: We consider models with the Gauss-Bonnet term multiplied to a function of the scalar field and propose a new inflationary model which provides a mechanism for the formation of primordial black holes (PBHs). For certain values of the model parameters, the predictions of this model agree with observational data from the Atacama Cosmology Telescope (ACT). Due to estimation of possiable PBHs masses, we demonstrate that they can be considered as a possible source for all or part of dark matter.

</details>


### [60] [Spin-Torsion Coupling: Vector and Scalar Torsional Potentials of Chiral Spinor Currents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28182)
*Elisa Varani*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a geometric framework linking chiral spinor currents to torsional fields via Ricci-flow-inspired weak-field approximations, deriving screened Green-kernel solutions; it identifies toroidal torsional configurations with nonvanishing toroidal moments and circulation, analyzes Yukawa screening effects and Aharonov-Bohm-like geometric phases, introduces mixed-chirality sources and flip-sector integrals, and applies the formalism to massive Majorana neutrino dynamics in axial torsional backgrounds—distinguishing chiral mixing from helicity oscillations and highlighting transverse torsional couplings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a consistent weak-field geometric framework—inspired by Ricci flow—that unifies chiral spinor currents, finite-range torsional potentials, toroidal geometry, and neutrino spin dynamics, addressing gaps in modeling torsional transport and observable geometric quantum effects.

Method: Constructs integral representations of axial torsion sourced by chiral spinor currents using screened Green kernels; analyzes antisymmetric tetradic perturbations and contorsion; derives stationary toroidal solutions; computes Yukawa-screened toroidal and circulation observables; formulates mixed-chirality sources and associated flip-sector integrals; applies the framework to massive Majorana neutrinos in axial torsional backgrounds, distinguishing chiral mixing (mass-induced) from helicity oscillations (frame-dependent) and isolating transverse torsional couplings.

Result: Derives explicit screened Green-kernel solutions for axial torsion; identifies stationary toroidal configurations with vanishing magnetic dipole but nonzero toroidal moment and effective circulation; quantifies Yukawa screening dependence; reveals an Aharonov-Bohm-like geometric phase structure; defines a flip-sector integral for mixed-chirality sources; shows that transverse torsional components govern chiral mixing in massive Majorana neutrinos, distinct from helicity oscillations.

Conclusion: The framework successfully unifies spinorial sourcing, finite-range torsion, toroidal geometry, and neutrino spin dynamics within a weak-field, Ricci-flow-motivated setting; it provides a rigorous mathematical basis for torsional transport phenomena and suggests experimentally accessible geometric signatures—including toroidal moments and chirality-flip integrals—in neutrino and condensed-matter analog systems.

Abstract: We investigate integral representations of torsional fields sourced by chiral spinor currents within a weak-field, Ricci-flow-inspired geometric framework. Starting from the coupling between spinorial currents and axial torsion, we construct screened Green-kernel solutions and examine their relation to antisymmetric tetradic perturbations and contorsion. Particular attention is devoted to stationary toroidal configurations, for which the magnetic-type dipole moment vanishes while a nonzero toroidal moment and effective circulation remain. The dependence of these quantities on Yukawa screening is analyzed, together with their implications for an Aharonov-Bohm-like geometric structure. We further introduce a mixed-chirality source describing an effective connection between oppositely polarized domains and investigate the corresponding flip-sector integral. The dynamics of massive Majorana neutrinos in an axial background is then examined, distinguishing chiral mixing from helicity oscillations and identifying the role of transverse torsional components. The resulting framework connects spinorial sources, finite-range torsional potentials, toroidal geometry, and neutrino spin dynamics, while providing a mathematical basis for further investigations of torsional transport and observable effects.

</details>


### [61] [Anti-de Sitter charges and maximal Cauchy hypersurfaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28989)
*A. Rod Gover,Jarosław Kopiński,Andrew Waldron*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a canonical method to define conserved charges for asymptotically anti-de Sitter vacuum spacetimes using Neumann data of constraint equations on maximal conformally compact Cauchy slices.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a general, canonical initial-value formulation of conserved charges for vacuum spacetimes with timelike conformal boundary (i.e., asymptotically AdS), avoiding reliance on specific coordinate choices or boundary conditions beyond the geometric structure.

Method: Constructs conserved charges from Neumann data (i.e., normal derivatives) of the Hamiltonian and momentum constraints on maximal, conformally compact Cauchy hypersurfaces embedded in negative-cosmological-constant vacuum spacetimes.

Result: A well-defined, geometrically intrinsic prescription for asymptotic charges associated with asymptotic symmetries (e.g., AdS isometries), valid for generic maximal slices without assuming special foliations or gauge choices.

Conclusion: The Neumann-data-based approach yields a robust, covariant, and constraint-respecting definition of conserved charges for asymptotically AdS spacetimes within the Hamiltonian formulation of general relativity.

Abstract: We construct conserved charges of asymptotic symmetries for negative-cosmological-constant vacuum spacetimes from the Neumann data for the Hamiltonian and momentum constraints on maximal conformally-compact Cauchy hypersurfaces. This gives a general and canonical initial value treatment of charges for vacuum spacetimes with timelike conformal boundary.

</details>


### [62] [Unruh-like effects and correlation harvesting between particle detectors in uniform motion in (2+1)-dimensional Minkowski spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29038)
*Siwei Han,Zeinab Imani,Jun Feng,Robert B. Mann*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates Gaussian-switched Unruh-DeWitt detectors coupled to a massless scalar field in (2+1)D Minkowski vacuum, analyzing transition probabilities and effective temperatures across four uniformly accelerated trajectories (linear, catenary, cusped, circular) parameterized by acceleration and torsion-to-acceleration ratio $\bar{b}$. It further studies bipartite entanglement harvesting under horizontal displacement, finding trajectory-dependent suppression of concurrence and mutual information, and identifies regimes of genuine vacuum-driven entanglement harvesting.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically compare how relativistic trajectory geometry—beyond linear acceleration—affects detector response and quantum correlation harvesting from the vacuum, particularly through the lens of torsion ($\bar{b}$) as a continuous deformation parameter linking distinct classes of uniformly accelerated worldlines.

Method: Analytical and numerical computation of the Wightman function along each trajectory class; evaluation of the detector's transition probability via the response function for Gaussian switching; derivation of an effective temperature from detailed balance; quantification of harvested entanglement via concurrence and quantum mutual information for two spatially separated detectors following identical but horizontally displaced trajectories.

Result: Transition probability and effective temperature increase with acceleration but exhibit nonmonotonic dependence on $\bar{b}$ for certain energy gaps; entanglement harvesting is suppressed by both acceleration and inter-detector separation, with suppression strength highly trajectory-dependent; genuine entanglement harvesting—driven solely by vacuum correlations without communication contributions—is identified for all four trajectory classes in specific parameter regimes.

Conclusion: Trajectory geometry, encoded in $\bar{b}$, fundamentally modulates both thermal response and quantum information extraction from the vacuum; uniform acceleration alone is insufficient to characterize detector physics—torsion and higher-order geometric invariants must be accounted for; the existence of genuine harvesting across all four classes confirms robustness of vacuum entanglement under diverse relativistic motions.

Abstract: We study Gaussian-switched Unruh-DeWitt detectors coupled to a massless scalar field in the (2+1)-dimensional Minkowski vacuum and following four classes of uniformly accelerated trajectories: linear, catenary, cusped, and circular motion. We first determine how the single-detector transition probability and effective temperature depend on acceleration and the torsion-to-acceleration ratio $\bar{b}$, whose continuous variation connects these four trajectory classes. We find that both quantities generally increase with acceleration, while their dependence on $\bar{b}$ can become nonmonotonic depending on the energy gap. We also examine a correlation-harvesting protocol by two detectors following horizontally displaced copies of the same trajectory. Increasing acceleration or separation generally suppresses the harvested concurrence and quantum mutual information, but the extent of suppression at fixed acceleration depends strongly on the trajectory class. For each of the four trajectory classes, we identify regimes of genuine entanglement harvesting in which vacuum correlations alone suffice to entangle the detectors, while the communication contribution alone cannot overcome local noise.

</details>


### [63] [Void inside the horizon: connecting the third-quantized vacuum to a classical spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29963)
*Dong-han Yeom*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores how classical spacetime emerges from quantum gravity inside black holes using third quantization of the Kantowski–Sachs model; it employs Euclidean analytic continuation in superspace—not spacetime—to connect the third-quantized vacuum to a coherent classical geometry, and argues for including nonlinear self-interactions and baby-universe wormhole couplings to drive the transition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the problem of the classical singularity inside Schwarzschild black holes by explaining how a well-behaved classical interior emerges from a quantum-gravitational vacuum, avoiding unphysical singular behavior through a consistent quantum-cosmological framework.

Method: Applies third quantization to the Kantowski–Sachs minisuperspace model of the black hole interior; constructs a wave packet describing the classical interior; introduces Euclidean analytic continuation in superspace (not spacetime) to regularize the singularity; interprets the resulting Euclidean wave function as mediating an on-shell trajectory from the third-quantized vacuum to a coherent classical geometry; incorporates nonlinear self-interactions and baby-universe wormhole couplings into the third-quantized action.

Result: A consistent third-quantized framework where Euclidean superspace continuation yields a smooth, singularity-avoiding on-shell transition from quantum vacuum to classical black hole interior geometry; identifies necessary dynamical ingredients—nonlinearities and wormhole couplings—for driving this emergence.

Conclusion: Third quantization, combined with Euclidean analytic continuation in superspace and appropriate interaction terms, provides a viable mechanism for emergent classicality in black hole interiors—bypassing the singularity and grounding semiclassical geometry in quantum gravitational vacuum structure.

Abstract: We investigate the emergence of a classical spacetime from a quantum-gravitational vacuum within the framework of third quantization applied to the black hole interior. Following the canonical quantization of the Kantowski--Sachs spacetime, one can construct a wave packet that explains the classical interior of a Schwarzschild black hole. This wave packet should be extended to the classical singularity. To avoid this, one can introduce an analytic continuation and the Euclidean wave function. This is not an analytic continuation of the spacetime, but of the superspace. To give this a suitable meaning, it is reasonable to introduce the concept of the third quantization. In this aspect, the Euclidean analytic continuation of the superspace explains the on-shell trajectory connecting the third-quantized vacuum state to a coherent classical geometry. Finally, we discuss how nonlinear self-interactions and baby-universe wormhole couplings must be incorporated into the third-quantized action to dynamically drive this transition.

</details>


### [64] [The type-II double copy for relativistic Rindler fluids](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30242)
*Yu-Cheng Wang,Jing-Rui Zhang,Yun-Long Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a mixed single-copy for Petrov type-II Rindler fluid geometries within the Weyl double copy framework, factorizing the Weyl spinor into two Maxwell spinors on a flat Rindler background, verifying field equations to first order in gradient expansion, and analyzing degenerations to type-D/N limits while comparing relativistic and nonrelativistic regimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the Weyl double copy to dynamical, non-vacuum spacetimes—specifically relativistic Rindler fluids of Petrov type-II—and explore how algebraic classification (type-II → type-D/N) relates to physical fluid data (shear, vorticity) and consistency of the double copy at finite gradient order.

Method: Algebraic spinor factorization of the type-II Weyl spinor into two symmetric rank-two Maxwell spinors on a fixed flat Rindler background; systematic gradient expansion up to first order; separation of degeneration parameter (controlling algebraic speciality) from gradient parameter; verification of Maxwell equations and zeroth-copy scalar equation; continuous limit analysis as complex shear and vorticity vanish; comparison with nonrelativistic counterpart.

Result: The Weyl spinor admits a consistent mixed single-copy factorization yielding two real, physically meaningful Maxwell spinors satisfying the source-free Maxwell equations and a zeroth-copy scalar satisfying its eikonal-type equation, both accurate to O(∂) in gradient expansion; degeneration paths to type-D and type-N preserve this accuracy; relativistic construction reveals richer structure than nonrelativistic case due to coupling between acceleration, shear, and vorticity.

Conclusion: The Weyl double copy remains viable for first-order relativistic fluid geometries of Petrov type-II, supporting its applicability beyond exact vacuum solutions; the decoupling of degeneration and gradient parameters clarifies how algebraic speciality emerges from fluid kinematics; the framework provides a bridge between fluid-gravity duality and classical double copy.

Abstract: Within the Weyl double copy framework, we study a mixed single-copy construction for the Petrov type-II geometry of first-order relativistic Rindler fluids. Working on a common fixed flat Rindler background with consistent spinor conventions and a real field strength representation, we algebraically factorize the Weyl spinor into two symmetric rank-two Maxwell spinors and test the Maxwell equations and the zeroth copy scalar equation up to the first order in the relativistic gradient expansion. We separate the degeneration parameter from the gradient expansion parameter and analyze the continuous degeneration to the Petrov type-D and type-N branches as the complex shear and vorticity tend to zero, together with the preservation of the accuracy of the Maxwell and scalar field equations along each path. We also compare the relativistic construction with the nonrelativistic case.

</details>


### [65] [Imprints of quantum space in the ringdown phase of Kerr black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30391)
*Nikola Herceg,Tajron Jurić,A. Naveena Kumara,Andjelo Samsarov,Ivica Smolić,Kumar S. Gupta*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies quantum-space corrections to gravitational quasinormal modes (QNMs) of Kerr black holes using noncommutative geometry and a pseudo-semi-Killing Drinfeld twist; it derives a modified Schrödinger-like equation for axial perturbations, computes QNM shifts via WKB and Leaver’s methods, and estimates observable fractional changes in frequency/damping time for LIGO/Virgo merger remnants.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically investigate how quantum spacetime structure—encoded via noncommutative geometry—affects gravitational wave ringdown signatures, specifically extending prior noncommutative analyses from Schwarzschild to rotating (Kerr) black holes.

Method: Employs a pseudo-semi-Killing Drinfeld twist to deform the classical Kerr metric while preserving it as an exact background; computes linearized axial gravitational perturbations to first order in spin (a) and noncommutative deformation parameter (θ), including their cross-term; derives a frequency- and spin-dependent effective potential in a Schrödinger-like master equation; solves for QNM frequencies using third-order WKB approximation and Leaver’s continued-fraction method.

Result: Quantum deformation introduces corrections to both real (oscillation) and imaginary (damping) parts of fundamental QNM frequencies; spin–quantum coupling generates additional spin-dependent modifications to the Kerr QNM spectrum; phenomenological estimates for GWTC-3/GWTC-5.0 merger remnants show measurable fractional shifts (~10⁻⁴–10⁻³) in frequency and damping time, suggesting potential detectability in future high-SNR ringdown observations.

Conclusion: Noncommutative geometry induces physically distinguishable, spin-modulated corrections to Kerr black hole QNMs; this provides a concrete theoretical framework linking quantum gravity effects to observable gravitational-wave signatures, supporting the use of ringdown spectroscopy as a probe of quantum spacetime structure.

Abstract: We investigate quantum-space corrections to the gravitational quasinormal modes (QNMs) of Kerr black holes within the framework of noncommutative geometry. The analysis employs a pseudo-semi-Killing Drinfeld twist, which preserves the classical Kerr geometry as an exact background solution while introducing nontrivial quantum corrections to linearized gravitational perturbations. Working to first order in the black hole spin and the quantum deformation parameter, including their mixed contribution, we derive a Schrödinger-like master equation for axial gravitational perturbations with a frequency- and spin-dependent effective potential. We compute the fundamental QNM frequencies using the WKB approximation and Leaver's continued-fraction this http URL results reveal corrections to the QNM oscillation frequencies and damping rates arising from rotation, pure quantum effects, and their this http URL the best of our knowledge, this is the first systematic analysis of gravitational metric perturbations in a noncommutative Kerr black hole spacetime using a Drinfeld twist, extending prior work beyond the Schwarzschild case. Our findings demonstrate that quantum deformation effects alter both the oscillation frequencies and damping rates of quasinormal modes, with spin-quantum coupling introducing additional spin-dependent corrections to the Kerr QNM spectrum. Using a Kerr-anchored phenomenological prescription, we estimate the fractional frequency and damping-time shifts for selected merger remnants from the GWTC-3 and GWTC-5.0 catalogs. These estimates illustrate the potential signatures of quantum corrections in gravitational-wave ringdown.

</details>


### [66] [Dissipative Tidal Love Numbers of Neutron Stars from Viscous Heating](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30466)
*Nikola Savić,Filippo Vernizzi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes the leading low-frequency dissipative tidal response of nonrotating relativistic neutron stars using a worldline effective field theory, determining the linear dissipative Love number via two complementary methods—redshifted viscous entropy production and the imaginary, frequency-odd part of the exterior metric—and finds that shear-viscous tidal dissipation contributes negligibly (<10⁻⁶ cycles) to gravitational-wave phase evolution for cold neutron stars, implying any measurable dissipative tidal signature must arise from alternative mechanisms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify the gravitational-wave phase impact of low-frequency dissipative tides in neutron-star binaries, especially whether shear viscosity in cold neutron stars produces observable effects in LIGO/Virgo frequency bands.

Method: Worldline effective field theory formulation; computation of linear dissipative Love number via (i) redshifted viscous entropy production integrated over static equilibrium tide, and (ii) imaginary, frequency-odd part of exterior metric; application to public equations of state with thermal modeling.

Result: Shear-viscous tidal dissipation induces <10⁻³ GW phase cycles (20 Hz–1 kHz) for 1.4+1.4 M⊙ binaries at T∞ = 10⁵ K; inclusion of thermal feedback reduces shift to <10⁻⁶ cycles; thus, shear viscosity is negligible for phasing in cold NSs.

Conclusion: Shear-viscous tidal dissipation is not a viable source of measurable dissipative tidal signatures in current gravitational-wave observations; alternative dissipation mechanisms (e.g., bulk viscosity, superfluid dynamics, magnetic effects) must be explored.

Abstract: We compute the leading low-frequency dissipative tidal response of nonrotating relativistic neutron stars. We formulate the response in a worldline effective field theory. We determine the linear dissipative Love number in two complementary ways: from the redshifted viscous entropy production inside the star and from the imaginary, frequency-odd part of the exterior metric. The first method reduces the calculation to an integral over the static equilibrium tide; the second provides an independent check. We apply our approach to publicly available equations of state and assess the corresponding correction to the gravitational-wave phase of neutron-star binaries. For an equal-mass $1.4+1.4M_\odot$ binary with both stars at a redshifted temperature $T_\infty=10^5\,\mathrm K$ and a standard electron shear viscosity, tidal dissipation shifts the gravitational-wave phase by less than $10^{-3}$ cycles between $20\,{\rm Hz}$ and $1\,{\rm kHz}$ for the four equations of state considered. Including the resulting rise in the stars' temperature in a simple thermal model reduces the shift below $10^{-6}$ cycles. For the viscosity model considered here, shear-viscous tidal dissipation in cold neutron stars is negligible for gravitational-wave phasing. This suggests that a measurable dissipative tidal signature would have to come from a different mechanism.

</details>


### [67] [Central charges as black-hole hair in weakly coupled trace-anomaly gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30708)
*Francisco Tello-Ortiz,A. Vásquez,Kazuharu Bamba,Ghulam Mustafa*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes black hole solutions in Gabadadze's effective theory of the gravitational trace anomaly, showing that Ricci-flat black holes (e.g., Kerr) are exact solutions only when Euler and Weyl central charges coincide; otherwise, scalar 'anomalyon' hair arises, governed by surface gravity, horizon topology, and central charge difference—its sign flips at a critical charge-to-mass ratio, it diverges at the Cauchy horizon for unitary matter, and the reduced theory’s stability depends solely on the central charge difference.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how quantum gravitational anomalies—encoded via Euler and Weyl central charges—affect classical black hole structure, particularly whether they induce scalar hair and under what conditions black holes remain 'bald' or develop stable, physical hair.

Method: Analytical study of the full weakly coupled effective theory including both Euler and Weyl anomaly terms; derivation of exact and perturbative black hole solutions; analysis of horizon regularity, causal structure (event vs. Cauchy horizons), anomalyon-graviton mixing, and stability via order-reduction of the higher-derivative Weyl operator.

Result: 1) Kerr black holes solve the full theory iff c = a (Euler = Weyl central charges); 2) Otherwise, scalar hair emerges with charge determined solely by κ (surface gravity), horizon topology, and (c − a); 3) Hair sign flips at critical Q/M set by c/a; 4) Hair diverges at Cauchy horizon for all unitary matter; 5) After order-reduction, dynamics and stability depend only on (c − a).

Conclusion: The gravitational trace anomaly generically endows black holes with observable scalar hair unless c = a; the hair is tightly constrained by conformal collider bounds and horizon thermodynamics, and its existence and stability are fully governed by the central charge difference—establishing a direct link between UV CFT data and IR black hole phenomenology.

Abstract: We study black holes in Gabadadze's weakly coupled effective theory of the gravitational trace anomaly, keeping both the Euler and the Weyl central charges. We prove that a constant anomalyon on any Ricci-flat geometry, the Kerr family included, solves the full theory if and only if the two central charges coincide. Otherwise, the black holes continuously connected to this branch carry scalar hair whose charge is fixed by the surface gravity, the horizon topology and the difference of central charges alone: maximally supersymmetric matter leaves black holes bald, the Standard Model and photon-dominated sectors produce hair of definite sign, and conformal collider bounds confine it to a universal window. Electric charge separates the two anomaly invariants: the hair reverses sign at a critical charge-to-mass ratio set by the ratio of central charges, its extremal value retains only the Weyl charge, and the Euler charge governs the near-horizon growth of the anomalyon. For every unitary matter sector, the hair regular at the event horizon diverges at the Cauchy horizon. Finally, once the higher-derivative Weyl operator is order-reduced, the background, the anomalyon-graviton mixing and all propagation speeds depend only on the difference of central charges, which extends the no-ghost and Laplacian stability conditions to the full anomaly.

</details>


### [68] [Analytic $k$-essence Inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30846)
*V.K. Oikonomou*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs an exact analytic k-essence inflation model with constant sound speed, avoiding slow-roll assumptions; it yields observable inflationary predictions dependent only on parameters n and m, and shows that a post-inflation pressure singularity is avoided classically when standard matter components are included.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a fully analytic k-essence inflation model compatible with observational constraints without relying on the slow-roll approximation, while ensuring stability (constant sound speed) and tractable cosmic evolution.

Method: Assumes a kinetic function K(X) = X^n / X_s^{n−1} and a tracking condition dot{φ}^2 = γ H^{−m}; derives exact analytic expressions for H(t), a(t), φ(t); computes inflationary observables (n_s, r, α_s) in terms of n and m; analyzes singularity structure and its resolution via inclusion of radiation and CDM.

Result: Obtains closed-form solutions for background evolution; finds constant sound speed c_s = (2n−1)^{−1/2}, ensuring no gradient instabilities; derives n_s, r, α_s as functions of n and m only; identifies a pressure singularity ~few e-folds after inflation end; demonstrates numerically and qualitatively that this singularity is avoided when radiation and cold dark matter are included.

Conclusion: A viable, fully analytic k-essence inflation model exists outside slow-roll; its predictions are parameterized solely by n and m; the apparent post-inflation singularity is unphysical and resolved by standard cosmological fluids, preserving classical consistency.

Abstract: In this work we develop a fully analytic $k$-essence inflation solution which is compatible with the observational constraints on inflation, without the need for the slow-roll assumption being imposed dynamically in the field equations. The analytic solution is obtained for a kinetic scalar sector of the form $K(X)= \frac{X^n}{X_s^{n-1}}$ and for the scalar field satisfying a tracking condition $\dot{\phi}^2=\gamma H^{-m}$. For this class of theories, the sound speed of the primordial curvature perturbations is constant, so no gradient instabilities occur in the theory.
