The Hubble rate, the scale factor and the scalar field can be analytically expressed as functions of the cosmic time, and the observational indices of inflation depend solely on the parameters $n$ and $m$. The resulting evolution is inflationary and it develops a pressure singularity a few $e$-foldings after the end of inflation. However, using qualitative and quantitative numerical arguments, we show that the pressure singularity is classically avoided when the matter fluids of radiation and cold dark matter are taken into account.

</details>


### [69] [Curvature-history tensor memory in inflationary cosmology: Component closure, ghost-free dynamics, and CMB BipoSH likelihood](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28106)
*G. M. Rhythm*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes a generally covariant inflationary model where curvature-driven activation of a tensor-memory order parameter induces scale-dependent statistical anisotropy in the primordial power spectrum, realized via a Gauss–Bonnet–Kretschmann curvature source and a dressed tensor field formalism that preserves symplectic structure and avoids Ostrogradsky instabilities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a physically consistent, generally covariant inflationary mechanism that generates observable statistical anisotropy (e.g., quadrupolar asymmetry) without violating unitarity or introducing ghost modes, while linking it to a geometric memory effect encoded in a symmetric-tracefree tensor order parameter.

Method: Uses the four-dimensional Gauss–Bonnet completion of the Kretschmann scalar as a curvature source; introduces a dressed tensor field $Q_{\mu\nu} = \mathcal{E}_C \widehat{Q}_{\mu\nu}$ with exponential differential operator dressing $\mathcal{E}_C = \exp[B(C)\Box_{\widehat{Q}}/2]$; performs exact quadratic Hessian derivation, ADM constraint elimination via block Schur complement, retarded Cauchy formulation to fix homogeneous modes, and nonlinear ADM Legendre analysis to assess stability against Ostrogradsky ghosts; constructs cut-sky Gaussian and quadratic maximum-likelihood estimators for the predicted BipoSH covariance.

Result: Derives a leading anisotropic curvature action yielding an infrared-regular oscillatory primordial quadrupole and exact scale-dependent $T/E$ BipoSH covariance; confirms the absence of Ostrogradsky instability near the inflationary solution; obtains subdominant memory energy on an approximately constant activated plateau; provides concrete observational estimators for the predicted anisotropy.

Conclusion: The model realizes a geometric, memory-mediated mechanism for primordial statistical anisotropy within a stable, ghost-free, generally covariant framework—offering a testable alternative to standard isotropic inflation, with distinct signatures in CMB BipoSH statistics.

Abstract: We present a generally covariant inflationary model in which curvature history activates a spatial symmetric-tracefree tensor-memory order parameter and generates a scale-dependent quadrupolar departure from primordial statistical isotropy. The curvature source is the four-dimensional Gauss--Bonnet completion of the Kretschmann invariant. A local $(C,\chi)$ representation is used only to implement the retarded theory: fixed retarded Cauchy data remove the homogeneous response modes, so the negative-sign localized combination is not an independent phase-space degree of freedom. The projected tensor sector is described by the dressed field $Q_{\mu\nu}=\mathcal E_C\widehat Q_{\mu\nu}$ with $\mathcal E_C=\exp[B(C)\Box_{\widehat Q}/2]$. Its raw-field Hessian has the exact congruence form $\mathcal E_C^\dagger(\Box_Q-\mathsf M^2)\mathcal E_C$, so an invertible dressing preserves the solution count and the sign of the physical symplectic norm even when $[B(C),\Box_Q]\ne0$. We derive the full quadratic Hessian, eliminate the ADM constraints and retarded response variables through an exact block Schur complement, and obtain the leading anisotropic curvature action. A nonlinear ADM Legendre analysis shows that the representative inflationary solution remains far from the singular threshold associated with an Ostrogradsky branch. A smooth curvature-dependent phase potential gives an approximately constant activated plateau with subdominant memory energy. Finite activation then produces an infrared-regular oscillatory primordial quadrupole and an exact scale-dependent $T/E$ BipoSH covariance. We also give the corresponding cut-sky Gaussian and quadratic maximum-likelihood estimators.

</details>


### [70] [Generalization of the Theory of Relativity Based on Finsler Geometry, Incorporating a Fundamental Mass at High Energies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28141)
*Rustam Ibadov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper generalizes relativity by introducing a fundamental invariant mass M alongside c, leading to a Lorentzian Randers–Finsler spacetime geometry; it yields testable deviations for massive particles (bounded energy E < M, deformed time dilation, composition-dependent free fall), preserves standard light propagation and de Sitter space, and embeds Kadyshevsky’s hypothesis in a geometric, phenomenologically constrained framework.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To geometrize Kadyshevsky's hypothesis of a fundamental mass scale M as a second invariant (alongside c), thereby extending relativity while ensuring theoretical consistency (e.g., relativity principle, causality) and experimental testability via deviations from GR and SR.

Method: Constructs a projectively deformed mass shell in momentum space, computes its exact Legendre transform to obtain a closed-form Finsler metric $F_\mu$ — specifically a Lorentzian Randers metric $F_\mu = \alpha + \beta$, where $\alpha$ is derived from $g$ and $\beta$ from a timelike covector $u$ — interprets it as a Zermelo navigation metric with shifted symmetry center $W$, derives particle kinematics and field equations (Finsler–Einstein tensor), and solves cosmological dynamics exactly for flat FLRW.

Result: Derives a consistent two-invariant relativity with $F_\mu$ exhibiting Lorentz symmetry centered at displaced origin $W$; predicts $E < M$, modified time dilation, composition-dependent equivalence principle violation $\eta \simeq \Delta m/M$, unaltered null geodesics, exact de Sitter solution, and Friedmann equations with $\mu \dot{H}$ corrections; bounds $M$ using UHECR, MICROSCOPE, and electron-EDM data.

Conclusion: A geometric, self-consistent extension of relativity with two universal invariants ($c$ and $M$) is realized via a Lorentzian Randers–Finsler structure; it preserves core relativistic features for light while predicting distinctive, constrained signatures for massive matter, rendering Kadyshevsky’s fundamental mass hypothesis both rigorous and empirically falsifiable.

Abstract: We generalize special and general relativity so that, besides the speed of light $c$, an invariant fundamental mass $M$ bounds the energy of every massive particle. The exact Legendre transform of a projectively deformed mass shell gives, for a particle of mass $m$, a closed-form Finsler function $F_\mu$, $\mu=m/M$: a Lorentzian Randers metric built from the spacetime metric $g$ and a unit timelike covector $u$. Its indicatrix is the Minkowski hyperboloid displaced by $W=\mu u/(1+\mu)$, so $F_\mu$ is a Zermelo navigation metric whose symmetry group, the Lorentz group centred at $W$, realizes a relativity principle with two invariants, $c$ and $M$. Light propagates exactly as in Einstein's theory, while massive particles obey $E<M$, a deformed time-dilation law and a composition-dependent free fall, $\eta\simeq\Delta m/M$. The Finsler--Einstein tensor equation, solved exactly for a spatially flat universe, gives Friedmann equations with corrections $\propto\mu\dot H$ and leaves de~Sitter space exact. Observed particle energies, equivalence-principle tests and electron-EDM limits constrain $M$. The construction makes Kadyshevsky's fundamental-mass hypothesis geometric and testable.

</details>


### [71] [Stationary branches of non-canonical spinor theories and their cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28149)
*Ugo Moschella,Mario Novello,Oliver F. Piattella*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes a non-canonical spinor field coupled to gravity, identifying two solution branches: a regular branch obeying the massless Dirac equation with vacuum energy from a Lagrangian constant, and a stationary branch where the spinor equations are identically satisfied and its energy-momentum tensor mimics a cosmological constant regardless of configuration; in cosmology, both act as Λ-like sources, and with a potential, the stationary branch yields ΛCDM-like expansion while keeping vacuum energy tunable via a Lagrangian constant.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how non-canonical fermionic Lagrangians—depending on the kinetic scalar and scalar bilinear—can generate cosmological-constant-like behavior and alternative dark energy/dark matter phenomenology without fine-tuning vacuum energy.

Method: Analytical derivation of field equations for a minimally coupled spinor-gravity system with a general Lagrangian L = L(X, Y), where X is the fermionic kinetic scalar and Y the scalar bilinear; classification of solutions into regular and stationary branches; analysis of energy-momentum tensor structure, cosmological evolution in FLRW spacetime, and asymptotic dynamics with potentials.

Result: Two disjoint solution branches emerge: (1) regular branch — spinor obeys massless Dirac equation, contributes constant vacuum energy; (2) stationary branch — spinor equations trivially satisfied, T_μν ≡ −Λ_g g_μν for arbitrary spinor configuration, yielding indeterminate evolution; in FLRW, both yield effective Λ; with a potential, late-time attractor is stationary, producing pressureless component + effective Λ; quadratic model exactly reproduces ΛCDM background expansion.

Conclusion: Non-canonical spinor Lagrangians admit a stationary branch that dynamically generates an effective cosmological constant independent of quantum vacuum contributions, offering a classical mechanism to decouple observed cosmic acceleration from Planck-scale vacuum energy, while matching ΛCDM expansion history.

Abstract: We study a classical spinor field minimally coupled to gravity and described by a non-canonical Lagrangian depending on the fermionic kinetic scalar and the scalar bilinear. For purely kinetic theories, we show that the field equations split the solutions into two distinct branches. On the regular branch, the spinor satisfies the massless Dirac equation and contributes a vacuum energy determined by an additive constant in the Lagrangian. On the stationary branch, the spinor field equations become identically satisfied, while the energy-momentum tensor is exactly that of a cosmological constant for any spinor configuration and in any spacetime. The spinor evolution is therefore not uniquely determined by the field equations. In a spatially flat homogeneous universe, both branches act as cosmological-constant sources. We also give the dilution law for homogeneous configurations in theories with explicit dependence on the scalar bilinear. When a potential with a nonzero minimum is introduced, the stationary configuration becomes the late-time state of the cosmological evolution, and the approach to it generates a pressureless component in addition to an effective cosmological constant. For a simple quadratic model, the background expansion reproduces exactly that of the standard $\Lambda$CDM cosmology, while the value of the vacuum energy remains controlled by an additive constant in the Lagrangian.

</details>


### [72] [Optical appearance of a non-commutative black hole surrounded by an accretion disk](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28151)
*Adailton Azevêdo Araújo Filho,Narges Heidari,Alesandro Ferreira dos Santos,Shahin Mamedov,Valdir Barbosa Bezerra,Amilcar Rabelo de Queiroz,Ali Övgün*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how non-commutative geometry modifies the optical appearance of a Schwarzschild black hole with a thin accretion disk, finding significant changes in photon orbits, ISCO radius, critical impact parameter, and observed intensity features due to the Lorentzian matter distribution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore observational signatures of non-commutative spacetime geometry via black hole imaging—specifically, how a Lorentzian smeared mass distribution alters lensing, emission morphology, and relativistic spectral features compared to classical Schwarzschild black holes.

Method: Numerical ray-tracing in a non-commutative Schwarzschild metric with Lorentzian effective exterior; computation of null geodesics, transfer functions, bolometric intensity for static and circular emitters; inclusion of gravitational redshift, orbital time dilation, and Doppler boosting.

Result: Increasing non-commutativity parameter θ = Θ/M² from 0 to 0.03 reduces critical impact parameter by ~16.1% and ISCO radius by ~23.7%; higher-order lensing features contract accordingly; direct peak shifts <1%; peak intensity and integrated flux increase by ~6.5% and ~4.3%; radial emission profile affects central brightness depression; orbital motion induces asymmetry in inclined images.

Conclusion: Non-commutative corrections produce quantifiable, potentially observable imprints on black hole shadows and disk emission—particularly in higher-order photon rings and flux normalization—making them testable with next-generation very-long-baseline interferometry.

Abstract: We investigate the optical appearance of a non--commutative Schwarzschild black hole surrounded by a transparent, geometrically thin emitting disk. A Lorentzian matter distribution supplies the effective exterior metric. We compute photon trajectories, disk transfer functions, and the observed bolometric intensity for static and circular emitters, taking into account gravitational redshift, orbital time dilation, and Doppler modulation. At fixed asymptotic mass, increasing $\theta=\Theta/M^2$ from zero to $0.03$ reduces the critical impact parameter by approximately $16.1\%$ and the innermost stable circular orbit radius by $23.7\%$. The narrow higher-order features follow this contraction, while the broad direct peak shifts by less than $1\%$ for a fixed emission profile. With the same source normalization, the peak direct intensity and the signal integrated over the screen aperture increase by approximately $6.5\%$ and $4.3\%$, respectively. Moreover, different radial emission profiles produce distinct central brightness depressions at the same critical curve, and orbital motion generates an asymmetry between the approaching and receding sides of inclined images.

</details>


### [73] [Kinetic moment estimates and a one-sided Lyapunov barrier for the relativistic fermionic Michie-King equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28278)
*Dorota Bors,Robert Stańczy*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the relativistic fermionic RAR equation of state under fixed Tolman–Klein invariants, derives exact kinetic-moment expressions for pressure and its derivative, establishes a global Cauchy–Schwarz relation implying vector-field domination by a linear EOS, constructs a refined Lyapunov barrier to bound the compactness ratio $2Gm(r)/(rc^2)$ explicitly (yielding 0.6879… and sharpened 0.6734…), and corrects a logarithmic coefficient in prior linear-comparator formulas.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain rigorous, explicit upper bounds on stellar compactness for regular, spherically symmetric, relativistic fermion stars governed by the cutoff Ruffini–Argüelles–Rueda (RAR) equation of state—without relying on numerical integration or separate existence proofs—by exploiting structural properties of the Tolman–Oppenheimer–Volkoff (TOV) system and kinetic moment identities.

Method: Analytical derivation using kinetic theory: three exact moment relations for pressure and its radial derivative; application of the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality to obtain a global constraint between pressure and density gradient; construction of a filled, one-sided Lyapunov barrier inspired by Bors–Stańczy methods; boundary analysis of the barrier in the physical $(x,y)$-wedge; evaluation along stationary curves (e.g., diagonal) to sharpen bounds; correction of a previously misstated logarithmic prefactor in a linear-comparator estimate.

Result: An explicit Lambert-function-based upper bound $2Gm(r)/(rc^2) < 0.6879212521$ for all regular TOV solutions with the RAR EOS; sharpened to $0.6734385929$ along the diagonal where the compactness ratio is stationary; verification that the Lyapunov barrier fully controls outward excursions—including post-exit behavior—ensuring global validity; correction of a logarithmic coefficient in the linear-comparator formula used in prior literature.

Conclusion: The RAR equation of state admits analytically tractable, physically meaningful compactness bounds strictly below the Buchdahl limit, achievable via kinetic-moment identities and Lyapunov barrier techniques—demonstrating that sharp analytical control is possible even for complex relativistic fermionic EOSs without numerical assumptions.

Abstract: We study the cutoff Ruffini--Argüelles--Rueda (RAR), or relativistic fermionic Michie--King, equation of state along an equilibrium family with fixed Tolman--Klein invariants. Three kinetic moments give exact formulas for both the pressure-to-density ratio and the derivative of pressure. Cauchy--Schwarz yields a global relation between these two quantities. It implies vertical vector-field domination of the associated Tolman--Oppenheimer--Volkoff system by the linear equation of state $P=\rho c^2/\sqrt3$ in the physical wedge $0<y\leq3x$. We construct a filled, one-sided version of the Bors--Stańczy Lyapunov barrier and check every boundary. This controls all outward excursions, including those after a lower exit from a Lyapunov sublevel. For regular configurations the resulting explicit Lambert bound is $2Gm(r)/(rc^2)<0.6879212521$. Using the diagonal where this ratio has a stationary value sharpens the bound to $0.6734385929$. The argument is conditional on a regular TOV configuration and does not require a separate finite-radius existence theorem. We also identify and correct a logarithmic coefficient in the previously used explicit linear-comparator formula.

</details>


### [74] [Radial stability of nonlinearly scalarized black holes in Einstein-phantom scalar-Gauss-Bonnet theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28523)
*De-Cheng Zou,Yun Soo Myung,Meng-Yun Lai,Hyat Huang,Wen-Cong Gan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates radial stability of nonlinearly scalarized black holes in Einstein-phantom scalar-Gauss-Bonnet theory, identifying two branches (lower and primary); the lower branch is unstable (one growing mode), while the primary branch can be stable via S-deformation, especially for exponential coupling; thermodynamic stability is linked to radial stability; canonical scalarized black holes with polynomial coupling show analogous instability/stability patterns.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the radial stability of phantom and canonical nonlinearly scalarized black holes in Gauss-Bonnet gravity, and to clarify the relationship between dynamical (radial) stability and thermodynamic stability.

Method: Linearized coupled scalar-metric perturbations are reduced to a Schrödinger-type equation; radial stability is assessed via existence of growing modes and application of the S-deformation method; thermodynamic analysis is performed in parallel for both branches.

Result: Lower-branch phantom scalarized black holes (including pure quartic solutions) possess one growing mode → unstable; primary-branch solutions admit positive zero-energy modes → stable under S-deformation, particularly for exponential coupling; canonical scalarized black holes with polynomial coupling replicate this dichotomy (unstable lower, stable primary).

Conclusion: Radial stability strongly depends on the branch and coupling type: exponential coupling enables stable primary-branch phantom scalarized black holes, whereas lower branches are generically unstable; thermodynamic and radial stability are correlated; results extend to canonical scalarized black holes, confirming robustness of the two-branch stability structure.

Abstract: We study the radial stability of nonlinearly scalarized black holes found in Einstein--phantom scalar--Gauss--Bonnet theory with polynomial and exponential couplings. Two branches of lower and primary are found for phantom scalarized black holes. The coupled scalar--metric perturbations reduce to a Schrödinger-type equation, after eliminating redundant metric perturbations. The lower branch including the pure quartic solutions has one growing mode, while positive zero-energy solutions for the primary branch could establish stability through the $S$-deformation method. Stable primary solutions are found for the exponential coupling. Also, we explore the connection between radial stability and thermodynamic analysis for two branches. Furthermore, we show that canonical scalarized black holes obtained from Einstein--scalar--Gauss--Bonnet theory with polynomial coupling reveals unstable lower and stable primary branches.

</details>


### [75] [Numerical Integration of Geodesic Motion in Non-Schwarzschild Spacetimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28694)
*Jan Pijáček,Georgios Lukes-Gerakopoulos,Ondřej Zelenka*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a numerical method in Wolfram Mathematica to solve geodesic motion around a Schwarzschild black hole with a thin Vogt-Letelier disk, addressing challenges from broken spherical symmetry and metric discontinuities at the disk; it employs four-velocity renormalization for stable integration and analyzes chaotic behavior via Poincaré sections and rotation number spectra.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To study geodesic dynamics in a non-integrable spacetime formed by adding a thin accretion disk (Vogt-Letelier model) to a Schwarzschild black hole—where spherical symmetry breaking induces chaos and numerical challenges arise near the disk due to metric derivative discontinuities.

Method: Numerical integration of timelike geodesics in Mathematica, with specialized handling of disk crossings via four-velocity renormalization to preserve normalization and improve stability; dynamical analysis using Poincaré sections and rotation number spectra to characterize regular vs. chaotic orbits.

Result: A robust numerical scheme for geodesic integration across the thin disk is achieved; phase-space analysis reveals mixed dynamics—coexistence of regular and chaotic orbits—quantified by rotation numbers and Poincaré maps.

Conclusion: The re-normalization method effectively handles metric discontinuities and conserves the timelike normalization condition; the system exhibits weak integrability and rich dynamical structure, making it suitable for studying relativistic disk–black hole interactions and onset of chaos in astrophysical spacetimes.

Abstract: This contribution presents a method for numerically solving geodesic motion in Wolfram Mathematica applied on a system consisting of a Schwarzschild black hole surrounded by a thin Vogt-Letelier disk. While geodesic motion in the Schwarzschild spacetime is fully integrable due to its high degree of symmetry, the addition of a disk breaks the spherical symmetry and leads to a weak-integrable dynamical system. Particular attention is paid to the treatment of particle trajectories near the thin disk, where discontinuities in the derivatives of the metric may affect numerical stability and the conservation of integrals of motion. Several numerical treatments of disk crossings were implemented, arriving to a method based on the re-normalization of the four-velocity. The phase-space of the system is investigated using Poincaré sections and rotation number spectra.

</details>


### [76] [Gravitomagnetic Frame Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28731)
*Ruggiero Matteo Luca,Astesiano Davide*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how spinning test particles respond to a finite-duration plane gravitational wave in the detector's proper frame, finding that the integrated gravitomagnetic field induces a persistent relative spin rotation—termed 'spin memory'—which serves as a local, operational probe of the magnetic part of spacetime curvature.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify and characterize a local, observable gravitational memory effect tied directly to the magnetic (spin-coupling) part of spacetime curvature, beyond standard displacement or velocity memories.

Method: Uses Fermi normal coordinates and gravitoelectromagnetic decomposition; computes spin evolution at linear order in wave amplitude and leading order in spatial separation from a reference worldline; focuses on time-integrated gravitomagnetic field (pulse area).

Result: A finite gravitomagnetic pulse area induces a residual, post-wave relative rotation between spatially separated spins—a local frame-memory effect with velocity-type structure.

Conclusion: The spin memory effect provides a direct, operational, local measure of the integrated magnetic component of Riemann curvature, distinct from known displacement/velocity memories and detectable via spin precession measurements.

Abstract: We investigate the response of spatially separated spinning test particles to a finite-duration plane gravitational wave in the proper detector frame, {working to linear order in the wave amplitude.} Using Fermi normal coordinates and the gravitoelectromagnetic decomposition of the local metric, the spin evolution is governed, at leading order in the distance from the reference world-line, by the magnetic part of the Riemann tensor. A finite curvature profile with a non-vanishing gravitomagnetic pulse area induces a residual relative rotation of the spins, proportional to the time integral of the local gravitomagnetic field. The resulting change persists after the curvature has vanished and may be interpreted as a local frame-memory observable. This effect has the structure of a velocity-type memory of finite plane-wave curvature profiles and provides a direct operational probe of the integrated magnetic part of spacetime curvature.

</details>


### [77] [The shadow of rotating FJNW metric](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28990)
*Mina Shokrollahi,Behrouz Mirza,Mahnaz Tavakoli Kachi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the astrophysical properties—specifically the black hole shadow and light deflection—of the rotating Fisher-Janis-Newman-Winicour (FJNW) spacetime using the Hamilton-Jacobi formalism.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore observable astrophysical signatures (shadow size and light deflection) of the recently derived rotating FJNW metric, a scalar–tensor alternative to Kerr black holes.

Method: Solves the Hamilton-Jacobi equation to derive photon orbits and compute the shadow boundary; employs geodesic analysis to obtain the weak-field gravitational deflection angle of light.

Result: Shadow size increases monotonically with both the spin parameter a and the scalar field parameter γ; light deflection angle is computed in the rotating FJNW geometry, showing dependence on both a and γ.

Conclusion: The rotating FJNW metric yields qualitatively distinct shadow and lensing features compared to Kerr, offering potential observational discriminators for scalar-tensor gravity.

Abstract: We investigate the astrophysical aspects of the rotating Fisher-Janis-Newman-Winicour (FJNW) metric recently derived in [1]. The Hamilton-Jacobi equation is used to obtain the shadow of the rotating FJNW metric. We find that increasing the angular momentum parameter a and the metric parameter \gamma leads to an increase in the size of the shadow. Finally, we study the deflection angle of light in the rotating FJNW spacetime.

</details>


### [78] [Quasinormal Modes and Excitation Factors of a Parity-Symmetric Beyond-Horndeski Black Hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29052)
*Roman A. Konoplya,Milena Skvortsova*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes quasinormal modes (QNMs) and excitation factors of scalar, electromagnetic, and Dirac perturbations on a parity-symmetric beyond-Horndeski black hole, using high-order WKB, Padé resummation, Cesàro convergence diagnostics, time-domain evolution with Prony, Chebyshev pseudospectral methods, and Green-function residue integration. It finds that mode behavior depends critically on normalization (metric-scale vs. ADM-mass), reveals limitations of sixth-order WKB in strongly deformed regimes, and identifies tortoise-coordinate barrier displacement as the dominant cause of large excitation-factor deviations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously characterize linear stability and observational signatures (e.g., ringdown) of black holes in beyond-Horndeski gravity—a theoretically motivated extension of GR with second-order equations and parity symmetry—by resolving discrepancies and methodological limitations in prior QNM studies.

Method: Combined high-precision numerical and analytical techniques: (i) very-high-order WKB expansion with Padé approximants and Cesàro means for convergence control; (ii) independent time-domain simulations with Prony analysis; (iii) Chebyshev pseudospectral collocation for robust frequency scanning in both metric-scale and physical ADM-mass units; (iv) complex-contour integration of retarded Green functions to compute excitation factors.

Result: In metric-scale normalization, increasing deformation lowers QNM frequencies and damping rates; in ADM-mass units, strongly deformed solutions show nonmonotonic, enhanced deviations due to strong asymptotic mass variation. Sixth-order WKB fails significantly (up to 9% in fundamental damping, O(1) in overtones). Excitation factors deviate by >100× from Schwarzschild in tortoise-coordinate convention, but shrink substantially when the potential-barrier maximum is used as origin.

Conclusion: The quasinormal spectrum and excitation factors of beyond-Horndeski black holes are highly sensitive to parametrization and methodological choices; reliable predictions require high-order analytic approximations validated against spectral numerics and proper coordinate-aware interpretation of excitation physics—especially the tortoise-coordinate location of the effective potential barrier.

Abstract: We study scalar, electromagnetic, and Dirac perturbations of an asymptotically flat black hole in parity-symmetric beyond-Horndeski gravity. The quasinormal spectrum is computed with very-high-order WKB expansions and Padé resummation, with Cesàro means used to diagnose internal convergence. The dominant modes are independently tested by time-domain evolution with Prony extraction and by Chebyshev pseudospectral collocation; the latter also provides systematic frequency scans in both the metric-scale and physical ADM-mass normalizations. Excitation factors are obtained separately from Green-function residues by complex-contour integration. In the fixed metric-scale normalization, approaching Schwarzschild generally increases both the oscillation frequency and damping rate, whereas stronger deformation shifts the modes toward lower frequencies and weaker damping. In ADM-mass units, the mildly deformed solutions remain close to Schwarzschild, while the most deformed sector becomes enhanced and nonmonotonic because the asymptotic mass itself varies strongly. The independent methods agree very well, however the sixth-order WKB approximation used in [G. Antoniou, T. D. Pappas, P. Kanti, Phys. Rev. D 112, 084013 (2025)] is insufficient in certain regions of the parameter space: the fundamental-mode discrepancy reaches nine percent in the damping rate and the first-overtone discrepancy can be of order unity. The Schwarzschild excitation factors are recovered. In the strongly deformed regime, their magnitudes can differ from the Schwarzschild values by more than two orders of magnitude in the asymptotic tortoise-coordinate convention. We show that this large effect is predominantly due to the deformation-dependent displacement of the potential barrier in the tortoise coordinate: when its maximum is used as the origin, the excitation factors exhibit much more modest deviations.

</details>


### [79] [The Schwarzschild Ringdown Time Prescription on Critical Kerr Plunges](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29054)
*Jingxu Wu,Junyi Zhang,Daniil Stepanenko,Maxim Khlopov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes quasinormal-mode convergence in Schwarzschild and Kerr black hole plunges, linking constant-amplitude QNM onset to advanced time at r=0 for Schwarzschild, and extending the analysis to prograde critical Kerr plunges where inner horizon behavior forces logarithmic divergence of ingoing time v; by matching v to -u_K at the interior zero of the radial potential P(r₀)=0, it derives a parameter-free onset hierarchy that recovers known Schwarzschild results in the a→0 limit and predicts specific onset times and two-stage decay/frequency locking for a/M = 0.9.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the understanding of constant-amplitude quasinormal-mode (QNM) convergence from Schwarzschild to astrophysically relevant Kerr black holes, particularly for critical prograde plunges where inner horizon physics and causal structure significantly alter the temporal onset and decay behavior of gravitational wave ringdown.

Method: Analyzes the causal structure and null coordinates (v, u_K) near the inner horizon of Kerr black holes; exploits the sign condition P(r_-)<0 to deduce logarithmic divergence of the ingoing Eddington–Finkelstein time v; matches v to the outgoing Kerr coordinate -u_K at the unique interior radius r₀ where the radial potential P(r₀)=0; constructs a parameter-free hierarchy of onset times (u_H, u_{r₀}, u_C) based on this matching; evaluates predictions in the Schwarzschild limit (a→0) and for a/M=0.9 eccentric plunge.

Result: Derives a parameter-free onset hierarchy (u_H, u_{r₀}, u_C) = (6.85M, 11.35M, 13.35M) for a/M = 0.9; confirms recovery of the known Schwarzschild onset offset 9.435M as a→0; predicts two-stage residual decay and frequency locking in the ringdown of critical Kerr plunges.

Conclusion: The constant-amplitude QNM onset is governed by a universal causal matching condition at the interior zero of the radial potential, not just by the source’s advanced time at r=0; this framework unifies Schwarzschild and Kerr plunge ringdown phenomenology and yields quantitative, testable predictions for strong-field gravitational wave signatures.

Abstract: For a Schwarzschild plunge, constant-amplitude quasinormal-mode convergence has been tied to the source's advanced time at $r=0$, lying $9.435M$ after the $(2,2)$ strain peak. On real prograde critical Kerr plunges, $P(r_-)<0$ forces the ingoing time $v$ to diverge logarithmically at the inner horizon while $u_K$ stays finite. Matching $v$ to $-u_K$ at the unique interior zero $P(r_0)=0$ yields a parameter-free onset hierarchy $(u_{\rm H},u_{r_0},u_{\rm C})$ that recovers $9.435M$ as $a\to 0$ and predicts two-stage residual decay and frequency locking at $(6.85M,11.35M,13.35M)$ on an $a/M=0.9$ eccentric plunge.

</details>


### [80] [QTG minimons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29109)
*Valeri P. Frolov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies horizonless, static, spherically symmetric regular solutions ('QTG minimons') in quasitopological gravity, incorporating vacuum polarization via coupling to the Polyakov action; it derives a constrained first-order system, finds de Sitter-like cores in 4D Hayward- and Born–Infeld-type models, observes nonuniform quantum effects near the black hole threshold, and proposes an extremal AdS₂×S^{D−2} throat geometry ('QTG extremons') in the critical limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore regular, horizonless alternatives to black holes in quasitopological gravity—specifically below the mass threshold for black hole formation—and to assess how quantum vacuum polarization (via 2D conformal matter) affects their structure, stability, and critical behavior.

Method: Derives a constrained first-order system from the spherically reduced QTG action coupled to the Polyakov action for 2D conformal matter; performs central series expansions in D ≥ 4; constructs and numerically analyzes 4D solutions for Hayward-type and Born–Infeld-type characteristic functions h(p); examines asymptotic mass scaling and central blueshift under quantum coupling; infers limiting throat geometry via smooth constant-radius limit in proper distance.

Result: QTG minimons exhibit de Sitter-like cores in both Hayward- and Born–Infeld-type models; asymptotic mass decreases with quantum coupling along the near-critical branch; central blueshift diverges even for small coupling; vacuum polarization induces nonuniform perturbations near criticality; a candidate extremal AdS₂×S^{D−2} throat geometry ('QTG extremons') emerges in the smooth constant-radius limit.

Conclusion: Vacuum polarization qualitatively modifies the structure of horizonless QTG solutions near the black hole threshold, supporting the existence of a new class of extremal, regular geometries (QTG extremons) with universal near-horizon AdS₂×S^{D−2} geometry—suggesting a robust quantum-gravitational regularization of black hole singularities and horizons.

Abstract: We study static, spherically symmetric, regular horizonless solutions of quasitopological gravity (QTG), which we call QTG minimons. These configurations form the small-mass branch below the threshold for regular black holes. We include vacuum polarization by coupling the spherically reduced QTG action to the Polyakov action for two-dimensional conformal matter. For an arbitrary characteristic function $h(p)$, we derive a constrained first-order system in the static, asymptotically empty state. We obtain central expansions in $D\ge4$ and analyze four-dimensional solutions in the Hayward-type and Born--Infeld-type models. Both models retain a de Sitter-like core. Along the near-critical branch, the asymptotic mass decreases with the quantum coupling, while the central blueshift can become large even for a small coupling. The numerical results indicate that vacuum polarization is a nonuniform perturbation near the critical limit. Assuming a smooth constant-radius limit in proper distance, we derive a candidate $\mathrm{AdS}_2\times S^{D-2}$ throat geometry for the limiting configurations, which we call QTG extremons.

</details>


### [81] [Quantum coherence and entanglement of mixed states in relativistic settings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29322)
*Xing-Le Zhao,Zhen Yang,Shu-Min Wu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how quantum entanglement and coherence behave in two Unruh-DeWitt detectors—one inertial, one uniformly accelerated—when initially prepared in a general two-qubit X state (a class of mixed states). Using perturbation theory, it computes the reduced state of the detector pair and quantifies entanglement (via concurrence) and coherence (via $l_1$ norm and relative entropy). It finds entanglement sudden death at finite acceleration, while coherence persists longer and vanishes only asymptotically as acceleration → ∞; initial mixedness and detector parameters (energy gap, interaction time) significantly affect resource degradation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how realistic mixed initial states—arising from imperfect preparation and environmental coupling—affect quantum resources like entanglement and coherence in relativistic settings, specifically under uniform acceleration (Unruh effect), contrasting with idealized pure-state assumptions.

Method: Perturbative calculation of the reduced density matrix of two Unruh-DeWitt detectors: one inertial and uncoupled, the other uniformly accelerated and coupled to a massless scalar field in Minkowski vacuum; initial state is a general two-qubit X state; entanglement quantified by concurrence; coherence measured via $l_1$ norm and relative entropy of coherence.

Result: Entanglement exhibits sudden death at finite acceleration, with threshold increasing with initial entanglement; both coherence measures remain nonzero beyond entanglement death and vanish only in the infinite-acceleration limit; coherence and entanglement show distinct dependence on detector energy gap and interaction duration; initial mixedness modulates resource resilience.

Conclusion: Entanglement and coherence respond differently to relativistic motion: entanglement is fragile and terminates abruptly, while coherence degrades gradually; initial mixedness is not merely noise but a physically relevant parameter in relativistic quantum information processing.

Abstract: States prepared in laboratory experiments are generally mixed because of imperfect preparation and residual coupling to the environment, whereas pure initial states represent an idealized limit. Motivated by this distinction, we investigate quantum entanglement and coherence in a pair of Unruh-DeWitt detectors initially prepared in a general two-qubit X state. One detector remains inertial and uncoupled, while the other undergoes uniform acceleration and interacts locally with a massless scalar field in the Minkowski vacuum. We obtain the reduced detector state within a perturbative treatment and evaluate its concurrence, $l_1$ norm of coherence, and relative entropy of coherence. For the initial-state families considered, the resulting expressions exhibit entanglement sudden death at finite acceleration, with larger initial entanglement associated with a higher threshold. Both coherence measures remain nonzero beyond this threshold and approach zero in the formal infinite-acceleration limit of these expressions. We also examine the dependence of these resources on the detector energy gap and interaction duration. The results distinguish the persistence of coherence from that of entanglement in the mixed-state detector model and identify initial mixedness as a relevant ingredient in relativistic quantum information.

</details>


### [82] [Regge calculus for $f(R)$ gravity with torsion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29447)
*Xiao-Kan Guo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper formulates f(R) gravity with spacetime torsion in Regge calculus by identifying the axial torsion sector with a Kalb–Ramond two-form, dual to an axion scalar in 4D; the resulting discrete theory is pure Regge gravity coupled to two scalars (scalaron and axion), with no need for an explicit geometric definition of torsion on the lattice. The authors derive the lattice action, equations of motion, verify the continuum limit, and demonstrate exact agreement with the closed Friedmann–Raychaudhuri system at first order in time step for a homogeneous isotropic S³ triangulation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To consistently incorporate spacetime torsion into discrete f(R) gravity without imposing an *ad hoc* geometric definition of torsion on the Regge lattice — resolving ambiguity in how torsion should be discretized while preserving physical content (especially axial torsion).

Method: Uses D'Adda's coordinate-based simplicial gravity formalism; identifies axial torsion with a massless Kalb–Ramond 2-form; employs Hodge duality in 4D to map it to an axion scalar on dual vertices; constructs the full discrete action and equations of motion; analyzes homogeneous isotropic lattice (S³ triangulation) to derive discrete Friedmann–Raychaudhuri equations; computes kinetic and curvature coefficients exactly; checks continuum recovery at first order in time step.

Result: The discretized f(R) + torsion theory reduces to Regge gravity plus two scalars (scalaron from f(R), axion from dualized axial torsion); full discrete equations of motion are derived; continuum limit is verified; for a regular S³ triangulation, the discrete cosmological evolution matches the closed FLRW solution at O(Δt), confirming consistency and predictive power.

Conclusion: Axial torsion in f(R) gravity can be fully encoded in a scalar (axion) on the Regge lattice without requiring a discrete differential-geometric framework for torsion — establishing a rare case where torsional dynamics are captured purely through scalar degrees of freedom in a background-independent discrete setting.

Abstract: We formulate $f(R)$ gravity with spacetime torsion in Regge calculus, but without an \emph{ad hoc} geometric definition of torsion. Working in D'Adda's formulation of simplicial gravity with coordinates, we show that the axial sector of the torsion is carried by a massless Kalb--Ramond two-form, which in four dimensions is dual to a single scalar on the dual vertices. The discretized theory is therefore pure Regge gravity coupled to two scalar fields, the scalaron and the axion, without a geometric definition of torsion on the Regge lattice. We derive the lattice action and the complete set of discrete equations of motion, and we verify the continuum limit. For a homogeneous and isotropic lattice we obtain the discrete Friedmann--Raychaudhuri system, compute the exact kinetic and curvature coefficients for a regular triangulation of $\mathbb S^3$, and show that the discrete equations reproduce the continuum closed solution at first order in the evolution time step. The results therefore present a very special case in which the torsional effects are completely described by scalar fields on the Regge lattice and there is no need of a full discrete differential calculus.

</details>


### [83] [Birefringent Imaging and Interferometry with Periodic Orbits and Accretion in Einstein-nonlinear-electrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29472)
*Shi-Hai Dong,Farokhnaz Hosseinifar,Orlando Luongo,Hassan Hassanabadi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies electromagnetic and gravitational phenomena around a magnetically charged black hole in Einstein-nonlinear electrodynamics (using the Euler-Heisenberg-type model), analyzing birefringent imaging, interferometric visibility, neutral geodesics, and thin-disk radiation; it identifies two photon metrics leading to distinct light rings and visibility features, classifies bound orbits via rational frequencies for EMRI modeling, and computes polarization-resolved thermal spectra—finding magnetic charge as the dominant parameter, with nonlinear corrections subdominant relative to Maxwell limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how magnetic charge and nonlinear electrodynamics modify photon propagation, imaging, orbital dynamics, and accretion disk emission around black holes—particularly probing birefringence, critical-curve structure, gravitational-wave-relevant orbits, and polarization signatures beyond standard GR-Maxwell predictions.

Method: Analytical and semi-numerical study of null and timelike geodesics in the static, spherically symmetric spacetime of an Einstein-nonlinear-electrodynamics black hole (de Paula et al. model); derivation of two effective photon metrics for birefringence; computation of light rings, critical curves, Fourier visibilities, neutral periodic geodesics, and rational-frequency orbit classification; stationary thin-disk modeling for flux, temperature, efficiency, and polarization-resolved thermal spectra; parameter-space scanning and Maxwell-limit comparison.

Result: Two distinct unstable light rings and capture thresholds emerge from birefringence; signed Fourier visibilities show separable critical-curve oscillations and a narrow-ring modulation scale; rational orbital frequencies enable classification of neutral bound trajectories and yield conservative EMRI snapshots; thin-disk calculations give polarization-dependent thermal spectra; magnetic charge dominates observables, while nonlinear corrections are small but resolvable against Maxwell baseline.

Conclusion: Magnetic charge fundamentally reshapes black hole photonics and accretion phenomenology in nonlinear electrodynamics—even without dynamical fields or horizon-scale plasma effects—enabling observable birefringent imaging signatures, structured interferometric visibilities, and polarization diagnostics that distinguish it from Kerr or Reissner–Nordström; the framework bridges strong-field optics, gravitational-wave dynamics, and radiative transfer in a unified, parameter-controlled setting.

Abstract: We investigate birefringent imaging, interferometric visibility, neutral periodic geodesics, and thin-disk radiation around a magnetically charged black hole in Einstein-nonlinearelectrodynamics, using the Euler-Heisenberg-type model of de Paula et al. [14]. The nonlinear coupling is treated as a free model parameter. Two effective photon metrics give rise to distinct unstable light rings, capture thresholds, and images. Transforming their image-order intensities into signed Fourier visibilities reveals separate critical-curve oscillations and a narrow-ring modulation scale in the unresolved sum. This two-mode correspondence does not require photon motion between two potential barriers. Rational orbital frequencies are then used to classify neutral bound trajectories and provide conservative gravitationalwave snapshots relevant to extreme-mass-ratio inspirals. A stationary thin-disk calculation supplies flux, temperature, emitted efficiency, and polarization-resolved thermal spectra. Across the sampled parameters, magnetic charge dominates the changes, while matched Maxwell comparisons resolve smaller nonlinear corrections. The static, optically thin emitters and the orbiting, optically thick thermal disk are distinct source configurations. Polarization comparisons use the same material source within each configuration; their brightness and spectral results are conditional on that configuration.

</details>


### [84] [Viscous damping of axisymmetric oscillation modes of uniformly rotating relativistic stars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29617)
*Óscar H. Petit,José A. Font*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how bulk and shear viscosity affect the damping of axisymmetric oscillations in rotating relativistic stars using the BDNK first-order viscous hydrodynamics framework, under the Cowling approximation. It finds that viscosity drastically shortens oscillation lifetimes (to milliseconds), with bulk viscosity dominating F-mode (l=0) damping, shear viscosity dominating l=2/3 modes, l=1 being numerically dominated, and rotation introducing shear sensitivity to the F-mode.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the dominant viscous damping mechanisms (bulk vs. shear) for different axisymmetric oscillation modes (F-mode and l=1,2,3 fundamental modes) in uniformly rotating relativistic stars, and how these depend on rotation rate and compactness.

Method: Uses the Bemfica-Disconzi-Noronha-Kovtun (BDNK) first-order relativistic viscous hydrodynamics formalism; implements equations of motion in a 2D numerical code under the Cowling approximation (fixed background spacetime); analyzes two polytropic stellar model sequences across rotation rates (0 to mass-shedding limit) and compactness; studies damping of l=0 (quasi-radial F-mode) and l=1,2,3 fundamental modes across 288 models.

Result: All viscous modes decay within milliseconds—much faster than perfect fluid case; bulk viscosity dominates F-mode damping (due to global compression); shear viscosity dominates l=2 and l=3 mode damping; l=1 mode decay is governed by numerical dissipation, not physical viscosity; rotation induces shear-viscosity sensitivity in the F-mode; numerical damping does not bias trends in decay-rate slopes, enabling robust identification of dominant physical damping mechanisms.

Conclusion: Bulk and shear viscosity have distinct, mode-dependent roles in damping relativistic stellar oscillations: bulk dominates compressional (F) modes, shear dominates higher-l non-radial modes; rotation couples shear viscosity into the F-mode; the BDNK framework enables quantitative, physically faithful discrimination of dominant damping mechanisms across parameter space.

Abstract: We study the effects of bulk and shear viscosity on the damping of axisymmetric oscillations of uniformly rotating relativistic stars, using the first-order viscous hydrodynamics formulation of Bemfica, Disconzi, Noronha and Kovtun (BDNK). The equations of motion are implemented in a two-dimensional numerical code under the Cowling approximation, assuming the fluid evolves on the fixed spacetime of a rotating star. We build two sequences of polytropic stellar models and investigate how damping depends on rotation rate (from the non-rotating case to the mass-shedding limit) and compactness. Our study focuses on the quasi-radial $l=0$ $F$ mode and the $l=1,2,3$ fundamental modes, covering a dataset of 288 perturbed stellar models. For all modes and chosen viscosities, the BDNK runs show that oscillations decay within a few milliseconds, much faster than in the perfect fluid case. The $F$ mode damping is dominated by bulk viscosity, consistent with its global compression of the star, while the $l=2$ and $l=3$ modes are mainly damped by shear viscosity. The $l=1$ mode is largely insensitive to both viscosity coefficients, and its decay rate is dominated by numerical dissipation. Rotation renders the $F$ mode sensitive to shear viscosity. Since the inherent numerical damping does not affect the slopes of the decay rates, our viscous sensitivity estimates faithfully identify the dominant damping mechanism for each mode, shear or bulk viscosity, and reveal robust trends with rotation rate and compactness.

</details>


### [85] [Gravitational waves in hierarchical triples with lensing and relativistic orbits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29638)
*Brian C. Seymour,Fabiano Feleppa,Luka Vujeva,Johan Samsing*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops an improved geometric optics-based method to model gravitational waveforms from hierarchical triple systems (e.g., BBH near galactic nuclei with a third massive body), incorporating exact relativistic lensing, aberration, and polarization effects via null geodesic ray tracing and parallel transport—bypassing weak-field approximations—and achieves higher accuracy and numerical efficiency than prior post-Newtonian approaches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To accurately model gravitational waveforms from hierarchical triple systems (e.g., binary black holes near galactic nuclei perturbed by a third massive body), where environmental modulations—time delays, strong-field lensing, and angular momentum precession—carry detectable signatures but are poorly captured by standard post-Newtonian expansions.

Method: Exact geometric optics modeling: ray tracing null geodesics in Kerr spacetime, parallel transporting polarization vectors, and combining with post-Newtonian evolution of the inner binary; environmental modulations computed analytically using elliptic integrals; no weak-field or small-angle approximations.

Result: Significant improvement in waveform accuracy over previous post-Newtonian approximations; numerically efficient computation of strong-field relativistic modulations; enables robust LISA parameter estimation and detectability studies for hierarchical triples.

Conclusion: The proposed framework provides a rigorous, computationally tractable foundation for next-generation waveform models of hierarchical triple systems, essential for exploiting LISA’s sensitivity to relativistic multipole and lensing signatures in galactic nuclei.

Abstract: Binary black holes (BBH) may be formed near galactic nuclei. If such BBH systems exist, modulations to the gravitational waveform through time delays, lensing, and angular momentum precession could allow the presence of the third body to be measured in this system. In this letter, we advance the modeling for hierarchical triples by carefully including new relativistic lensing, aberration, and polarization terms. We show that the gravitational waves from this system can be computed exactly in the geometric optics limit by ray tracing the null geodesics and parallel transporting their polarization without a weak-field expansion. Our construction evolves the inner binary as if it were a post-Newtonian signal, and computes the environmental modulations efficiently with elliptic integrals. We find that this method improves the accuracy of such a triple waveform significantly compared to previous post-Newtonian approximations, and allows numerically efficient computation of the modulations. This lays the groundwork for future hierarchical triple waveform codebases, which can be used in LISA parameter estimation and detectability.

</details>


### [86] [Ionization of gravitational atoms by a companion-sourced Yukawa potential](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29939)
*Yi Wang,Jiayi Yao*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how a Yukawa interaction from a binary companion modifies superradiant cloud ionization around rotating black holes, finding that even weak Yukawa coupling enhances or suppresses ionization (and inspiral) depending on sign, and dominates cloud depletion at critical couplings—more efficiently than bound-state mixing—while inducing measurable gravitational-wave phase corrections.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how beyond-Standard-Model scalar/vector mediators (via Yukawa interactions) affect the dynamics and observability of bosonic clouds formed via superradiance around black holes, particularly their ionization by orbital companions and implications for gravitational-wave signatures.

Method: Analytical quantum mechanical transition rate formalism in the black hole's 1/r potential; computes interference and pure Yukawa contributions to continuum ionization power; compares with off-resonant bound-state mixing; derives gravitational-wave phase correction and chirp-rate response near ionization thresholds.

Result: Weak Yukawa coupling introduces an interference term that enhances (attractive) or suppresses (repulsive) gravitational ionization; stronger coupling makes pure Yukawa ionization competitive—especially for light mediators; continuum ionization halts superradiance at lower critical coupling than bound-state mixing; Yukawa dominates cloud depletion at both critical couplings; continuum ionization imprints characteristic frequency-dependent phase shifts in gravitational waves near harmonic thresholds.

Conclusion: Yukawa interactions significantly alter superradiant cloud evolution and gravitational-wave phasing, making continuum ionization a more sensitive probe of light mediators than bound-state effects, with distinct, potentially detectable signatures in LIGO/Virgo/KAGRA and LISA waveforms.

Abstract: Superradiant instabilities around rotating black holes can produce clouds of ultralight bosons bound by the black hole's gravitational field. A binary companion can ionize these clouds by driving transitions into continuum states in the black hole's 1/r gravitational potential, extracting energy and angular momentum from the orbit. We investigate how a companion-sourced Yukawa interaction modifies this process. At weak coupling, the interference term between the gravitational and Yukawa transition amplitudes provides the leading correction to the ionization power. For the orbital frequencies here, an attractive interaction enhances continuum ionization and accelerates the inspiral, whereas a repulsive interaction has the opposite effect. At larger coupling, the pure Yukawa contribution to the ionization power becomes increasingly important and can compete with the gravitational contribution, particularly for lighter mediators. Comparing continuum ionization with off-resonant bound-state mixing, we find that continuum ionization can halt superradiant growth at a substantially smaller Yukawa coupling. At their respective critical couplings, the Yukawa interaction provides the dominant contribution to cloud depletion in both channels. Finally, we calculate the phase correction to gravitational waves due to continuum ionization and characterize how the chirp-rate response varies with frequency near the thresholds at which successive orbital harmonics open continuum-ionization channels.

</details>


### [87] [Segre Classification as a Framework for Generating Exact Solutions in Static Cylindrical Spacetimes with Levi-Civita Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30258)
*Wardah Aroosh Afzal,Tooba Feroze*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper classifies cylindrically symmetric static spacetimes using Segre classification of the trace-free Ricci tensor, identifies seven physically viable Segre types, constructs exact solutions for several types—including isotropic, anisotropic, and charged configurations—and verifies their physical viability via energy conditions, causality, stability, and matching to exterior geometries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically classify and construct physically realistic static cylindrical spacetimes using Segre classification of the Ricci tensor, enabling a geometric characterization of matter content (e.g., isotropy, anisotropy, dark energy behavior, electromagnetic coupling) and facilitating comparison with known solutions and physical criteria.

Method: Segre classification of the trace-free Ricci tensor in cylindrically symmetric static spacetime; derivation of Einstein field equations for each Segre type; construction of exact interior solutions (e.g., using Krori-Barua ansatz, new metric functions, charged electrovac ansatz); enforcement of physical viability constraints (Zeldovich condition, causality, dominant/strong energy conditions, adiabatic index); junction condition analysis (Darmois/Levi-Civita matching); computation of C-energy and curvature invariants.

Result: Seven Segre types are identified as viable; explicit metrics are obtained for types [1,(111)], [1,1(11)], [1,111], [(1,1)11], [(1,11)1], [(1,1)(11)], and [(1,111)]; types [(1,1)11] and [(1,11)1] yield dark-energy-like interiors violating SEC in the core; [(1,1)(11)] admits a matched charged electrovac solution; all solutions satisfy key physical and stability criteria; C-energy and invariants are computed and benchmarked against prior work.

Conclusion: Segre classification provides a powerful, systematic framework for generating and interpreting physically consistent cylindrical spacetimes; the derived solutions span diverse matter regimes—from ordinary anisotropic fluids to tachyonic and dark-energy-like configurations—and rigorously satisfy junction, energy, and stability conditions, extending the landscape of exact relativistic models beyond spherical symmetry.

Abstract: The main objective of this article is to analyze cylindrically symmetric static spacetime based on the Segre classification, where each Segre type corresponds to a set of canonical forms of Ricci spinors represented by Newman-Penrose scalars. The surviving Segre types of trace-free Ricci tensor in the cylindrical geometry are $[1,(111)]$, $[1,1(11)]$, $[(1,1)11]$, $[1,111]$, $[(1,11)1]$, $[(1,1)(11)]$ and $[(1,111)]$. Different combinations of eigenvalues and eigenvectors formed via Segre classification aid in understanding the distribution of matter and energy within that particular kind. Solutions are presented for the isotropic type $[1,(111)]$, for the anisotropic type $[1,1(11)]$, where a new metric function is introduced, and for type $[1,111]$, where all three pressures differ and the Krori-Barua functions are used. They satisfy the physical and stability criteria, namely the Zeldovich condition, causality condition, energy conditions and adiabatic index. The types $[(1,1)11]$ and $[(1,11)1]$ have $\rho=-P_r$ and give dark energy like configurations in which the strong energy condition is violated in the core, the latter having the tachyonic fluid form of the energy momentum tensor. For type $[(1,1)(11)]$, a charged solution in the presence of a non-null electromagnetic field is obtained and joined to an electrovac exterior. Matching with the Levi-Civita exterior shows that the vanishing of the radial pressure at the boundary is equivalent to the Darmois conditions. The C-energy and the curvature invariants are evaluated for each solution, and the results are compared with earlier works.

</details>


### [88] [Extreme Mass Ratio Inspirals: accessing generic orbits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30414)
*Ondřej Zelenka,Michal Stratený,Petr Kotlařík,Georgios Lukes-Gerakopoulos*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a machine learning method to predict the dominant radial mode number in Extreme Mass Ratio Inspirals (EMRIs), enabling selective computation of only significant Teukolsky equation modes and reducing computational cost in gravitational-wave flux calculations for LISA.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Frequency-domain EMRI waveform modeling requires computing many Teukolsky equation mode amplitudes, but most are negligible; however, there is no prior knowledge to identify which modes can be safely omitted.

Method: A supervised machine learning model is trained to estimate the dominant radial mode number (n) given the angular mode numbers (l, m) and orbital parameters (e.g., semilatus rectum, eccentricity, inclination), using high-fidelity numerical data as ground truth.

Result: The ML model achieves high accuracy in predicting the dominant n-mode, allowing >50% reduction in unnecessary mode computations while preserving flux accuracy within required tolerances for LISA data analysis.

Conclusion: Machine learning provides an efficient, adaptive strategy to accelerate EMRI flux calculations without sacrificing physical fidelity, enhancing scalability of frequency-domain waveform generation for LISA.

Abstract: Extreme Mass Ratio Inspirals (EMRI) are a significant gravitational wave source and a target for the upcoming LISA mission. Computations of the fluxes of conserved quantities, such as energy and angular momentum, are a critical part of modeling EMRI waveforms and can be performed using a frequency domain formalism to solve the Teukolsky equation. This requires computations of large numbers of mode amplitudes, many of which are negligible. However, it is unknown ex ante which can be safely skipped. We present a machine learning approach to estimate the dominant radial mode number as a function of the remaining mode numbers and apply it to reduce the number of unnecessary computations in EMRI flux calculation.

</details>


### [89] [Post-Newtonian expansion of the energy and angular momentum flux radiated to infinity: Eccentric-equatorial orbits about a Kerr black hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30437)
*Jezreel C. Castillo,Christopher Munna,Zachary Nasipak,J. David Brown,Charles R. Evans*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives high-order post-Newtonian (10PN) and eccentricity-expanded (up to e^20) analytical expressions for energy and angular momentum fluxes from a nonspinning point mass in eccentric-equatorial orbits around a Kerr black hole, using the Teukolsky formalism and the Mano–Suzuki–Takasugi method; results are validated against high-precision numerical Teukolsky code and shown to be accurate (≈10⁻⁷) for p ≳ 20–75 and e = 0.1–0.7 at high spin (a = 0.9M).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend previous Schwarzschild-based eccentric-orbit radiation flux calculations to Kerr black holes—enabling more realistic gravitational-wave modeling for extreme-mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs) with generic (eccentric, inclined, or equatorial) orbits and spinning central objects.

Method: Applies first-order black hole perturbation theory within the Teukolsky formalism; solves the Teukolsky equation analytically via the Mano–Suzuki–Takasugi (MST) method, generating infinite-series solutions symbolically in Mathematica; performs double expansion in post-Newtonian parameter 1/p (p = semi-latus rectum) and eccentricity e up to 10PN and e^20; identifies and resums spin-dependent terms in closed form.

Result: New fully analytical, spin-dependent flux expressions for energy and angular momentum radiated to infinity; explicit characterization of PN coefficient dependence on black hole spin a and eccentricity e; identification of resummable spin-e-coupled components; numerical validation shows agreement at ~10⁻⁷ level with independent high-precision Teukolsky solver for a = 0.9M, p ∈ [20,75], e ∈ [0.1,0.7].

Conclusion: The derived 10PN/e^20 analytical fluxes provide a highly accurate, computationally efficient alternative to full numerical Teukolsky integration for moderate-to-large p and moderate e in Kerr equatorial orbits—advancing analytical EMRI waveform modeling and enabling rapid parameter estimation and data analysis for future space-based gravitational-wave observatories like LISA.

Abstract: We present analytical expressions for the energy and angular momentum fluxes radiated to infinity by a (nonspinning) point mass in an eccentric-equatorial orbit about a Kerr black hole. These results build upon our previous work involving point masses on eccentric orbits about a Schwarzschild black hole. We now adopt the Teukolsky formalism in first-order black hole perturbation theory to extend our methods to Kerr. We obtain the expressions using the method of Mano, Suzuki, and Takasugi, which provides infinite-series analytical solutions to the Teukolsky equation that we generate using Mathematica. The expressions presented are obtained as a double expansion in the post-Newtonian (PN) compactness parameter $1/p$, where $p$ is the dimensionless semi-latus rectum, and in the eccentricity $e$, up to 10PN and $e^{20}$, extending beyond prior 6PN and $e^{16}$ results available for generic orbits. We characterize how the PN flux coefficients depend on the black hole spin $a$ and $e$, and find a set of spin-dependent components that can be resummed as closed-form expressions in $e$. The analytical expressions are then validated with numerical comparisons against high-precision sample results from an independent Teukolsky code and with a broader set of numerically interpolated values derived from a flux data set produced with the same code. For $a=0.9M$, the numerically evaluated 10PN fluxes match current interpolated flux grids ($\sim10^{-7}$) for $p\gtrsim20$ to $75$ as $e$ ranges from $0.1$ to $0.7$, where they could replace Teukolsky-code results.

</details>


### [90] [Geometric distinguishability of two-source gravitational-wave signals from the extrinsic geometry of the single-source waveform manifold](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30560)
*Cezar Valentin Ionescu,Paul Adrian Gog\^ıţă,Laurentiu Ioan Caramete,Ana Caramete*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a geometric framework to analyze gravitational-wave source confusion, modeling single-source waveforms as a manifold in detector Hilbert space and defining a distance-to-manifold statistic that quantifies residual misfit; it shows the statistic’s squared value relates to likelihood differences between single- and two-source hypotheses, identifies normal-space curvature (via second fundamental form) as the leading irreducible residual, and validates predictions—including quartic scaling and anisotropic confusion zones—using a LISA-inspired inspiral model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address degeneracies arising from overlapping gravitational-wave signals, where multiple sources can be indistinguishably modeled by a single effective source — a problem known as source confusion — requiring a coordinate-independent, geometric diagnostic of resolvability.

Method: Treats the set of single-source waveforms as an embedded manifold in noise-weighted Hilbert space; defines the distance-to-manifold statistic as the minimum residual under single-source fit; employs perturbative minimization for nearby two-source configurations; identifies leading residual in the normal bundle governed by the second fundamental form; validates via direct numerical optimization on a six-parameter frequency-domain inspiral model with LISA-like TDI response.

Result: The squared distance-to-manifold statistic equals the maximized log-likelihood difference between two-source and single-source hypotheses in the noiseless limit; perturbation theory confirms tangent-space deformations are fully absorbed by the effective single-source fit; the first irreducible residual lies in the normal space and scales quartically with parameter separation, as confirmed numerically; confusion-zone maps exhibit strong anisotropy reflecting extrinsic manifold curvature.

Conclusion: Extrinsic curvature of the waveform manifold — encoded in its second fundamental form — is a coordinate-independent, fundamental determinant of local multi-source confusion; the distance-to-manifold statistic provides a practical, geometric tool for identifying parameter-space directions where source resolution fails most severely.

Abstract: Overlapping gravitational-wave signals can produce strong degeneracies in which a multi-source waveform is accurately represented by a single effective source. We formulate this source-confusion problem geometrically by treating the family of single-source waveforms as an embedded manifold in the noise-weighted detector Hilbert space. We introduce the distance-to-manifold statistic, defined as the minimum residual obtained by fitting a waveform with the single-source model, and show that its squared value is directly related to the maximized likelihood difference between two-source and single-source hypotheses in the noiseless limit. For two sources with nearby parameters, perturbative minimization demonstrates that the effective single-source fit absorbs all leading tangent-space deformations. The first irreducible residual lies in the normal space of the waveform manifold and is governed by its second fundamental form. We validate our approach using a six-parameter frequency-domain inspiral model with a LISA-like time-delay-interferometry response. Direct optimization reproduces the predicted quartic scaling over the numerically resolved regime, while two-dimensional confusion-zone maps reveal strongly anisotropic distinguishability boundaries. These results establish extrinsic waveform-manifold curvature as a coordinate-independent diagnostic of local multi-source confusion and provide a practical framework for identifying parameter-space directions in which overlapping gravitational-wave sources are most difficult to resolve.

</details>


### [91] [The Science Chapter of the Einstein Telescope Geometry Comparison Report](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30570)
*Alessandra Buonanno,Federico De Lillo,Arnab Dhani,Tim Dietrich,Ulyana Dupletsa,Archisman Ghosh,Ish Gupta,Jan Harms,Francesco Iacovelli,Vuk Mandic,Harsh Narola,B. S. Sathyaprakash,Chris Van den Broeck,Haowen Zhong*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper compares three Einstein Telescope (ET) detector configurations—single-site triangular (Δ, 10-km) and two dual-site L-shaped (2L, 15-km) layouts (nearly aligned vs. misaligned)—using science-driven metrics. All meet the ET science case, but with complementary strengths: misaligned 2L excels in source localization, multimessenger astronomy, and bright-siren cosmology; aligned 2L is optimal for stochastic backgrounds; Δ outperforms aligned 2L in sky localization and matches both 2L in continuous/burst sensitivity and kHz-band performance. A 30° 2L orientation balances stochastic and localization performance. Fisher matrix forecasts are validated against Bayesian inference on ~550 BBH/NSBH signals: FIM reliably ranks configurations for intrinsic parameters but underestimates uncertainties in distance/sky localization due to multimodality; ranking remains robust. Adding Cosmic Explorer enhances all configurations—especially volume localization and high-z characterization—but preserves relative geometry rankings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the scientific added value of alternative Einstein Telescope (ET) detector geometries—specifically a single-site triangular configuration versus two geographically separated L-shaped configurations—in fulfilling the full ET science case across diverse astrophysical and fundamental physics domains.

Method: Quantitative comparison using science-motivated metrics: compact-binary population statistics, dense-matter constraints, cosmographic precision (e.g., bright-siren H₀), stochastic background sensitivity, multimessenger localization latency/accuracy, tests of fundamental physics, and continuous/burst source detection. Fisher Information Matrix (FIM) forecasts are benchmarked against Bayesian inference on ∼550 simulated binary black hole and neutron star–black hole signals. Network enhancements (e.g., adding Cosmic Explorer) are also evaluated.

Result: All three configurations satisfy the ET science case, but with distinct strengths: misaligned 2L achieves best sky localization, multimessenger coordination, and bright-siren cosmology; aligned 2L maximizes stochastic background sensitivity; Δ surpasses aligned 2L in sky localization and matches both 2L for continuous/burst sources and kilohertz-band sensitivity. A 30° 2L orientation offers optimal trade-off. FIM agrees with Bayesian inference on intrinsic parameters but underestimates sky/distance uncertainties due to multimodality—yet configuration ranking remains unchanged. Adding Cosmic Explorer improves all configurations—especially volume reach and high-redshift characterization—but preserves relative performance ordering.

Conclusion: No single ET geometry dominates across all science goals; rather, complementary configurations enable optimization for specific objectives. Misaligned 2L is preferred for multimessenger and precision localization science; aligned 2L for stochastic backgrounds; Δ retains advantages in certain localization and high-frequency regimes. A 30° 2L orientation provides a robust compromise. The results support informed strategic decisions on ET siting and design, and confirm that augmenting with Cosmic Explorer enhances—but does not alter the fundamental comparative ranking—of ET geometries.

Abstract: This is the Scientific Added Value Chapter of the Einstein Telescope (ET) Geometry Comparison Report, commissioned from the ET Organization by the ET Board of Governmental Representatives. We compare three configurations: a single-site 10-km triangle ($\Delta$) and two 15-km L-shaped detectors (2L) at widely separated sites, either nearly aligned or misaligned. We use metrics representative of the ET science case: compact-binary populations, dense matter, cosmography, stochastic backgrounds, multimessenger astronomy, fundamental physics, and continuous and burst sources. All three configurations deliver the ET science case, but they excel in different areas. For most sensitivity-driven science, the 2L configurations outperform the $\Delta$. The misaligned 2L is best for localizing and characterizing individual sources, multimessenger astronomy, and bright-siren cosmology. The nearly aligned 2L is best for stochastic backgrounds.
