The $\Delta$ localizes sources better than the aligned 2L and performs comparably to both 2L configurations for continuous and burst sources and at kilohertz frequencies. A 2L orientation of $30^\circ$ is a good compromise between stochastic-background sensitivity and localization. We validate Fisher information matrix (FIM) forecasts against Bayesian inference for $\sim550$ binary black hole and neutron star-black hole signals. Despite substantial differences, the FIM shows no systematic preference for either configuration for intrinsic parameters. For distance and sky localization, the FIM cannot capture multimodality and long-range correlations, leading to larger discrepancies, which however do not affect the ranking. Adding Cosmic Explorer to the network improves the reach of all three ET configurations, especially in volume localization and characterization of high-redshift sources. Differences between geometries shrink, but the ranking is largely unchanged.

</details>


### [92] [Constraining Regular Black Hole Models with the Effective Field Theory treatment of General Relativity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30616)
*Sami Viollet*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper uses perturbative effective field theory (EFT) predictions in general relativity to constrain non-perturbative quantum gravity models of black holes—particularly regular black hole metrics—by comparing their static particle interaction energy with EFT-derived quantum corrections.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge the gap between perturbative EFT predictions and non-perturbative quantum gravity models by establishing a consistency criterion for quantum-corrected black hole geometries.

Method: Derives the leading-order quantum correction to the interaction energy of a static particle near a Schwarzschild black hole using GR-as-EFT; constructs a test by requiring that modified (regular) black hole metrics reproduce this correction at low energy; applies the test to the Hayward metric and solves for the most general spherically symmetric, asymptotically flat metric satisfying the constraint.

Result: The Hayward metric fails to reproduce the EFT-predicted quantum correction; the most general metric compatible with the perturbative result is identified as a two-parameter family of regular black hole metrics with specific asymptotic and near-horizon constraints.

Conclusion: Perturbative EFT provides a robust benchmark for falsifying or guiding non-perturbative quantum black hole models; only metrics satisfying precise curvature and expansion constraints can be consistent with low-energy quantum gravity expectations.

Abstract: In this paper, we present a method for using perturbative predictions from treating general relativity as an effective field theory to constrain non-perturbative approaches to quantum gravity in black hole contexts. We start by reviewing a known result from this framework, which estimates the first quantum corrections to the interaction energy of a static particle near a black hole. We then propose a way to test non-perturbative quantum black hole models - specifically those formulated as modified Schwarzschild metrics, also known as regular black holes - against this perturbative result. To illustrate, we apply our method to the Hayward metric, showing that it does not align with the perturbative prediction, and we identify the most general metric that can reproduce the expected result.

</details>


### [93] [Detecting Frame Dragging Effect with Next-Generation Spaceborne Clocks in a LEO-DRO Satellite Configuration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30726)
*Li-Fang Li,Hanlin Song,Xing Bian,Tong Liu,Shao-Jiang Wang,Song He,Yang Li,Jiaxing Leng,Ziren Luo,Peng Xu,Rong-Gen Cai*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: Proposes a satellite-based relativistic frequency comparison between LEO and DRO to directly measure Earth's frame-dragging effect (~10⁻²⁰) using next-generation optical clocks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable direct, high-precision measurement of Earth's rotation-induced frame-dragging (gravitomagnetic) signal—currently inaccessible at required ~10⁻²⁰ fractional frequency sensitivity—by exploiting orbital geometry and advances in optical clock stability.

Method: Develops a complete post-Newtonian relativistic model for frequency comparison between a LEO and a DRO satellite, including Earth’s multipole moments, lunar/solar tidal potentials, and the gravitomagnetic vector potential; leverages ultra-stable strontium optical lattice clocks with demonstrated instability down to 2.5×10⁻¹⁹ at 1 day.

Result: Shows that the frame-dragging signal is ~10⁻²⁰ in fractional frequency, within reach of near-future optical clocks; establishes theoretical framework enabling its isolation and measurement.

Conclusion: A LEO–DRO satellite pair, combined with sub-10⁻¹⁹ optical clocks, provides a feasible platform for the first direct detection of Earth’s frame-dragging effect, offering new constraints on gravitomagnetism and implications for relativistic clock synchronization.

Abstract: Our approach employs a satellite constellation comprising one satellite in low Earth orbit (LEO) and one satellite in distant retrograde orbit (DRO). This configuration is designed to enhance the sensitivity to the Earth's rotation-induced frame-dragging signal while providing a stable and gravitationally isolated frequency reference. We derive a complete relativistic formulation for the frequency comparison between LEO and DRO, incorporating the post-Newtonian metric, higher-order Earth multipoles, lunar and solar tidal potentials, and the gravitomagnetic vector potential. The frame-dragging signal is of order $10^{-20}$, a level that is becoming increasingly accessible with next-generation optical clocks. Recent advances in zero-dead-time strontium optical lattice clocks have demonstrated a fractional frequency instability of $2.9\times10^{-19}$ at an averaging time of 20,000~s, with an estimated one-day stability of $2.5\times10^{-19}$. With continued advances in clock stability, achieving fractional-frequency sensitivity at the $10^{-20}$ level appears feasible in the near term, potentially enabling the direct measurement of a frame-dragging signal of the same order. Such a measurement would constrain Earth's gravitomagnetic field, enabling future investigations of gravitomagnetic effects on Einstein clock synchronization.

</details>


### [94] [Schwarzschild-de Sitter space is not rigid](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31019)
*Philippe G. LeFloch,Luc Rozoy*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: Constructs a family of static axisymmetric vacuum solutions with two additional black-hole horizons of prescribed area, starting from a sub-extremal Schwarzschild-de Sitter spacetime; proves regularity, analyticity, and asymptotic behavior via multi-scale gluing and elliptic analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend known black hole solutions by adding controlled black hole horizons while preserving regularity and symmetry, addressing existence and geometric structure of static vacuum metrics with multiple horizons in de Sitter space.

Method: Multi-scale gluing: combines local Schwarzschild models near new horizons with global Green tensor matching; enforces solvability via localized tensor sources and integral constraints to eliminate infinitesimal translations; uses reflection symmetry and radial variation to cancel leading-order source terms; employs uniform elliptic estimates and spherical heat equation to control lapse geometry.

Result: Existence of a smooth 2-parameter family (mass and horizon area) of static axisymmetric vacuum metrics with three regular horizons (cosmological + two black hole), real-analytic up to boundary; precise asymptotics as mass → 0, including toroidal level sets of lapse maxima and Schwarzschild blow-up limits at added horizons.

Conclusion: Demonstrates flexible construction of multi-horizon static vacuum spacetimes in positive Λ cosmology, revealing universal geometric features of the lapse function and validating a robust gluing framework for nonlinear elliptic problems on compact manifolds with boundary.

Abstract: Starting from any compact sub-extremal Schwarzschild-de Sitter region with positive cosmological constant, we construct a family of static axisymmetric vacuum solutions with two additional black-hole horizons, each of prescribed area. All horizons and the symmetry axis are regular, and the metric and lapse are real analytic up to the boundary. As the mass parameter tends to zero, the metric and lapse converge smoothly to the reference solution away from two antipodal points on its sphere of maximal lapse. Rescaling near either added horizon yields the Schwarzschild spatial metric in the limit. For small prescribed mass, the lapse attains its maximum precisely on one equatorial circle. Its Hessian is negative definite in the transverse plane, with the smaller eigenvalue magnitude asymptotic to a positive constant times the added mass. Nearby regular level sets are tori. The proof combines gluing at separated scales with uniform elliptic estimates. An integral condition removes infinitesimal translations of each Schwarzschild model, while a localized tensor source restores solvability of the linearized equation. Matching the local Schwarzschild corrections to a global Green tensor identifies the leading source coefficients. Reflection makes these coefficients equal, and varying the common radial position of the centers makes them vanish. A radial Green function and the heat equation on the sphere determine the angular variation of the lapse and the geometry of its maximum set.

</details>


### [95] [Lyapunov Stability of a Schwarzschild Black Hole Surrounded by the Kiselev Anisotropic Fluid](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31035)
*Saswati Roy,Paramita Debnath,Shubham Kala,Hemwati Nandan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies geodesic motion and stability of circular orbits around a Schwarzschild black hole immersed in Kiselev anisotropic fluid, analyzing photon spheres, ISCO/OSCO, and using coordinate-time Lyapunov exponents to characterize orbital stability across six equation-of-state parameters ω and their admissible normalization N.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how surrounding anisotropic fluid (Kiselev type) modifies black hole spacetime geometry and orbital dynamics—particularly photon sphere stability, ISCO/OSCO existence, and implications for astrophysical observables like black hole shadows and accretion disks.

Method: Analytical derivation and analysis of effective potentials for null and timelike geodesics; computation of photon sphere radius, ISCO, OSCO; evaluation of coordinate-time Lyapunov exponent λ_t^T; systematic variation over ω ∈ {−1, −2/3, −1/3, 0, 1/3, 1} and physically allowed N ranges constrained by horizon structure.

Result: Photon sphere is always unstable but its instability weakens with N for ω ≤ 0 and vanishes at extremality for ω = −1, −2/3; non-monotonic behavior for ω = 1/3 and strengthening for ω = 1; stable circular orbits exist between ISCO and OSCO only for ω = −1, −2/3 (merging at critical N), while for ω ≥ −1/3 they exist for all r > ISCO; closed-form expression for (λ_t^T)² sign-flips exactly at ISCO/OSCO, confirming Lyapunov criterion validity; Schwarzschild–de Sitter, mass-shifted Schwarzschild, and Reissner–Nordström emerge as limiting cases.

Conclusion: The Kiselev fluid background significantly alters orbital stability structure: presence of OSCO and bounded stable orbit regions are unique to ω < −1/3, while ω ≥ −1/3 restores unbounded stable orbits beyond ISCO; the Lyapunov exponent provides a robust dynamical stability indicator matching effective potential analysis; results extend standard black hole perturbation frameworks and inform observational signatures in modified gravity or exotic matter scenarios.

Abstract: We investigate the geodesic structure and the stability of circular orbits around a Schwarzschild black hole surrounded by a Kiselev anisotropic fluid, characterised by the equation-of-state parameter $\omega$ and the normalisation parameter $N$. We consider six representative values, $\omega=-1,-2/3,-1/3,0,1/3$ and $1$, over the admissible range of $N$ determined by the horizon structure, including the corresponding extremal limits. Using the effective potentials for null and timelike geodesics, we determine the photon sphere, the innermost stable circular orbit (ISCO) and the outermost stable circular orbit (OSCO), and examine their stability through coordinate-time Lyapunov exponents. The photon sphere is unstable for all values of $\omega$ and $N$ considered. Its instability weakens with increasing $N$ for $\omega\leq0$ and vanishes at extremality for $\omega=-1$ and $-2/3$, whereas it varies non-monotonically with $N$ for $\omega=1/3$ and strengthens for $\omega=1$. For $\omega=-1$ and $-2/3$, stable circular orbits are confined between the ISCO and the OSCO, which merge at a critical value of $N$ beyond which no stable circular orbits exist. In contrast, for $\omega\geq-1/3$ stable circular orbits exist at all radii beyond the ISCO, whose location varies with $N$. We also derive a closed-form expression for the squared coordinate-time Lyapunov exponent $(\lambda_t^T)^2$, valid for arbitrary $\omega$, whose sign changes exactly at the ISCO and OSCO, so that the Lyapunov criterion reproduces the boundaries of stable circular motion. The Schwarzschild--de Sitter, mass-shifted Schwarzschild and Reissner--Nordström geometries are recovered as special cases, and the results may have implications for black-hole shadows, thin accretion discs and quasi-periodic oscillations.

</details>


### [96] [Relativistic dynamical friction and accretion for Kerr black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31061)
*Nicholas Speeney,Soumodeep Mitra,Emanuele Berti,Andrea Maselli,Sumanta Chakraborty*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes relativistic dynamical friction and accretion on compact objects in EMRIs around Kerr black holes embedded in dark matter spikes, revealing spin-dependent friction reversal near the ISCO due to frame-dragging—invalidating local-density-only prescriptions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To accurately model dark matter effects on extreme-mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs) detectable by LISA, particularly how DM spikes around massive black holes influence orbital evolution via dynamical friction and accretion—going beyond Schwarzschild approximations and local-density assumptions.

Method: Computes relativistic dynamical friction and accretion forces on a non-spinning compact object in circular equatorial orbits around a Kerr black hole embedded in a collisionless, adiabatically formed DM spike (initially Hernquist). Momentum transfer is obtained by integrating relativistic scattering and capture cross sections over the full anisotropic phase space, accounting for frame-dragging and bound particle co-rotation.

Result: Dynamical friction scales with local DM density far from the black hole but weakens and reverses sign near the ISCO for rapidly spinning BHs (a ≳ 0.37) due to frame-dragging-induced co-rotation of bound DM; similar reversal occurs for non-rotating BHs only with unphysically large initial angular momentum/anisotropy; Chandrasekhar-like prescriptions overestimate friction.

Conclusion: Full phase-space structure—not just local density—is essential for modeling DM-induced orbital evolution in EMRIs; standard local approximations fail near the ISCO of spinning BHs, impacting LISA-based inference of BH environments and DM distributions.

Abstract: Extreme-mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs) observed by LISA will probe the environments of massive black holes (BHs), including the dark matter (DM) overdensities expected to form around them. We compute the relativistic dynamical friction and accretion experienced by a non-spinning compact object on circular equatorial orbits around a Kerr BH embedded in a collisionless DM distribution, extending previous results in Schwarzschild. The DM is modeled as a spike grown adiabatically from an initial Hernquist profile, and the momentum transfer is obtained by integrating relativistic scattering and capture cross sections over the full, generically anisotropic phase space seen by the secondary. Far from the primary the friction tracks the local DM density, which increases with spin. Near the innermost stable circular orbit of rapidly spinning BHs $(a\gtrsim 0.37)$, however, frame dragging forces bound DM particles to co-rotate, so that the secondary is overtaken by the surrounding medium: the friction weakens and eventually reverses sign where the DM density peaks. We show that a similar reversal can be mimicked around a non-rotating BH if the initial halo carries large net angular momentum, although this requires anisotropies beyond those found in cosmological simulations. We also show that commonly used Chandrasekhar-like prescriptions, which depend only on the local density, can overestimate the friction force. Our results demonstrate that the full phase-space structure of the environment must be included when modeling DM effects on EMRIs.

</details>


### [97] [Enhancing eccentricity through tidal resonance in binary neutron star systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31172)
*Ivan Markin,Natalie Williams,Tim Dietrich*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates that orbital eccentricity in binary neutron star systems can arise during late inspiral due to f-mode tidal resonance with a retrograde-spinning neutron star—challenging the assumption that observed eccentricity always indicates dynamical formation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate whether measurable orbital eccentricity in binary neutron star mergers necessarily implies dynamical formation (e.g., in dense stellar environments), or whether it can emerge intrinsically during late inspiral via stellar oscillation resonances.

Method: Numerical-relativity simulations of highly spinning, unequal spin-orientation (prograde vs. retrograde) binary neutron star mergers; analysis of f-mode resonance excitation, angular momentum transfer, and spin evolution; validation against a resonance locking model incorporating spin-dependent f-mode frequency shifts.

Result: Persistent f-mode resonance occurs only with the retrograde-spinning star, driving secular spin-down and inducing measurable orbital eccentricity during late inspiral; spin evolution is quantitatively consistent with resonance locking predictions.

Conclusion: Eccentricity in binary neutron stars need not reflect formation history—it can be generated dynamically during inspiral via spin-dependent tidal resonances, necessitating revised waveform models for rapidly spinning systems.

Abstract: Orbital eccentricity of binary neutron star systems is a key parameter to reveal their formation history. Since binaries formed in isolation are expected to circularize long before merger, measurable eccentricity suggests dynamical formation in dense stellar environments. However, using numerical-relativity simulations, we show that eccentricity can be introduced in the late inspiral of an initially quasicircular binary, due to the $f$-mode resonance of one of the stars. We simulate the merger of two highly spinning neutron stars, one star spinning prograde and the other retrograde. Persistent tidal resonance with the $f$ mode of the retrograde-spinning star is observed, accompanied by a gradual change of its spin due to angular momentum transfer from the orbit. This spin change can be well explained by a simple resonance locking model that accounts for the spin-dependent shift of the $f$-mode frequency. These findings reveal an origin of eccentricity in binary neutron stars that is independent of their formation history and provide important information for modeling binary neutron star dynamics with rapidly spinning stars.

</details>


### [98] [Optimal Statistic for a Stochastic Gravitational Wave Background using Pulsar-Timing--Astrometry Cross Correlations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31196)
*Kimberly K. Boddy,Jeffrey S. Hazboun,Kris Pardo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops correlation statistics for detecting stochastic gravitational wave backgrounds using astrometric stellar deflections, analogous to pulsar timing methods, and introduces cross-correlation between astrometry and pulsar timing data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend gravitational wave detection to higher frequencies and provide complementary coverage to pulsar timing arrays by leveraging astrometric measurements of stellar angular deflections induced by GWs.

Method: Derives a correlation statistic for pairwise stellar angular deflections in astrometry, establishes a unified formalism with pulsar timing, and formulates a cross-correlation statistic between stellar deflections and pulsar timing residuals; computes signal-to-noise ratios and derives scaling laws for weak- and intermediate-signal regimes.

Result: Analytical expressions for auto-correlation (astrometry–astrometry) and cross-correlation (astrometry–pulsar timing) statistics; quantitative SNR estimates and asymptotic scaling laws for stochastic GW background detection.

Conclusion: Astrometric GW searches are theoretically viable and synergistic with pulsar timing; the proposed correlation framework enables joint analysis and enhances sensitivity across overlapping frequency bands.

Abstract: Astrometric measurements have emerged as a viable probe of gravitational waves (GWs), complementing pulsar timing searches by providing overlapping and higher GW frequency coverage. At its core, the search for GWs using astrometry is very similar to the search with pulsar timing data: GWs induce a change in pulse arrival times along the line of sight to a pulsar and a transverse angular deflection in the apparent position of a star. In analogy to pulsar timing, we develop a correlation statistic between pairs of stellar deflections for astrometry to search for a stochastic GW background. With a common formalism for both astrometry and pulsar timing, we also develop the cross-correlation statistic between stellar deflections and pulsar timing residuals. We estimate the signal-to-noise ratios from the correlation statistics and provide scaling laws in the weak- and intermediate-signal regimes.

</details>


### [99] [On the existence of constants of motion for multipolar test-body dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31200)
*Paul Ramond*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives a Hamiltonian formulation for the motion of an extended test body with arbitrary spin-induced and tidal multipoles in curved spacetime, proves exact conservation of kinematic mass and spin norm, constructs high-order Carter-type constants for Kerr black holes up to hexadecapolar (quartic-in-spin) order—valid only if the body’s multipoles match those of a Kerr black hole—and shows that no such constants exist at quintic-in-spin order, revealing a hidden symmetry unique to black-hole binaries that breaks beyond quartic order.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand whether spinning compact objects in Kerr spacetime admit hidden symmetries (i.e., additional conserved quantities beyond energy and angular momentum), and to determine whether such symmetries distinguish black holes from other compact objects based on their multipole structure.

Method: Covariant Hamiltonian formalism for extended test bodies with arbitrary adiabatic tidal and spin-induced multipoles; construction of generalized Carter–Rüdiger constants via separation of variables and symmetry analysis in Kerr geometry; analytic derivation and numerical verification of obstructions to constant existence at quintic-in-spin order.

Result: 1) Conservative equations of motion follow from H = −½μ² + μU, where U encodes all multipoles covariantly; 2) Kinematic mass μ and spin norm are exactly conserved; 3) Hexadecapolar (quartic-in-spin) Carter–Rüdiger constants exist iff the body’s multipoles match the Kerr black hole’s; 4) No such constants exist at quintic-in-spin order for any conservative multipole structure.

Conclusion: Black-hole binaries possess a perturbative dynamical hidden symmetry—manifested as high-order constants of motion—that is absent for generic compact objects; this symmetry holds up to quartic-in-spin order but breaks at quintic order, indicating a fundamental limitation to integrability and uniqueness of black-hole multipole structure.

Abstract: We show that the conservative equations of motion of an extended test body, with force and torque sourced by spin-induced and adiabatic tidal multipoles of arbitrary order, are the flow of the Hamiltonian $H=-\tfrac12\mu^2+\mu U$, where $\mu$ is the body's kinematic mass and all multipoles enter through a covariant scalar potential $U$. For such bodies, a certain notion of mass and a spin norm are shown to be exactly conserved, at all multipolar orders and in any background. We then apply our framework to the Kerr spacetime, and construct extensions of the Carter and Rüdiger constants through hexadecapolar order (quartic in spin). They are found to exist if, and only if, the body's multipoles are those of a Kerr black hole. Together with the energy and angular momentum, they make the dynamics of a spinning test black hole in Kerr perturbatively integrable through that order. Lastly, at the next, quintic-in-spin order, an analytic criterion implemented numerically reveals that neither constant admits a continuation, for any conservative multipoles. In the conservative, test-body limit, black-hole binaries thus carry a dynamical hidden symmetry shared by no other compact objects, which persists to high multipolar order but ultimately breaks.

</details>


<div id='hep-th'></div>

# hep-th [[Back]](#toc)

### [100] [Non-invertible Duality Defects in Four-Dimensional $\mathbb{Z}_N$ Lattice Gauge Theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30489)
*Satoshi Yamaguchi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs topological interfaces realizing dualities in 4D Z_N lattice gauge theories with generalized plaquette actions; at self-dual points, these become non-invertible duality defects, and their interplay with center symmetry defects—including junctions and crossing relations—is systematically analyzed.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically construct and characterize topological interfaces that implement known dualities in 4D Z_N lattice gauge theories, and to explore their role as non-invertible symmetries—especially at self-dual points—and their compatibility with center symmetry.

Method: Explicit construction of topological interfaces by assigning local weights to lattice building blocks (plaquettes, edges, vertices) and proving invariance under Pachner moves (or equivalent local lattice deformations); analysis of defect junctions using fusion and crossing constraints; identification of duality defects at self-dual couplings via operator algebra and symmetry classification.

Result: Topological interfaces implementing generalized Z_N dualities are rigorously constructed and shown to be invariant under lattice refinement; at self-dual points, they yield non-invertible duality defects; center symmetry defects and their junctions with duality interfaces are classified, and multiple exact crossing relations are derived.

Conclusion: Dualities in 4D Z_N lattice gauge theories admit a precise realization as topological interfaces, which—when self-dual—generate non-invertible symmetries; their fusion with center symmetry defects reveals a rich algebraic structure governed by consistent crossing relations, enriching the landscape of generalized symmetries in quantum gauge theories.

Abstract: We consider four-dimensional $\mathbb{Z}_N$ lattice gauge theories with generalized plaquette actions. These theories are known to exhibit dualities. We construct topological interfaces implementing these dualities by assigning a weight to each building block and proving topological invariance. At self-dual couplings, these interfaces become duality defects representing non-invertible symmetries. We also study center symmetry defects and their junctions with the duality interfaces and derive several crossing relations.

</details>


### [101] [An all-orders proof of gradient flow for multiscalar $ϕ^4$ theory in four dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28206)
*Marten Reehorst*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proves that the renormalisation group (RG) flow of multiscalar φ⁴ theory in d=4 is a gradient flow to all orders in perturbation theory, using Weyl consistency conditions and novel Ward identities; it establishes positivity of the metric, confirms six-loop gradient-flow constraints, and links antisymmetric contributions to symmetric metric and potential terms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve whether the RG flow of multiscalar φ⁴ theory in four dimensions admits a gradient structure — i.e., whether it can be written as dgᵢ/dt = −Gᵢⱼ ∂ⱼV for some positive-definite metric G and potential V — which has implications for irreversibility, monotonicity (e.g., c-theorem analogues), and structural consistency of QFTs.

Method: Uses Osborn–Jack Weyl consistency conditions for local couplings; assumes finiteness of the generating functional with local couplings and asymptotic control of massive Feynman integrals; derives a new Ward identity for the two-point function of marginal operators with sources for field-rotation currents and mass operator; rewrites antisymmetric contributions in the gradient formula via correlation functions; identifies symmetric metric and potential contributions explicitly.

Result: The RG beta functions are shown to be gradient-like: dgᵢ/dt = −Gᵢⱼ ∂ⱼV, where G is positive-definite to all orders; the antisymmetric part of the naive metric is fully absorbed into symmetric redefinitions; the four six-loop constraints on field-rotation coefficients (previously conjectured by Pannell–Stergiou) are rigorously derived and verified by explicit computation.

Conclusion: The perturbative RG flow of 4D multiscalar φ⁴ theory is a genuine gradient flow with a positive-definite metric and potential, confirming structural consistency and extending the applicability of gradient-flow formalism beyond fixed points — a result robust to all orders in perturbation theory.

Abstract: We prove that the renormalisation group flow of multiscalar $\phi^4$ theory in four dimensions is a gradient flow to all orders in perturbation theory. The proof uses the Weyl consistency conditions of Osborn and Jack, and it assumes two properties of perturbation theory: that the generating functional with local couplings is finite (also assumed by Osborn and Jack), and that the known expansion of massive Feynman integrals at large momenta holds with a bound on its remainder at each momentum. The consistency conditions give a gradient formula in which the tensor that multiplies the beta function is allowed to have an antisymmetric part, determined by coefficients of the Weyl anomaly. We derive a Ward identity for the two-point function of the marginal operators at constant couplings away from fixed points, with sources for the currents of field rotations and for the mass operator. It re-expresses one of the anti-symmetric contributions in terms of two-point functions and the first moment of the three-point function. We show that, after this rewrite, each of the resulting terms is equivalent to a symmetric contribution to the metric and a contribution to the potential, and we give both explicitly. The metric is positive definite to all orders in perturbation theory. The proof implies in particular the four relations among five- and six-loop coefficients of the field rotation, which Pannell and Stergiou derived as the conditions for a gradient flow at six loops. We check by explicit computation that these relations indeed hold. A companion paper treats $d=4-\varepsilon$ and other critical dimensions with a Ward identity on the sphere.

</details>


### [102] [Relativistic Hydrodynamics as an Intersection Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28735)
*Nikita Nekrasov,Paul Wiegmann*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper reformulates ideal relativistic hydrodynamics geometrically using symplectic geometry: it recasts the Lichnerowicz–Carter system as the intersection of a coisotropic subvariety (encoding diffeomorphism and momentum-shift constraints) and a Lagrangian submanifold (determined by metric and equation of state) in a symplectic space of differential forms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a fully covariant, geometric foundation for relativistic hydrodynamics that unifies conservation laws, symmetries, and thermodynamic structure within symplectic geometry.

Method: Uses symplectic geometry on a phase space of spacetime differential forms (degrees 0,1,3,4); identifies spacetime diffeomorphisms and momentum one-form shifts as Hamiltonian actions; constructs coisotropic constraint subvariety via moment map zero locus; embeds physical dynamics as intersection with a Lagrangian submanifold defined by metric and equation of state.

Result: Reproduces the Lichnerowicz–Carter equations as a clean geometric intersection; derives canonical flow parameter, circulation, and Ertel invariants intrinsically from constraints; identifies energy–momentum conservation with covariance of the constitutive Lagrangian.

Conclusion: Ideal relativistic hydrodynamics admits a natural, symmetry-respecting formulation in symplectic geometry, revealing deep links between fluid transport, conservation laws, thermodynamics, and underlying geometric structures—extending to nonrelativistic limits and hints at connections to Calabi–Yau geometry via entropy-level helicity.

Abstract: Ideal relativistic hydrodynamics admits a covariant description as an intersection problem in a symplectic space associated with spacetime. The independent fields are differential forms of degrees 0,1,3,4. Spacetime diffeomorphisms and shifts of the momentum one-form act Hamiltonianly; the zero locus of their moment map is a coisotropic subvariety encoding the transport equations. The spacetime metric and equation of state determine a Lagrangian submanifold, whose intersection with the coisotropic subvariety is precisely the Lichnerowicz--Carter system. We explain the geometric meaning of the canonical flow parameter, derive circulation and Ertel invariants directly from the constraints, and identify energy--momentum conservation with covariance of the constitutive Lagrangian. The nonrelativistic limit and a relation of entropy-level helicity to split Calabi--Yau geometry are briefly indicated.

</details>


### [103] [Infrared finite scattering theory: Non-relativistic Coulomb scattering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29691)
*Kartik Prabhu,Shivam Upadhyay*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a perturbative formulation of the Dollard scattering operator for non-relativistic Coulomb scattering, proving cancellation of infrared divergences to all orders; it reformulates the problem in the Heisenberg picture via a time-dependent canonical transformation and extends the LSZ reduction formalism to accommodate long-range asymptotics, laying groundwork for treating infrared divergences in relativistic QFT.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the lack of explicit perturbative calculations within Dollard's framework for Coulomb scattering and to establish a rigorous, generalizable foundation for handling infrared divergences—especially in relativistic QFT with massless fields and long-range interactions.

Method: Constructs a perturbative expansion of the Dollard scattering operator ensuring exact cancellation of Coulomb (logarithmic) divergences at each order; employs a time-dependent canonical transformation to define asymptotic phase-space variables free of logarithmic corrections; builds the Heisenberg-picture S-matrix using multiple complementary approaches and adapts the LSZ reduction formalism to the modified asymptotic dynamics.

Result: Demonstrates explicit divergence cancellation up to second Born order; recovers the exact distributional Coulomb scattering amplitude, including forward-scattering; establishes equivalence between the constructed Heisenberg S-matrix and the Dollard operator; formulates an infrared-adapted LSZ formalism applicable to long-range relativistic QFT.

Conclusion: The Dollard scattering framework can be made fully perturbative and systematically implemented via canonical asymptotic variables; this provides a robust, field-theoretic generalization of scattering theory for infrared-safe observables in theories with massless fields and Coulomb-like interactions.

Abstract: We revisit non-relativistic quantum-mechanical scattering in a Coulomb potential. The long-range nature of the interaction leads to divergent integrals in conventional S-matrix calculations using the Born series. Dollard showed that a well-defined scattering operator can be constructed by replacing the free Hamiltonian $ H_0 $ with an appropriate asymptotic Hamiltonian $ H_{\rm D} $. However, explicit perturbative calculations within Dollard's framework have remained scarce. We develop a perturbative formulation of the Dollard scattering operator in which the Coulomb divergences cancel to all orders. Perturbatively, we demonstrate this cancellation explicitly through second Born order and show that the resulting amplitude reproduces the known exact distributional Coulomb amplitude, including its forward-scattering contribution. With the aim of generalizing our analysis to quantum field theory, we reformulate the problem in the Heisenberg picture. We construct a new set of canonical variables through a time-dependent canonical transformation that removes the logarithmic Coulomb correction from the asymptotic phase-space coordinates. Using these variables, we construct the corresponding Heisenberg S-matrix through several complementary approaches. This leads to a formulation of the Lehmann--Symanzik--Zimmermann (LSZ) reduction formalism adapted to the modified asymptotic dynamics, which we relate to the Dollard scattering operator. This construction provides a framework for extending the LSZ formalism to infrared divergences in relativistic quantum field theories with long-range interactions and massless fields.

</details>


### [104] [Darboux transformation for the ASDYM equations: Solitons and instantons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30433)
*Shangshuai Li*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper uses a one-fold Darboux transformation for anti-self-dual Yang–Mills equations to express the Lagrangian density via τ-functions; it parametrizes soliton τ-functions by Grassmannian points Gr(N,M;ℂ), constructs associated fields, and recasts the ADHM instanton construction in Darboux terms—recovering Osborn’s Lagrangian formula and yielding the gauge connection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and clarify the geometric and algebraic structures underlying solitons and instantons in anti-self-dual Yang–Mills theory by leveraging Darboux transformations and τ-function formalism.

Method: Applies a one-fold Darboux transformation to the anti-self-dual Yang–Mills equations; derives τ-function representation of the Yang–Mills Lagrangian density; parametrizes exponential-soliton τ-functions using complex Grassmannians Gr(N,M;ℂ); reinterprets the ADHM construction as a Darboux transformation.

Result: Explicit τ-function expression for the Yang–Mills Lagrangian; Grassmannian parametrization of soliton solutions and corresponding field constructions; reformulation of ADHM instantons via Darboux transformation; recovery of Osborn’s Lagrangian formula and derivation of the associated gauge connection.

Conclusion: The Darboux transformation provides a unifying framework linking soliton and instanton solutions of anti-self-dual Yang–Mills theory, revealing deep connections between integrable systems, τ-functions, Grassmannians, and the ADHM construction.

Abstract: Using a one-fold Darboux transformation for the anti-self-dual Yang--Mills equations, we express the Yang--Mills Lagrangian density in terms of $\tau$-functions. For solitons constructed from exponential functions, we show that the associated $\tau$-functions admit a parametrization by arbitrary points in the Grassmannian Gr(N,M;$\mathbb{C}$), and construct the corresponding Yang--Mills fields. For instantons arising from the Atiyah--Drinfeld--Hitchin--Manin (ADHM) construction, we reinterpret the ADHM construction in terms of a one-fold Darboux transformation, recover Osborn's formula for the Yang--Mills Lagrangian density, and obtain the corresponding gauge connection.

</details>


### [105] [Gradient formula from the sphere for perturbative RG flows near critical dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31050)
*Marten Reehorst*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a gradient formula for perturbative renormalisation group flows near critical dimensions (d = d_c − ε), linking the beta function to a potential proportional to the sphere free energy F̃, under assumptions of finiteness and local Weyl anomaly; it proves the formula to all orders for several scalar and fermionic theories on S^d, but treats gauge theories as conjectural.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously derive a gradient flow structure for RG beta functions in perturbative QFT near critical dimensions, grounded in geometric and conformal properties of the renormalised functional on the sphere S^d — especially to relate the potential to the universal sphere free energy F̃ and extend Zamolodchikov-type c-theorems to higher dimensions.

Method: Analyzes the renormalised functional with local couplings on S^d; derives Ward identities (including anomaly-free combination), uses finiteness, local Weyl anomaly, conformal Killing vector Ward identity, and rotation covariance; constructs a scheme where the gradient metric remains nondegenerate; verifies assumptions from finiteness for specific models (ϕ⁴, Yukawa, sextic/cubic scalars) in dimensional regularization.

Result: Derives an exact gradient formula β_i = G_ij ∂_j V where G_ij is symmetric and equals the two-point function on S^d, and V is proportional to the sphere free energy F̃ at fixed points; proves its all-orders validity for several perturbative theories in d = 4−ε, 3−ε, 6−ε; shows source terms enter via Weyl variation; confirms metric nondegeneracy under invertibility of a free-theory matrix.

Conclusion: A robust gradient structure exists for RG flows near criticality on S^d for broad classes of perturbative QFTs, unifying free energy, anomalies, and beta functions; however, extension to interacting gauge theories remains open and relies on unproven assumptions.

Abstract: We study the gradient property of perturbative renormalisation group flows in $d=d_c-\varepsilon$, with a potential and a metric regular at $\varepsilon=0$ up to logarithms of gauge couplings. We state properties of the renormalised functional with local couplings on the sphere $S^d$: finiteness, a local Weyl anomaly, the Ward identity of a conformal Killing vector, rotation properties and statements on the free theory. From them we derive a Ward identity without anomaly for a combination of the functional and its anomaly. Its first variation is a gradient formula whose symmetric metric contains the two-point function on the sphere and whose potential at fixed points is, in an extended form of the properties, proportional to the sphere free energy $\tilde F$ up to a constant. Sources of relevant operators enter through their Weyl variation, and in a scheme which we construct the gradient formula persists, with a nondegenerate metric if a matrix of the free theory is invertible. For multiscalar $\phi^4$ theory and for scalars and Dirac fermions with Yukawa and quartic couplings in $d=4-\varepsilon$, for sextic scalars in $d=3-\varepsilon$ and for cubic scalars in $d=6-\varepsilon$, we derive all these properties from a finiteness hypothesis. The gradient formula then holds to all orders for the beta function defined on the sphere. For interacting gauge theories the properties remain assumptions.

</details>


### [106] [Tensionless M5-brane in light-cone gauge. The spinor moving frame method is wanted](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31189)
*Igor Bandos*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper fully fixes the light-cone gauge for the tensionless limit of the M5-brane action, resolving a nontrivial κ-symmetry gauge-fixing problem via a spinor moving frame method built on a nondegenerate vector quadratic in the PST scalar derivative and gauge field strength; it also compares with tensionless p-branes lacking worldvolume gauge fields.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To consistently perform light-cone gauge fixing for the tensionless M5-brane—whose action was recently derived—despite complications arising from κ-symmetry projector dependence on a nonvanishing, nontrivial gauge field strength in that limit.

Method: Adapted the spinor moving frame formalism: constructed a moving frame on a nonvanishing, nondegenerate vector formed from the square of the worldvolume gauge field strength and the derivative of the PST scalar; used this to covariantly fix light-cone gauge and handle κ-symmetry.

Result: A fully light-cone gauge-fixed action and equations of motion for the tensionless M5-brane; identification of residual symmetries; and a parallel spinor moving frame formulation for standard (gauge-field-free) null-p-branes.

Conclusion: The spinor moving frame method successfully resolves the κ-symmetry gauge-fixing obstruction in the tensionless M5-brane limit, enabling consistent light-cone quantization; the approach distinguishes the M5-brane’s richer structure from simpler null-p-branes due to its intrinsic worldvolume gauge dynamics.

Abstract: We perform the complete light-cone gauge fixing for the tensionless limit of the M-theory 5-brane the action for which was recently obtained in [1]. The problem of gauge fixing of local fermionic $\kappa$-symmetry becomes quite nontrivial because the projector of that is constructed from the gauge field strength which in its turn is required to be nonvanaishing and nontrivial in this limit. To resolve this problem the spinor moving frame method has been adapted. The corresponding moving frame is constructed on a vector quadratic in the gauge field strength, which is required to be nonvanishing and nondegenerate in the tensionless limit, and the derivative of the PST (Pasti-Sorokin-Tonin) scalar. The symmetries of the gauge fixed action and equations of motion are briefly discussed. To make a comparison with the case of usual tensionless p-branes (null-p-branes), those which do not carry worldvolume gauge fields, we also elaborate their spinor moving frame formulation.

</details>


### [107] [Horizon mergers and initial test-charge acceleration in charged black-hole geometries with anisotropic sources](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28095)
*Saeed Noori Gashti,Mohammad Ali S. Afshar,Mohammad Reza Alipour*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes horizon mergers and initial radial acceleration of charged test particles in static, spherically symmetric black-hole spacetimes modified by string clouds, Kiselev-type dark energy, or logarithmic dark matter profiles; it derives horizon-merger conditions, classifies Kiselev branches, computes charge-to-mass thresholds for outward acceleration, identifies structural distinctions among profiles (e.g., infimum vs. finite global minimum of λ), and clarifies limitations—e.g., no derivation of two-black-hole forces or cosmic acceleration mechanisms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize how different exotic matter profiles (string cloud, Kiselev power-law, logarithmic) affect black hole horizon structure and charged test-particle kinematics—specifically horizon mergers and initial outward acceleration—while disentangling electromagnetic contributions and assessing geometric versus phenomenological interpretations of stress-energy.

Method: Analytical general relativity: uses static, spherically symmetric metrics with generic mass functions incorporating string cloud, Kiselev (power-law), and logarithmic stress-energy components; separates Maxwell contribution to isolate effective environmental stress tensors; derives horizon merger conditions via root analysis of metric function g_{tt}=0; computes initial radial acceleration of static-release charged particles using geodesic equation; identifies critical charge-to-mass ratios λ=q/μ and expresses them in terms of normalized charge z=Q/Q_ext; performs stability and extremum analysis of balance radii and thresholds.

Result: Horizon merger conditions depend on profile type: inner-event merger charge sets only an upper bound for Kiselev, but subextremal string-cloud and logarithmic cases impose both upper and lower bounds for three-horizon configurations; the local λ-threshold for outward acceleration has infimum 1/z at infinity for string cloud, while logarithmic profile yields a finite global minimum (e.g., λ_*≈1.251125 for α/M=0.4, Q/M=1.2); neutral balance radius is unique and radially unstable in nondegenerate Kiselev patches; density matching fixes normalization conditionally—not observationally.

Conclusion: The geometric effects of exotic matter profiles on black hole horizons and test-particle dynamics are qualitatively distinct and analytically tractable; however, these results describe kinematic and structural bounds—not physical mechanisms for cosmic acceleration, binary interactions, or quantum gravity conjectures like the Weak Gravity Conjecture.

Abstract: We study horizon mergers and the initial radial acceleration of charged test particles in static, spherically symmetric black-hole geometries with a string cloud, a Kiselev power-law component, or a logarithmic profile conventionally. After separating the Maxwell contribution, the effective environmental stress tensors are anisotropic and are not identified with homogeneous quintessence or pressureless cold dark matter. Using a common mass-function formulation, we derive the horizon-merger conditions and classify the Kiselev branches. In this family, the inner-event merger charge provides only an upper bound: three distinct positive horizons can additionally require a positive lower charge bound associated with the event-cosmological merger. For a test particle released from rest relative to a static observer, we derive the local charge-to-mass threshold for outward initial acceleration. Writing $\lambda=q/\mu$ and $z=Q/Q_{\rm ext}$, where $Q_{\rm ext}$ is the inner-event merger charge, the subextremal string-cloud threshold has infimum $1/z$, approached only at infinity. For the subextremal logarithmic profile, the threshold exceeds unity throughout the finite exterior and attains a finite global minimum. For $\alpha/M=0.4$ and $Q/M=1.2$, this minimum is $\lambda_*\simeq1.251125$. In a nondegenerate Kiselev static patch, the neutral balance radius is unique and radially unstable. Density matching supplies conditional profile normalizations, not observational fits. For the logarithmic profile, the integration parameter is distinguished from the mass combination inferred from circular motion. These results characterize geometric horizon bounds and test-particle dynamics; they do not establish a two-black-hole force law or a mechanism for cosmic acceleration, nor do they constitute a test of the Weak Gravity Conjecture.

</details>


### [108] [Level repulsion and the quantum viscosity of near-extremal black branes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28203)
*Panos Betzios,Olga Papadoulaki,Maria Myrto Pegioudi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies quantum corrections to shear viscosity (η) of near-extremal black branes in JT gravity, showing that perturbative one-loop calculations diverge, but non-perturbative completion via matrix integrals yields finite, universal η/s behavior governed by spectral statistics—level repulsion suppresses DC viscosity, while a double-scaling limit reveals saturation and temperature-dependent scaling; at ultra-low temperatures near the spectral edge, the Airy kernel governs exact quenched results, and bulk observables are robust across different non-perturbative completions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the unphysical divergence of shear viscosity η/s at low temperatures in the one-loop approximation of JT gravity, and to understand how non-perturbative quantum gravity effects—encoded in matrix integral duals—determine universal transport properties through spectral statistics.

Method: Combines the Kubo formula for shear viscosity with spectral analysis of the matrix integral dual of JT gravity; employs double-scaling limits, level-spacing scaling (ω_sp ∝ e^{−S₀}), Schwarzian effective theory, Airy kernel asymptotics at the spectral edge, and comparison across string-equation-defined non-perturbative completions (e.g., bounded spectra).

Result: DC viscosity vanishes due to level repulsion at finite S₀; a finite universal η emerges when ω/ω_sp is held fixed; η saturates at η_max ≈ 0.174 (ω/ω_sp) η_sc in an intermediate regime; below T ∼ e^{−2S₀/3}/C, genus expansion fails and Airy kernel gives exact quenched η/s > 0; annealed averages depend on completion details at O(1), but quenched edge results vary only by ~few % across completions.

Conclusion: Shear viscosity in near-extremal black branes is not governed by semiclassical hydrodynamics at low T, but by universal random-matrix-theoretic features of the quantum gravity spectrum; non-perturbative completion cures the one-loop divergence and yields robust, finite η/s ratios, establishing a concrete link between spectral statistics and holographic transport.

Abstract: Below the gap scale of the AdS$_2$ throat, quantum fluctuations make the shear viscosity of near-extremal black branes grow as $(CT)^{-1/2}$ in the disk approximation of JT gravity, and the one-loop $\eta/s$ diverges. To cure this divergence, we include the non-perturbative completion of JT gravity as a matrix integral. The Kubo formula then probes universal features of level correlations. At finite extremal entropy $S_0$, level repulsion makes the DC viscosity vanish. A finite, universal viscosity survives in a double-scaling limit in which the probe frequency $\omega$ is held in a fixed ratio to the level-spacing scale $\omega_{\rm sp}\equiv e^{-S_0}/C$. At fixed $\omega$, with $\omega_{\rm sp}\ll\omega\ll e^{S_0/3}\omega_{\rm sp}$ the Schwarzian growth then saturates at $\eta_{\max}\simeq0.174\,(\omega/\omega_{\rm sp})\,\eta_{sc}$, where $\eta_{sc}$ is the semiclassical viscosity, and the viscosity decreases at lower temperatures. At the extremely low temperature scale $CT\sim e^{-2S_0/3}$ the genus expansion and the one-loop entropy computations completely break down. There we start probing the edge of the spectrum and the problem reduces exactly to the Airy kernel. We find that the ratio of the quenched averages of $\eta$ and $s$ is finite and positive at every non-zero temperature. We compare our results with explicit completions defined by the string equation, in which the spectrum is bounded from below. The bulk results are the same in these completions and the quenched edge results change only by a few per cent, while the annealed quantities depend on the non-perturbative completion at order one.

</details>


### [109] [The index from a super-point](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28204)
*Dhanvarsh Annamalai,Hyunook Hur,Thomas Hussel,Monica Jinwoo Kang,Craig Lawrie,Jaewon Song*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper generalizes the algebro-geometric construction of filtered graded rings for 4d N=2 SCFTs to include fermionic Schur generators, resulting in Z₂-graded-commutative rings; the Macdonald index is recovered via Hilbert series of a generalized arc space with derivative-inclusive ideals; explicit computations are performed for free vector multiplets, N=4 SYM (su(2), su(3)), rank-one N=3 SCFTs, and the Ê₆(SU(2)) SCFT, where the VOA A(6) is shown not classically free but becomes index-matching upon adding three jet-ring relations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the known algebro-geometric correspondence between Higgs branches, VOAs, and superconformal indices to 4d N=2 SCFTs whose Schur sector contains fermionic generators — a case not covered by previous commutative ring constructions.

Method: Constructs Z₂-graded-commutative filtered graded rings encoding both Higgs branch geometry and Macdonald index; defines a generalized arc space whose Hilbert series yields the index; computes defining ideals as sums of Jacobian ideals of superpotentials or N=4 superfield components; analyzes Zhu's C₂-algebra and its jet-ring extensions for the Ê₆(SU(2)) theory.

Result: The Macdonald index is reproduced as the Hilbert series of a generalized arc space for all studied theories; for rank-one N=3 SCFTs and N=4 SYM, the ideal is expressed via Jacobian ideals of superpotentials/superfields; for Ê₆(SU(2)), Zhu's C₂-algebra of A(6) fails to reproduce the vacuum character, but adding three relations in the first jet ring restores agreement up to q⁵¹.

Conclusion: The framework successfully accommodates fermionic Schur generators via Z₂-graded rings and generalized arc spaces; classical freeness fails for Ê₆(SU(2)), necessitating jet-ring corrections — indicating that the associated VOA’s chiral algebra structure requires higher-order derivative constraints beyond Zhu’s C₂-algebra.

Abstract: We extend the algebro-geometric construction of filtered graded rings that encode both the Higgs branch and the Macdonald index of 4d $\mathcal{N}=2$ superconformal field theories to theories whose Schur sector contains fermionic generators. The rings are then $\mathbb{Z}_2$-graded-commutative, and the index is reproduced by the Hilbert series of a generalized arc space, whose defining ideal may contain relations among the generators and their derivatives. We study the free $\mathcal{N}=2$ vector multiplet, $\mathcal{N}=4$ super Yang--Mills with gauge algebras $\mathfrak{su}(2)$ and $\mathfrak{su}(3)$, the rank-one $\mathcal{N}=3$ SCFTs, and the $\widehat{E}_6(SU(2))$ SCFT. For the rank-one $\mathcal{N}=3$ SCFTs, the ideal is the sum of the Jacobian ideals of a single superpotential and of its two supersymmetry variations. For $\mathcal{N}=4$ super Yang--Mills, the ideal is similarly the sum of the Jacobian ideals of the components of a single $\mathcal{N}=4$ superfield, which is a singlet of the $\mathfrak{su}(2)$ flavor symmetry for gauge algebra $\mathfrak{su}(2)$, and a triplet for $\mathfrak{su}(3)$. The associated VOA for the $\widehat{E}_6(SU(2))$ theory is given by the Feigin--Feigin--Tipunin $\mathcal{A}(6)$ algebra. The arc space of Zhu's $C_2$-algebra of $\mathcal{A}(6)$ does not reproduce the vacuum character, and therefore $\mathcal{A}(6)$ is not classically free. Supplementing Zhu's $C_2$-algebra by three relations in the first jet ring, the generalized arc space reproduces the Macdonald index of $\widehat{E}_6(SU(2))$ up to, at least, order $q^{51}$.

</details>


### [110] [The Gradient Structure of Perturbative RG Flows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28205)
*Lorenzo Benfatto,William H. Pannell,Andreas Stergiou*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper resolves a long-standing obstruction to gradient flow structure in perturbative RG flows by using a conformal Ward identity on the sphere, establishing that the beta function is the gradient of a potential A w.r.t. a symmetric, positive-definite metric to all orders; it proves the ̃F-theorem perturbatively and clarifies scheme dependence and exceptions (e.g., free scalar near d=6).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To remove the persistent antisymmetric contribution to the coupling-space metric that obstructs gradient flow structure in Weyl-consistent RG flows—unresolved since Jack and Osborn (1980s)—and thereby establish a fully gradient formulation valid to all perturbative orders.

Method: Leverages a conformal Ward identity on the d-sphere to fix a renormalisation scheme where the antisymmetric part of the metric vanishes; derives the gradient property beta^i = G^{ij} \partial_j A with symmetric G_{ij} determined by two-point functions of perturbing operators; analyzes fixed-point values, unitarity constraints, and operator-source mixing under curvature.

Result: The beta function is proven to be a gradient of A w.r.t. a symmetric, unitarity-guaranteed positive-definite metric to all loop orders in perturbation theory; A equals \widetilde{F} = \sin(\pi d/2) \ln Z_{S^d} at fixed points, implying the \widetilde{F}-theorem for perturbative flows; source mixing with curvature is shown irrelevant except for free scalar near d=6,10,...

Conclusion: Weyl consistency conditions, augmented by the spherical conformal Ward identity, yield a fully gradient RG flow formulation in arbitrary dimension d, confirming the \widetilde{F}-theorem perturbatively; the only known exception to gradient property is \phi^3 theory near six dimensions due to free scalar source subtleties.

Abstract: Weyl consistency conditions show that perturbative renormalisation group flows in two, four and six dimensions are almost gradient flows. The obstruction is an antisymmetric contribution to the metric on the space of couplings, which has resisted removal since the work of Jack and Osborn more than 35 years ago. We show that a conformal Ward identity on the sphere allows it to be removed by a choice of scheme. The beta function is then the gradient of a potential $A$ with respect to a symmetric metric, to all orders in perturbation theory. The Ward identity gives this result in any dimension $d$, in theories with and without a Weyl anomaly. At leading order the metric is fixed by the two-point functions of the perturbing operators, so unitarity makes it positive definite at weak coupling. At fixed points $A$ equals $\widetilde{F}=\sin\tfrac{\pi d}{2}\,\ln Z_{S^{d}}$, so the $\widetilde{F}$-theorem follows for perturbative flows to all loop orders. We show that sources of relevant operators, which on the sphere mix with the curvature, do not affect these results. In unitary theories the only exception is the source of a free scalar field near $d=6,10,\ldots\,$, so that for $\phi^3$ theory near six dimensions the gradient property remains open.

</details>


### [111] [Bring Your Own Instanton: Monopole Catalysis from Sourced SU(2) Instantons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28208)
*Csaba Csáki,Larissa Kiriliuk,Zhihan Liu,Rotem Ovadia*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper identifies 'sourced SU(2) instantons'—novel topological gauge configurations induced by fermions—as the mechanism behind Rubakov-Callan proton decay catalysis on 't Hooft-Polyakov monopoles; unlike BPST instantons, they yield unsuppressed rates, and their winding number is tied to fermionic zero modes via a proposed index theorem; the leading-order fermion correlator is computed explicitly, showing catalysis is an IR effect without core penetration.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the mechanism and rate suppression of Rubakov-Callan catalysis of proton decay in the presence of magnetic monopoles, particularly whether it relies on conventional (exponentially suppressed) instanton effects or a distinct topological structure.

Method: Analytical field-theoretic analysis using topological gauge theory, identification and characterization of 'sourced SU(2) instantons', derivation of fermionic zero-mode counting, formulation of a conjectured index theorem, and explicit computation of fermion 2-point correlators in monopole background (both asymptotic and core regions).

Result: Rubakov-Callan catalysis proceeds via sourced SU(2) instantons carrying unit topological charge and induced by fermions; these avoid exponential suppression; a direct relation between instanton winding number and number of fermionic zero modes is established; closed-form expressions for the leading-order fermion correlator are obtained, confirming catalysis occurs dominantly in the infrared without fermions entering the monopole core.

Conclusion: Rubakov-Callan catalysis is governed by sourced instantons—an intrinsically fermion-coupled topological sector—not vacuum instantons; it is an IR-dominated, unsuppressed process, implying enhanced phenomenological viability and necessitating revision of standard instanton-based rate estimates in grand unified theories with monopoles.

Abstract: We show that Rubakov-Callan catalysis of proton decay in the field of a 't Hooft-Polyakov monopole proceeds through "sourced SU(2) instantons", which are novel topological gauge configurations sourced by fermions and carrying unit instanton number. Unlike vacuum BPST instantons, these sourced instantons do not give rise to exponentially suppressed rates. We relate the winding number to the number of fermionic zero modes, suggesting an underlying index theorem for sourced instantons. We also calculate the closed-form expression for the leading-order correlator for fermion insertions both outside and inside the monopole core. Our results indicate that Rubakov-Callan catalysis is an IR effect where the scattering fermions do not reach the monopole core.

</details>


### [112] [Wormholes do not contribute to supersymmetric partition functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28210)
*Jan Boruch,Douglas Stanford*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Wormholes connecting multiple asymptotic boundaries always break some symmetries; for fermionic symmetries, their contributions to supersymmetric partition function moments vanish — extending prior work on supersymmetric indices — though nonzero ensemble variance can persist via non-geometric wormholes preserving all continuous symmetries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand whether wormholes contribute to moments of supersymmetric partition functions, particularly in light of recent results on supersymmetric indices and the role of symmetry breaking in gravitational path integrals.

Method: Analysis of symmetry properties of multi-boundary wormhole configurations, focusing on spontaneous symmetry breaking under boundary conditions and cancellation mechanisms for fermionic symmetries; application to SYK-like models to examine ensemble variance.

Result: Wormholes always spontaneously break at least some symmetries preserved by boundary conditions; for fermionic symmetries, each orbit’s contribution vanishes, so wormholes do not contribute to moments of supersymmetric partition functions; however, non-geometric wormhole configurations can generate nonzero ensemble variance.

Conclusion: Geometric wormholes are forbidden from contributing to supersymmetric partition function moments due to fermionic symmetry cancellation, but ensemble variance remains possible via symmetry-preserving non-geometric configurations.

Abstract: We show that wormholes connecting multiple asymptotic boundaries always spontaneously break at least some of the symmetries preserved by their boundary conditions. Their configurations then form nontrivial orbits of the broken symmetry, and for the case of a fermionic symmetry, the contribution of each orbit vanishes. We conclude that wormholes do not contribute to moments of supersymmetric partition functions. This extends results on supersymmetric indices by Iliesiu, Kologlu, and Turiaci. We emphasize that supersymmetric partition functions can still have a nonzero ensemble variance. In SYK-like examples that we analyze, this arises due to the contribution of ``non-geometric wormhole'' configurations that preserve all continuous symmetries.

</details>


### [113] [The chiral $M^{3,2}$ theory: partition functions beyond large-$N$ and quantum holography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28594)
*Xuao Zhang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper identifies families of Bethe vacua in the chiral $M^{3,2}$ theory with $N^{3/2}$-scaling free energies, derives all-order perturbative $1/N$ expansions, links vacuum degeneracy to baryonic magnetic flux at a special Chern–Simons coupling, and proposes an all-order Airy formula for the $S^3$ partition function—tested via four-derivative and one-loop holographic corrections.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the microscopic origin of $N^{3/2}$ scaling in 3D $\mathcal{N}=2$ SCFTs dual to M-theory on $AdS_4 \\times M^{3,2}$, and to test AdS/CFT beyond leading order via precise $S^3$ partition function analysis.

Method: Bethe ansatz analysis of supersymmetric localization on $S^3$, high-precision numerical evaluation of the partition function, asymptotic expansion in the 't Hooft and Cardy limits, identification of vacuum families via mixed Chern–Simons couplings and baryonic flux quantization.

Result: Closed-form all-order $1/N$ expansions for free energies of Bethe vacua; distinction of vacua by baryonic magnetic flux at critical mixed Chern–Simons level; numerical evidence and analytic proposal of an all-order Airy formula for $Z_{S^3}$; matching of subleading four-derivative and one-loop supergravity corrections.

Conclusion: The $M^{3,2}$ theory realizes a rich vacuum structure consistent with M-theory holography at finite $N$, where baryonic flux labels distinct AdS$_4$ vacua, and the Airy deformation captures quantum gravity corrections beyond classical supergravity.

Abstract: We identify families of Bethe vacua in the chiral $M^{3,2}$ theory whose individual free energies grow as $N^{3/2}$. High-precision data support closed forms for their all-order perturbative $1/N$ expansions. At a distinguished mixed Chern--Simons coupling, the baryonic magnetic flux distinguishes members of this family of vacua. We also study the three-sphere partition function numerically in the 't Hooft limit and Cardy limit, and propose an all-order Airy formula. The four-derivative and one-loop corrections are precision tests of holography.

</details>


### [114] [Massive Particles in Carrollian Holography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28780)
*Bin Chen,Zezhou Hu,Reiko Liu,Wen-Jie Ma*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a 3D Carrollian holographic description for a massive scalar field in 4D flat spacetime, introducing a bulk-to-boundary integral transform that preserves Poincaré symmetry, enforces null-state decoupling as a local boundary constraint, and enables computation of correlators and OPE data—requiring analytic continuation to complex momenta.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how massive particles are encoded in boundary operators within Carrollian holography—a key open problem at the interface of flat-space holography and ultra-relativistic (Carrollian) symmetry.

Method: Constructs a three-dimensional Carrollian boundary theory dual to a massive scalar in four-dimensional Minkowski space; derives an explicit bulk-to-boundary integral transform; identifies infinite primary states required for nonzero mass; imposes descendant relations from absence of bulk spin; shows equivalence to null-state decoupling; derives two- and three-point functions and formal OPE coefficients; analyzes analytic continuation necessity in Lorentzian signature.

Result: A symmetry-preserving integral transform mapping bulk massive scalar data to constrained boundary fields; a local differential constraint on boundary operators arising from null-state decoupling; explicit expressions for Carrollian two- and three-point correlators and associated OPE coefficients; demonstration that analytic continuation to complex momenta is essential for the transform in Lorentzian signature.

Conclusion: The construction provides a concrete, symmetry-respecting framework for Carrollian holography of massive fields, enabling future studies of flat-space scattering amplitudes via boundary correlators and bulk reconstruction from Carrollian data.

Abstract: Understanding how massive particles are encoded in boundary operators is a central problem in Carrollian holography. We construct a three-dimensional Carrollian description of a massive scalar in four-dimensional flat spacetime, together with an explicit bulk-to-boundary integral transform. In this construction, nonzero mass requires infinitely many linearly independent primary states, while the absence of bulk spin imposes relations among their descendants. We show that these relations are equivalent to null-state decoupling and give a local constraint on the boundary fields. The integral transform preserves the full Poincaré symmetry and maps bulk scalar data to fields satisfying this constraint. We also obtain two- and three-point functions and the associated formal operator product expansion coefficients. The integral transform evaluates bulk data at complex momenta, making analytic continuation essential in Lorentzian signature. This construction provides a concrete starting point for relating massive scattering amplitudes to Carrollian correlators and for reconstructing bulk fields from boundary data.

</details>


### [115] [Thermodynamics of hard wall model of AdS/QCD at finite chemical potential with Gauss-Bonnet corrections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28884)
*Dharm Veer Singh,Shobhit Sachan*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates the confinement/deconfinement transition in a 5D hard-wall AdS/QCD model within Einstein–Maxwell–Gauss–Bonnet gravity, incorporating backreaction from finite quark density; it derives analytic transition lines, shows how Gauss–Bonnet coupling affects transition temperature and phase coexistence, and computes density-dependent ρ-meson properties in the confined phase.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how finite quark density and higher-curvature (Gauss–Bonnet) corrections affect the confinement/deconfinement transition in holographic QCD, bridging predictions with lattice QCD and nuclear matter phenomenology.

Method: Analytic thermodynamic analysis of black hole (deconfined) vs. IR-cutoff thermal AdS (confined) solutions in 5D Einstein–Maxwell–Gauss–Bonnet gravity with backreacted quark matter; derivation of transition conditions at fixed chemical potential and fixed quark number density; WKB analysis of ρ-meson spectra in the confined phase.

Result: Closed-form transition lines obtained; negative Gauss–Bonnet coupling raises transition temperature and extends confined phase to higher densities; zero-density transition temperature matches lattice QCD for causality-respecting coupling; ρ-meson and radial excitation masses increase monotonically with density (~1% rise at nuclear density), interpreted via reduced effective confining size.

Conclusion: Gauss–Bonnet curvature corrections quantitatively tune the confinement transition and mesonic observables in dense matter, supporting the viability of higher-derivative holographic models for QCD thermodynamics and in-medium hadron physics.

Abstract: We study the confinement/deconfinement transition of the hard wall model of AdS/QCD at finite quark density in five-dimensional Einstein--Maxwell--Gauss--Bonnet gravity, with the back-reaction of the quark matter on the geometry included. The deconfined phase is a charged Gauss--Bonnet black hole, and the confined phase is thermally charged AdS cut off in the infrared. The infrared value of the gauge field, which Lee, Park and Sin fixed by requiring a consistent quark number, becomes a function of the Gauss--Bonnet coupling. We obtain the transition line in closed form at fixed quark chemical potential and at fixed quark number density. At fixed density the transition passes through a region where the two phases coexist. A negative Gauss--Bonnet coupling raises the transition temperature and extends the confined phase to higher density. A value inside the causality bound gives a transition temperature at zero density close to the lattice value. In the dense confined phase we compute the masses and decay constants of the $\rho$ meson and its radial excitations. All masses increase with density. A WKB analysis relates the increase to a shorter effective size of the confining region, and at nuclear density the $\rho$ mass rises by about one percent.

</details>


### [116] [Finite-Temperature Stabilization of a KKLT Modulus in Three-Dimensional Lattice Simulations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29000)
*Saya Mahmoudi,Furkan Utku Biber,Hyukjung Kim,Heeseung Zoe*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper uses 3D Langevin lattice simulations to show that cosmological stabilization of a KKLT modulus can fail locally due to stochastic fluctuations near the capture threshold, even when the spatially averaged field appears stabilized.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether homogeneous KKLT modulus stabilization remains robust under realistic inhomogeneous, thermal, and stochastic conditions during early-universe evolution.

Method: Three-dimensional Langevin lattice simulations incorporating an evolving thermal bath, dissipation, spatial gradients, and stochastic noise; supplemented by linear Langevin theory and discretization convergence tests.

Result: Near-critical trajectories exhibit prolonged residence near the unstable barrier (~1 e-fold), enabling tachyonic amplification of long-wavelength fluctuations; localized barrier crossing occurs despite mean-field recapture, and the barrier-crossing fraction is insensitive to discretization within a narrow stochastic layer near threshold.

Conclusion: Spatial averaging masks local instability: successful capture of the spatially averaged modulus does not ensure global stabilization—stochastic effects can drive localized regions over the barrier, undermining vacuum stability.

Abstract: We investigate the cosmological stabilization of a KKLT modulus through three-dimensional Langevin lattice simulations incorporating an evolving thermal bath, dissipation, spatial gradients, and stochastic fluctuations. While the spatially averaged modulus closely follows homogeneous evolution away from the capture threshold, near-critical trajectories linger near the unstable potential barrier for approximately one e-fold, allowing long-wavelength fluctuations to undergo tachyonic amplification and drive localized regions across the barrier even as the mean field turns back toward the stabilized vacuum. Linear Langevin theory reproduces the fluctuation growth, while convergence tests identify a narrow stochastic layer in which the barrier-crossing fraction shows no systematic dependence on the discretization at a fixed effective distance from the threshold. These results demonstrate that capture of the spatially averaged modulus does not guarantee that every spatial region remains within the stabilizing barrier.

</details>


### [117] [Exact stress-tensor fluctuations from conformal symmetry and black hole radiation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29060)
*José Navarro-Salas,Roberto Balbinot,Alessandro Fabbri*

Main category: hep-th
