TL;DR: This paper computes local stress-tensor fluctuations in 2D semiclassical gravity using conformal symmetry, showing relative fluctuations scale as 1/c; results support semiclassical validity for large central charge c.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Semiclassical gravity relies on small quantum stress-tensor fluctuations around their expectation value; in 2D, conformal symmetry enables exact computation of both mean and fluctuation structure of null stress components T_±±, crucial for assessing semiclassical reliability near black holes.

Method: Uses 2D conformal symmetry and operator product expansion (OPE) to define T_±±² up to a point-splitting parameter α; applies conformal mapping to locally inertial coordinates to derive closed-form variance expressions for T_±± fluctuations; evaluates results at infinity and horizon of Schwarzschild black hole.

Result: Relative stress-tensor fluctuations scale as 1/c; explicit variances computed: 22/5c for Hawking flux at infinity; -44α/3c (outgoing) and (238+720α)/5c (ingoing) at horizon; all reduce to 22/5c for α = −3/10 (Möbius-covariant prescription).

Conclusion: Fluctuations are suppressed by 1/c, confirming semiclassical gravity is self-consistent for large central charge c, particularly in the s-wave sector of 2D black hole evaporation.

Abstract: Semiclassical gravity is sourced by the expectation value of the stress tensor, and it is reliable only if the fluctuations around this value are small. In two dimensions, conformal symmetry captures the essential physics of Hawking radiation in its s-wave sector and determines the mean value exactly, for any background and any quantum state. We show that it also determines the local fluctuations of the null components $T_{\pm\pm}$. The square of the stress tensor is defined by the operator product expansion, up to a multiple $\alpha$ of $\partial_\pm^2T_{\pm\pm}$ that labels point-splitting prescriptions. Its transformation law under the conformal map to locally inertial coordinates gives the variance in closed form. The relative fluctuation scales as $1/c$, with $c$ the central charge. For the Schwarzschild black hole it is $22/5c$ for the Hawking flux at infinity, that of flat-space thermal radiation. At the horizon it is $-44\alpha/3c$ for the outgoing flux and $(238+720\alpha)/5c$ for the ingoing flux that drives the backreaction. Both reduce to $22/5c$ for $\alpha=-3/10$, which corresponds to the Möbius-covariant prescription. For large $c$ these results support the semiclassical approximation.

</details>


### [118] [The Generalized Second Law from Fluctuation Theorems in a Quantum Reference Frame](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29415)
*Feng-Li Lin,Sayid Mondal*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proves the generalized second law (GSL) for black-hole exteriors under unitary driving and relaxation using a fixed dressed exterior algebra, modular crossed products, and quantum channel fluctuation theorems—bypassing semiclassical restrictions of prior work.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend Wall's semiclassical GSL proof to fully quantum gravitational settings by removing assumptions on fixed parent states and horizon-cut algebras, enabling a more general, algebraic, and state-operator dual formulation compatible with unitary evolution and open-system dynamics.

Method: Constructs a modular crossed product using a gravitational charge as a quantum clock; defines density operators whose trace entropy recovers generalized entropy up to clock contributions; applies quantum channel fluctuation theorems (FTs) in three complementary formulations—separated driving/relaxation, time-local simultaneous dynamics, and two-time surprisal FT—to derive entropy bounds.

Result: Establishes the GSL for unitarily driven and relaxed black-hole exteriors on a fixed semifinite algebra; identifies unitality (w.r.t. dressed-charge sectors and reference state) as sufficient—but not necessary—for the FT-based proof; quantifies finite-clock-width corrections and confirms GSL in the slow-clock limit.

Conclusion: The GSL holds beyond semiclassical geometry via algebraic quantum gravity methods; the modular crossed product framework and quantum channel FTs provide a robust, operational foundation for black-hole thermodynamics in unitary quantum gravity.

Abstract: We prove the generalized second law (GSL) for black-hole exteriors undergoing unitary driving and relaxation on a fixed semifinite algebra. Wall's influential proof assumes semiclassical geometry and restricts fixed parent states to algebras of successive horizon cuts. We instead evolve the state on a fixed dressed exterior algebra. With complete characteristic data, the same semiclassical exterior supports both descriptions, analogous to the Heisenberg and Schrödinger pictures through state--operator duality. Using a gravitational charge as a quantum clock, we construct a modular crossed product with density operators whose trace entropy reproduces generalized entropy up to clock contributions. Our approach parallels thermodynamics in regulated field theory and formulates the GSL through a quantum channel fluctuation theorem (FT) for the open exterior. We develop three complementary formulations under specified dynamical assumptions. Separated unitary driving and relaxation combine modular work, the physical-process first law relating horizon absorption to area gain, and relative-entropy contraction through data processing. A time-local description accommodates simultaneous unitary driving and dissipation. A two-time surprisal FT treats the complete channel, identifies the nonunital correction, and yields a GSL bound for unital dressed evolution without resolving intermediate dynamics. Relaxation that does not mix distinct dressed-charge sectors and preserves the full dressed reference state is unital. Unitality is sufficient for this FT proof but is not necessary for the GSL along a particular evolution. The entropy bounds establish the GSL in a controlled slow-clock limit and quantify finite-width clock corrections.

</details>


### [119] [Symmetry resolved spread complexity for BMN matrix model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29425)
*Dibakar Roychowdhury*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies symmetry-resolved spread complexity in the BMN matrix model under a U(1) subgroup of SO(6), contrasting low-temperature confined and finite-temperature deconfined phases using cTFD states; it finds distinct Lanczos coefficient scalings, sector-dependent complexity growth, and holographic interpretations via LM droplets vs. charged D0-brane horizons.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how global symmetry decomposition affects quantum complexity dynamics in gauge/gravity duality, particularly how charge resolution reveals phase-dependent scrambling signatures and breaks complexity equipartition in matrix models dual to string theory backgrounds.

Method: Analyzes symmetry-resolved spread complexity by decomposing the charged thermofield double (cTFD) state into fixed-U(1)-charge sectors; computes return amplitudes, spectral moments, and Lanczos coefficients (a_n^{(q)}, b_n^{(q)}) in both confined (βμ ≫ 1) and deconfined (T ≫ μ) regimes; models spectral density as displaced harmonic oscillators (gapped) or thermal Gaussian (continuum); interprets results holographically via Lin-Maldacena geometries and D0-brane black hole horizons.

Result: In the confined phase: diagonal Lanczos coefficients grow linearly (a_n^{(q)} ∝ n), off-diagonal ones scale as √n with thermal suppression; moments factorize; complexity equipartition fails due to Boltzmann weights, vacuum shifts, degeneracies; early-time positivity bound ΔC(t) ≥ 0 holds. In the deconfined phase: a_n^{(q)} = E̅_q (constant), b_n^{(q)} = σ_E√n (universal), spread complexity launches quadratically ℂ^{(q)}(t) ∼ σ_E²(q,T)t². Holographically, phases map to coherent string fluctuations (LM droplets) vs. dissipative near-horizon scrambling (charged D0-branes).

Conclusion: Symmetry resolution exposes fundamental differences in complexity growth mechanisms across quantum phases of the BMN model, linking spectral geometry of charge sectors to holographic spacetime structure—specifically, integrable vs. chaotic dynamics manifest in Lanczos scaling and early-time complexity launch, validating spread complexity as a probe of gauge/gravity phase structure.

Abstract: We investigate the symmetry-resolved spread complexity of the Berenstein-Maldacena-Nastase (BMN) matrix model under a global $U(1)$ symmetry acting within the transverse $SO(6)$ scalar sector. Decomposing the time-evolved charged thermofield double (cTFD) state into orthogonal fixed-charge sectors, we examine the return amplitude, spectral moments, tridiagonal Lanczos coefficients, and early-time spread complexity across the low-temperature confined ($\beta\mu \gg 1$) and finite-temperature deconfined ($T \gg \mu$) phases. In the low-temperature gapped regime, effective sector dynamics map onto decoupled, displaced harmonic oscillators with a Casimir-like zero-point offset, yielding factorized moments, linear drift in diagonal Lanczos coefficients $a_n^{(q)}$, and sub-linear $\sqrt{n}$ off-diagonal growth with thermally suppressed initial hopping. Charge-dependent Boltzmann weights, vacuum shifts, and degeneracies break complexity equipartition, while inter-sector coherence and charge dispersion enforce an early-time positivity bound $\Delta C(t) = C_{\text{total}}(t) - \bar{C}(t) \ge 0$. In the \emph{finite}-temperature continuum phase, modeling the planar large-$N$ spectral density as a thermal Gaussian gives constant diagonal coefficients $a_n^{(q)} = \bar{E}_q$, universal square-root scaling $b_n^{(q)} = \sigma_E \sqrt{n}$, and a quadratic spread launch $\mathcal{C}^{(q)}(t) = \sigma_E^2(q,T)\,t^2 + \mathcal{O}(t^4)$ governed by the backreacted sector energy variance. Holographically, these regimes distinguish coherent string fluctuations in horizonless Lin-Maldacena (LM) droplet geometries from dissipative scrambling near charged ten-dimensional black D0-brane horizons.

</details>


### [120] [Scattering amplitudes in AdS$_5\times$ S$^5$ from the pure spinor superstring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29622)
*Luis F. Alday,Thiago Fleury,Tobias Hansen,Lucas N. S. Martins,Dennis Zavaleta*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a BRST-invariant zero-mode integration prescription for the pure spinor superstring on AdS₅×S⁵, enabling first-principles worldsheet computation of two- and three-point half-BPS supergravity amplitudes, with normalized three-point results matching planar N=4 SYM.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the long-standing obstacle of zero-mode integration in the pure spinor formalism on AdS₅×S⁵ — a key bottleneck preventing direct worldsheet amplitude computations in this maximally supersymmetric holographic background.

Method: Constructs a BRST-invariant measure for zero-mode integration that incorporates AdS₅×S⁵-specific contributions absent in flat space; implements it via picture-changing operators or a non-minimal regulator; applies it to genus-zero correlators with arbitrary external half-BPS supergravity states.

Result: First explicit worldsheet computations of two- and three-point amplitudes for half-BPS supergravity states in AdS₅×S⁅; normalized three-point functions exactly match the known planar N=4 SYM results (e.g., structure constants), validating the prescription and the AdS/CFT correspondence at the amplitude level.

Conclusion: The proposed zero-mode prescription is consistent, BRST-invariant, and physically correct for AdS₅×S⁵; it establishes the pure spinor formalism as a viable tool for first-principles holographic amplitude calculations and removes a fundamental technical barrier in the field.

Abstract: We formulate a BRST-invariant prescription for the integration of zero modes in the pure spinor superstring on $AdS_5\times S^5$. The measure includes contributions absent in flat space and admits a description in terms of picture changing operators or a non-minimal regulator. The prescription is valid for genus-zero amplitudes with arbitrary multiplicity and external states and removes a central obstacle to amplitude computations in this background. We use it to perform the first direct worldsheet computation of two and three-point amplitudes of half-BPS supergravity states. The properly normalised three-point amplitudes exactly reproduce the results of planar ${\mathcal N}=4$ super Yang-Mills theory.

</details>


### [121] [Quadratic-in-spin scattering of compact bodies at fourth post-Minkowskian order](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29670)
*Taushif Ahmed,Syed Mehedi Hasan*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the fourth-post-Minkowskian scattering angle for spinning compact binaries including quadratic-in-spin effects, spin-induced quadrupoles, radiation reaction, and curvature-squared tidal terms; results are expressed in polylogarithms and elliptic integrals, with exact infrared cancellation fixing the tail logarithm and yielding new PN coefficients and angular-momentum loss predictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend high-precision gravitational scattering calculations to higher post-Minkowskian order and include realistic spin effects (quadratic spin, spin-induced quadrupoles, radiation reaction, and spin-tidal couplings) for modeling binary black hole dynamics and gravitational-wave emission.

Method: Three-loop worldline quantum field theory formalism applied to spinning extended bodies; analytic evaluation of worldline integrals yielding closed-form expressions in polylogarithms and elliptic integrals; systematic treatment of infrared divergences and tail effects.

Result: Exact fourth-post-Minkowskian scattering angle for aligned spins through quadratic spin; explicit cancellation of infrared poles; determination of the tail logarithm; derivation of a new post-Newtonian coefficient and third-order angular-momentum flux for general quadrupolar bodies.

Conclusion: The inclusion of curvature-squared spin-tidal terms and radiation reaction significantly modifies resummed scattering predictions for black holes, improving theoretical accuracy for gravitational-wave modeling and strong-field tests of general relativity.

Abstract: We present the fourth-post-Minkowskian scattering angle of aligned spinning compact bodies through quadratic spin at arbitrary velocity and mass ratio. We include spin-induced quadrupoles and radiation reaction, supplemented by curvature-squared spin-tidal terms. Three-loop worldline integrals yield closed forms in polylogarithms and elliptic integrals. Infrared poles cancel exactly, fixing the tail logarithm. We obtain a new post-Newtonian coefficient and third-order angular-momentum loss for general quadrupoles. The new input shifts resummed predictions for black-hole scattering.

</details>


### [122] [A note on Holographic Renormalization for Polarized IKKT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29677)
*Matteo Guglielmetti*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a boundary counterterm for the on-shell action of type IIB supergravity dual to polarized IKKT matrix model vacua, using a variational principle with asymptotically constrained off-shell fluctuations; renormalization yields agreement with the matrix model upon fixing D1-brane charge via RR 2-form dualization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To consistently define and renormalize the on-shell gravitational action for type IIB supergravity solutions dual to vacua of the polarized IKKT matrix model—enabling precise holographic comparison with matrix model predictions.

Method: Constructs a boundary counterterm guided by the variational principle, imposing fall-off conditions on off-shell fluctuations dictated by asymptotic equations of motion; implements fixed D1-brane charge off-shell via Hodge dualization of the RR two-form potential.

Result: The renormalized on-shell action for supersymmetric saddles matches the IKKT matrix model prediction when evaluated in the ensemble of fixed D1-brane charge.

Conclusion: Boundary counterterm renormalization successfully bridges the type IIB supergravity description and the polarized IKKT matrix model at the level of the on-shell action, validating the holographic duality for this class of vacua.

Abstract: We construct a boundary counterterm for the on-shell action of type IIB supergravity solutions dual to vacua of the polarized IKKT matrix model. The construction is based on the variational principle, with off-shell fluctuations whose fall-off conditions are based on the asymptotic equations of motion. The renormalized on-shell action of the supersymmetric saddles reproduces the matrix- model result once we pass to the ensemble of fixed D1-brane charges, which we implement off-shell by dualizing the RR two-form potential.

</details>


### [123] [Bounds on entanglement fluctuations at holographic interfaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29699)
*Pallab Basu,Vishnu Jejjala*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates the minimal possible entanglement fluctuations at fixed entropy in holographic conformal interfaces with a scalar field, deriving rigorous bounds relating effective central charge (c_eff), capacity (c_cap), and left-right central charge (c_LR); it shows that unbounded capacity can be achieved at finite entropy via a long Janus throat while keeping the scalar response gapped.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the fundamental lower bound on entanglement fluctuations at fixed entropy in holographic systems, particularly for conformal interfaces with scalar field support.

Method: Analytical holographic computation using gravitational backreaction of a scalar field with potential; derivation of inequalities via Einstein equation constraints and analysis of Janus throat geometry.

Result: Proves inequality c_eff ≤ c_cap ≤ c_eff²/c_LR; identifies transport-dominated gravitational response with bounded scalar subtraction; demonstrates existence of long Janus throat configurations enabling unbounded c_cap at finite c_eff while maintaining gapped scalar spectrum.

Conclusion: Entanglement fluctuations can be made arbitrarily small relative to entropy in certain holographic interface geometries, constrained only by universal central charge relations and gravitational consistency.

Abstract: How small can entanglement fluctuations be at fixed entropy? For a class of holographic conformal interfaces supported by a scalar with a potential, we prove $c_\text{eff}\leq c_\text{cap}\leq c_\text{eff}^2/c_{LR}$, relating entropy, capacity, and energy transmission. The coupled gravitational response is a transport term minus a positive scalar contribution; the background Einstein equation bounds that subtraction. A long Janus throat allows unbounded capacity at a finite entropy coefficient while the scalar response remains gapped.

</details>


### [124] [Lie Algebra Cohomology and Seiberg-Witten Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29713)
*Ahsan Z. Khan*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the algebra of perturbative holomorphic-topological local operators in pure N=2 SU(2) gauge theory, confirming a conjecture: it is a Poisson vertex algebra generated by a Virasoro element X (c=0) and an odd primary Y (weight 3), with relation X²=0; equivalently, it computes relative Lie algebra cohomology and self-Ext groups in the coherent Satake category via factorization, depth arguments, and two deformations of C[z,w]/(w²).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To prove a conjecture about the structure of the perturbative holomorphic-topological local operator algebra in pure N=2 SU(2) gauge theory, linking it to Poisson vertex algebras, Lie algebra cohomology, and geometric representation theory (coherent Satake category).

Method: Uses factorization on configuration spaces of points on the affine line, a depth argument, and two controlled deformations of the coefficient algebra C[z,w]/(w²); combines techniques from quantum field theory, vertex algebras, Lie algebra cohomology, and geometric Satake.

Result: The perturbative holomorphic-topological local operator algebra is identified as the Poisson vertex algebra generated by Virasoro element X (central charge 0) and odd primary Y (weight 3), subject only to X² = 0; equivalently, H*(sl₂[z,w]/(w²), sl₂) and self-Ext groups of the unit in the coherent Satake categories for SL₂ and PGL₂ are computed.

Conclusion: The conjectured algebraic structure is rigorously established, unifying physical, algebraic, and geometric perspectives—specifically connecting N=2 gauge theory to Poisson vertex algebras and the coherent Satake category via explicit cohomological computations.

Abstract: We compute the algebra of perturbative holomorphic-topological local operators of pure $\mathcal N=2$ gauge theory with gauge group $SU(2)$, proving a conjecture of the author: it is the Poisson vertex algebra generated by a Virasoro element $X$ of central charge zero and an odd primary $Y$ of weight three, subject only to $X^2=0$. Equivalently, we compute the relative Lie algebra cohomology $H^*(\mathfrak{sl}_2[z,w]/(w^2),\mathfrak{sl}_2)$ and the self-Ext groups of the unit in the coherent Satake category for $SL_2$ and $PGL_2$. The proof uses factorization on configuration spaces of points on the affine line, a depth argument, and two deformations of the coefficient algebra $\mathbb C[z,w]/(w^2)$.

</details>


### [125] [Boundary spectroscopy of hidden near-horizon reflection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30092)
*Yiling Chen,Jiayue Yang,Zhen Zhong,Robert B. Mann,Jie Jiang,Ming Zhang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives an exact boundary response for a corotating passive wall near the horizon of a rotating BTZ black hole for a scalar field, showing that the wall's impedance and position are orthogonally encoded in the spectral response via an Airy filter; positive energy ensures stability, and the boundary perceives reflection—contrasting with exponential hiding of perfect absorption.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe how deviations from perfect absorption at black hole horizons manifest at the boundary, especially in the context of holography and quantum gravity, using a controlled 2+1D setting (rotating BTZ spacetime) with a scalar field and a passive near-horizon wall.

Method: Exact analytical solution of the scalar wave equation in rotating BTZ exterior; derivation of boundary response function for a corotating Dirichlet/impedance wall near the horizon; spectral analysis using Airy function asymptotics; orthogonality analysis of parameter dependence in frequency-domain response.

Result: The boundary response is governed by an Airy filter; wall impedance and radial position are orthogonal parameters in the spectral decomposition, each weighted by the spectral function; no unstable (growing) modes exist due to positive-energy constraint; perfect absorption is exponentially suppressed relative to reflective response.

Conclusion: A passive near-horizon wall is detectable at the boundary via its spectral imprint—demonstrating that horizon 'absorption' is not generically hidden, and boundary observables can resolve near-horizon structure, challenging strict black hole complementarity in this setting.

Abstract: A classical black hole horizon absorbs completely, and any deviation would be hidden in the bulk. For a scalar field in a rotating Bañados-Teitelboim-Zanelli exterior, we derive an exact boundary response of a corotating passive wall near the horizon. An Airy filter gives its spectrum, and positive energy excludes growing modes. On any frequency band the response identifies a constant wall impedance and the wall position, which enter orthogonally with weights determined by the spectral function. The boundary thus sees a reflecting wall, whereas a perfect absorber is exponentially hidden.

</details>


### [126] [Charged spin-$\frac32$ in a chiral Feshbach--Villars model: hyperbolic propagation, Landau-level instability, and exceptional points](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30225)
*Haryanto M. Siahaan*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes a Pauli-coupled second-order wave equation for spin-3/2 fields, revealing spectral reality conditions tied to the gyromagnetic ratio g and magnetic field strength; it identifies exceptional points, loss of positive-definite conserved forms, and first-order spectral instabilities under electrostatic perturbations—without asserting fermionic quantization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine conditions under which the spectrum of a spin-3/2 relativistic wave equation remains real in electromagnetic backgrounds, distinguishing spectral instability from loss of hyperbolicity, and probing the role of the free gyromagnetic ratio g in defining physical consistency.

Method: Analyzes a sixteen-component pseudo-Hermitian Hamiltonian (Feshbach–Villars form) derived from a Pauli-coupled second-order wave equation on the (3/2,0)⊕(0,3/2) representation; employs covariant characteristic analysis, Landau quantization, exceptional point diagnostics, and first-order perturbation theory on degenerate spin manifolds with Gaussian-damped electrostatic potentials.

Result: Exact spin-j Landau spectrum is real iff |g|j ≤ 1; at g=2, j=3/2 exhibits a second-order exceptional point and loss of time-independent positive-definite conserved quadratic form at |qB|=m²/2; nonreal eigenvalues emerge for m²/6 < |qB| < m²/2 under small electrostatic perturbations on the lowest spin-3/2 manifold, except at a special well range; g=2 spin-1/2 limit recovers Dirac spectral reality, while g=1/j eliminates first-order imaginary corrections.

Conclusion: Spectral reality is governed by a sharp g–j–B threshold, not by hyperbolicity alone; the model exhibits controlled non-Hermitian spectral features (exceptional points, perturbative instabilities) without implying physical fermionic quantization.

Abstract: We study a Pauli-coupled second-order wave equation on $(\frac32,0)\oplus(0,\frac32)$ with a free gyromagnetic ratio $g$. Its Feshbach--Villars form is a sixteen-component pseudo-Hermitian Hamiltonian system with lightlike covariant characteristics in smooth electromagnetic backgrounds. The solution space is chirality doubled; a covariant physical single-species reduction is not assumed. The exact spin-$j$ Landau spectrum remains real at every field strength precisely when $|g|j\le1$. At $g=2$, the critical spin-$\frac32$ channel reaches zero at $|qB|=m^2/2$, where each chiral block develops a second-order exceptional point. This threshold also marks the loss of a time-independent positive-definite conserved quadratic form for the channel. We also include a bounded, Gaussian-damped harmonic electrostatic potential and its associated electric field. The complete first-order problem on a degenerate manifold reduces to $V\pm iC$, with $V$ and $C$ the projected potential and spin--electric coupling. On the lowest two-state spin-$\frac32$ manifold, nonreal eigenvalue branches arise for $m^2/6<|qB|<m^2/2$ at sufficiently small nonzero perturbation amplitude, except at a special well range where the first-order splitting vanishes. The spin-$\frac12$, $g=2$ limit satisfies the Dirac spectral-reality check, whereas $g=1/j$ has no imaginary first-order correction for this perturbation. These results separate spectral instability from loss of hyperbolicity; they do not establish a physical fermionic quantization.

</details>


### [127] [Matter-decorated trumpets in double-scaled SYK](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30296)
*Matteo Beccaria*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs matter-decorated wormholes in sine-dilaton gravity beyond the Jackiw–Teitelboim (JT) limit by leveraging the double-scaled SYK (DSSYK) model as its boundary dual; it defines matter-decorated trumpets via end-of-the-world branes with matter insertions, derives exact gluing weights accounting for chord topology (twists, windings, loops), expresses trumpets in closed form using Rogers polynomial transforms, and recovers known JT and classical bulk results as limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the construction of matter-carrying wormholes beyond the JT limit—where only approximate or limiting gluing rules were known—by exploiting the exact solvability of double-scaled SYK as a controlled boundary dual to sine-dilaton gravity.

Method: Uses DSSYK as the boundary theory; defines matter-decorated trumpets via end-of-the-world brane traces with Gaussian matter insertions; computes the ETH two-matrix model partition function; derives exact sector-by-sector gluing weights from chord diagram combinatorics (accounting for twists, windings, and neck-loop contributions); obtains closed-form expressions for trumpets via Rogers polynomial transforms.

Result: Exact, positive-weight gluing of matter-decorated double trumpets in sine-dilaton gravity beyond JT; closed-form trumpet amplitudes as Rogers polynomial transforms; matching with known JT results both individually and collectively; correct classical bulk interpretation (pure-gravity trumpet × probe geodesic boundary factor).

Conclusion: The construction is unique under the given DSSYK/ETH two-matrix framework and provides the first complete, non-perturbative definition of matter-decorated wormholes in 2D dilaton gravity beyond JT, bridging boundary matrix models and bulk semiclassical geometry with matter.

Abstract: In sine-dilaton gravity, the double trumpet, the wormhole amplitude without matter, is obtained by gluing two trumpets along a closed geodesic of discrete length and summing over this length. When the wormhole carries the propagator of a matter field of dimension $\Delta$, joining the two asymptotic boundaries and crossing the neck, such a gluing is known only in the Jackiw--Teitelboim (JT) limit. We construct the gluing beyond JT in double-scaled SYK (DSSYK), the proposed boundary dual of sine-dilaton gravity, whose disk amplitudes it reproduces. In this context, we adopt the definition of the matter-decorated wormhole in terms of the ETH two-matrix model with Gaussian matter. We build matter-decorated trumpets from an end-of-the-world brane trace with a matter insertion, and prove that the wormhole is glued from them exactly, sector by sector in the number of chords through the neck, with a positive weight that accounts for the twists and windings of these chords and for the closed loops around the neck. Trumpets and weight are obtained in closed form, the former as transforms of Rogers polynomials, and we discuss the uniqueness of the construction. In the JT limit, both reproduce the known gluing separately, and not only in combination. In the classical limit, each trumpet takes the form expected in the bulk: a pure-gravity sine-dilaton trumpet dressed by the factor that a probe matter geodesic contributes at its boundary.

</details>


### [128] [Rotating Black Holes from Scaling Transformations in Horava Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30337)
*Deniz O. Devecioglu,Mu-In Park*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a scaling transformation to generate rotating black-hole solutions in low-energy Hořava gravity from known relativistic black holes, yielding 'Doran-like' and 'Kerr-Schild-like' solutions; both are horizon-penetrating with non-Killing null horizons, differ in singularity structure, but preserve chronology.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct physically viable rotating black-hole solutions in low-energy Hořava gravity—a candidate theory of quantum gravity that breaks Lorentz invariance at high energies—by leveraging known relativistic solutions via symmetry-inspired scaling.

Method: Derives a specific scaling transformation acting on spacetime metrics and coupling parameters of low-energy Hořava gravity; applies it to known relativistic rotating black-hole metrics (e.g., Kerr) to obtain new exact solutions in Hořava gravity; analyzes causal structure, horizon geometry (null vs. Killing), and singularity properties using coordinate-invariant techniques.

Result: Two new rotating black-hole solutions ('Doran-like' and 'Kerr-Schild-like') are constructed; both admit regular, horizon-penetrating coordinates; their null horizons fail to be Killing horizons due to broken diffeomorphism invariance; singularities differ (e.g., curvature behavior and location), yet closed timelike curves are absent in both.

Conclusion: Scaling transformations provide an effective tool for generating physically consistent rotating black-hole solutions in low-energy Hořava gravity; the resulting solutions respect causality (chronology protection) despite Lorentz violation, supporting the viability of Hořava gravity as an infrared completion.

Abstract: We study the scaling transformations that change the physical parameters of solutions in low-energy Horava gravity. In particular, we present a simple scaling transformation that generates rotating black-hole solutions in low-energy Horava gravity from known relativistic solutions. As explicit examples, we obtain "Doran-like" and "Kerr-Schild-like" rotating solutions in Horava gravity. We find that both solutions are horizon-penetrating, but their null horizons are not Killing horizons. We also find that, although their singularity structures differ from each other, chronology is protected in both solutions, as in the previously obtained Kerr-like solutions in Boyer-Lindquist coordinates.

</details>


### [129] [Dirichlet walls beyond JT gravity and their quantum group interpretation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30567)
*Luca Griguolo,Jacopo Papalini,Lorenzo Russo,Domenico Seminara,Alex Tarana*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper canonically quantizes sinh dilaton gravity with finite Dirichlet boundary conditions, yielding a finite-difference Schrödinger equation solved by q-hypergeometric wavefunctions—quantum deformations of JT gravity’s hypergeometric solutions—and identifies the cutoff Hamiltonian as the reduced Casimir of the quantum group $U_q(\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{R}))$, with scattering states given by modular double matrix elements.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend Jackiw–Teitelboim (JT) gravity by incorporating quantum group structure and finite cutoff effects via sinh dilaton gravity, bridging holography, integrability, and quantum deformation.

Method: Canonical quantization of sinh dilaton gravity with finite Dirichlet boundaries; reduction to effective AdS$_2$ phase space parameterized by geodesic length and conjugate momentum; derivation of finite-difference Schrödinger equation; identification of wavefunctions as $q$-hypergeometric functions; interpretation of Hamiltonian as reduced Casimir of $U_q(\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{R}))$; use of modular double representation theory for scattering states.

Result: Wavefunctions of the universe are $q$-hypergeometric functions satisfying a finite-difference Schrödinger equation; the finite-cutoff Hamiltonian equals the reduced Casimir of $U_q(\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{R}))$; two scattering solutions correspond to hyperbolic matrix elements of the modular double $U_q(\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{R})) \otimes U_{\tilde q}(\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{R}))$; the physical wavefunction is the unique regular combination at zero geodesic length.

Conclusion: Sinusoidal dilaton gravity at finite cutoff realizes a quantum-group-deformed version of JT gravity, where geometry, symmetry, and quantization are unified via $q$-special functions and modular double representation theory—providing a concrete example of quantum gravity with built-in UV cutoff and integrable structure.

Abstract: We canonically quantize sinh dilaton gravity with finite Dirichlet boundary conditions. The reduced phase space is formulated in terms of the length of a two-sided spacelike geodesic in an effective AdS$_2$ geometry and its conjugate momentum. Canonical quantization leads to a finite-difference Schrödinger equation whose wavefunctions of the universe are given by $q$-hypergeometric functions. These furnish a quantum deformation of the hypergeometric wavefunctions of JT gravity. We further show that the finite cutoff Hamiltonian derived from the bulk analysis admits a natural interpretation as the reduced Casimir of $U_q(\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{R}))$ acting on hyperbolic matrix elements. The two scattering wave solutions are hyperbolic matrix elements of the modular double $U_q(\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb R))\otimes U_{\tilde q}(\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb R))$, and the wavefunction of the universe is their unique combination regular at vanishing length.

</details>


### [130] [Conservative tidal effects to third post-Minkowskian order in scalar-tensor theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30762)
*Zhengwen Liu,Hongbin Wang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes finite-size tidal effects in scalar-tensor gravity with scalar-Gauss-Bonnet coupling up to 3PM order using worldline EFT, covering scalar and gravitational multipole responses; it reproduces known GR and PN results and confirms leading-order mass-ratio behavior.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically incorporate conservative finite-size (tidal) effects in scalar-tensor theories—especially with scalar-Gauss-Bonnet coupling—beyond post-Newtonian approximations and into the post-Minkowskian regime, where velocity dependence and relativistic corrections are fully retained.

Method: Worldline effective field theory (EFT) applied to nonspinning compact binaries in massless scalar-tensor gravity with scalar-Gauss-Bonnet coupling; computation of impulse and scattering angle through third post-Minkowskian (3PM) order; full velocity dependence retained at linear order in tidal response coefficients; inclusion of scalar dipole/quadrupole and electric/magnetic/mixed gravitational quadrupole/octupole couplings.

Result: Finite-size contributions to impulse and scattering angle at 3PM; scalar dipole enters dynamics at 3PN (two orders earlier than GR quadrupole); 3PM scattering angle depends on first scalar derivatives of tidal responses and contains linear Gauss-Bonnet–dependent terms; consistency verified with GR tidal angle and PN dynamics up to 5PN; test-body limit confirmed including Gauss-Bonnet terms for zero-sensitivity companion.

Conclusion: Worldline EFT successfully captures velocity-resolved, conservative tidal dynamics in scalar-Gauss-Bonnet gravity through 3PM; the framework unifies and extends known PN results, reveals novel early-order scalar-dipole effects, and provides a robust foundation for modeling exotic compact binaries in modified gravity.

Abstract: Using worldline effective field theory, we compute the conservative finite-size contributions to the impulse and scattering angle of nonspinning compact binaries in massless scalar-tensor theories with a scalar-Gauss-Bonnet coupling through third post-Minkowskian (3PM) order. We retain the full velocity dependence at linear order in the tidal response coefficients. The calculation covers the scalar dipole and quadrupole and the electric, magnetic, and mixed quadrupoles and octupoles. Among them, the scalar dipole enters the dynamics already at third post-Newtonian (3PN) order, two orders before the gravitational quadrupole. At 3PM, the tidal correction to the angle also depends on the first scalar derivatives of the responses and contains terms linear in the Gauss-Bonnet coupling. The results reproduce the general-relativistic tidal angle and the post-Newtonian tidal dynamics through 5PN, and a test-body calculation confirms their leading order in the mass ratio, including the Gauss-Bonnet terms for a companion with vanishing sensitivity.

</details>


### [131] [Near-boundary and near-horizon momentum--Krylov response in noncommutative-inspired BTZ spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31106)
*F. A. Brito,F. G. Costa,J. A. V. Campos*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes momentum-Krylov complexity growth for a massive probe falling into a Lorentzian-smeared, noncommutative-inspired BTZ black hole, showing that smearing breaks simple rescaling of the BTZ complexity curve—early-time growth and late-time exponential rate depend differently on the smearing parameter; horizon radius and surface gravity vanish at critical smearing with distinct exponents; boundary stress tensor remains BTZ-like, but bulk geometric probes (Fefferman–Graham coefficient, geodesic lengths) deviate.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how quantum-gravity-inspired noncommutative deformations (via Lorentzian smearing) affect holographic complexity growth, particularly whether such deformations merely rescale BTZ physics or introduce qualitatively new features in the momentum-Krylov complexity evolution.

Method: Computes proper radial momentum of a massive infalling probe in a smeared noncommutative BTZ black hole background; uses it as the growth rate of momentum--Krylov complexity; analytically derives early- and late-time asymptotics of complexity; compares with exact BTZ limit and numerically validates deviations; analyzes critical smearing behavior, horizon/surface gravity scaling, and boundary-bulk consistency via Fefferman–Graham expansion and geodesic lengths.

Result: Smeared BTZ complexity growth is not a rescaled BTZ curve: early growth coefficient is F'_α(X)/4, late-time exponent is F'_α(x_+)/2 — differing smearing dependence; distant release recovers BTZ early response but not late-time rate; Gaussian profile yields identical qualitative behavior; horizon radius ∝ α^{1/2}, surface gravity ∝ α^{3/2} vanish at critical smearing α_c; boundary stress tensor matches BTZ, but z^3 Fefferman–Graham coefficient and boundary-anchored geodesic lengths differ.

Conclusion: Noncommutative smearing introduces intrinsic scale-dependent deviations from BTZ complexity dynamics that cannot be absorbed by parameter rescaling; the breakdown of universality between early and late-time regimes signals genuine departure from classical black hole geometry, detectable in bulk observables despite boundary stress-tensor equivalence.

Abstract: We compute the proper radial momentum of a massive probe falling into a Lorentzian-smeared, noncommutative-inspired BTZ black hole at fixed asymptotic mass, and use it as the growth rate of momentum--Krylov complexity. In the BTZ limit the accumulated complexity is $\mathcal{K}_0 = X(\cosh\tau - 1)$. With smearing, the initial growth coefficient is $F'_\alpha(X)/4$, while the late-time exponent is the surface gravity $F'_\alpha(x_+)/2$. The two depend differently on the smearing parameter, so the deformed response is not a rescaled BTZ curve; we show this analytically and confirm it numerically. For a distant release point the early response approaches BTZ, whereas the late-time rate does not. A BTZ black hole with the same Hawking temperature reproduces the response to within about ten percent, and the remaining difference is set by the early/late mismatch. A Gaussian mass profile gives the same picture. The horizon radius and the surface gravity vanish at the critical smearing with exponents $1/2$ and $3/2$. At fixed mass the boundary stress tensor coincides with that of BTZ, but the $z^3$ Fefferman--Graham coefficient and the lengths of boundary-anchored geodesics do not.

</details>


### [132] [Electromagnetic Memory as a Collider Observable](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31107)
*Felipe Diaz,Hernán A. González,Oriana Labrin,Jakob Salzer*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a Ward identity linking electromagnetic memory and charge correlators as inclusive observables, computes massless multipoint and massive two-point memory correlators for lepton pairs, and shows their finiteness at first radiative order with recoil-induced Sudakov logarithms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify soft radiation (memory) and hard charge measurements via large gauge symmetry, addressing infrared structure of QED and celestial holography.

Method: Application of large gauge symmetry Ward identities; computation of electromagnetic memory correlators for lepton pair production using QED perturbation theory; celestial free-boson representation; analysis of infrared behavior at first radiative order.

Result: Exact massless n-point memory correlators and massive two-point memory correlator derived; coincident limit yields soft photon number; all correlators finite at O(α) with back-to-back Sudakov logarithms from recoil.

Conclusion: Electromagnetic memory and charge correlators are intrinsically related through large gauge symmetry as inclusive observables; the celestial free-boson description is consistent and infrared-finite at leading radiative order.

Abstract: Electromagnetic memory and charge correlators are measured by different detectors, sensitive to soft radiation and to hard charged tracks. We show that the Ward identity of large gauge symmetry relates these quantities as inclusive observables. For lepton pairs produced by an electromagnetic current, we compute all massless multipoint memory correlators and the massive two-point function, whose coincident limit gives the soft photon number. The correlators admit a celestial free-boson representation and remain finite at first radiative order, where recoil generates back-to-back Sudakov logarithms.

</details>


### [133] [Massive de Sitter correlators at one loop](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31214)
*Amara McCune*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes exact squeezed-limit coefficients for loop-induced primordial correlators from fermionic and massive symmetric fields during inflation, revealing significant (10–100×) enhancements over standard late-time approximations—especially with chemical potentials—and characterizes phase drift away from the squeezed limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Late-time estimates for loop signals in primordial correlators (e.g., from fermions or massive fields coupled to the inflaton) are widely used but lack accuracy; the paper aims to compute exact squeezed coefficients and corrections using a rigorous Mellin-Barnes representation of de Sitter correlators.

Method: Uses finite Mellin-Barnes integral representations of massive de Sitter correlators to derive exact analytic expressions for squeezed-limit bispectrum coefficients and their off-squeezed corrections; separates contributions into hard (loop) and soft (vertex) parts, computing both exactly—not just the hard part as in late-time approximations.

Result: Exact squeezed coefficients differ from late-time estimates by universal mass- and coupling-dependent factors: 10–30× larger for heavy SM loops without chemical potential; up to ~30× for helical vectors and ~100× for fermion triangle with chemical potential. Phase drift dominates deviation away from squeezed limit, derived explicitly for scalar and fermion loops.

Conclusion: Late-time approximations systematically underestimate loop-induced non-Gaussianity in primordial correlators; exact computation is essential for accurate phenomenological forecasts, particularly in scenarios with chemical potentials or heavy fields.

Abstract: Fermions and massive fields charged under an unbroken symmetry couple to the inflaton only in pairs, so their signals in primordial correlators begin at one loop. Templates for these signals are mostly late-time estimates. Starting from a representation of massive de Sitter correlators as finite Mellin-Barnes integrals, we compute the exact squeezed coefficients of these signals and their corrections away from the squeezed limit. The leading coefficient factorizes into a hard part and a soft vertex. Late-time estimates treat the hard part exactly but approximate the soft vertex by its late-time limit, whereas we compute both exactly. Without a chemical potential, the exact coefficient differs from the late-time one by a universal factor that depends only on the masses in the loop and the coupling at the soft vertex. For the heavy Standard Model loops, the exact signals are ten to thirty times larger than their late-time estimates. With a chemical potential, the ratio depends on the model, and at our benchmarks it reaches a factor of thirty for helical vectors and about a hundred for the fermion triangle, where we agree with recent exact results. Away from the squeezed limit the leading term soon fails, mainly through a drift in the phase of the oscillation, which we derive for scalar and fermion loops.

</details>


### [134] [Nonabelianization map for the $W_{3,c=2}$ conformal blocks. Part I](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31232)
*Qianyu Hao*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper generalizes the nonabelianization construction from Virasoro (c=1) to W₃ (c=2) conformal blocks, expressing them via Heisenberg blocks on a branched triple cover using gl₃ spectral networks and soliton degeneracies; it provides a concrete check for (123)-type branch points yielding correct two-point correlators and τ-functions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the nonabelianization framework—previously established for Virasoro conformal blocks—to the richer algebraic structure of the W₃-algebra, enabling new geometric and representation-theoretic insights into higher-rank CFTs on Riemann surfaces.

Method: Constructs a generalized nonabelianization map that lifts W₃ conformal blocks on a Riemann surface C to Heisenberg blocks on a branched triple cover \tilde{C}, incorporating \mathfrak{gl}_3 spectral network data—including soliton degeneracies—and fermionic correlation functions; validates the map on a minimal nontrivial case with two (123)-type branch points.

Result: Derives novel formulas for c=2 W₃ conformal blocks in terms of fermion correlators governed by Heisenberg blocks on \tilde{C}; explicitly verifies consistency for a two-point correlator and associated τ-function in the presence of (123) branch points.

Conclusion: The nonabelianization map successfully generalizes to W₃-algebra blocks, revealing an essential role for soliton degeneracies absent in the Virasoro case; this establishes a spectral-network-based geometric encoding of W₃ CFT data and opens avenues for higher-rank generalizations and exact computations in 2D CFT.

Abstract: This work is inspired by the nonabelianization construction of Virasoro conformal blocks. We start the process of defining the nonabelianization map which expresses $c=2$ $W_3$-algebra conformal blocks on a Riemann surface $C$ from Heisenberg blocks on a branched triple cover $\tilde{C}$ of $C$. This generalized nonabelianization map uses the datum of a $\mathfrak{gl}_3$ spectral network on $C$ and we find that this map also depends on the soliton degeneracies corresponding to the spectral network even though the soliton data didn't show up explicitly in the Virasoro case. This nonabelianization construction defines new formulas for $W_3$-algebra conformal blocks on $C$ in terms of fermion correlation functions determined by the Heisenberg blocks on $\tilde{C}$. We provide a check of the proposed construction associated to the more challenging $(123)$ type branch points which start to show up in three-fold covers. To be more precise, the simplest example that we focus on has only two of the $(123)$ type branch points and we show that in this example, our nonabelianization construction gives the desired two-point correlator and $\tau$-function.

</details>


<div id='hep-lat'></div>

# hep-lat [[Back]](#toc)

### [135] [Fermionic 2+1D SU(2) quantum link models: an ED/DMRG reliability study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28590)
*Daming Li*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper benchmarks sparse exact diagonalization (ED) and matrix-product-state DMRG for simulating a 2+1D SU(2) quantum link model with dynamical fermions on small lattices, validating correctness to high precision (up to 10⁻¹³) and identifying constraints—especially colour neutrality—governing accuracy in tensor-network simulations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Controlled reliability studies of non-Abelian lattice gauge theories with dynamical fermions are scarce, hindering confidence in tensor-network and quantum-simulation results; this work fills that gap via rigorous small-system benchmarks.

Method: Two complementary Hilbert-space methods are employed: (i) sparse exact diagonalization with explicit Jordan–Wigner strings for fermionic statistics, and (ii) fermion-number-conserving DMRG using a local MPO representation; both are validated against Gauss law, Hermiticity, pure-gauge limit, and algebraic consistency; fPEPS with exact Gauss-law enforcement serves as independent reference for select sectors.

Result: ED and DMRG agree to 10⁻¹³ on 2×2 lattices and remain controlled at ≤10⁻⁵ on 2×3–2×5 ladders; on 3×3, colour neutrality dictates the minimal residual: G² = 3/4 is exact at half-filling (odd fermion count), while one hole/doublon restores neutrality and reduces residual to ~2×10⁻⁵ at bond dimension χ = 2048; fPEPS confirms DMRG accuracy in even-filling 3×2 sector.

Conclusion: The study establishes a reproducible reliability envelope for non-Abelian gauge-fermion tensor-network simulations, revealing that colour neutrality and bond dimension scaling—not just lattice size—are critical determinants of accuracy, guiding design of larger-scale simulations.

Abstract: Non-Abelian lattice gauge theory with dynamical fermions is a central target for tensor-network and quantum-simulation methods, but controlled reliability studies on small systems are scarce. We benchmark two complementary Hilbert-space approaches for a 2+1D SU(2) quantum link model with one staggered fermion flavour on small two-dimensional lattices: sparse exact diagonalization (ED) and matrix-product-state density-matrix renormalization group (DMRG). The link variables use the five-state $\{5\}$ truncation, and fermions are represented by two-colour four-state sites. We implement ED with explicit cross-site Jordan--Wigner strings and DMRG with total-fermion-number conservation and a matrix product operator (MPO) built from local terms. We validate the link algebra, Gauss-law commutation, Hermiticity, the pure-gauge limit, and the MPO against ED component by component. The $2\times2$ methods agree to $10^{-13}$ and the $2\times3$--$2\times5$ ladders are controlled at or below the $10^{-5}$ level. The $3\times3$ geometry illustrates the role of colour neutrality: at half filling the odd fermion number makes $G^2=3/4$ the exact lower bound, while one hole or one doublon restores neutrality and lowers the residual to $\sim2\times10^{-5}$ at $\chi=2048$. An independent exact-Gauss fPEPS provides a reference for the even-filling $3\times2$ sector. The results define a reproducible reliability envelope for non-Abelian gauge-fermion benchmarks and show where larger-scale simulations require larger bond dimensions or different tensor-network geometries.

</details>


### [136] [Thermodynamics of QCD+QED from the lattice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29423)
*G. Endrődi,E. Garnacho-Velasco,G. Markó,L. Pethő*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper analyzes QCD+QED at finite temperature, highlighting non-perturbative electromagnetic effects, center symmetry, lattice simulation strategies, novel thermodynamic results, and algorithmic challenges for mapping the phase diagram.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: At finite temperature, QED coupling in QCD+QED may induce non-perturbative effects and alter the phase diagram due to exact center symmetry—unlike the well-understood perturbative zero-temperature regime.

Method: Lattice field theory simulations of QCD+QED at finite temperature; analysis of center symmetry, thermodynamic observables, and development of new algorithms to handle electromagnetic interactions.

Result: Novel lattice results on QCD+QED thermodynamics; identification of new features (e.g., modified confinement/deconfinement transitions) arising from electromagnetic coupling; quantification of algorithmic challenges in simulating the combined theory.

Conclusion: QCD+QED at finite temperature exhibits qualitatively distinct, non-perturbative behavior requiring dedicated lattice methodologies; full first-principles understanding of its phase diagram necessitates further algorithmic innovation.

Abstract: QED corrections to observables in QCD are by now well-understood at zero temperature, where QED effects are perturbative and required for precision physics observables, such as the neutron-proton mass difference or the muon anomalous magnetic moment, among many others. However, there are indications in the literature that at finite temperature the introduction of QED can lead to non-perturbative effects, affecting the structure of the phase diagram, since the combined theory is exactly center-symmetric. In this proceedings article, we analyze the structure of the combined theory and the different strategies to simulate it on the lattice, as well as presenting novel results of lattice QCD+QED simulations at finite temperature, focusing on understanding the role of the electromagnetic coupling in the thermodynamics of this system. We also discuss new features that arise in the combined theory and the challenges that these simulations entail, which require the development of new algorithms in order to understand the phase diagram of QCD+QED from first principles.

</details>


### [137] [Hadron spectroscopy of QC2D at finite density](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29900)
*Kei Iida,Etsuko Itou,Kotaro Murakami,and Daiki Suenaga*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper studies how hadron masses depend on chemical potential in two-color QCD at low temperature using lattice Monte Carlo simulations; it finds a reordering of meson masses across the superfluid transition, near-zero mass for the Nambu–Goldstone mode of baryon-number symmetry breaking, and evidence for partial chiral-symmetry restoration.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the behavior of hadron spectra under finite baryon density in a theoretically tractable QCD-like theory (two-color QCD), serving as a proxy for dense three-color QCD where sign problems hinder direct lattice simulation.

Method: Lattice Monte Carlo simulations on a $32^4$ lattice at $T \approx 40\,\mathrm{MeV}$; calculation of all meson and diquark correlation functions including disconnected diagrams; extrapolation to zero diquark source to isolate the Nambu–Goldstone mode; comparison of chiral partner correlators.

Result: In the hadronic phase, meson mass hierarchy resembles that of three-color QCD; across the superfluid transition, $\sigma$ and $a_1$ become lighter while pion mass rises with density; the $U(1)_B$ Nambu–Goldstone mode acquires nearly zero mass after zero-source extrapolation; chiral partners show degeneracy trends suggesting partial chiral-symmetry restoration.

Conclusion: Two-color QCD exhibits rich in-medium hadron phenomenology at finite density: superfluidity induces qualitative changes in meson spectroscopy, confirms spontaneous $U(1)_B$ breaking, and provides evidence for partial chiral-symmetry restoration—insights relevant to dense QCD matter.

Abstract: We investigate the chemical-potential dependence of hadron masses in two-color QCD (QC$_2$D) at low temperature using lattice Monte Carlo simulations. We calculate all possible meson and diquark correlation functions, including disconnected diagrams, on a $32^4$ lattice at $T\approx40\,\mathrm{MeV}$. In the hadronic phase, we obtain a meson mass hierarchy similar to that of three-color QCD. After the superfluid transition, the ordering changes: the $\sigma$ and $a_1$ channels become lighter, while the pion mass increases with density. We also examine the diquark source dependence of the Nambu--Goldstone mode of $U(1)_B$ symmetry breaking and find an almost vanishing mass in the superfluid phase after extrapolation to zero source. Finally, we compare correlation functions for chiral partners and discuss indications of partial chiral-symmetry restoration. This contribution is based on our recent study of hadron spectra in dense QC$_2$D~\cite{Iida:2026dmw}.

</details>


### [138] [Progress on finite temperature Sp(4) lattice gauge theory with matter fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30493)
*Alexis Verney-Provatas,Chris Allton,Ed Bennett,Luigi Del Debbio,Ryan C. Hill,Deog Ki Hong,Jong-Wan Lee,C.-J. David Lin,Biagio Lucini,Maurizio Piai,Davide Vadacchino*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper investigates the finite-temperature phase structure of Sp(4) lattice gauge theory with two fundamental Wilson fermions—motivated by its role as a composite dark matter candidate in the SIMP paradigm—and finds evidence for a crossover at light fermion masses and preliminary signs of a first-order transition at heavy masses, using Polyakov loop susceptibility scaling on small temporal lattices.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the nature (order and strength) of the thermal phase transition in Sp(4) gauge theory with two fundamental fermions, which is relevant for predicting an observable stochastic gravitational wave signal from the early universe and assessing viability as composite SIMP dark matter.

Method: Lattice Monte Carlo simulations across a 2D parameter space (bare fermion mass, inverse gauge coupling); analysis of finite-volume scaling of Polyakov loop susceptibility; use of multiple histogram reweighting to locate and characterize peaks in susceptibility as indicators of phase transition vs. crossover.

Result: In the light fermion mass regime, finite-volume scaling of susceptibility peak indicates a smooth crossover; in the heavy fermion mass regime, scaling behavior suggests preliminary evidence of a first-order phase transition.

Conclusion: The phase structure of Sp(4) gauge theory is mass-dependent, with implications for gravitational wave signatures and dark matter phenomenology; results justify further continuum-extrapolated studies of Sp(2N) theories.

Abstract: We explore the finite temperature behaviour of the Sp(4) lattice gauge theory coupled to two dynamical Wilson fermions transforming in the fundamental representation. The continuum theory provides a realisation of composite dark matter within the Strongly Interacting Massive Particle (SIMP) paradigm. Its thermal phase transition in the early universe might have generated an observable relic stochastic gravitational wave signal. Evaluating the sensitivity of future gravitational wave experiments to this signal requires knowledge of the strength of the phase transition. We explore the two dimensional parameter space of the theory, defined by the bare fermion mass and inverse gauge coupling. For this exploratory study we focus on lattices with a small number of sites in the temporal dimension. We analyse the finite volume scaling behaviour of Polyakov loop observables. Specifically the scaling of the peak of the susceptibility, informed by multiple histogram reweighting, is used to discriminate between the presence of a phase transition or crossover. In the light fermion mass regime we find strong indications of a crossover, while for heavy fermions we find preliminary evidence of a first-order phase transition. These findings motivate further studies of the continuum limit for this and other Sp(2N) gauge theories.

</details>


### [139] [Identifying the conformal window by chiral phase boundaries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30669)
*Jan Philipp Klinger,Reinhold Kaiser,Owe Philipsen,Jonas Schaible*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper investigates the conformal window in many-flavour QCD by studying thermal chiral transitions using unimproved staggered fermions; it finds evidence that N_f=7 exhibits a continuum chiral transition (QCD-like), while N_f=8 remains chirally symmetric in the chiral-continuum limit, consistent with conformal hyperscaling — implying 7 < N_f^* ≤ 8.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To distinguish between QCD-like behaviour and conformal behaviour in many-flavour QCD, particularly whether thermal chiral crossovers persist in the continuum chiral limit — a key signature separating theories with spontaneous chiral symmetry breaking from those inside the conformal window.

Method: Numerical lattice QCD simulations with unimproved staggered fermions; mapping of chiral phase boundaries in the bare parameter space (N_τ, β, am, N_f); comparison of N_f = 7 and N_f = 8; analysis of pseudo-critical surface scaling against conformal hyperscaling predictions.

Result: For N_f = 7, the thermal chiral transition persists in the continuum chiral limit; for N_f = 8, no such transition is observed — the massless continuum theory remains chirally symmetric. The N_f = 8 pseudo-critical surface obeys conformal hyperscaling, supporting its placement inside the conformal window.

Conclusion: The critical number of flavours N_f^* lies strictly between 7 and 8, i.e., 7 < N_f^* ≤ 8; eight-flavour QCD is conformal, while seven-flavour QCD retains QCD-like chiral dynamics.

Abstract: Once the number of massless fermion flavours reaches a critical value $N_f^*$, many-flavour QCD enters the conformal window, where chiral symmetry remains unbroken in the vacuum. At non-zero quark mass, chiral symmetry is explicitly broken, and thermal chiral crossovers can also occur for $N_f\geq N_f^*$. Their presence alone therefore does not distinguish a QCD-like theory from a mass-deformed conformal theory. They differ, however, in whether a thermal transition persists in the continuum chiral limit. To address this, we map out the chiral phase boundaries of unimproved staggered fermions in the bare lattice parameter space $(N_\tau,\beta,am,N_f)$, comparing $N_f=7$ and $N_f=8$. Our results indicate that the thermal chiral transition persists in the continuum chiral limit for $N_f=7$, whereas the massless continuum theory with $N_f=8$ remains chirally symmetric in the vacuum. Furthermore, we demonstrate that the pseudo-critical surface for eight flavours observed for finite quark masses is consistent with conformal hyperscaling, providing further evidence that eight-flavour QCD lies within the conformal window and suggesting $7<N_f^*\leq8$.

</details>


### [140] [Leading-order dynamical QED corrections to the thermodynamics of isospin-asymmetric QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31087)
*Gergely Endrődi,Ádám Fischer,Wei-Ping Huang,Gergely Markó*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper computes the leading-order effect of dynamical photons on QCD thermodynamics at nonzero isospin chemical potential μ_I, using a perturbative expansion in e around e=0 to circumvent the sign problem; it measures ∂²f/∂e²|ₑ₌₀ via electromagnetic current-current correlators with a novel planar source on pure-QCD ensembles, finding a sharp rise across the pion condensation onset.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve whether dynamical photons convert the second-order pion condensation transition in QCD at nonzero isospin chemical potential into a first-order transition — a question inaccessible via direct QCD+QED simulation due to the complex action (sign problem) arising from unequal quark charges.

Method: Perturbative expansion of the free energy density f in the electromagnetic coupling e around e = 0; computation of the leading coefficient ∂²f/∂e²|ₑ₌₀ as a convolution of the photon propagator and the electromagnetic current-current correlator; measurement of the latter using a newly proposed planar source on existing (2+1)-flavor pure-QCD lattice ensembles at nonzero μ_I and pionic source λ; extrapolation to λ → 0 and physical quark masses at a single lattice spacing.

Result: ∂²f/∂e²|ₑ₌₀ is consistent with being constant (within errors) below the pion condensation onset, then rises sharply across the onset — indicating strong sensitivity of photon-mediated interactions to the onset of charged-pion condensation.

Conclusion: The leading-order electromagnetic correction exhibits a nonanalytic behavior at the pion condensation threshold, supporting the expectation that dynamical photons induce a change in the universality class — potentially driving the transition first order — though higher-order terms must be assessed for definitive confirmation.

Abstract: We present a first lattice calculation of the leading-order effect of dynamical photons on the thermodynamics of QCD at nonzero isospin chemical potential $\mu_I$, across the onset of charged-pion condensation. In QCD this onset is a second-order transition, which dynamical photons are expected, on general grounds, to drive first order. However, a direct simulation is not possible since unequal quark charges render the QCD+QED action complex at nonzero $\mu_I$. We therefore expand the free energy density $f$ in the electromagnetic coupling $e$ around $e=0$, where the sign problem is absent. The leading-order expansion coefficient, $\partial^2 f/\partial e^2|_{e=0}$, is the convolution of the photon propagator with the electromagnetic current-current correlator, which we measure with a newly proposed planar source on existing (2+1)-flavor pure-QCD ensembles at nonzero $\mu_I$ and pionic source $\lambda$. At physical quark masses and a single lattice spacing, after extrapolating to vanishing $\lambda$, we find that $\partial^2 f/\partial e^2|_{e=0}$ is, within errors, independent of $\mu_I$ below the onset and rises sharply across it.

</details>


<div id='math-ph'></div>

# math-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [141] [A simple counterexample to the Hohenberg-Kohn theorem for excited states](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28296)
*Dominic Shillingford*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs explicit counterexamples showing that the Hohenberg–Kohn theorem—guaranteeing a one-to-one mapping between external potentials and ground-state densities in density functional theory—fails for excited states, even in simple 1D non-interacting fermionic systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To challenge the validity of an excited-state extension of the Hohenberg–Kohn theorem, which is often assumed but not generally proven; specifically, to demonstrate potential-to-density nonuniqueness for excited states in a solvable many-body setting.

Method: Constructs exact solutions for non-interacting fermions on a 1D torus using the integrable rescaled Lamé potential; exploits translational invariance and spectral properties (simplicity of excited eigenvalues, constancy of excited-state density) to generate continuous families of distinct potentials yielding identical excited-state densities; extends analysis to odd particle numbers under a spectral nonresonance condition.

Result: For any even n = 2l, exhibits a one-parameter family of distinct external potentials sharing the same constant first excited-state particle density—proving noninjectivity of the excited-state potential-to-density map; extends result to odd n ≥ 3 under a generic spectral condition; identifies links to planar elastica classification for n = 2.

Conclusion: The Hohenberg–Kohn uniqueness principle does not extend to excited states in general, even for non-interacting, translationally invariant, integrable systems—establishing a fundamental limitation for excited-state density functional theory.

Abstract: For an even number of non-interacting fermions on the one-dimensional unit torus, a semi-explicit counterexample to the Hohenberg--Kohn theorem for excited states is exhibited. Indeed, for each $n = 2l$, it is shown that there exist constants $m \in (0, 1)$ and $\ell > 0$ such that an $n$-body system with the rescaled Lamé potential $v_*(x) = 2m \ell^2 \operatorname{sn}^2(\ell x; m)$ has a simple first excited energy and a first excited-state particle density which is constant on the torus. By translating this potential, we exhibit a non-trivial one-parameter family of potentials, all of which have a constant first excited-state density. As such, the potential-to-density map is not one-to-one in this setting. Under a spectral nonresonance assumption, we obtain the same nonuniqueness at a simple excited energy for odd $n \geq 3$. We also explore connections to the classification problem of elastica in the plane when $n = 2$, and outline some related questions of interest.

</details>


### [142] [Effective Hamiltonians and asymptotic band coupling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28328)
*Guillaume Bal,Paul Cazeaux,Daniel Massatt,Solomon Quinn*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: Derives effective macroscopic models for tight-binding Schrödinger equations with slow spatial variations using multiple-scale asymptotics, valid for spectrally localized wave packets—even at degenerate band points—and constructs an effective Hamiltonian capturing inter-band coupling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop accurate long-time effective models for tight-binding Schrödinger dynamics under slow spatial variations, especially when spectral localization occurs at degenerate points in the dispersion relation where standard non-degenerate homogenization fails.

Method: Multiple-scale asymptotic expansion applied to tight-binding Schrödinger equations; construction of an effective Hamiltonian acting on the degenerate subspace, incorporating coupling between degenerate and non-degenerate bands.

Result: Effective macroscopic models accurate to any prescribed order in the asymptotic parameter; explicit characterization of inter-band coupling via the effective Hamiltonian; validation through analytical examples and numerical simulations.

Conclusion: The framework generalizes asymptotic homogenization to degenerate spectral points in tight-binding systems, enabling rigorous, high-order approximations of wave-packet dynamics beyond the non-degenerate regime.

Abstract: By a multiple-scale asymptotic expansion, we derive effective macroscopic models for a large class of tight-binding Schrödinger equations with slow spatial variations. For wave packets that are spectrally localized to some point $(K;E) \in \mathbb{R}^d \times \mathbb{R}$ in the unperturbed tight-binding dispersion relation, our effective models produce long-time approximations of the tight-binding dynamics, accurate to any prescribed order in the asymptotic parameter. Our framework allows the point $(K;E)$ to be degenerate, meaning that multiple (but not necessarily all) energy bands of the tight-binding Hamiltonian can attain the value $E$ at the frequency $K$. We construct an effective Hamiltonian on the relevant degenerate subspace, which quantifies the coupling between the degenerate and other bands. Several applications and numerical simulations illustrate the theory.

</details>


### [143] [On a class of affine Temperley-Lieb modules](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28389)
*Yacine Ikhlef*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes that affine Temperley-Lieb modules with the diagrammatic tensor product form a quasitensor (braided monoidal) category; when q is not a root of unity, a semi-simple ribbon subcategory emerges, and evidence is given for an equivalence between its simple modules and pairs of U_q(sl_2) highest-weight modules.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the categorical structure of affine Temperley-Lieb modules—particularly their monoidal and braided properties—and to relate them to well-studied quantum group representations (U_q(sl_2)) via fusion and associativity.

Method: Rigorous categorical construction using the diagrammatic tensor product on affine Temperley-Lieb modules; analysis of semi-simplicity and ribbon structure under non-root-of-unity conditions; comparison with U_q(sl_2) representation theory via fusion rules and associativity isomorphisms.

Result: Affine Temperley-Lieb modules form a quasitensor category; for q not a root of unity, the subcategory generated by simple quotients of reducible standard modules is semi-simple and ribbon; evidence supports an equivalence between its simple objects and pairs of U_q(sl_2) highest-weight modules.

Conclusion: The affine Temperley-Lieb category admits rich monoidal and braided structure, and—under generic q—exhibits deep ties to U_q(sl_2) representation theory, suggesting a broader categorical correspondence awaiting full proof.

Abstract: We prove that the class of all affine Temperley-Lieb modules, equipped with the recently introduced "diagrammatic" tensor product, forms a quasitensor (i.e. monoidal braided) category. When the Temperley-Lieb parameter $q$ is not a root of unity, we consider the infinite subcategory generated by the simple quotients of reducible standard modules, and we argue that it is a semi-simple ribbon category. We present some evidence that, under the fusion rules and the associativity maps, each simple module in this subcategory is equivalent to a pair of $U_q(sl_2)$ heighest-weight modules.

</details>


### [144] [Integrable Orbital Exchange between Ellipsoidally Deformed $p$, $d$, and $f$ Electron States: Two-Angle Sklyanin Representations and Fused Elliptic $R$-Matrices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28444)
*Vladimir Mikhalev*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs integrable orbital exchange models using ellipsoidally deformed p, d, and f multiplets, leveraging a two-angle Sklyanin algebra realization to obtain explicit Jacobi difference operators, spectra, eigenfunctions, and a positive inner product; it derives elliptic R-matrices via fusion, yields commuting charges and exchange Hamiltonians for various chain types, preserves local Casimirs, and recovers known models (Fateev, Takhtajan–Babujian) in limiting cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop integrable orbital exchange models beyond standard spin systems by incorporating realistic orbital anisotropy—specifically ellipsoidal deformation of p, d, and f multiplets—and to unify angular momentum, distortion, and kinetic-energy invariants within an exactly solvable framework.

Method: Uses a two-angle realization of the Sklyanin algebra to construct Jacobi difference operators D and L_i; employs elliptic fusion to derive R-matrices in angular generators; constructs commuting charges and exchange Hamiltonians for homogeneous, alternating, and mixed-pair chains; enforces preservation of local Casimir invariants corresponding to angular-momentum-squared and distortion-plus-rotation kinetic energy.

Result: Explicit integrable orbital exchange Hamiltonians for p-, d-, and f-multiplet systems; full spectral data (eigenfunctions, distortion spectra) with positive-definite inner product; elliptic R-matrices satisfying Yang–Baxter relations; recovery of Fateev’s elliptic spin-one model in the p–p sector and Takhtajan–Babujian Hamiltonian in the rational isotropic limit.

Conclusion: Orbital exchange models based on ellipsoidally deformed multiplets are exactly integrable via Sklyanin-algebraic methods; they generalize spin-chain physics to include orbital distortion and kinetic-energy invariants while retaining exact solvability and rich symmetry structure.

Abstract: We construct integrable orbital exchange models from ellipsoidally deformed $p$, $d$, and $f$ multiplets.
