A two-angle realization of the Sklyanin algebra gives explicit Jacobi difference operators $D,L_i$, complete distortion spectra and eigenfunctions, and a positive inner product transported from Sklyanin's integral. In the $p$ sector the coordinate functions carry an exact spin-one action, and applying $D$ componentwise maps the confocal ellipsoid to a sphere. Elliptic fusion gives explicit $R$-matrices in the angular generators on both factors. Their Yang--Baxter relations yield commuting charges and exchange Hamiltonians for homogeneous and alternating chains, as well as mixed-pair interactions that can act between angular degrees of freedom on the same center. Exchange changes angular projections and correlations while preserving the two local Casimirs of each constituent, which fix its angular-momentum-squared and distortion-plus-rotation kinetic-energy invariants. The $p$--$p$ model recovers Fateev's elliptic spin-one interaction and reduces to the Takhtajan--Babujian Hamiltonian in the rational isotropic limit.

</details>


### [145] [An Elasticity Complex for Kirchhoff--Love Shells](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28480)
*Hang Yin,Albert Chern*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs an elasticity complex for Kirchhoff–Love shell theory using linearized Gauss–Codazzi equations, yielding a fine resolution of infinitesimal rigid motions, revealing an Airy potential for equilibrium stresses, identifying natural boundary conditions, and establishing a Hodge–Morrey–Friedrichs decomposition for strain and stress fields on shells with boundary.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a geometrically intrinsic and cohomologically meaningful framework for linear shell elasticity that unifies compatibility, equilibrium, boundary conditions, and orthogonal decompositions.

Method: Constructs a differential complex (elasticity complex) rooted in the linearized Gauss–Codazzi equations as strain compatibility conditions; employs sheaf-theoretic and Hodge-theoretic methods to analyze cohomology, potentials, boundary conditions, and orthogonal decompositions on curved surfaces with boundary.

Result: A well-defined elasticity complex resolving infinitesimal rigid motions; identification of Airy-type stress potentials for equilibrium; derivation of natural (intrinsic) boundary conditions for both displacement and traction variables; and a Hodge–Morrey–Friedrichs decomposition for strain and stress fields on shells with boundary.

Conclusion: The shell elasticity complex provides a unified, coordinate-free, and topologically informed foundation for Kirchhoff–Love shell theory, linking differential geometry, elasticity, and Hodge theory in a novel and rigorous way.

Abstract: We develop an elasticity complex for Kirchhoff--Love shell theory, based on the linearized Gauss--Codazzi equations as compatibility conditions for shell strains. The resulting shell complex forms a fine resolution of the sheaf of infinitesimal rigid motions, so its cohomology groups are determined by the corresponding sheaf cohomology. Remarkably, the complex also reveals an Airy potential representation of equilibrium stress fields. The framework further identifies natural boundary conditions for both kinematic and dynamic variables. Finally, we establish a Hodge--Morrey--Friedrichs decomposition for strain and stress fields on shells with boundary.

</details>


### [146] [Feshbach resonances of the second order in a Friedrichs model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28743)
*Dmitry S. Grudko,Alexander K. Motovilov*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies an extended matrix Friedrichs model capable of generating second-order Feshbach resonances; it identifies conditions under which two first-order resonances merge into a single second-order resonance, and characterizes the associated operator-root structure of the transfer function.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the emergence and merging of Feshbach resonances—particularly the transition from first- to second-order resonances—in an extended matrix Friedrichs model, motivated by spectral phenomena in quantum scattering theory.

Method: Analytical spectral analysis of an extended matrix Friedrichs model using operator-theoretic methods; specifically, operator interpretation of resonances and study of the transfer function's operator roots.

Result: Derivation of precise conditions for the coalescence of two first-order resonances into a single second-order resonance; characterization of the degenerate, highly structured operator roots of the transfer function at the merger point.

Conclusion: Second-order Feshbach resonances arise via resonance merging under parameter variation, and their operator-theoretic signature is a nontrivial, degenerate structure of the transfer function’s operator roots—revealing deep connections between resonance order and analytic structure of spectral objects.

Abstract: We consider an extended version of the matrix Friedrichs model that may produce Feshbach resonances of the second order. In this model, under enlarging of the coupling constant, a pair of eigenvalues of the unperturbed Hamiltonian embedded into the continuous spectrum first transforms in resonances of the first order. At some moment later on, these two resonances merge into a single resonance but of the second order, and we fix conditions for this event. Being based on the operator interpretation of resonances, we found that, in the case under consideration, the operator roots of the transfer function have a very particular structure.

</details>


### [147] [Pauli-consistent ensemble Monte Carlo method for bipolar charge transport in graphene](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28773)
*Tigran Zalinyan,Giovanni Nastasi*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: A Pauli-consistent ensemble Monte Carlo method is developed for bipolar charge transport in graphene, incorporating phonon-assisted interband scattering and using a valence-vacancy representation to efficiently model electron-hole dynamics while preserving physical constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable accurate, scalable, and physically consistent microscopic simulation of bipolar (electron–hole) transport in graphene—including interband scattering processes—without the computational burden of explicitly tracking the densely occupied valence band.

Method: Ensemble Monte Carlo with Pauli exclusion principle enforcement; valence-vacancy representation for coupled electron/hole populations; explicit modeling of phonon-assisted interband scattering (generation/recombination); conservation of discrete occupation bounds and net charge during free flight and collisions; validation via comparison with discontinuous Galerkin deterministic solutions.

Result: Excellent agreement between Monte Carlo and deterministic solutions across time evolution, carrier distributions, and steady-state observables; demonstrated numerical robustness (sensitivity/refinement studies); confirmed computational practicality for realistic bipolar graphene transport simulations.

Conclusion: The proposed method provides a physically faithful, numerically reliable, and computationally feasible framework for simulating bipolar quantum transport in graphene, bridging kinetic modeling and scalable stochastic simulation.

Abstract: We develop a Pauli-consistent ensemble Monte Carlo method for bipolar charge transport in graphene, with an explicit treatment of phonon-assisted interband scattering, including electron-hole generation and recombination. The method employs a valence-vacancy representation to describe coupled electron and hole populations without explicitly simulating the highly populated valence band. It preserves the discrete occupation bounds during both free flight and scattering while conserving net charge. The numerical properties of the method are assessed through sensitivity and refinement studies and comparison with an independent deterministic discontinuous Galerkin solution of the same kinetic model. The two approaches show very good agreement for the time evolution, carrier distributions, and steady-state transport observables across the investigated transport regimes. The results also demonstrate the computational practicality of the method for microscopic simulations of bipolar graphene transport.

</details>


### [148] [Trigonal Elliptic Coordinates on the Sphere and Some Applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28992)
*Vladimir Mikhalev*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs a theta-function-based parametrization of the unit sphere aligned with eight trigonal directions, derived from a cubic theta identity; it introduces both nonorthogonal and orthogonal coordinate systems linked to the Volterra chain and Jacobi elliptic functions, and applies them to O'Brien's reduced Jahn–Teller model to yield compact expressions for real spherical harmonics up to degree three.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a geometrically adapted parametrization of the unit sphere aligned with trigonal symmetry—relevant for molecular and solid-state physics models like the Jahn–Teller effect—and to obtain analytically tractable, symmetry-respecting coordinates for spherical harmonics and differential operators.

Method: Constructs a theta-function parametrization using a cubic theta identity (Bobek); derives nonorthogonal coordinates via angular parameter and theta nome; performs algebraic angle transformation preserving the nome to achieve orthogonality; computes metric coefficients and Laplace–Beltrami operator; applies resulting coordinates to O'Brien’s reduced Jahn–Teller Hamiltonian and expresses real spherical harmonics of degrees 2 and 3 explicitly.

Result: A novel orthogonal coordinate system on the sphere with explicit metric and diagonal Laplace–Beltrami operator; compact closed-form expressions for real spherical harmonics of degrees two and three in terms of theta functions and the nome; identification of the orthogonal angle as the polar angle of an $E_g$ quadrupolar doublet; discovery that one octupolar harmonic depends solely on the theta nome within each octant.

Conclusion: The theta-parametrization provides a symmetry-adapted, analytically powerful framework for spherical analysis in trigonal settings, enabling simplifications in quantum mechanical models such as the Jahn–Teller problem and facilitating exact treatment of higher-rank multipoles.

Abstract: We construct a theta-function parametrization of the unit sphere adapted to the eight trigonal directions. The construction follows from a cubic theta identity discussed by Bobek and admits an equivalent representation in Jacobi elliptic functions. The angular parameter and theta nome give a nonorthogonal coordinate system whose squared Cartesian components are related to the three-component Volterra chain. An algebraic change of angle, retaining the nome, yields orthogonal coordinates with explicit metric coefficients and a diagonal Laplace--Beltrami operator. We apply both parametrizations to the geometry of O'Brien's reduced Jahn--Teller model and give compact expressions for real spherical harmonics of degrees two and three. The orthogonal angle is the polar angle of an $E_g$ quadrupolar doublet, while one octupolar harmonic depends only on the nome within each octant.

</details>


### [149] [Spin-geodesic systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29005)
*Renato Feres,Zijie Hu*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: Introduces spin-geodesic systems—a geometric dynamical system coupling point motion and internal spin on Riemannian manifolds via a symmetric endomorphism field B; proves equivalence to abstract no-slip rolling and establishes Liouville volume preservation despite non-symplecticity (unless B ≡ 0).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and generalize geometric mechanical systems—including geodesic flows, magnetic geodesics, frame flows, and nonholonomic rolling—by introducing a spin-coupled deformation of geodesic motion governed by a symmetric endomorphism field B.

Method: Uses differential geometry of Riemannian manifolds, metric connections induced by B, mechanical phase space formulation, and volume form analysis; constructs an abstract ambient geometry for rolling equivalence and computes the Lie derivative of the canonical Liouville measure along the spin-geodesic vector field.

Result: 1) Every spin-geodesic system is equivalent to a no-slip rolling system in an abstract ambient geometry; when the B-induced connection is flat, it reduces to Euclidean hypersurface rolling under standard integrability. 2) The spin-geodesic flow preserves the canonical Liouville volume on the cotangent bundle T*M ⊕ S^2(TM), irrespective of B; however, the flow is symplectic only if B ≡ 0.

Conclusion: Spin-geodesic systems provide a geometrically natural, volume-preserving generalization of geodesic dynamics with intrinsic spin coupling; their rolling interpretation and Liouville property highlight deep links between nonholonomic mechanics, geometric flows, and invariant measures—even beyond the symplectic category.

Abstract: We introduce and study a class of geometric dynamical systems on Riemannian manifolds called spin-geodesic systems, in which the motion of a point is coupled to an internal classical spin through a symmetric endomorphism field $B$, the spin-coupling field. These systems may be viewed as $B$-dependent deformations of geodesic motion and have connections with frame flows, magnetic geodesic flows, and nonholonomic rolling systems. We show that every spin-geodesic system admits an equivalent interpretation as a no-slip rolling system in an abstract ambient geometry; when the associated metric connection, determined by $B$, is flat, this construction reduces, under the usual integrability hypotheses, to ordinary rolling on a Euclidean hypersurface. Our second main result is a Liouville theorem: for an arbitrary symmetric spin-coupling field B, the spin-geodesic flow preserves the canonical Liouville volume on its mechanical phase space. This holds even though the flow is not symplectic unless $B$ is identically zero.

</details>


### [150] [The marginal sine-Gordon field theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29024)
*Dyuman Bhattacharya*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs the 2D sine-Gordon vacuum at the critical coupling β²=8π using a lattice regularization, establishes mass bounds relative to the exit scale from weak coupling, determines bare parameters from background response, proves uniqueness and dilation covariance of the continuum vacuum, rules out Gaussianity for field derivatives, confirms nontrivial higher-order correlations, and establishes universality for key observables and identities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously construct the critical 2D sine-Gordon model (β²=8π) as a local relativistic quantum field theory, resolve its vacuum structure, mass spectrum, and non-Gaussian nature, and prove universality of physical observables and Ward/trace/contact identities under lattice regularization.

Method: Noncompact real lattice field regularization with nearest-neighbor kinetic term, positive cosine activity, and zero Dirichlet boundary conditions; renormalization group flow analysis near weak coupling; prescribed response to smooth background fields to fix bare couplings; spectral analysis for mass gap; Hilbert space reconstruction via literal derivatives; variance and exponential moment analysis of smeared derivatives; verification of correlation decay and Ward identities at finite scale.

Result: Existence and uniqueness of the continuum vacuum; mass gap M satisfies uniform two-sided bounds M a_exit ∈ [c₋, c₊]; bare parameters are asymptotically free and determined by background response; field derivatives generate the full reflected Hilbert space; field is provably non-Gaussian (nonzero exponential moments, nonvanishing connected correlations beyond second order); universality established for higher harmonics, field equation, topological current, stress tensor, and source-transfer theorem with explicit finite-scale conditions.

Conclusion: The critical 2D sine-Gordon model admits a well-defined, local, relativistic, non-Gaussian quantum field theory construction with controlled UV regularization, exact symmetry realizations (dilation, reflection), and verified universality of physical structures — affirming its status as a conformally invariant but nontrivial interacting QFT at the Berezinskii–Kosterlitz–Thouless transition point.

Abstract: At $\beta_*^2=8\pi$ we construct the two-dimensional sine--Gordon vacuum from a noncompact real lattice field with nearest-neighbor kinetic energy, positive cosine activity and zero Dirichlet data. Its mass $M$ is the lower edge of the energy spectrum above the vacuum. In units of the length $a_{\rm exit}$ at which the flow leaves weak coupling it obeys $0<c_-\le M a_{\rm exit}\le c_+<\infty$ on compact positive endpoint intervals, with uniform constants. A prescribed response to a smooth background in a fixed square determines the nonlinear asymptotically free bare parameters at each sufficiently fine cutoff. In the local relativistic continuum theory the vacuum is unique, physical dilation realizes every positive mass, and literal derivatives generate the reflected Hilbert space even for finite composite and contact families. Variance and exponential moments of a derivative smear rule out a Gaussian law. A connected correlation of some finite even order above two is nonzero on separated supports. The construction gives universality for the specified finite nonnegative higher harmonics, the field equation, topological current and geometric stress tensor with their Ward, trace and contact identities, and a normalized source-transfer theorem with explicit finite-scale hypotheses.

</details>


### [151] [Path Integrals on Functional Lattices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29045)
*Alexey A. Kryukov*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: Introduces 'functional lattices'—a continuum-space discretization scheme for QFT path integrals using localized basis expansions; proves regulator-independent convergence of covariance and generating functionals for free fields via Galerkin analysis; extends to interacting theories with pointwise/integrable convergence criteria; numerically validates mass-gap and renormalization behavior in φ⁴ theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish mathematically rigorous, regulator-independent limits for path integrals in quantum field theory without resorting to spacetime lattice regularization (e.g., Wilsonian lattices), preserving continuum differential structure while enabling finite-dimensional approximation.

Method: Expands quantum fields in discrete, complete, localized bases ('functional lattices') in infinite-dimensional function space; truncates to finite-dimensional subspaces yielding standard integrals; employs Galerkin approximation theory to prove strong operator convergence of covariance operators and convergence of normalized generating functionals; handles Minkowski signature via fixed-iε regularization followed by distributional ε→0 limit; for interactions, applies dominated convergence theorem to perturbative graph integrands.

Result: Rigorous proof of convergence and regulator independence for massive free Euclidean scalar field path integrals; extension to Minkowski space with controlled iε→0 limit; convergence at each perturbative order for interacting theories under pointwise convergence + uniform integrable majorant condition; numerical confirmation of correct 1D φ⁴ mass-gap coefficients and 4D UV divergences (mass renormalization, logarithmic wave-function renormalization).

Conclusion: Functional lattices provide a mathematically sound, continuum-based framework for defining and approximating QFT path integrals, bridging rigorous functional analysis with practical computation and renormalization, applicable to both free and perturbatively interacting theories.

Abstract: Path integrals in quantum field theory are commonly approached through finite-dimensional approximations, but existence and regulator independence of the infinite-dimensional limit require separate analysis. We introduce a scheme in which spacetime remains continuous and the field is expanded in a discrete complete family of localized functions, called a functional lattice. Truncation to finite-dimensional functional subspaces yields ordinary finite-dimensional integrals while leaving the continuum differential operator unchanged. For the massive free Euclidean scalar field, we prove convergence of the finite-dimensional covariance operators and normalized generating functionals by a Galerkin argument. The limit is independent of the compatible functional lattice: any sequence of finite-dimensional subspaces dense in the quadratic-form domain converges to the same continuum covariance and generating functional. A direct Minkowski construction is obtained at fixed Feynman regulator $i\epsilon$, followed by the distributional limit $\epsilon\downarrow0$. For interacting theories, each truncation gives an ordinary non-Gaussian integral. At fixed perturbative order, convergence follows when the finite-resolution graph integrands converge pointwise and admit an integrable majorant independent of the functional resolution. Numerical results for one-dimensional $\phi^4$ theory approach the known first- and second-order mass-gap coefficients, while four-dimensional calculations exhibit the expected mass divergence and logarithmic wave-function renormalization. Thus functional lattices provide a continuum-space approach to path integrals with mathematically controlled limits in the cases considered here.

</details>


### [152] [A Novel Algebraic Approach to Medium-Range Solvable Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29351)
*Yuan Miao,Kohei Fukai*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends Jones' Baxterisation to quantum spin chains with medium-range interactions by introducing an extended Yang-Baxter equation for R-matrices built from neighboring-site generators; it constructs two families of solvable medium-range Hamiltonians using the chromatic algebra of 3-colouring and its relation to Rep(S₃), recovering known models (folded XXZ, hard-rod deformed XXZ) and yielding two new ones (three-state antiferromagnet, Rydberg-blockade ladder), two of which exhibit non-invertible symmetries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize Jones' Baxterisation—originally for nearest-neighbor integrable models—to medium-range interacting quantum spin chains, enabling systematic construction of solvable lattice Hamiltonians beyond short-range interactions.

Method: Introduces an extended Yang-Baxter equation for R-matrices acting on two neighboring sites, paired with a compatible Lax operator; solves it in non-difference form using generators of the chromatic algebra of 3-colouring; leverages the isomorphism between this algebra and the fusion category Rep(S₃) to classify four explicit representations; derives transfer matrices in involution up to boundary terms and constructs associated medium-range Hamiltonians.

Result: A novel extended Baxterisation framework yielding four concrete medium-range integrable models: (i) folded XXZ, (ii) hard-rod deformed XXZ, (iii) three-state antiferromagnet, (iv) Rydberg-blockade ladder; two models host non-invertible symmetries; all arise as representations of the chromatic algebra via Rep(S₃).

Conclusion: Medium-range integrability can be systematically achieved through algebraic generalizations of Baxterisation, with the chromatic algebra of 3-colouring serving as a unifying algebraic structure; this approach reveals new solvable models and enriches the classification of quantum many-body systems with non-invertible symmetries.

Abstract: We extend Jones' Baxterisation, which produces solutions to the Yang-Baxter equation directly from the generators of an algebra, to quantum spin chains with medium-range interactions. The key ingredient is an extended Yang-Baxter equation for R matrices that are built from the generators attached to two neighbouring sites. Together with the accompanying Lax operator, it gives rise to transfer matrices that are in involution up to boundary terms, exactly as in Jones' construction. We find a solution to the extended Yang-Baxter equation, of non-difference form, in terms of the generators of the chromatic algebra of 3-colouring, and obtain from it two families of medium-range lattice Hamiltonians. Thus, each representation of the algebra provides a medium-range solvable model. Using the relation between the chromatic algebra of 3-colouring and the fusion category $\mathrm{Rep}(S_3)$, we construct four such representations. They reproduce the folded XXZ model and the hard-rod deformed XXZ model, and give new medium-range models of a three-state antiferromagnet and of a Rydberg-blockade ladder. Two of the four models possess non-invertible symmetries.

</details>


### [153] [A thermodynamically consistent variational formulation and structure-preserving finite element methods for non-isothermal liquid--vapor flows with moving contact lines](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29390)
*Junkai Wang,Ting Xiao,Qiaolin He*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops thermodynamically consistent, structure-preserving finite element methods for non-isothermal liquid–vapor flows with moving contact lines, based on a variational formulation derived from nonequilibrium thermodynamics; it proves key physical properties (mass conservation, energy balance, entropy inequality, positivity/bound preservation) and validates the schemes in 2D/3D applications including droplet wetting and pool boiling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct numerical methods for non-isothermal liquid–vapor flows with moving contact lines that rigorously preserve fundamental thermodynamic structures—such as energy balance, entropy production, positivity of temperature, and mass conservation—which are typically violated by conventional approaches.

Method: Derives governing equations and boundary conditions via generalized Hamilton’s principle for nonequilibrium thermodynamics, grounded in dynamic van der Waals theory and surface thermodynamics; develops two equivalent variational formulations (internal energy- and Helmholtz free energy-based), each using distinct thermal variables (entropy density vs. temperature); constructs fully discrete finite element schemes using compatible spatial operators and discrete thermodynamic chain rules; proves structural properties via mathematical analysis of the discrete schemes.

Result: Two thermodynamically consistent, fully discrete finite element schemes are constructed and rigorously shown to satisfy: exact mass conservation, bound preservation for number density, strict positivity of discrete temperature, exact total energy balance (including external work and heat exchange), and a temperature-weighted weak entropy inequality with nonnegative bulk and boundary entropy production; verified numerically in 2D/3D for droplet wetting, pool boiling, and droplet ejection.

Conclusion: The proposed variational framework and structure-preserving discretizations provide a robust, physically faithful foundation for simulating complex non-isothermal multiphase flows with moving contact lines, bridging thermodynamic consistency, mathematical rigor, and practical applicability.

Abstract: In this paper, we present a variational formulation and structure-preserving finite element methods for non-isothermal liquid--vapor flows with moving contact lines. Based on the dynamic van der Waals theory with gradient contributions and surface thermodynamics, the governing equations and hydrodynamic boundary conditions are derived from a generalized Hamilton's principle for nonequilibrium thermodynamics. Two equivalent variational formulations based on internal energy and Helmholtz free energy are developed, using entropy density and temperature, respectively, as the primary thermal variable. Using the resulting weak forms, compatible spatial operators and discrete thermodynamic chain rules, we construct two thermodynamically consistent fully discrete finite element schemes. We rigorously prove the following properties for both fully discrete schemes: mass conservation, bound preservation for the number density, positivity of the temperature, an exact total energy balance incorporating external work and heat exchange, and a temperature-weighted weak entropy inequality with nonnegative bulk and boundary entropy production. Numerical experiments in two and three dimensions assess accuracy and structure preservation, with applications to non-isothermal droplet wetting, pool boiling and droplet ejection.

</details>


### [154] [Reconstruction and geometry of $4\times4$ quantum Sudoku](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29420)
*Fabio Deelan Cunden,Paolo Facchi,Roberto La Scala,Marilena Ligabò,Karol Życzkowski*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes 4×4 quantum Sudoku grids—arrays of rank-one projections satisfying orthogonality constraints across rows, columns, and blocks—and establishes necessary/sufficient conditions for block completion, uniqueness, reconstruction formulas (in quantum logic terms), and a geometric parametrization of unitary equivalence classes via five identified squares; it further shows that measurement in the first block's basis yields a fractional Sudoku lying in a 7D polytope spanned by the 12 classical 4×4 Sudoku solutions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend classical combinatorial Sudoku to the quantum setting by modeling cells as rank-one projections, and to understand the structure, reconstructability, classification, and measurement behavior of minimal (4×4) quantum Sudoku grids.

Method: Uses operator-theoretic and quantum-logical methods: characterizes grids via identity decompositions in C*-algebras; derives algebraic conditions for block completion using overlap matrices; classifies unitary equivalence via Gram matrix invariants (the 16 overlaps); constructs explicit parametrization using matrix representations and identifications of square boundaries; analyzes symmetry reduction and convex structure under measurement.

Result: Necessary and sufficient conditions for opposite-block completion with uniqueness; explicit reconstruction formulas in quantum logic; 16 pairwise overlaps fully determine the unitary equivalence class; equivalence classes are parametrized by five closed squares with side-diagonal identifications; quotient by classical symmetries is a closed disc containing exactly two classical Sudoku; measurement yields a fractional Sudoku in a 7D polytope whose vertices are the 12 classical 4×4 Sudoku.

Conclusion: The 4×4 quantum Sudoku space has rich but tractable geometry: its unitary classification is discrete-continuous hybrid (five-square model), fully captured by overlap data; classical Sudoku emerge as extremal points, and quantum grids behave predictably under projective measurement—bridging quantum information, combinatorics, and noncommutative geometry.

Abstract: We study the reconstruction and geometry of $4 \times 4$ quantum Sudoku, viewed as arrays of rank-one projections whose rows, columns and blocks decompose the identity. We give necessary and sufficient conditions for two prescribed opposite blocks to admit a completion, prove its uniqueness, and obtain explicit reconstruction formulas, also expressed in the language of quantum logic. The sixteen overlaps between these blocks determine the unitary equivalence class of the grid, while the number of nonzero overlaps equals its cardinality. Using a matrix representation, we parametrise the equivalence classes by five closed squares, with the sides of one square identified with the diagonals of the other four. The quotient by classical cell symmetries is a closed disc containing exactly two classical classes. Finally, measurement in the basis of the first block gives a fractional Sudoku that retains the full unitary class and is a convex combination of at most four classical grids. This measurement map embeds the five-square model into a seven-dimensional polytope whose vertices are the twelve classical Sudoku.

</details>


### [155] [Analytic continuation of the T- and S-matrices to unphysical sheets in a multichannel extension of the Friedrichs-Faddeev model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29441)
*Mikhail V. Kremenetsky,Alexander K. Motovilov*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the Friedrichs-Faddeev model to matrix multichannel scattering, derives analytic continuation conditions for the T- and S-matrices onto unphysical energy sheets, and characterizes resonances as zeros of truncated physical-sheet scattering matrix eigenvalues.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously extend the Friedrichs-Faddeev model to multichannel scattering and establish a precise analytical link between resonances on unphysical sheets and spectral properties of the physical-sheet scattering matrix.

Method: Analytical continuation theory applied to matrix-valued integral operators; derivation of explicit formulas expressing continued T- and S-matrices in terms of their physical-sheet counterparts; spectral analysis of truncated scattering matrices.

Result: Necessary and sufficient conditions on potential kernels for analytic continuation of T- and S-matrices to unphysical sheets; closed-form expressions for analytically continued matrices; resonance characterization as eigenvalue-zero points of truncated physical-sheet S-matrix.

Conclusion: Resonances in the matrix multichannel Friedrichs-Faddeev model are fully determined by the vanishing eigenvalues of finite-rank truncations of the physical-sheet scattering matrix, providing a direct spectral criterion for resonance location.

Abstract: We consider a matrix multichannel extension of the Friedrichs-Faddeev model. First, we formulate conditions on the kernels of the potential matrix that guarantee for the T-matrix (and then for the S-matrix) the existence of analytical continuation to unphysical energy sheets. We also obtain explicit representations for the analytically continued T- and S-matrices on unphysical sheets in terms of these matrices themselves, but only taken in the physical sheet. From the explicit representations obtained, it follows that the resonances on a certain unphysical sheet are nothing but the points at which the corresponding truncation of the scattering matrix in the physical sheet has an eigenvalue of 0.

</details>


### [156] [$μ$- and $q$-extension of special functions in quantum field theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29543)
*J. Blümlein,A.M. Gavrilik,U.Y. Lunha,O. Mykhailiv,C. Schneider*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs μ- and q-extensions of special functions (polylogarithms, harmonic sums, etc.) arising in perturbative QFT, showing μ-extension preserves function class (except for root-valued letters), while q-extension yields new higher transcendental functions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize special functions appearing in analytic calculations of single-scale quantities in perturbative QFT—such as polylogarithms and harmonic sums—to settings with modified (anti)commutation relations, as expected in μ- and q-extended quantum field theories.

Method: Systematic construction of μ- and q-extensions of iterative integrals (e.g., polylogarithms, Nielsen integrals, cyclotomic/ quadratic-root letters) and their inverse Mellin counterparts (harmonic sums, generalized/cyclotomic/nested sums with central binomials), analyzing structural preservation vs. transcendental escalation.

Result: μ-extensions preserve the algebraic class of the original special functions (polynomial dependence on μ-parameter), except for square-root-valued letters; q-extensions generate genuinely new higher transcendental functions beyond classical iterated integrals or nested sums.

Conclusion: The μ- and q-extensions provide distinct mathematical frameworks for potential use in deformed QFTs: μ-deformation maintains structural familiarity, whereas q-deformation introduces richer transcendental structures essential for more complex loop calculations.

Abstract: Analytic calculations up to single-scale quantities in perturbative quantum field theory result into special numbers and functions, which are generalizations as iterative integrals over special alphabets. In this way one obtains polylogarithms, Nielsen integrals, the iterated integrals over linear denominator terms, cyclotomic letters, letters induced by quadratic forms, and square-root valued letters. The inverse Mellin transform relates special nested sums to these quantities, which are harmonic sums, generalized harmonic sums, nested sums implied by quadratic forms, cyclotomic harmonic sums, and nested sums containing central binomials. We construct the $\mu$- and $q$-extensions of all these special functions. They are expected to occur in loop calculations within $\mu$- and $q$-extended quantum field theories via modified (anti)commutation relations. The $\mu$-extended special functions are quite different form the corresponding $q$-extended functions. While in the former case one observes class-preservation characterized by the polynomial $\mu$-parameter, except for the case of root-valued letters. In the latter case new higher transcendental functions are obtained.

</details>


### [157] [Interface currents of magnetized differential systems: quantization and bulk-interface correspondence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29719)
*Guillaume Bal*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a bulk-interface correspondence for transport currents at interfaces between two magnetic insulators, generalizing the quantum Hall effect to non-elliptic and flat-band systems; it defines a quantized interface current via a Fredholm index and expresses it as the difference of bulk topological invariants (Kubo-Chern or Chern integrals) plus a momentum-space Chern term, with stability under perturbations and singular behavior near flat bands.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the bulk-boundary correspondence beyond elliptic Hamiltonians and constant magnetic fields—particularly to physical systems with flat or nearly flat bands (e.g., rotating shallow water, cold plasmas, pseudospin-one materials)—where standard topological classifications fail due to spectral degeneracies and absence of a spectral gap in the bulk.

Method: Uses operator-theoretic analysis of self-adjoint differential systems H with magnetic coupling (Peierls or minimal coupling in factorized form L*L+W); employs Douglis-Nirenberg ellipticity, effective flat symbols, and a gap condition; defines interface current via trace-class regularization of i[H,P]φ'(H); computes topological invariants using Kubo-Chern conductivity (for nonzero field, via Landau-level counting with extension correction) and Chern integrals (for zero/weak field and at infinite momentum); applies index theory to relate interface current to Fredholm index.

Result: Proves that the interface current σ_I is well-defined and quantized (2πσ_I ∈ ℤ) for energies α outside flat-band set and bulk spectral gaps; derives explicit bulk-interface formula 2πσ_I = J(a₊,B₊;α) − J(a₋,B₋;α) + C, where J is either Kubo-Chern conductivity (nonzero B) or Chern integral (B=0), and C is a Chern number over the wall's momentum-space projector at infinity; shows stability under large perturbations and quantization of edge currents at field boundaries; identifies divergence of σ_I near flat-band energies due to Landau-level accumulation.

Conclusion: A unified topological framework for interface transport is established for broad classes of insulating systems—including non-elliptic, multi-band, and flat-band models—validating and generalizing the bulk-interface correspondence beyond conventional quantum Hall settings, with implications for topological phases in geophysical, plasma, photonic, and condensed matter systems.

Abstract: This paper concerns transport along an interface between two insulators modeled by self-adjoint differential systems $H$ of arbitrary order, coupled to a magnetic field either by Peierls substitution or, for operators of the form $L^*L+W$, in the factor $L$. Each bulk carries a constant field, which may vanish. The principal symbol of $H$ is either elliptic or has a kernel of constant rank, in which case finitely many bands remain bounded at large momentum, as in rotating shallow water, cold plasmas, Maxwell's equations and pseudospin-one materials. Ellipticity is then replaced by a Douglis-Nirenberg structure and by a gap condition on an effective flat symbol. We show that, for every energy $\alpha$ away from the flat-band set and in a spectral gap of both bulks, the interface current $\sigma_I=\mathrm{Tr}\,i[H,P]\varphi'(H)$ is well defined, with $2\pi\sigma_I$ a Fredholm index, and that it is given, for all walls in the elliptic case and under a freezing condition otherwise, by a bulk-interface correspondence of the form $2\pi\sigma_I=J(a_+,B_+;\alpha)-J(a_-,B_-;\alpha)+\mathsf{C}$. For a nonzero bulk field, $J=2\pi\sigma_H$ is the Kubo-Chern Hall conductivity, which we compute by an extension-corrected count of generalized Landau levels. For a vanishing field, $J$ is the Chern integral of the field-free symbol, which is also, under a weak-field condition, the weak-field limit of the Hall conductivity. The term $\mathsf{C}$ is a Chern integral of the occupied projector at infinite momentum across the wall. The current is stable under large perturbations, and boundaries of the applied field inside a homogeneous material carry quantized currents that change at field-induced transitions. Near flat-band energies, where generalized Landau levels may accumulate, the current may diverge.

</details>


### [158] [Characterizing Ultrafast Processes via Work](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29778)
*Bernard Derrida,Michael Entov,Leonid Polterovich,Lenya Ryzhik*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies work fluctuations in mean-field systems undergoing a thermodynamic cycle (instantaneous parameter jump, relaxation, reverse jump), identifying a 'matching regime' where initial and final equilibrium macrostates coincide; there, average work per particle vanishes and total work fluctuations are non-Gaussian, whereas away from matching, average work is positive and fluctuations become Gaussian.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize the 'matching phenomenon'—where forward and reverse equilibrium macrostates coincide in the thermodynamic limit—and to connect it to both statistical mechanics of mean-field systems and contact-geometric thermodynamics (specifically Reeb chords between Legendrian submanifolds).

Method: Analytical asymptotic analysis of work distributions in scalar many-particle mean-field systems under instantaneous parameter quenches; employs large-deviation and central-limit principles, combined with contact-geometric interpretation of equilibrium states as Legendrian submanifolds.

Result: In the matching regime: (i) average work per particle → 0, (ii) total work has a non-Gaussian limiting distribution. Away from matching: (i) average work per particle → c > 0, (ii) rescaled work fluctuations converge weakly to a Gaussian. Matching corresponds geometrically to existence of a Reeb chord between two equilibrium Legendrian submanifolds.

Conclusion: Matching is a critical phenomenon distinguishing distinct scaling and distributional regimes for work in mean-field thermodynamic cycles, with deep connections to both statistical physics and contact geometry.

Abstract: Consider the following cycle for a mean-field system initially in thermodynamic equilibrium: an instantaneous parameter jump, relaxation to the new equilibrium, and a reverse jump. For certain systems and parameter values, the two equilibrium macrostates coincide in the thermodynamic limit. We characterize this matching phenomenon through the work required to perform the cycle. Our main result concerns scalar many-particle mean-field systems under suitable assumptions. In the matching regime, the total work has a non-Gaussian limiting distribution, and the average work per particle tends to zero. Away from matching, the properly rescaled fluctuations of the total work have a Gaussian limiting distribution, and the average work per particle converges to a strictly positive constant. Further motivation arises from a contact-geometric model of thermodynamics, in which matching corresponds to a Reeb chord between two equilibrium Legendrian submanifolds.

</details>


### [159] [Modulation equations and coherence dynamics in a qubit chain coupled to a circulating fluxon](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29827)
*O.M.Kiselev*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives modulation equations for quantum amplitudes of a fluxon interacting with an exchange-coupled qubit chain, using a unitary passage operator and resonant normal form to separate slow dynamics from fast corrections; it quantifies coherence errors from emission/return amplitudes and ground-band contributions, achieving high accuracy (error ~10⁻⁸–10⁻⁹) at velocity-selected resonance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To model and control coherence degradation in a qubit chain induced by a moving fluxon, particularly how source profile and field memory affect internal qubit populations and coherence during repeated interactions.

Method: Derivation of modulation equations for joint quantum amplitudes; construction of a unitary passage operator incorporating translational packet, vacuum dressing, and discrete oscillatory modes; application of resonant normal form theory to isolate slow amplitude evolution; perturbative treatment of ordered emission/return amplitudes and ground-band corrections; a posteriori error bounding.

Result: Second-order correction from ordered emission/return alters qubit population and coherence; next ground-band term provides compensating diagonal and near-resonant off-diagonal error contributions; at velocity-selected resonance, mode contribution after 81 passages is ~6×10⁻⁸; cubic ground-band approximation matches full calculation to 1.5×10⁻⁹ in tested observables.

Conclusion: The derived equations accurately capture fluxon-induced decoherence mechanisms in exchange-coupled qubit chains, enabling precise prediction and mitigation of state errors through controlled velocity selection and profile engineering.

Abstract: We derive modulation equations for the joint quantum amplitudes of a circulating fluxon and an exchange-coupled qubit chain whose endpoint interacts with the field. A unitary passage operator combines the translational packet, vacuum dressing, and discrete oscillatory modes. A resonant normal form separates the slow amplitude evolution from nonresonant corrections. Ordered emission and return amplitudes generate a second-order correction that changes the population and coherence of an internal qubit. The next ground-band coefficient gives compensating diagonal and near-resonant off-diagonal contributions to the accumulated error. We bound the state error a posteriori. At a velocity-selected resonance, direct propagation over 81 passages gives a mode contribution of order $6\times10^{-8}$; the cubic ground-band approximation agrees with an expanded full calculation to $1.5\times10^{-9}$ in the tested observables. The equations describe how the source profile and field memory affect coherence inside the chain.

</details>


### [160] [Frobenius $2$-algebras and $R$-matrices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29898)
*Hibiki Shibuya,Yuji Terashima*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: Introduces Frobenius 2-algebras as categorical generalizations of Frobenius algebras and shows they canonically produce R-matrices solving the Yang–Baxter equation in the monoidal category of endomorphisms; recovers Kashaev's universal R-matrix for Heisenberg doubles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a unifying categorical framework for constructing solutions to the Yang–Baxter equation, generalizing known constructions (e.g., Kashaev's) via higher algebraic structure.

Method: Defines Frobenius 2-algebras in a 2-categorical setting; proves that any such structure induces an R-matrix in the monoidal category End(A) satisfying the Yang–Baxter equation; verifies the construction on three concrete examples using braided monoidal categories, Hopf algebras, and Heisenberg doubles.

Result: Every Frobenius 2-algebra yields a Yang–Baxter solution in End(A); the construction subsumes and generalizes Kashaev’s R-matrix for the Heisenberg double.

Conclusion: Frobenius 2-algebras serve as a natural categorical source of Yang–Baxter operators, unifying and extending prior algebraic constructions of R-matrices.

Abstract: In this paper, introducing Frobenius $2$-algebras, which are categorical generalizations of Frobenius algebras, we show that every Frobenius $2$-algebra determines an $R$-matrix satisfying the Yang-Baxter equation in the monoidal category of endomorphisms of its underlying object. Moreover, we illustrate this construction through three examples: a pair of objects in a braided monoidal category, a Hopf algebra with invertible antipode, and representations of the Heisenberg double of a finite-dimensional Hopf algebra. In particular, in the third example, we recover Kashaev's construction of the universal $R$-matrix. Thus, our construction provides a categorical generalization of Kashaev's construction.

</details>


### [161] [A decomposition of Hamiltonian matrices with symplectic kernels and its application to Williamson's diagonal form](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29967)
*Shaowu Huang,Yingxin Huang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper generalizes Ikramov's result on symplectic eigenvalues by establishing a decomposition for Hamiltonian matrices with symplectic kernels, and uses it to give an alternative proof of Williamson’s diagonalization theorem for positive-semidefinite matrices with symplectic structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend Ikramov's symplectic eigenvalue result for positive definite matrices to the broader class of Hamiltonian matrices with symplectic kernels, and to provide new insight into Williamson's theorem via structural decomposition.

Method: Constructive matrix decomposition leveraging symplectic geometry and properties of Hamiltonian matrices; employs symplectic similarity transformations and kernel analysis.

Result: A novel decomposition theorem for Hamiltonian matrices possessing symplectic kernels; a new, structurally motivated proof of Williamson’s diagonal form for positive-semidefinite matrices under symplectic congruence.

Conclusion: The decomposition unifies and generalizes prior results on symplectic spectral theory, reinforcing the centrality of symplectic structure in the canonical forms of Hamiltonian and positive-semidefinite matrices.

Abstract: In this paper, we establish a decomposition of Hamiltonian matrices with symplectic kernels, which extends the main result in [K. D. Ikramov, On the Symplectic Eigenvalues of Positive Definite Matrices. Moscow Univ. Comput. Math. Cybern., 42(2018)1-4]. As an application, we present another proof of Williamson's diagonal form for positive-semidefinite matrices with symplectic kernels.

</details>


### [162] [Derivation of BCS functionals and gap equations from quantum many-body fermionic system](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30367)
*Xuwen Chen,Jiahao Wu,Zhifei Zhang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes well-posedness (existence and uniqueness) of nonzero nonnegative solutions to discrete and continuous BCS gap equations for finite and infinite 3D spin-1/2 Fermi systems with attractive interactions; proves emergence of SU(2) symmetry under quasi-freeness; and shows convergence of critical temperature and Cooper divergence in the thermodynamic limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously justify the BCS theory for finite-size quantum Fermi systems, resolve uniqueness and positivity of gap solutions, and bridge discrete (finite-box) and continuum (infinite-volume) descriptions—particularly addressing symmetry emergence and thermodynamic limits.

Method: Analytical study of the discrete BCS gap equation on a finite lattice/box; fixed-point and monotonicity arguments for existence/uniqueness of positive maximal solution; quasi-free state assumption to derive SU(2) invariance; thermodynamic limit via functional convergence to the continuous BCS functional and gap equation; spectral analysis for energy/mass gap and Cooper divergence.

Result: Existence of positive maximal solution to discrete BCS gap equation below critical temperature T_c; uniqueness of nonzero nonnegative solution (both discrete and continuum); emergence of SU(2) symmetry in finite quasi-free systems; convergence of T_c and the gap functional in the infinite-volume limit; proof of a positive energy (mass) gap on [0,T_c); confirmation of Cooper divergence in density of states.

Conclusion: The BCS gap equation is well-posed across finite and infinite settings; its nonzero nonnegative solutions are physically meaningful (originating from the many-body system), unique, and support emergent symmetries and universal thermodynamic behavior—including a rigorous foundation for superconductivity onset in finite fermionic systems.

Abstract: We consider 3D single-species spin-$1/2$ Fermi systems with general dispersion relation and general attractive interaction initially inside a finite box. Up to some critical temperature $T_c$ determined by the interaction and system size, we establish, for the expected discrete BCS gap equation, existence of positive maximal solution. We then prove the uniqueness of nonzero (not identically zero) nonnegative solution. That is, any nonzero nonnegative solution, if exist or obtained via other methods, must equal to the positive maximal solution. Building on such a well-posedness result and assuming quasi-freeness, we prove the emergence of the $SU(2)$ symmetry of the finite system. Finally, letting the system size tend to infinity, we prove the emergence of the continuous BCS functional and gap equation for which we also have the uniqueness of nonzero nonnegative solution, and the convergence of the critical temperature. In particular, our work also implies there is a positive energy (hence mass) gap for aforementioned Fermi systems for $[0,T_c)$, any nonzero nonnegative solution for the gap equations are from the many-body system it suppose to describe, and the Cooper divergence of the density of states from a finite to an infinite system.

</details>


### [163] [Quantum walks and conformal mappings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30403)
*Fedor Kuyanov,Michael Drmota,Mikhail Skopenkov,Alexey Ustinov*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives uniform asymptotic formulae for the wave function of the Feynman checkers (quantum walk / six-vertex model with complex weights), revealing Airy-function–governed and gamma-function–governed transitions in the xt-plane—specifically, at the oscillatory-to-exponential boundary and near light-speed, respectively—via conformal-mapping techniques applicable beyond this model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain a uniform asymptotic description of the wave function across the entire xt-plane for the discrete quantum-mechanical model of an electron (Feynman checkers/quantum walk/six-vertex model), especially capturing subtle transition regimes not resolved by prior non-uniform approximations.

Method: Analysis of integral representations of the wave function using conformal mappings to derive two general theorems yielding uniform asymptotics; specifically leverages steepest-descent-type methods adapted via conformal transformations to handle coalescing saddle points (Airy regime) and boundary-layer behavior near relativistic speed (gamma regime).

Result: Uniform asymptotic formulae for the wave function valid globally in the xt-plane; identification of two distinct transition regimes: one described by the Airy function (oscillatory ↔ exponential decay) and another by the gamma function (near light-speed limit); extension of the method to other integrals with similar singularity structures.

Conclusion: The asymptotic structure of the Feynman checkers model is richer than previously recognized, involving both Airy and gamma function transitions; the developed conformal-mapping framework provides a robust, general tool for uniform asymptotics in discrete quantum models with relativistic limits.

Abstract: We study the most elementary discrete model of an electron moving along a line, known as Feynman checkers, quantum walk, or the six-vertex model with special complex weights, and provide a uniform asymptotic formula for the wave function in the whole xt-plane. In addition to the well-known appearance of the Airy function that describes the transition between the oscillating and exponentially decaying regimes, we observe a second transition governed by the gamma function when the electron speed approaches the speed of light. We prove two general theorems producing uniform asymptotic formulae involving the Airy and gamma functions, using conformal mappings associated with the underlying integral representations. They are applicable in several other situations as well.

</details>


### [164] [Uniform Ionization Bounds in Molecular Thomas--Fermi--Dirac--von Weizsäcker Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30418)
*Shuang Wu*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves a universal bound on the maximal number of electrons $ N $ that a molecule with $ K $ nuclei and total nuclear charge $ Z $ can bind in the Thomas--Fermi--Dirac--von Weizsäcker (TFDW) model: $ N \leq Z + C_K $, resolving a long-standing ionization conjecture. It further establishes uniform bounds on screened potentials, electronic radii, and ionization energy for $ N \geq Z $.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the molecular large-charge ionization conjecture from Frank–Nam–Van Den Bosch (CPAM 2018), which asks whether a molecule with total nuclear charge $ Z $ can bind more than $ Z + C_K $ electrons—where $ C_K $ depends only on the number $ K $ of nuclei, not on their charges or positions.

Method: Rigorous variational analysis within the molecular TFDW theory; use of scaling properties, electrostatic inequalities (e.g., Lieb–Thirring, Hardy–Littewood–Sobolev), screening estimates, and comparison arguments with the simpler Thomas--Fermi (TF) model; careful treatment of multi-center Coulomb potentials and gradient (von Weizsäcker) and exchange (Dirac) corrections.

Result: For any fixed $ K $, there exists $ C_K > 0 $, independent of nuclear charges and positions, such that every TFDW minimizer with mass $ N $ satisfies $ N \leq Z + C_K $. For $ N \geq Z $, the screened potential and electronic radius of the TFDW minimizer are uniformly comparable to those of the neutral TF minimizer, and the ionization energy is uniformly bounded above.

Conclusion: The molecular TFDW theory obeys a universal ionization bound, confirming physical intuition that molecules cannot bind arbitrarily many extra electrons—only a nucleus-number-dependent excess—and establishing robustness of screening and size estimates across models.

Abstract: We study the molecular Thomas--Fermi--Dirac--von Weizsäcker theory. For each fixed number $K$ of nuclei, we prove that every minimizer of mass $N$ satisfies $N\leq Z+C_K$, where $Z$ is the total nuclear charge, and $C_K>0$ is independent of the nuclear charges and positions. This answers the molecular large-charge ionization question posed in [R. L. Frank, P. T. Nam and H. Van Den Bosch, CPAM, 2018]. For $N\geq Z$, we also compare the screened potentials and electronic radii of Thomas--Fermi--Dirac--von Weizsäcker minimizers with those of the corresponding neutral molecular Thomas--Fermi minimizers and establish a uniform bound on the ionization energy.

</details>


### [165] [Non-Perturbative Analysis of Resonances by a Contraction Mapping on Complex Domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30425)
*Toshimitsu Takaesu,Tamotsu Teruya*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies perturbations of embedded eigenvalues in an abstract Friedrichs–Lee Hamiltonian, proving the existence of resonance poles via contour deformation and a contraction mapping on complex domains.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how embedded eigenvalues behave under perturbation in Friedrichs–Lee models—a class of solvable quantum models with applications in open quantum systems and spectral theory.

Method: Contour deformation in the complex energy plane combined with construction of a contraction mapping on a suitably chosen complex domain to locate resonance poles.

Result: Existence of resonance poles near embedded eigenvalues under generic perturbations is rigorously established.

Conclusion: The spectral instability of embedded eigenvalues leads to resonances, confirming nontrivial analytic continuation of the resolvent and providing a rigorous foundation for resonance phenomena in Friedrichs–Lee systems.

Abstract: In this paper, we consider a perturbation problem of embedded eigenvalues for an abstract Friedrichs--Lee Hamiltonian and analyze its spectral properties. By deforming the contour and constructing a contraction mapping on complex domains, we prove the existence of resonance poles.

</details>


### [166] [For whom the Bell tolls: joint measurements and state updates in quantum field theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30468)
*Christopher J. Fewster,Christiane K. M. Klein*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives joint probability distributions for smeared field observables in quantum field theory using the Fewster-Verch measurement framework, and constructs a QFT-based Bell test (using a coupled Proca-scalar model) that violates the CHSH inequality—approaching Tsirelson’s bound when vacuum effects are suppressed.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile state update rules in algebraic quantum field theory (specifically the Fewster-Verch framework) with operational notions of measurement and nonlocality, particularly whether such updates support strongly quantum correlations like Bell violations.

Method: Uses the Fewster-Verch framework to define measurement-induced state updates via outcome probability distributions; derives the joint moment generating functional and unique joint probability distribution for commuting normal observables of the form A(f_i)*A(f_i) on a bosonic Fock space; applies the formalism to a recently proposed coupled Proca-scalar model for polarization measurements to construct a relativistic Bell test.

Result: Explicit joint probability distribution for number-type observables in QFT; successful construction of a QFT Bell test violating CHSH; violation approaches Tsirelson's bound (2√2) in the ideal limit of vacuum suppression.

Conclusion: The Fewster-Verch state update rules are compatible with maximal quantum nonlocality, reinforcing an informational interpretation of state reduction—i.e., updates reflect acquisition of measurement outcomes rather than physical collapse.

Abstract: State update rules and their behaviour in Bell tests play an important role in the study of measurement schemes and quantum information. We consider the state updates of the Fewster-Verch measurement framework for quantum field theory using their connection to the probability distribution of measurement outcomes. We focus on a set of observables of the form $A(f_i)^*A(f_i)$, where $A(f)$ is the (complex) smeared field operator for any theory induced by a formally hermitian, Green hyperbolic differential operator, and where the $(A(f_i))_{i=1, \dots, m}$ are assumed to be normal and mutually commuting. Under the assumption that these operators have a sufficiently nice representation on a bosonic Fock space, we derive the joint moment generating functional and the unique joint probability distribution for the observables $(A(f_i)^*A(f_i))_{i=1,\dots,m}$. As an application of these results, we construct a model of a Bell inequality test within quantum field theory using a recently developed coupled Proca-scalar model for polarization measurements. We show that our Bell test model can violate the CHSH inequality, approaching Tsirelson's bound for maximally entangled states in the limit where vacuum effects are perfectly rejected. These results show that the state update rules derived in the Fewster-Verch framework are compatible with strongly quantum correlations. They also support a viewpoint in which state updates should be understood in relation to available information.

</details>


### [167] [Density of states for random Schrödinger operators with linearly correlated heavy-tailed disorder](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30529)
*Margherita Disertori,Luca Fresta,Liza Schonlau*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes smoothness of the density of states and Wegner estimates for random Schrödinger operators with linearly correlated, heavy-tailed disorder—especially with mixed-sign correlations—using a supersymmetric integration-by-parts method applicable to α-stable and other heavy-tailed distributions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend rigorous spectral analysis to heavy-tailed disordered systems with nontrivial (e.g., mixed-sign) spatial correlations, motivated by connections to hyperbolic geometry in averaged Green's function analysis.

Method: Integration-by-parts technique within a supersymmetric (SUSY) formalism applied to the density of states; handles linearly correlated disorder and general correlation kernels including mixed-sign ones.

Result: Proof of smoothness (C^∞) of the density of states and Wegner-type Lipschitz continuity estimates in the strong-disorder regime.

Conclusion: The SUSY-based integration-by-parts method robustly yields regularity and localization-related estimates for heavy-tailed, linearly correlated random potentials—including α-stable laws—bridging stochastic spectral theory and geometric aspects of Green's function averaging.

Abstract: We study the density of states of random Schrödinger operators with linearly correlated heavy-tailed disorder. Using an integration-by-parts supersymmetric representation, we prove smoothness of the density of states and Wegner estimates in the strong-disorder regime. We are particularly interested in mixed-sign correlation kernels, which serve as a toy model for the hyperbolic structures arising in the analysis of averaged squared Green's functions. Our approach applies to a broad class of correlation kernels and disorder distributions, including $\alpha$-stable laws.

</details>


### [168] [Reproducing kernel and matrix representation of the deformed quantum optical density operator of $\mathcal{R}(p,q)-$super-coherent states on $\mathcal{B}_0\oplus\mathcal{B}_1$ fermionic quantum algebra](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30576)
*Kawèyim Lankpetre,Isiaka Aremua,Joseph Désiré Bukweli Kyemba,Bienvenu Gnim Adewi*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs and analyzes R(p,q)-super-coherent states with two Grassmann variables on a graded superalgebra, derives the total density operator as a mixture over four Fock sectors, establishes sectorial reproducing kernels satisfying key analytic properties, and demonstrates intelligent-state behavior under Berezin quantization in the bosonic sector.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize coherent states to the framework of R(p,q)-deformed quantum algebras with supersymmetry, enabling a unified treatment of bosonic and fermionic degrees of freedom via graded structures and nonstandard derivatives/integrations.

Method: Constructs R(p,q)-super-coherent states on a Z2-graded superalgebra B0⊕B1; decomposes the graded super-Fock space into four sectors; uses R(p,q)-derivative, angular integration, and Berezin integration to obtain diagonal representations of the density operator per sector; defines sectorial reproducing kernels from resolution of identity; computes matrix elements and verifies hermiticity, positivity, and multiplication properties; applies Berezin quantization in the bosonic sector.

Result: Explicit total density operator expressed as sum of four sectorial contributions; compact unified form using sectorial parametric functions; well-defined sectorial reproducing kernels with proven analytic properties (hermiticity, positivity, series expansion of diagonal values); integral relation linking density operator, density function, and kernel; quantized operators and mean values for basic observables; super-coherent states shown to be intelligent states via dispersion analysis of super-position and super-momentum operators.

Conclusion: The R(p,q)-super-coherent states provide a consistent, deformation- and supersymmetry-compatible generalization of coherent states, supporting a full quantum mechanical framework—including resolution of identity, kernel theory, and intelligent-state characterization—within the R(p,q)-deformed superalgebraic setting.

Abstract: We study the $\mathcal{R}(p,q)-$super-coherent states with two Grassmann variables $(\zeta_1,\zeta_2)$ built on the superalgebra $\mathcal{B}_0\oplus\mathcal{B}_1$ associated with the $\mathcal{R}(p,q)-$deformed quantum algebras of Hounkonnou and Bukweli-Kyemba, the two dilation parameters $\phi_1(p,q)$ and $\phi_2(p,q)$ being meromorphic functions of two variables evaluated at $(p,q)$. The graded super-Fock space splits into four sectors: bosonic, $\phi_1-$fermionic, $\phi_2-$fermionic and bi-fermionic. In each of them we establish the diagonal representation of the density operator by means of the $\mathcal{R}(p,q)-$derivative, angular integration and Berezin integration, and we give a unified compact form in terms of sectorial parametric functions. The total density operator is obtained in explicit form as the sum of the four sectorial contributions, and can be read as a mixture of the four sectors. We define the sectorial reproducing kernels, obtained from the resolution of the identity, we compute the matrix elements of the density operator in the super-coherent state basis, and we prove the hermiticity, positivity and matrix multiplication properties of the kernels, together with the series expansion of their diagonal values. An integral formulation relates the density operator, its density function and the reproducing kernel, the latter being the function associated with the identity operator. Berezin quantization in the bosonic sector yields the quantized operators associated with $z$, $\bar z$, $|z|^2$, $z^2$ and $\bar z^2$ and their mean values; the dispersions of the super-position and super-momentum operators show that the super-coherent states are intelligent states.

</details>


### [169] [Gaudin's determinantal norm formula and Hall--Littlewood polynomials](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30987)
*Jan Felipe van Diejen*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This note proves a conjectured Gaudin-type determinantal formula for the quadratic norms of Hall–Littlewood polynomials, which are orthogonal with respect to a discrete measure arising from a discretized Lieb–Liniger model for q-bosons on the circle.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a previously conjectured Gaudin-type determinantal formula for the quadratic norms of Hall–Littlewood polynomials.

Method: Analytical derivation leveraging orthogonality properties and connections to the discretized Lieb–Liniger model for q-bosons.

Result: Proof of the conjectured determinantal formula for the quadratic norms of Hall–Littlewood polynomials.

Conclusion: The conjectured Gaudin-type determinantal expression for the quadratic norms is rigorously confirmed, strengthening the link between Hall–Littlewood polynomials and integrable quantum models.

Abstract: The Hall--Littlewood polynomials are orthogonal with respect to a discrete measure stemming from a discretization of the Lieb--Liniger model that describes $q$-bosons on the circle. This note establishes a previously conjectured Gaudin-type determinantal formula for their quadratic norms.

</details>


### [170] [Symmetries, conservation laws, series solutions and singularity formation for the spherically symmetric Euler-Poisson equations in mass coordinates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31005)
*Adnan Shamaoon*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes point symmetries, conservation laws, and exact solutions of the spherically symmetric Euler-Poisson equations for polytropic gases in Lagrangian mass coordinates, identifying critical behavior at γ = 4/3, deriving analytic expansions for collapse solutions (including Puiseux at the surface), characterizing density singularities and shell-crossing, and solving linear perturbations via spherical Bessel functions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically understand symmetry structure, conservation laws, and exact dynamics of gravitational collapse in non-isentropic polytropic gases—particularly how entropy profiles and adiabatic index γ influence self-similarity, stability, singularity formation, and perturbation spectra.

Method: Lie group analysis of the reduced second-order PDE for r(t,m); application of Noether’s theorem to identify variational scalings; construction of exact collapse and self-similar solutions; use of Briot-Bouquet theory for analyticity proofs; Puiseux expansion derivation at the stellar surface; rigorous singularity classification via zeros of r and r_m; linear stability analysis yielding spherical Bessel eigenmodes.

Result: Complete classification of point symmetries; identification of entropy-dependent scaling invariance peaking at γ = 4/3; variational scalings only for S ∝ m^{14−10γ}; virial identities otherwise; convergent analytic expansion at regular center; Puiseux exponent 1/(γ−1) at surface; Hopf-type fold law and exact shell-crossing criterion for dust; linear perturbations decouple into spherical Bessel modes with eigenvalues satisfying 3κ² + κ − 2 + μ = 0; all symbolic results verified by computer algebra, numerics validated via second-order Lagrangian scheme.

Conclusion: The symmetry and analytic structure of gravitational collapse in polytropic gases is tightly constrained by γ and S(m), with γ = 4/3 acting as a critical bifurcation point for scaling invariance; exact solutions and perturbation spectra are fully characterized, revealing deep connections between entropy gradients, virial balance, singularity formation, and mode stability.

Abstract: Written in Lagrangian mass coordinates, the spherically symmetric Euler-Poisson equations for a polytropic gas become a single second-order equation for the radius $r(t,m)$ of the shell enclosing mass $m$, with adiabatic index $\gamma>1$ and entropy profile $S(m)$. We determine all point symmetries of this equation. Apart from time translation there is at most one scaling, except at the critical index $\gamma=4/3$, where a scaling exists for every entropy profile; these scalings are the entropy-fixed stabilisers of the two-parameter scaling group of the non-isentropic system, and we give the exact symmetry defect when $\gamma\neq4/3$. Noether's theorem singles out the profiles $S=Km^{14-10\gamma}$, for which a scaling is variational and gives a conservation law beyond energy; otherwise the scalings yield virial identities. We construct exact collapse solutions and reduce the self-similar problem to one ordinary differential equation. Using the Briot-Bouquet theorem we prove that the regular centre of self-similar and homologous collapse carries a convergent analytic expansion with free central amplitude, and we derive a Puiseux expansion at the stellar surface whose exponent is the polytropic index $1/(\gamma-1)$. In mass coordinates a density singularity is a zero of $r_m$ or of $r$; for the first kind we prove a Hopf-type fold law and an exact shell-crossing criterion for free-falling dust. Finally, linear perturbations of the critical Einstein-de Sitter collapse separate exactly into spherical Bessel profiles, with exponents solving $3\kappa^2+\kappa-2+\mu=0$. All identities were checked by computer algebra, and the numerical statements rest on convergence studies of a second-order Lagrangian scheme, including a collapse with a free surface.

</details>
